# PAYMENT-END-TO-END-1 Unified Execution Risks (2026-07-17)

- MITIGATED: The PAYMENT-INTENTS-1 residual gap for workflow-to-dispatch wiring is closed at source level. Workflow output now executes through `PaymentWorkflowExecutionService`, `PaymentIntentDispatcher`, canonical handlers, SQL Order persistence, and `IEventPublisher`.
- MITIGATED: Duplicate persistence/event risk for repeated canonical intent execution is closed at source level by collapsing already-applied Order status intents before `SaveChanges` and before Order Domain event publication.
- ACCEPTED: Durable intent-execution ledgering, live channel drills, projection-lag dashboards, environment proof, and provider/channel production rollout remain future operational certification work. Current correctness uses SQL Order state plus existing outbox/consumer idempotency.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/Intents/PaymentIntentInfrastructure.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/PaymentEndToEnd1CertificationTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/PAYMENT_END_TO_END_1_CERTIFICATION.md`

# PAYMENT-INTENTS-1 Infrastructure Risks (2026-07-17)

- MITIGATED: Dispatcher business-logic drift is blocked by a source-certified `PaymentIntentDispatcher` that only resolves registered handlers and rejects missing/duplicate registrations.
- MITIGATED: Provider/channel coupling is blocked in the implemented intent handlers by canonical `IPaymentWorkflowIntent` input and source guards forbidding provider, UI, Storefront, CommerceHub, Affiliate, notification, and direct RabbitMQ dependencies.
- MITIGATED: Payment lifecycle ownership drift is blocked by handlers mutating only Order status through the authorized order-status boundary and refusing rejection after money recognition; `PaymentStatus` remains owned by `IPaymentLifecycleService`.
- RESIDUAL: Durable intent-execution forensics, operational dashboards, environment proof, and live channel/provider rollout remain future production-readiness scope. Workflow-to-dispatch wiring and replay collapse are source-certified by `PAYMENT-END-TO-END-1`.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/Intents/PaymentIntentInfrastructure.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/Intents/PaymentIntentInfrastructureServiceCollectionExtensions.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/PaymentIntentInfrastructureCertificationTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/PAYMENT_INTENTS_1_INFRASTRUCTURE.md`

# PAYMENT-1.2 Payment Policy Evaluation Pipeline Risks (2026-07-10)

- MITIGATED BY DESIGN: Pipeline-stage drift is blocked by a canonical deterministic stage order from evaluation admission through audit recording, decision return, and completion.
- MITIGATED BY DESIGN: Evaluation-local state cannot become authority because `PipelineContext` is limited to one evaluation and remains separate from immutable `PolicyContext`, source-owned facts, Runtime state, Provider authority, and Governance authority.
- MITIGATED BY DESIGN: Silent allow is blocked by fail-closed handling for missing required facts, invalid authority, version mismatch, missing mandatory policy/rule, replay failure, audit failure, unknown capability, unknown constraint, dependency cycles, unresolved conflicts, non-deterministic rules, and side-effect attempts.
- MITIGATED BY DESIGN: Conflict drift is blocked by explicit precedence where invalid authority/replay/version/policy/rule/audit failures, compliance/fraud/eligibility denies, quarantine, recovery, manual review, retry, alternatives, required evidence, constrained allow, and allow resolve deterministically.
- ACCEPTED: PAYMENT-1.2 is architecture only. Policy persistence, governance workflow implementation, audit storage, replay tooling, runtime invocation, operational controls, parallel execution implementation, and provider-specific regression suites remain future phases.
- RESIDUAL: Production implementation must prevent mutable `PipelineContext` leakage, draft/unpublished policy selection, non-authoritative fact ingestion, audit-after-allow shortcuts, hidden runtime fallback, adapter/provider side effects during evaluation, non-deterministic parallel rule execution, incomplete conflict traces, and missing replay artifacts.

# PAYMENT-1.1 Payment Policy Framework Risks (2026-07-10)

- MITIGATED BY DESIGN: Policy growth cannot require Runtime redesign when new behavior enters through governed facts, rule catalog versions, policy versions, immutable versioning, and replay-safe audit.
- MITIGATED BY DESIGN: Rule/fact ownership drift is blocked by the framework rule that facts are authoritative inputs and rules never own, fetch, mutate, or repair facts.
- MITIGATED BY DESIGN: Policy replay drift is blocked by immutable policy, rule, catalog, fact schema, evaluation, audit, and replay versions plus input snapshot and decision hash audit requirements.
- ACCEPTED: PAYMENT-1.1 is architecture only. Policy persistence, authoring workflows, governance UI, replay tooling, runtime invocation, operational controls, and provider-specific regression suites remain future phases.
- RESIDUAL: Production implementation must prevent draft leakage, unapproved publication, self-approval, stale effective-date selection, missing-version replay, non-authoritative fact ingestion, hidden runtime branching, adapter policy edits, and incomplete audit snapshots.

## PASS T8 Treasury Analytics Authority

- status: MITIGATED for inspected source paths; analytics remains derivative and non-monetary.
- impact: No inspected T8 runtime blocker remains. Treasury analytics owns derivative dashboards, exports, summaries, and projection freshness checks only; SQL ledger and CommerceHub financial transactions remain the financial authority.
- residual risk: Production dashboard freshness, alerting, query-plan, and environment evidence remain operational hardening work.

## PASS T6 Treasury Reconciliation Authority

- status: MITIGATED for inspected source paths; reconciliation remains derivative and non-monetary.
- impact: No inspected T6 runtime blocker remains. Treasury reconciliation can detect bank/provider/ledger drift and force quarantine or certified recovery, but cannot create money or complete money movement without certified SQL authority.
- residual risk: Production reconciliation alert packs, external evidence retention, bank statement import proof, and chaos drills remain operational hardening work.

- residual risk: Deployment-time currency parity scans and production alerting remain operational hardening work.

## PASS 16.5F-B CommerceHub publication currency policy enforced; DB constraints and chaos proof remain incomplete

- impact: Non-EGP catalog price authority is rejected at CommerceHub publication boundaries. Publish, republish, listing update, bulk publish, bulk listing update, replay, recovery, auto-healing, catch-up reindex, tenant reindex, and global reindex cannot restore live CommerceHub visibility for non-EGP-priced products.
- current containment: `CommerceHubPublicationCurrencyPolicy` validates publication/update commands; merchant bulk operations validate visibility-increasing/listing-update operations per row; frontend publication UI disables blocked rows and shows the EGP-only rule; `CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay` and reindex expected-count filtering converge recovery/replay to EGP-only visibility.
- residual risk: There is still no database-level publication-currency constraint, no production SQL/Mongo/Elasticsearch currency parity scanner, and no PostgreSQL-backed mixed-currency chaos harness for publish, replay, recovery, reindex, cart, checkout, refund, payout, and ledger flows.
- prevention rule: Do not add CommerceHub publish, republish, bulk publish, listing update, replay, recovery, reindex, projection, frontend, or validation paths that can create or refresh live CommerceHub visibility unless current product price currency authority is EGP. Current catalog price currency authority is `Tenant.Currency` until a product/variant currency authority is introduced and certified.

## PASS 16.5C CommerceHub COD risk-control runtime certifiable; COD monitoring and chaos proof remain incomplete

- impact: CommerceHub can block financially unsafe vendors from new COD-risk exposure on inspected paths. Vendor COD commission debt is SQL-ledger-derived, hard-limit suspension occurs at `amountOwed >= 1000 EGP`, checkout rejects suspended vendors, and public discovery/search/detail/homepage/recommendation-like surfaces suppress financially suspended vendors.

- residual risk: Public launch still lacks deployment-verified COD debt threshold alerts, suspended-checkout attempt dashboards, visibility-suppression drift dashboards, repayment approval anomaly alerts, production query-plan review, and a PostgreSQL-backed chaos harness for concurrent checkout, COD delivery/debt creation, settlement, repayment approval, refund reversal, replay, and recovery races.
- prevention rule: Do not introduce CommerceHub COD debt, threshold, suspension, reactivation, checkout, discovery, repayment, settlement, refund, dashboard, recovery, replay, monitoring, or frontend paths that treat frontend state, MongoDB, Redis, Elasticsearch, dashboard counters, event payloads, or cached visibility as risk-control authority. Preserve SQL-ledger debt recomposition, exact-threshold crossing evidence, hard-limit suspension, checkout rejection, public visibility suppression, existing-order continuity, and PASS 16.5C regression freezes.

## PASS 16.3 CommerceHub cart runtime certifiable; monitoring and cart-ledger proof remain incomplete

- impact: Customers can build, maintain, price, and validate CommerceHub carts through the certified runtime path. Invalid product, variant, price, and inventory state cannot survive backend sanitation, pricing, or checkout authority checks.
- current containment: `CommerceHubCartService` sanitizes saved/authenticated carts against current SQL listing/product/variant/tenant/stock/price/publication-visibility authority (including `IsOnlineVisible` and `PublishStatus == Published`); `CommerceHubCheckoutService` locks/version-checks the persisted cart and recomputes price, stock, shipping, promo, vendor, readiness, and totals before order creation; frontend guest-to-customer adoption preserves the guest snapshot until backend persistence succeeds.

- prevention rule: Do not treat frontend cart totals, local storage, price snapshots, displayed stock, or derivative product DTOs as cart authority. Future cart, merge, pricing, inventory, checkout handoff, telemetry, or UX changes must preserve backend SQL recomposition, customer ownership, optimistic version conflict behavior, checkout locks, sanitization publication visibility parity, and PASS 16.3 regression freezes.

- prevention rule: Do not add homepage, featured, promoted, search, autocomplete, category, filter, product-card, detail, recommendation, SEO, or deep-link code that returns product visibility from Elasticsearch, MongoDB, Redis, homepage JSON, analytics, or frontend state without current SQL authority recomposition.

## PASS 16.1 CommerceHub customer identity launch remains blocked

- impact: Customers can register, sign in, use authenticated order/refund surfaces, and transition a guest cart into an account, but they cannot recover a forgotten password, verify mailbox ownership, edit their profile, change credentials, or investigate identity incidents to a launch-grade standard.
- current containment: PASS 16.1 made registration transactional across customer and governance-profile creation, maps concurrent normalized-email insertion races to `EMAIL_EXISTS`, rejects suspended customers during login and JWT validation, and preserves guest cart contents until authenticated adoption completes. Existing JWTs remain HttpOnly-cookie-backed, CSRF-protected, JTI-revocable, and subordinate to the durable session cutoff.
- launch blockers: CommerceHub password recovery; email verification and resend; profile update; password and email change; customer-facing revoke-all UX; append-only SQL identity lifecycle evidence; identity-specific metrics, dashboards, and alerts; runtime chaos coverage for reset, verification, and email-change races.
- prevention rule: Do not classify CommerceHub customer identity as launch-ready until every PASS 16.1 blocker in `docs/architecture/customer-identity-certification-findings.md` is implemented, tested, and deliberately reclassified. Preserve SQL customer authority, transactional registration, suspended-account cutoff, and guest-cart adoption behavior.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubCustomerAuthService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CustomerTokenRevocationService.cs`, `PointOfSale.CommerceHub/src/store/cart-store.ts`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CustomerIdentityCertificationTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CustomerSessionLifecycleCertificationTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CustomerRecoveryCertificationTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/customer-identity-certification-findings.md`

## PRODUCTION READINESS CLOSURE 1 evidence database hardening implemented; deployment verification required

- impact: Ordinary application, worker, reporting, and maintenance identities can no longer rewrite or delete certified PostgreSQL forensic evidence after the closure migration and role provisioning are applied.
- current containment: `20260601190000_EvidenceDatabaseHardening` installs irreversible PostgreSQL triggers rejecting `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, and `TRUNCATE`. `ProvisionForensicEvidenceRoles.sql` transfers evidence ownership away from runtime identities and grants runtime writers `SELECT` / `INSERT` only. `VerifyForensicEvidenceDatabaseHardening.sql` detects ACL, trigger, and inventory drift and probes direct-SQL rejection.
- residual risk: Production still needs provisioning execution and retained verification evidence. A PostgreSQL superuser or reviewed migration identity can deliberately disable protections. External WORM/SIEM retention, outage-surviving tenant-audit handoff, alert packs, and backup/restore/operator rehearsal evidence remain launch blockers.
- prevention rule: Do not claim environment closure until provisioning and verification output are retained. Future evidence inventory, migration, retention, tooling, or role changes must update the closure scripts, tests, findings, and `.ai-context` freeze together.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Migrations/20260601190000_EvidenceDatabaseHardening.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Scripts/ProvisionForensicEvidenceRoles.sql`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Scripts/VerifyForensicEvidenceDatabaseHardening.sql`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/EvidenceDatabaseHardeningCertificationTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/evidence-database-hardening-certification-findings.md`

Date: 2026-05-27

This file consolidates current system risks from code and architecture documents.
Similar findings are merged. Already mitigated issues are listed once.

## CONFIRMED_RISK

No currently validated confirmed corruption risk remains from the 2026-05-12 (completed) system-of-systems full-stack adversarial audit revalidation after the latest hardening pass.

### Dangerous-primitive governance is restored on the reviewed guard boundary, but still partial repo-wide
- title: The focused architecture guard is green again for reviewed raw-SQL/idempotency primitives and adjacent dangerous primitives, but future bypasses are still possible outside the explicitly governed boundary
- affected systems: `CrossSystemAuthorityRegressionGuardTests`, raw SQL idempotency/locking helpers in `ProductVariantsController`, `SalesReturnsController`, `CommerceHubFinanceReconciliationJob`, infrastructure fence internals in `ProjectionEventFence`, and dev-only schema repair SQL in `TestAuthController`
- root cause: The architecture source guard that blocks new direct SQL/bulk mutation paths relies on a static allowlist/exclusion model, and the broader codebase still contains specialized direct Mongo/ES/cache primitives that cannot be made universally safe by convention alone.
- impact: The previously red direct-SQL governance defect is mitigated, and the same guard file now also fences reviewed Tier-1 `ReplaceOneAsync`, raw Elasticsearch client, and Redis pattern invalidation paths. This restores enforcement credibility on the inspected boundary, but does not mean every specialized projection in the repo is globally auto-governed.
- mitigation status: Mitigated on 2026-05-21 for the reviewed source-guard boundary. Focused tests `CrossSystemAuthorityRegressionGuardTests`, `GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests`, `EventOutboxConsistencyArchitectureTests`, and `CheckoutConcurrencyHardeningSourceTests` passed locally after the allowlists and dedicated assertions were aligned.
- residual operational risk: Future raw-SQL additions, full-document replacements, raw ES writes, or cache fanout can still be normalized or copied into new files if engineers do not extend the focused allowlists and reviewed-pattern assertions in the same change. Some dangerous primitives must remain available for development tooling, specialized search projections, and local reconciliation/locking flows.
- prevention rule: Any approved raw-SQL locking/idempotency/reconciliation primitive, Tier-1 full-document `ReplaceOneAsync`, raw Elasticsearch client write, or Redis pattern invalidation fanout must update the relevant architecture-test allowlist and dedicated reviewed-pattern assertion in the same change. Future Tier-1 flows must still prefer existing authority services/writers and must not treat low-level primitives as an alternative governance model.
- references: `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemAuthorityRegressionGuardTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/ProductVariantsController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/SalesReturnsController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubFinanceReconciliationJob.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/ProjectionEventFence.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/TestAuthController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ServiceDiscoveryProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Development/CommerceHub/DevelopmentCommerceHubSeedService.cs`

## LIKELY_RISK

No currently validated likely-risk remains from the 2026-05-12 (completed) system-of-systems full-stack adversarial audit revalidation after the latest hardening pass.

Audit note:
- The 2026-05-11 full-stack pass explicitly revalidated tenant resolution, storefront checkout/payment authority, CommerceHub checkout/payment recovery, critical SQL-backed projection repair, payout queue operator authority, customer/session revocation boundaries, and frontend/backend retry-state contracts.
- The 2026-05-12 (completed) revalidation rechecked the same hardened boundaries against the live worktree, validated one additional duplicate-projection freshness gap in critical storefront/CommerceHub order reads, and the current worktree closes that path after focused architecture validation.
- A 2026-05-12 follow-up revalidated the broader architecture guardrail suite after aligning stale source assertions with the current SQL-order, fail-closed promotion, and EGP-only vendor-repayment authority boundaries.
- The current worktree did not produce a second evidence-backed permanent inconsistency, duplicate-financial-operation, or cross-tenant leakage finding beyond the mitigated and accepted-tradeoff entries captured below.

## MITIGATED_RISK

### CommerceHub price override persistence is audit-ready while activation remains disabled
- title: CommerceHub override storage, attribution metadata, and derivative diagnostics are prepared without changing runtime price authority
- affected systems: `CommerceHubListing`, `ApplicationDbContext`, CommerceHub listing migration, `CommerceHubProjectionConsumer`, `CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay`, CommerceHub Mongo documents, CommerceHub Elasticsearch documents, cart, checkout, and `CommerceHubPriceAuthority`
- root cause: PASS 13.3 centralized item-price resolution but future controlled override activation still lacked SQL attribution fields, migration strategy, derivative metadata, and regression coverage proving preparation did not activate override pricing.
- impact: PASS 13.4 adds nullable SQL override-audit metadata and derivative `PriceSource` / `OverridePresent` diagnostics while preserving disabled overrides. Final CommerceHub item price remains current SQL `ProductVariant.EffectiveOnlinePrice`; `CommerceHubPriceAuthority.OverridesEnabled` remains `false`; `CommerceHubListings.CommerceHubPrice` remains rejected, cleared, and constrained to null.
- mitigation status: Runtime prep, migration, certification documentation, regression-freeze tests, and `.ai-context` governance updates added on 2026-05-31.
- residual operational risk: The platform is not ready for controlled activation. Merchant manage permission, marketplace audit/emergency-disable permissions, merchant mutation workflow, admin UI, immutable audit trail, activation flags, cart/checkout/search/projection parity testing, dashboards, alerting, migration preflight, and rollback remain required.
- prevention rule: Future override activation or override-adjacent pricing changes must keep customer-visible pricing unchanged until activation is explicitly certified, preserve SQL variant-price authority, and update PASS 13.4 tests/docs in the same change.
- references: `PointOfSale.Core/Entities/CommerceHubListing.cs`, `PointOfSale.Core/Pricing/CommerceHubPriceAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Migrations/20260531134000_CommerceHubPriceOverridePersistenceAudit.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CommerceHubPriceOverridePersistenceCertificationTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/commercehub-price-override-persistence-audit-certification-findings.md`

### CommerceHub price resolution is centralized while overrides remain disabled
- title: Reviewed CommerceHub publish, cart, checkout, projection, search, replay, recovery, reindex, and auto-healing price paths now route through `CommerceHubPriceAuthority` without changing runtime pricing
- affected systems: `CommerceHubPriceAuthority`, `CommerceHubCommandHandlers`, `CommerceHubCartService`, `CommerceHubCheckoutService`, `CommerceHubProjectionConsumer`, `CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay`, CommerceHub query/detail hydration, reindex, and auto-healing
- root cause: PASS 13.2 froze the future resolver shape, but current pricing logic was still distributed across handlers, cart, checkout, projection, and search overlay code.
- impact: PASS 13.3 introduces the centralized resolver while preserving the current final price as `ProductVariant.EffectiveOnlinePrice`. Override-shaped output exists for future work, but `OverridesEnabled` is false and `CommerceHubPrice` remains disabled/cleared/constrained.
- mitigation status: Runtime resolver adoption, certification tests, documentation, and `.ai-context` governance updates added on 2026-05-31.
- residual operational risk: Future override activation still requires explicit PASS 13.4 governance, persistence, UI, migration, replay/recovery, and parity certification before rollout.
- prevention rule: Future CommerceHub pricing paths must use `CommerceHubPriceAuthority`, keep overrides disabled until certified, and update PASS 13.3 tests/docs in the same change.

### Marketplace governance precedence now has a documented certification boundary
- title: Reviewed CommerceHub merchant intent, suppression, vendor governance, category/product governance, search/discovery, replay, recovery, auditability, and chaos behavior are certified as subordinate to current SQL marketplace governance authority
- affected systems: `CommerceHubListing`, `CommerceHubCommandHandlers`, `CommerceHubVendorVisibilityService`, `CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay`, `CommerceHubReindexService`, CommerceHub public query/search/detail paths, homepage/discovery sanitation, and auto-healing
- root cause: PASS 12.1 through PASS 12.5 certified publication, taxonomy, attributes, pricing, variants, and inventory, but governance precedence needed one explicit boundary. Public query paths filtered suspended/banned vendors, and suspension normally hid listings, but shared search overlay and SQL-visible reindex identity scans did not independently suppress current suspended/banned vendor governance when stale SQL listing intent still claimed publication.
- impact: PASS 12.6 documents governance inventory, merchant-vs-governance precedence, suppression, vendor governance, category/product governance, search/discovery, replay/recovery, auditability, and chaos findings. Runtime hardening makes `CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay` and CommerceHub reindex SQL-visible identities fail closed for vendors whose current SQL governance profile is `Suspended` or `Banned`.

- residual operational risk: The listing lifecycle remains boolean-based, and no immutable governance ledger, universal SQL-vs-derivative parity scanner, universal chaos harness, SIEM-grade audit sink, generated governance registry, or path-specific certification for future feed/export/support/ranking/global-script visibility writers was introduced.
- prevention rule: Future merchant publication, listing governance, vendor reactivation, suppression, search, discovery, replay, recovery, reindex, feed/export, support, analytics, ranking, localization, or global-script paths must preserve current SQL governance precedence, suppress suspended/banned vendor governance before public visibility, clear stale restore intent when SQL authority is gone, and update PASS 12.6 tests/docs in the same change.

- affected systems: `CommerceHubListing`, `AdminCommerceHubController`, `CommerceHubCommandHandlers`, `CommerceHubVendorVisibilityService`, `CommerceHubProjectionConsumer`, `CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay`, `CommerceHubReindexService`, `CommerceHubAutoHealingService`, CommerceHub query/search/homepage surfaces
- root cause: PASS 11.3 certified marketplace governance decisions, but publication intent needed an explicit boundary proving merchant requests cannot bypass governance or resurrect visibility. Tenant unpublish also reused the publish/update governance gate, which could block withdrawal of publication intent after governance restriction.
- impact: PASS 12.1 documents publication inventory, lifecycle, merchant intent, governance, product authority, ownership, replay/recovery, search/discovery, auditability, and chaos findings. Runtime hardening keeps publish/update governance-controlled while allowing tenant-owned unpublish to clear intent because it can only reduce visibility.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardening, source-freeze tests, certification documentation, and `.ai-context` governance updates added on 2026-05-30.
- residual operational risk: The current listing lifecycle remains boolean-based and no immutable publication ledger, generated publication registry, universal parity scanner, universal chaos harness, enterprise moderation workflow, or path-specific certification for future bulk/feed/export/support/ranking/global-script publication paths was introduced.
- prevention rule: Publication intent is request authority only. Future publish, unpublish, republish, update, clone, bulk publish, bulk unpublish, replay, recovery, reindex, feed/export, cache-warmer, support, ranking, analytics, or global-script paths that touch CommerceHub publication must declare authority owner, request owner, enforcement owner, SQL authority checks, replay behavior, auditability, and PASS 12.1 tests/docs in the same change.

### Super-admin identity and session authority now has a documented SQL-authoritative certification boundary
- title: Reviewed platform-operator login, refresh, logout, token-version replay, governance restriction, password rotation, middleware routing, legacy endpoint scope, auditability, and chaos behavior are certified against current SQL super-admin authority
- affected systems: `SuperAdminAuthService`, `SuperAdminPlatformAuthority`, `SuperAdminPlatformAuthorityMiddleware`, `SuperAdminOperationalAuditMiddleware`, `UserGovernanceService`, `SuperAdminManagementService`, legacy `MarketplaceController` service reindex/diagnostics, `AdminManualPaymentController`

- prevention rule: Do not return public search, autocomplete, SEO, sitemap, feed/export, structured-data, or public-index visibility from Elasticsearch, MongoDB, Redis, replay payloads, or rebuild payloads unless current SQL authority still accepts the entity.

### Homepage, discovery, recommendations, and affiliate discovery now revalidate current SQL authority

- affected systems: `StorefrontHomeService`, `HomepageSectionReadService`, `MarketplaceHomepageReadModelProjectionService`, `MarketplaceReadController`, `AffiliateProductDiscoveryService`, `PublicAffiliateProductsController`, homepage Redis caches, CommerceHub homepage Mongo projections, affiliate Elasticsearch documents

Storefront homepage and CommerceHub homepage protections remain SQL-first.

### Product detail, media, search hydration, and product SEO now revalidate current SQL authority
- title: Stale Redis, Mongo, Elasticsearch, sitemap, or legacy variant-image state can no longer decide reviewed storefront product detail, media, search hydration, or SEO visibility
- affected systems: `StorefrontProductService`, `StorefrontProductsController`, `ProductSearchService`, `ProductDiscoveryService`, `SeoService`, `ProductSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay`, storefront product Redis caches, Mongo `ProductDocument`, Elasticsearch product documents, product sitemap/slug/SEO caches
- root cause: Public product detail/list reads, product SEO/breadcrumb/sitemap/slug surfaces, and storefront search/discovery hydration could use derivative product documents or search hits as final return authority after SQL product/media/variant/publication state changed.

Feed/export/cache-warmer/CDN purge/support/global-script paths remain path-specific certification debt.
- prevention rule: Do not return, cache, hydrate, index, rebuild, or repair public product/detail/media/SEO visibility from Redis, MongoDB, Elasticsearch, CDN URLs, replay payloads, legacy product image fields, or legacy variant image strings unless current SQL product/media/variant/category/unit/publication and tenant authority still accepts the product.

### Storefront category, navigation, breadcrumb, and category SEO visibility now revalidate current SQL lineage
- title: Stale Redis, Mongo, navigation, breadcrumb, or sitemap state can no longer decide reviewed storefront category public structure after SQL category lineage changes
- affected systems: `StorefrontCategoryService`, `StorefrontNavigationController`, `SeoService`, Redis category/navigation/SEO caches, Mongo category/navigation projections, category breadcrumbs, category sitemap URLs, category slug resolution
- root cause: Public category tree/by-slug reads, category SEO/breadcrumb/sitemap generation, and navigation category links could use derivative Redis/Mongo/navigation state as return authority even after current SQL category lineage was deleted, orphaned, cyclic, self-referential, or missing a parent.
- impact: POS-ADMIN-CERTIFICATION-01 PASS 9.2 now requires active tenant authority plus current SQL category lineage before reviewed category tree, category route, category SEO, category sitemap, category breadcrumb, or navigation category-link visibility is returned.

- prevention rule: Do not return or rebuild storefront category structure, category routes, category SEO, breadcrumbs, or category navigation visibility from Redis, MongoDB, Elasticsearch, navigation JSON, sitemap caches, route caches, or replay payloads unless current SQL category lineage still accepts the category.

### Storefront CMS public visibility now revalidates current SQL authority
- title: Stale Redis, Mongo, sitemap, or Elasticsearch CMS state can no longer decide storefront CMS public visibility on the reviewed surfaces
- affected systems: `CmsPageReadService`, `SeoService`, `StorefrontSearchController`, `AdminSearchSyncController.SyncCmsPages`, `cmspages` Elasticsearch index, Redis CMS/sitemap caches, storefront CMS public routes

Cached CMS slug results revalidate active tenant lineage, CMS projection publish replay mirrors the same SQL publication predicate, and tenant-admin CMS search repair now indexes current SQL-published pages and deletes tenant-scoped CMS search documents missing from SQL authority.

- prevention rule: Do not return or rebuild storefront CMS visibility from Redis, MongoDB, Elasticsearch, sitemap caches, route caches, SEO state, or replay payloads unless current SQL CMS publication and tenant authority still accepts the page. Missing or invalid SQL authority must fail closed by absence or stale derivative deletion.

### Legacy service-marketplace search recovery now requires current service, tenant, and provider-profile authority
- title: Replayed service events and global service-search drift repair can no longer restore public `marketplace_services` visibility from payloads or raw active service ids alone
- affected systems: `ServiceDiscoveryProjectionConsumer`, `ElasticsearchDriftReconciliationJob`, legacy service-marketplace Elasticsearch index `marketplace_services`, service search replay/recovery
- root cause: The tenant-admin service reindex endpoint had been scoped and hardened, but adjacent legacy service replay and global drift reconciliation still wrote Elasticsearch directly from event payloads or active service rows without proving active tenant lineage and active same-tenant provider-profile authority.
- impact: POS-ADMIN-CERTIFICATION-01 PASS 6 follow-up now reloads current SQL service/tenant/provider-profile authority before service search replay mutation and deletes stale search documents when that authority is missing. Global drift repair now declares SQL search authority and restores only services that survive active service, active tenant, and same-tenant active provider-profile joins.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardening, source freeze tests, and governance documentation added on 2026-05-27.
- residual operational risk: This hardens the reviewed legacy service search replay/reconciliation boundary, not future service ranking, localization, feed/export, support, cache-warmer, profile-governance, or global script surfaces.
- prevention rule: Legacy service-marketplace search mutation must not restore visibility from service event payloads, stale search documents, or raw active service ids alone. Current SQL active service, active tenant, and active same-tenant provider-profile authority must accept the document first; otherwise stale visibility must delete or remain absent.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ServiceDiscoveryProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Jobs/ElasticsearchDriftReconciliationJob.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/SqlTruthRebuildGuard.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/SqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/MarketplaceGovernanceCertificationTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/operational-tooling-certification-findings.md`

### Tenant-admin service-marketplace reindex can no longer recreate the global search index
- title: The legacy service-marketplace reindex endpoint no longer lets tenant admins delete/recreate the global `marketplace_services` Elasticsearch index or rebuild other tenants' public service documents
- affected systems: `MarketplaceController.ReindexServices`, service-marketplace Elasticsearch `marketplace_services`, tenant-admin marketplace repair, super-admin/platform recovery runbooks
- root cause: The older service-marketplace operational endpoint was role-gated to `Admin,SuperAdmin` but performed global index deletion/recreation and all-tenant rebuild. That made a tenant-admin repair route a global public-search mutation primitive.
- impact: POS-ADMIN-CERTIFICATION-01 PASS 5 follow-up now splits repair scope. Super admins retain global service-marketplace index recreation, while tenant admins run a tenant-scoped branch that indexes only current active non-deleted SQL `Service` rows for their tenant and deletes only stale tenant-owned service documents. Missing global index state fails closed with `GLOBAL_SEARCH_INDEX_UNAVAILABLE` for tenant admins.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardening, source freeze test, and governance/runbook documentation added on 2026-05-27.
- residual operational risk: This closes the reviewed legacy service-marketplace reindex endpoint, not every service discovery projection, drift reconciliation, ranking, feed/export, cache-warmer, analytics, or support path that may touch public service-search visibility.
- prevention rule: Tenant-admin public projection repair must stay tenant-scoped and must not delete/recreate global search indexes. Global service-marketplace index recreation, all-tenant service reindex, and equivalent broad public-search primitives require super-admin/platform scope plus docs/tests.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/MarketplaceController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/MarketplaceGovernanceCertificationTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/marketplace-governance-certification-findings.md`, `docs/architecture/dangerous-operational-primitives.md`, `docs/operations/operational-recovery-runbooks.md`

### Payment cancellation recovery outbox now has worker-restart overlap collapse
- title: Payment cancellation recovery can no longer actively invoke the payment lifecycle boundary twice for the same claimed outbox row under duplicate worker overlap
- affected systems: `PaymentCancellationOutboxMessage`, `PaymentCancellationOutboxProcessor`, `PaymentCancellationOutboxOptions`, payment cancellation recovery worker, `IPaymentLifecycleService`, operational backlog/replay runbooks
- root cause: The payment-cancellation outbox had bounded batch, retry, and backoff controls, but it lacked `ClaimedUntilUtc` row leasing before lifecycle invocation. A restarted or duplicate worker could enter `IPaymentLifecycleService` for the same unprocessed row before either worker saved `ProcessedAtUtc`.
- impact: POS-ADMIN-CERTIFICATION-01 PASS 7 adds row leases, explicit row-version concurrency, an order-scoped PostgreSQL advisory transaction lock where available, active-claim filtering, claim clearing on processed/manual-review/failure outcomes, bounded `ProcessingLeaseSeconds`, and active-overlap telemetry across both visible active claims and row-version claim races. The same pass also closes the equivalent stale-claim-save gap on `MarketplaceProjectionOutboxMessage` by adding `RowVersion`/`xmin` claim collapse, active-overlap telemetry, and 2026-05-29 duplicate-completion race telemetry after processed-event unique-key collapse.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardening, migration, runtime tests, architecture freeze tests, and governance documentation added on 2026-05-25, with duplicate-completion auditability follow-up documentation/runtime alignment on 2026-05-29.
- residual operational risk: `IPaymentLifecycleService` remains the payment authority and must stay idempotent. Operators must tune `ProcessingLeaseSeconds` from dependency latency; too-short leases can increase duplicate pickup under slow provider/lifecycle pressure, while too-long leases delay crash recovery.
- prevention rule: Payment cancellation recovery must claim rows before lifecycle work, keep active claims out of polling, clear claims after all terminal/retry outcomes, and never replace lifecycle replay with direct payment-status mutation or manual processed-row marking.
- references: `PointOfSale.Core/Entities/PaymentCancellationOutboxMessage.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Payment/PaymentCancellationOutboxProcessor.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Payment/PaymentCancellationOutboxOptions.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/Configurations/PaymentCancellationOutboxMessageConfiguration.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/PaymentCancellationOutboxOperationalScalabilityTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/ReplayRebuildChaosCertificationTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/replay-rebuild-chaos-certification-findings.md`

### Storefront footer replay now fails closed when tenant lineage is gone
- title: Delayed storefront footer replay can no longer preserve tenant-scoped public footer visibility after current SQL tenant authority is inactive, deleted, or missing
- affected systems: `StorefrontFooterProjectionConsumer`, `StorefrontFooterDocument`, storefront footer Mongo projection, delayed footer replay, public-content recovery
- root cause: The footer projection already used replay fencing and SQL footer-row recomposition, but it did not revalidate current tenant lineage before full Mongo replacement. A delayed footer event could therefore preserve an existing tenant-scoped footer document after tenant authority was no longer public.
- impact: PASS 7 follow-up now revalidates active SQL tenant lineage after replay fencing and before stale-event suppression or footer replacement. Missing, inactive, or deleted tenant authority deletes tenant-scoped footer visibility, records authority-failure and derivative-deletion convergence telemetry, completes the replay fence, and refuses stale public-content preservation from old events or Mongo state.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardening, source freeze tests, and governance documentation added on 2026-05-27.
- residual operational risk: This closes the reviewed storefront footer replay boundary only. Other future CMS/SEO/support-cache/export/content rebuild paths still require path-specific tenant-lineage, replay, telemetry, and fail-closed certification before they are treated as safe recovery primitives.
- prevention rule: Storefront footer full-document replacement must keep duplicate replay fencing, freshness filtering, current SQL tenant-lineage acceptance, fail-closed derivative deletion, and convergence telemetry. Do not preserve or rebuild tenant-scoped public footer visibility from Mongo, Redis, event payloads, or old repair inputs when tenant lineage is gone.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/StorefrontFooterProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/EventOutboxConsistencyArchitectureTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/ReplayRebuildChaosCertificationTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/replay-rebuild-chaos-certification-findings.md`

### Reviewed Redis invalidation replay now collapses before cache fanout
- title: Duplicate homepage, CMS, footer, marketplace product/order, and marketplace review cache invalidation replay can no longer repeatedly amplify Redis key deletion or pattern scans on the reviewed cache boundary
- affected systems: `RedisInvalidationConsumer`, `ProjectionEventFence`, Redis homepage/CMS/footer/marketplace product/order/review caches, replay storm and cache rebuild overlap recovery
- root cause: Product cache invalidation already used a durable replay fence, but adjacent homepage/CMS/footer/marketplace cache invalidation paths performed Redis key and pattern invalidation directly for every duplicate delivery.
- impact: POS-ADMIN-CERTIFICATION-01 PASS 7 follow-up on 2026-05-29 adds `ProjectionEventFence` around reviewed Redis invalidation side effects. Duplicate completed delivery and active overlap collapse before `RemoveByPatternAsync` or key deletion fanout, and marketplace-domain cache events preserve tenant scope in convergence telemetry when the event carries tenant evidence.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardening, runtime duplicate replay test, architecture freeze tests, and governance documentation added on 2026-05-29.
- residual operational risk: Redis remains derivative infrastructure and cache invalidation failures are still best-effort/retryable. This closes the reviewed cache invalidation consumer only, not future cache warmers, support-cache tools, feed/export cache writers, or analytics/reporting cache rebuild paths.
- prevention rule: Reviewed Redis invalidation replay must retain processed-event fencing before cache side effects. Do not remove the fence or add new high-volume cache pattern invalidation paths without replay amplification tests and documentation.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Consumers/RedisInvalidationConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/ProjectionEventFence.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/MarketplaceReviewInfrastructureTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/ReplayRebuildChaosCertificationTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/replay-rebuild-chaos-certification-findings.md`

### Admin search repair can no longer rebuild storefront product search visibility from partial SQL rows
- title: Tenant-admin storefront search repair no longer acts as a hidden partial-authority path that can repopulate Elasticsearch from incomplete SQL field copies
- affected systems: `AdminSearchSyncController`, storefront product search repair/reindex tooling, `ProductSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay`, `ProductSearchDocumentMapper`, dangerous operational primitive governance for tenant-admin search repair
- root cause: `AdminSearchSyncController.SyncProducts(...)` still indexed Elasticsearch documents from a baseline SQL row projection instead of the current SQL search authority overlay. Under recovery pressure that path could omit unit-authoritative storefront price, promotion/search enrichment, and fail-closed category-lineage suppression while still restoring public search visibility.
- impact: POS-ADMIN-CERTIFICATION-01 PASS 6 now recomposes each product through `ProductSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay` and maps search documents from the shared SQL-authoritative `ProductDocument` shape. Missing or invalid current SQL visibility deletes stale search documents instead of indexing a baseline document.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardening, runtime tests, architecture freeze tests, and governance documentation added on 2026-05-25.
- residual operational risk: This hardens one concrete tenant-admin storefront search repair surface, not every future CMS/SEO/analytics/cache-warmer/export/support search or rebuild tool. Those remain path-specific operational certification debt when expanded.
- prevention rule: Tenant-admin search repair/rebuild must not index from partial SQL rows, raw `SalePrice`, stale repair snapshots, or derivative documents. Product search repair must recompose current SQL visibility through `ProductSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay` and map Elasticsearch documents from the shared SQL-authoritative search document mapper before mutation.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AdminSearchSyncController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/ProductSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Documents/ProductSearchDocumentMapper.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Admin/AdminSearchSyncControllerTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/OperationalToolingCertificationFreezeTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/operational-tooling-certification-findings.md`

### Admin category search sync now deletes stale Elasticsearch visibility missing from SQL authority
- title: Tenant-admin category search repair no longer behaves like a one-way additive reindex that can preserve stale tenant-scoped category search visibility after SQL deletion
- affected systems: `AdminSearchSyncController`, tenant-admin category search repair/reindex tooling, `IUnifiedSearchIndexService`, dangerous operational primitive governance for reviewed category Elasticsearch mutation
- root cause: `AdminSearchSyncController.SyncCategories(...)` rebuilt current category search documents from the SQL lineage snapshot and deleted invalid-lineage categories it still saw in SQL, but it did not scrub tenant-scoped Elasticsearch category documents whose source SQL rows were gone entirely. Repeated sync runs could therefore preserve stale search visibility after SQL deletion.
- impact: PASS 6 follow-up now makes tenant-admin category search sync declare SQL rebuild authority and delete tenant-scoped stale category Elasticsearch documents missing from the current SQL authority set after the sync pass. The reviewed repair endpoint is now convergent under repeated recovery use instead of only ever indexing or lineage-filtering current rows.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardening, focused runtime tests, architecture freeze tests, and governance documentation added on 2026-05-27.
- residual operational risk: This closes the reviewed tenant-admin category search sync boundary, not every future category/search/CMS/SEO/export/support repair or rebuild path that may mutate Elasticsearch visibility. Those remain path-specific operational certification debt when expanded.
- prevention rule: Tenant-admin category search repair/rebuild must declare SQL rebuild authority and must delete tenant-scoped stale Elasticsearch category documents that are missing from current SQL category authority. One-way category search sync is not an approved recovery mode.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AdminSearchSyncController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/IUnifiedSearchIndexService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/UnifiedSearchIndexService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Admin/AdminSearchSyncControllerTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/OperationalToolingCertificationFreezeTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/operational-tooling-certification-findings.md`

### Admin product rebuild no longer mutates Redis keys directly from the repair controller
- title: Tenant-admin product read-model repair retains SQL-authoritative projection behavior while cache clearing is delegated to the reviewed tenant-scoped cache boundary
- affected systems: `AdminSyncController`, `IProductSqlAuthorityProjectionWriter`, `IProductCacheService`, product read-model rebuild/recovery, Redis product cache invalidation governance
- root cause: Product projection repair had been routed through the shared SQL writer, but the controller still enumerated Redis product keys and invoked direct key deletion after repair. That left a dangerous cache mutation primitive embedded in a tenant-admin recovery endpoint instead of the reviewed cache abstraction.
- impact: PASS 6 now routes bulk and single-product post-rebuild cache invalidation through `IProductCacheService.InvalidateAllProductsAsync(...)` and `InvalidateProductAsync(...)`, while leaving SQL-authoritative product projection convergence unchanged.
- mitigation status: Runtime boundary hardening, focused cache-delegation runtime tests, architecture freeze tests, and governance documentation added on 2026-05-25.
- residual operational risk: `IProductCacheService` intentionally remains a derivative cache invalidation boundary and cache failure is best-effort; future cache repair, warmer, CMS, SEO, export, or support endpoints still need path-specific governance if they manipulate Redis visibility.
- prevention rule: Tenant-admin rebuild controllers must not enumerate Redis keys or directly delete cache entries for derivative visibility repair when a reviewed tenant-scoped cache service exists. Cache state is never SQL authority.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AdminSyncController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Redis/Services/IProductCacheService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Redis/Services/ProductCacheService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Admin/AdminSyncControllerOperationalTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/OperationalToolingCertificationFreezeTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/operational-tooling-certification-findings.md`

### Admin data sync no longer preserves stale category or shipping-method Mongo documents after SQL deletion
- title: Tenant-admin category and shipping-method Mongo sync now converges stale derivative absence instead of acting like a one-way additive repair primitive
- affected systems: `AdminDataSyncController`, category Mongo sync, shipping-method Mongo sync, tenant-scoped operational rebuild/recovery, Mongo dangerous primitive governance
- root cause: `AdminDataSyncController` rebuilt current category and shipping-method Mongo documents from SQL rows, but it did not delete tenant-scoped Mongo documents whose source SQL rows had been hard-deleted or otherwise removed from the current authority set. Repeated sync runs could therefore preserve stale derivative visibility indefinitely.
- impact: PASS 6 follow-up now makes category and shipping-method sync declare `SqlTruthRebuildGuard.RequireSqlAuthority(...)` and delete tenant-scoped stale Mongo documents after the authoritative upsert pass. The reviewed sync endpoint is now convergent under repeated recovery use instead of only ever adding or updating derivative rows.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardening, focused runtime tests, architecture freeze tests, and governance documentation added on 2026-05-27.
- residual operational risk: This closes the reviewed category/shipping Mongo sync boundary, not every future tenant-scoped Mongo rebuild, cache warmer, CMS, SEO, export, or support repair path. Shipping-method storefront cache invalidation remains governed by its existing projection path and still requires path-specific review if a direct repair invalidation boundary is introduced.
- prevention rule: Tenant-admin Mongo rebuild/sync tooling must declare SQL rebuild authority and must delete tenant-scoped stale derivative documents that no longer exist in current SQL authority. One-way Mongo sync is not an approved recovery mode.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AdminDataSyncController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Admin/AdminDataSyncControllerOperationalTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/OperationalToolingCertificationFreezeTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/operational-tooling-certification-findings.md`

### Affiliate repair/reindex can no longer inject fallback tenant currency or preserve stale affiliate visibility
- title: Reviewed AF16E affiliate repair now fails closed on missing SQL tenant storefront/currency authority instead of manufacturing derivative currency
- affected systems: `AF16E_ReindexAffiliateProductsWithStorefrontUrl`, `AF16E_ReindexController`, public affiliate Mongo/search visibility, affiliate intelligence repair, tenant-scoped operational recovery, dangerous primitive governance
- root cause: The reviewed AF16E repair script updated MongoDB and Elasticsearch directly, but it still normalized blank tenant currency to `USD` and could skip tenants with missing storefront/currency authority while leaving stale affiliate visibility live.
- impact: PASS 6 now forces AF16E to normalize tenant currency through `CurrencyNormalizationHelper.NormalizeCurrencyOrThrow(...)`, declare affiliate repair authority through `SqlTruthRebuildGuard.RequireSqlAuthority(...)`, and delete stale public affiliate and affiliate-intelligence visibility when current tenant storefront/currency authority is missing.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardening, architecture/source freeze tests, and certification documentation added on 2026-05-25.
- residual operational risk: This narrows one reviewed affiliate repair primitive, not every future affiliate feed/export/cache-warmer/support repair path. AF16E still uses equivalent local SQL tenant checks rather than direct `TenantLineageAuthority` helper adoption.
- prevention rule: Affiliate repair/reindex, feed/export rebuild, and public-affiliate recovery must treat tenant storefront metadata and `Tenant.Currency` as current SQL authority only. Do not inject fallback currency, and do not preserve stale derivative visibility when SQL storefront/currency authority is missing.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Scripts/AF16E_ReindexAffiliateProductsWithStorefrontUrl.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/Admin/AF16E_ReindexController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/AffiliateCurrencyProjectionArchitectureTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/SemanticConvergenceChaosValidationTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/operational-tooling-certification-findings.md`

### POS admin access tokens now require current SQL-bound session authority
- title: Stale POS admin access-token and raw refresh-token reuse can no longer persist through logout, refresh rotation, role change, actor disable/delete, inactive tenant lineage, or current vendor governance ban on the inspected bearer boundary
- affected systems: `AuthController`, `TenantRegistrationService`, `PlatformController` onboarding-session restoration, default POS JWT validation in `Program.cs`, `PosSessionAuthority`, `Users.RefreshToken`, `AdminTenantAuthority`, POS tenant-admin operational APIs
- root cause: POS refresh credentials were stored as raw values and ordinary POS JWT validation accepted signed tenant/role claims without a current SQL session binding. Role-protected admin middleware reduced privileged stale-role exposure, but logout and refresh rotation did not immediately collapse ordinary authenticated POS access-token reuse.
- impact: POS access tokens now carry a `pos_session` binding to the current hashed SQL refresh session; every POS bearer authentication recomposes current SQL user/session/tenant/governance authority. Refresh credentials are hashed at rest, logout clears SQL session authority, logout now fails closed when revocation persistence is unavailable, rotation invalidates prior access tokens, legacy tokens without `pos_session` and expired current bindings fail closed, EF concurrency protection rejects overlapping stale rotation writes, and cached tenant-registration replay revalidates current SQL owner activity, tenant ownership, active tenant lineage, and tenant governance before restoring cookies.
- mitigation status: Runtime, tests, telemetry, and documentation hardened on 2026-05-24 and re-certified on 2026-05-26 in POS-ADMIN-CERTIFICATION-01 PASS 1.
- residual operational risk: The model intentionally supports one current POS session per user and does not add refresh-family compromise tracking, per-device session inventory, enterprise IAM/MFA/SSO/ABAC, a certified POS password-reset/session-management ceremony, or support impersonation. Unexpected logout persistence now surfaces as retryable failure instead of silent success, but a broader POS password-reset/admin session-management ceremony is still future debt.
- prevention rule: POS access tokens must remain bound to current SQL session authority; new POS refresh credentials must remain hash-only at rest; logout/password reset/account disable/role change/tenant governance operations must fail closed for stale sessions; future impersonation or delegated escalation requires explicit SQL authority, audit, revocation, replay tests, and docs.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AuthController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Platform/TenantRegistrationService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/PlatformController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Security/PosSessionAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Program.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/Configurations/UserConfiguration.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Security/PosSessionAuthorityTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Security/PosAuthenticationLifecycleTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Security/PosSessionConcurrencyTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/admin-authentication-session-certification-findings.md`

### Marketplace review replay can no longer reindex CommerceHub search from stale Mongo listing state
- title: Review/moderation replay cannot resurrect CommerceHub search visibility without current SQL listing authority
- affected systems: `MarketplaceProductReviewProjectionService`, CommerceHub review aggregate replay, CommerceHub search index sync, super-admin marketplace moderation/recovery, Phase 5 operational chaos certification
- root cause: Marketplace review projection recomputed review aggregates from SQL reviews, but its search sync path mapped existing `CommerceHubProductDocument` state directly into Elasticsearch. If a stale listing document remained after listing unpublish/delete or tenant visibility loss, review replay could attempt to reindex stale marketplace search visibility from Mongo rather than the CommerceHub SQL listing overlay.
- impact: SUPERADMIN-CON-01 Phase 5 routes review-triggered search sync through `CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay`. Current SQL `CommerceHubListing`, tenant, product, variant, category, stock, price, media, and publication authority now decide whether review-triggered search mutation indexes or deletes. Search status updates are tenant-filtered.
- mitigation status: Runtime, tests, and documentation hardened on 2026-05-24. `SuperAdminOperationalChaosCertificationTests` freeze that review replay uses the SQL overlay and no longer calls `CommerceHubSearchDocumentMapper.Map(document)` directly.
- residual operational risk: This hardens the review-triggered CommerceHub search path, not every review export, localization, ranking, feed/export, cache-warmer, support, analytics/reporting, or future global runbook path. Those remain path-specific certification debt when expanded.
- prevention rule: Review, moderation, ranking, feed, and recovery paths that mutate CommerceHub public search or marketplace visibility must revalidate current SQL listing authority or delete/tombstone visibility. Do not index from Mongo/read-model state alone.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Services/MarketplaceReviews/MarketplaceProductReviewBackboneServices.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/SuperAdminOperationalChaosCertificationTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/super-admin-operational-chaos-certification-findings.md`

### Super-admin platform mutations now have request-level operational audit coverage
- title: Legacy platform-policy mutation endpoints can no longer rely only on local structured logs for actor attribution after current SQL platform authority succeeds
- affected systems: `SuperAdminOperationalAuditMiddleware`, `SuperAdminPlatformAuthorityMiddleware`, legacy `RequireSuperAdmin` controllers such as tenant management, maintenance, development/debug tooling, platform finance, emergency/global operations, and super-admin operational audit documentation
- root cause: SUPERADMIN-CON-01 platform authority recomposed current SQL actor state for every `RequireSuperAdmin` endpoint, but older platform-policy mutation endpoints outside `/api/super-admin/*` did not uniformly write durable `ISuperAdminAuditService` records unless a domain controller already did so.
- impact: Phase 4 adds request-level operational audit for every non-read `RequireSuperAdmin` operation after SQL platform actor recomposition. Audit attribution uses the platform-authority context items for super-admin id, email, and token version, and records request id, method, path, endpoint, status, and coarse operation metadata. Existing domain audit remains the richer business record for refunds, payouts, vendor finance, user governance, accounting, store config, and marketplace management.
- mitigation status: Runtime, source-governance, and documentation hardened on 2026-05-24.
- residual operational risk: This is not a SIEM-grade immutable audit lake, enterprise IAM/SSO/ABAC, support break-glass workflow, request-body audit capture, or universal domain-specific audit for every older platform endpoint. The audit queue is still infrastructure; financial and lifecycle truth remains in SQL ledgers, lifecycle services, reconciliation records, and append-only domain audit tables.
- prevention rule: Non-read `RequireSuperAdmin` operations must retain request-level operational audit after `SuperAdminPlatformAuthorityMiddleware`. Future support impersonation, tenant override, emergency repair, global cache/search mutation, and dangerous primitive endpoints must add current SQL actor authority, explicit scope, durable audit, replay-safe convergence, docs, and tests in the same change.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/SuperAdmin/Infrastructure/SuperAdminOperationalAuditMiddleware.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Program.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/SuperAdmin/SuperAdminOperationalAuditMiddlewareTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/EmergencyGovernanceCertificationTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/emergency-governance-certification-findings.md`

### Tenant-admin CommerceHub recovery no longer invokes global search reindex
- title: Tenant-scoped CommerceHub search recovery cannot silently widen into all-listing marketplace reindex from tenant-admin scope
- affected systems: `AdminCommerceHubController`, `CommerceHubReindexService`, `CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay`, CommerceHub search recovery, marketplace outbox dead-letter retry, operational recovery runbooks
- root cause: The tenant-admin CommerceHub search reindex endpoint called the global `ReindexAsync` service. The underlying reindex still recomposed from SQL, but the operational scope was broader than the tenant-admin boundary documented for recovery tooling.
- impact: SUPERADMIN-CON-01 Phase 3 adds tenant-scoped `ReindexTenantAsync`, switches the tenant-admin endpoint to trusted tenant context, explicitly declares `CommerceHubListing` authority in CommerceHub search SQL recomposition, and adds bounded reindex pacing options plus regression tests.
- mitigation status: Runtime, tests, and documentation hardened on 2026-05-24.
- residual operational risk: Global all-listing reindex remains intentionally available for platform/runbook scope. No adaptive scheduler, fixed rebuild SLO, dead-letter UI, SIEM-grade audit store, or universal feed/export/cache-warmer/global-runbook certification was introduced.
- prevention rule: Tenant-admin recovery endpoints must use tenant-scoped rebuild/reindex entrypoints. Global search/cache/rebuild/replay forcing must remain platform authority or documented runbook scope, and CommerceHub search recovery must require current SQL listing authority.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AdminCommerceHubController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Jobs/CommerceHubReindexService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Jobs/CommerceHubReindexOptions.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/SuperAdminRecoverySafetyCertificationTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/super-admin-platform-authority-certification-findings.md`

### Marketplace public visibility now requires current SQL listing authority
- title: Product-only replay can no longer resurrect marketplace product read-model visibility after CommerceHub listing authority is gone
- affected systems: `MarketplaceProductReadModelProjectionService`, `MarketplaceProductReadRepository`, CommerceHub listing publication, homepage/curated references, CommerceHub Elasticsearch search ids, homepage projection replay
- root cause: Marketplace product read-model rebuilds recomposed current SQL product/variant/category/tenant state but did not require a current published SQL `CommerceHubListing`; homepage projection also used a post-side-effect processed marker rather than a pre-side-effect replay fence.
- impact: SUPERADMIN-CON-01 Phase 2 makes marketplace product visibility listing-authoritative, fences homepage replay before projection side effects, filters curated homepage references through active tenant and public variant lineage, and makes CommerceHub search document ids tenant-scoped for new writes while cleaning legacy listing-only ids.
- mitigation status: Runtime, tests, and documentation hardened on 2026-05-24. Focused marketplace read-model and search compatibility tests passed locally.
- residual operational risk: This does not certify every marketplace review, localization, CMS-adjacent, feed/export, cache warmer, ranking, or global runbook path. Legacy listing-only Elasticsearch documents may remain until tenant-aware replay/reindex/delete paths scrub them.
- prevention rule: Marketplace product visibility must require current SQL `CommerceHubListings` plus tenant/product/variant/category visibility. Homepage/recommendation references must be scrubbed from current SQL authority, duplicate replay must collapse before side effects, and CommerceHub search ids for tenant-owned listings must stay tenant-scoped.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/ReadModels/Marketplace/MarketplaceReadModelProjectionServices.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/Marketplace/HomepageProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/SuperAdmin/Services/SuperAdminHomepageSectionManagementService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Homepage/HomepageSectionReadService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Documents/CommerceHubProductSearchDocument.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/CommerceHubSearchIndexService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/MarketplaceReadModelProjectionTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/CommerceHubSearchIndexServiceCompatibilityTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/marketplace-governance-certification-findings.md`

### CommerceHub category lineage is now shared public visibility authority
- title: Leaf-only CommerceHub category checks can no longer preserve marketplace/search/homepage visibility when parent lineage is inactive, missing, self-referential, or cyclic
- affected systems: `CommerceHubCategoryLineageAuthority`, `CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay`, `CommerceHubProjectionConsumer`, `MarketplaceProductReadModelProjectionService`, `MarketplaceHomepageReadModelProjectionService`, `HomepageSectionReadService`, `SuperAdminHomepageSectionManagementService`, `CommerceHubQueryService`, `CommerceHubReindexService`
- root cause: Search overlay already walked CommerceHub category parent lineage, but several adjacent homepage/recommendation, read-model, validation, and reindex verification paths only checked the leaf category `IsActive` flag.
- impact: POS-ADMIN-CERTIFICATION-01 PASS 5 aligns those paths on a shared SQL category-lineage authority. Stale category lineage now deletes/suppresses search visibility, deletes marketplace read models, scrubs curated references, rejects super-admin curation, filters suggestions/categories, or excludes invalid listings from reindex verification counts.
- mitigation status: Runtime, regression tests, source-freeze tests, and documentation hardened on 2026-05-25.
- residual operational risk: Future ranking, feed/export, localization, review-export, support, analytics/reporting, cache-warmer, and global script paths still need path-specific SQL listing/category lineage certification before universal public-visibility claims.
- prevention rule: Do not treat CommerceHub leaf category activity, cached category names, homepage JSON, search hits, or Mongo listing category fields as public visibility authority. Use `CommerceHubCategoryLineageAuthority` or a documented equivalent before public marketplace visibility mutation or serve.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/CommerceHubCategoryLineageAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/ReadModels/Marketplace/MarketplaceReadModelProjectionServices.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Homepage/HomepageSectionReadService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/SuperAdmin/Services/SuperAdminHomepageSectionManagementService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubQueryService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Jobs/CommerceHubReindexService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/MarketplaceReadModelProjectionTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/MarketplaceGovernanceCertificationTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/marketplace-governance-certification-findings.md`

### CommerceHub search maintenance workers now revalidate SQL visibility and tenant-scoped delete semantics
- title: Search auto-healing and popularity replay can no longer reindex or partially delete CommerceHub visibility from stale Mongo state
- affected systems: `CommerceHubAutoHealingService`, `CommerceHubSearchAnalyticsBackgroundService`, `CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay`, `ICommerceHubSearchIndexService`, CommerceHub search recovery/analytics maintenance
- root cause: PASS 5 review found two adjacent maintenance paths still outside the hardened public-visibility boundary. Auto-healing deleted stale listings through the legacy listing-only search id in some fail-closed branches, which could leave tenant-scoped documents behind. Search analytics updated popularity in Mongo and then indexed/deleted search documents directly from Mongo publication state without recomposing current SQL listing/category/product authority.
- impact: Both workers now route search mutation back through `CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay`. When SQL visibility is missing they delete with tenant-scoped search identity whenever the tenant is known, cleaning both current tenant-scoped ids and legacy ids through the reviewed index service. Popularity maintenance remains derivative-only metadata layered onto SQL-authoritative visibility instead of becoming an alternate visibility write path.
- mitigation status: Runtime, source-freeze tests, and certification documentation hardened on 2026-05-25.
- residual operational risk: This closes the inspected recovery and analytics maintenance paths, not every future ranking engine, feed/export worker, cache warmer, localization writer, reporting pipeline, or support script that may later touch CommerceHub search visibility.
- prevention rule: Search maintenance, analytics, ranking, auto-healing, and repair workers must not index or delete CommerceHub visibility from Mongo publication state alone, and must not rely on listing-only delete semantics when tenant-owned search identity is known. Recompose through the SQL overlay and fail closed with tenant-scoped delete.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubAutoHealingService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubSearchAnalyticsBackgroundService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/CommerceHubSearchIndexService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/marketplace-governance-certification-findings.md`

### CommerceHub public media and review-summary search replay now recompose current SQL authority
- title: Stale derivative images and stale review-summary fields can no longer survive public visibility replay after SQL media or moderation truth changes
- affected systems: `CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay`, `CommerceHubProjectionConsumer`, `MarketplaceProductReadModelProjectionService`, CommerceHub reindex/auto-healing/review-triggered search sync, public marketplace cards
- root cause: After earlier listing/category visibility hardening, the search overlay and product read-model rebuild could still retain public image strings from legacy SQL columns or derivative documents when current SQL media linkage was missing, while overlay search writes retained Mongo-carried `AverageRating` and `ReviewCount` rather than rebuilding current approved review truth.
- impact: POS-ADMIN-CERTIFICATION-01 PASS 5 derives public CommerceHub images only from current SQL `ProductMedia` and `ProductVariants.MediaId`, clearing stale image visibility when ownership is not proven. Every search-overlay mutation now recomposes rating/count decoration from current approved, non-deleted SQL `ProductReviews`.
- mitigation status: Runtime, regression-freeze tests, and certification documentation hardened on 2026-05-25.
- residual operational risk: Normal derivative read/index propagation lag remains accepted; popularity/click ranking metadata remains derivative; CDN/browser caching, review exports, future ranking/feed/cache-warmer/support/reporting paths remain path-specific certification debt.
- prevention rule: No CommerceHub rebuild, reindex, replay, repair, or moderation-triggered search mutation may restore a public image from legacy/Mongo/search fields or restore rating/count decoration from derivative review summaries. Current SQL media linkage and approved review rows must recompose those fields.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/CommerceHubProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/ReadModels/Marketplace/MarketplaceReadModelProjectionServices.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/ProductVariantLifecycleAuthorityTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/MarketplaceReadModelProjectionTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/MarketplaceGovernanceCertificationTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/marketplace-governance-certification-findings.md`

### CommerceHub product-detail reads now recompose current SQL public visibility authority
- title: Published Mongo listing state can no longer keep the CommerceHub detail endpoint visible after SQL listing/category/media authority is gone
- affected systems: `CommerceHubQueryService`, `CommerceHubReadRepository`, CommerceHub product-detail API, CommerceHub SQL search overlay, storefront Mongo product detail hydration
- root cause: PASS 5 hardened search, homepage, read-model rebuild, and maintenance paths, but `CommerceHubQueryService.GetProductByIdAsync(...)` still loaded a published Mongo `CommerceHubProductDocument` and storefront `ProductDocument` directly. That bypassed the SQL overlay on the final public detail read surface, allowing stale listing visibility, stale CommerceHub category identity, or legacy variant `ImageUrl` fallback to survive after replay/rebuild drift.
- impact: PASS 5 now reapplies `CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay` before serving CommerceHub detail data, returns `null` when current SQL listing/category/publication authority no longer proves visibility, keeps CommerceHub category identity on the listing-authoritative value, and stops detail variant-image resolution from restoring legacy per-variant image strings when authoritative media is missing.
- mitigation status: Runtime, detail-mapping regression tests, source-freeze tests, and certification documentation hardened on 2026-05-25.
- residual operational risk: This closes the inspected CommerceHub detail surface, not every future localization layer, recommendation widget, cache warmer, feed/export reader, support tool, or storefront detail path that may later compose marketplace-facing detail data.
- prevention rule: Public CommerceHub detail reads are derivative visibility only. A published Mongo listing, storefront product category name, legacy variant `ImageUrl`, or cached detail payload is not final authority; detail reads must recompose current SQL listing/category/media/stock/review visibility before serve and fail closed when SQL authority is gone.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubQueryService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/CommerceHubQueryServiceDetailMappingTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/MarketplaceGovernanceCertificationTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/marketplace-governance-certification-findings.md`

### Super-admin platform authority now recomposes from current SQL on platform endpoints
- title: Stale super-admin access-token evidence can no longer authorize `RequireSuperAdmin` platform operations after token rotation, logout, password change, deletion, or governance restriction
