- affected systems: `SuperAdminPlatformAuthority`, `SuperAdminPlatformAuthorityMiddleware`, `SuperAdminJwt`, `SuperAdmins`, `UserGovernanceProfiles`, super-admin controllers, tenant/platform management controllers using `RequireSuperAdmin`, global/all-tenant runbook primitives
- root cause: Super-admin authentication used a dedicated JWT scheme and SQL-backed login/refresh, but ordinary protected requests validated role/auth-type claims only. A still-valid access token could remain usable until expiry after current SQL authority changed.
- impact: SUPERADMIN-CON-01 adds current-SQL platform actor recomposition for every `RequireSuperAdmin` endpoint. Access tokens carry `token_version`; current SQL `SuperAdmins.TokenVersion`, row existence, and governance profile status are checked at request time. Stale access-token replay fails closed with `SUPER_ADMIN_SCOPE_FORBIDDEN` and platform-authority telemetry. The 2026-05-26 follow-on hardening also rechecks current governance during refresh issuance and revokes stored refresh authority when governance restriction rejects login.
- mitigation status: Runtime, tests, and documentation hardened on 2026-05-24 and extended on 2026-05-26. Focused tests cover current SQL acceptance, stale token rejection, deleted super-admin rejection, governance restriction rejection, token-version claim emission, password-change session revocation, governance-restricted refresh rejection, governance-action refresh revocation, middleware registration, and certification documentation.
- residual operational risk: This does not introduce enterprise IAM/SSO/ABAC, support impersonation ceremonies, SIEM-grade audit storage, adaptive global scheduling, fixed global rebuild SLOs, or universal durable audit records for older platform-policy endpoints. Global/all-tenant repair remains intentionally flexible under explicit platform authority and runbook scope.
- prevention rule: Super-admin JWT claims are candidate evidence only. New platform/global operations must use `RequireSuperAdmin`, pass current SQL platform authority, name their SQL authority owner, preserve replay/rebuild semantics, document dangerous primitive use, and add focused tests/docs in the same change.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/SuperAdmin/Infrastructure/SuperAdminPlatformAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/SuperAdmin/Infrastructure/SuperAdminPlatformAuthorityMiddleware.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/SuperAdmin/Services/SuperAdminAuthService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/SuperAdmin/Services/SuperAdminManagementService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/SuperAdmin/SuperAdminPlatformAuthorityTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/SuperAdminPlatformAuthorityCertificationTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/super-admin-platform-authority-certification-findings.md`

### Tenant admin actor authority now recomposes from current SQL scope on privileged endpoints
- title: Stale admin JWT role/session evidence can no longer authorize inspected tenant-scoped operational controls after current SQL actor or tenant authority changes
- affected systems: `AdminTenantAuthority`, `AdminTenantAuthorityMiddleware`, tenant-scoped POS/admin role endpoints, admin data sync repair endpoints, order/shipment/inventory admin mutation, storefront configuration and CMS/publication controls, search reindex/cache controls, AF16E affiliate repair/reindex tooling
- root cause: ASP.NET role authorization validated JWT claims, but a still-valid token could carry an old role or tenant claim after the current SQL user was disabled, role-downgraded, moved, or after the tenant was deactivated. Some repair tooling also continued to express tenant scope through the compatibility `X-Tenant-Id` header or through all-tenant script entrypoints.
- impact: TENANT-CON-01 Phase 3 adds SQL-backed actor authority after normal authorization. Privileged tenant-bound endpoints now reload the current `Users` row, current role, active actor state, trusted tenant context, and active tenant lineage before proceeding. Stale admin-session replay fails closed with `ADMIN_SCOPE_FORBIDDEN`. Admin data sync, order admin, shipment admin, inventory admin, store-config, and CMS/publication controls consume `ITenantContext.RequiredTenantId`; tenant admins cannot delete/recreate global search indexes; AF16E admin reindex now calls a tenant-scoped script entrypoint.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-24 for inspected role-protected tenant admin/operational endpoints and concrete repair/reindex controls. Focused `AdminTenantAuthorityTests` passed locally, and `PointOfSale.API` built successfully with shared compilation disabled.
- residual operational risk: This does not implement enterprise IAM, delegated org administration, external SSO governance, or a complete support impersonation ceremony. Bare `[Authorize]` endpoints without role metadata are not treated as tenant-admin mutation surfaces by the middleware. Future global repair jobs, support tools, tenant switching, impersonation, platform runbooks, and cache warmers must add explicit SQL actor/platform authority and replay/rebuild coverage before mutating tenant-scoped operational state.
- prevention rule: JWT role/tenant claims, frontend tenant selectors, raw headers, query tenant ids, support-dashboard state, and repair payload scope are evidence only. Tenant-scoped admin mutation or operational tooling must recompose current SQL actor authority plus current SQL tenant lineage at request time, and global/platform operations must be fenced behind explicit platform authority or reviewed runbooks.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Security/AdminTenantAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Middleware/AdminTenantAuthorityMiddleware.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AdminDataSyncController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AdminOrdersController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AdminShipmentsController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AdminInventoryController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AdminStoreConfigController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AdminCmsPagesController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/SearchReindexController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/Admin/AF16E_ReindexController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Scripts/AF16E_ReindexAffiliateProductsWithStorefrontUrl.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Security/AdminTenantAuthorityTests.cs`

### Tenant lineage authority is now explicit on the inspected storefront-config and admin-search repair boundaries
- title: Stale tenant ownership can no longer preserve storefront config projection visibility or drive admin search repair scope on hardened paths
- affected systems: `TenantLineageAuthority`, storefront config Mongo projection, Redis store-config cache invalidation, admin Elasticsearch sync/rebuild endpoints, tenant-scoped repair/rebuild inputs
- root cause: Tenant filters and middleware were already present, but the inspected repair/projection paths still expressed tenant authority locally. Storefront config projection did not require current active tenant lineage before preserving derivative config visibility, and admin search sync reparsed `X-Tenant-Id` locally instead of consuming trusted tenant context.
- impact: TENANT-CON-01 adds a shared SQL tenant-lineage helper and applies it to storefront config projection. Store-config replay now requires current SQL `StoreConfig` joined to an active owning `Tenant`; missing, inactive, deleted, moved, or cross-tenant lineage deletes the tenant-scoped Mongo projection and invalidates the tenant's store-config Redis key. Admin search sync now uses `ITenantContext.RequiredTenantId`, so repair scope converges through trusted middleware resolution.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-22 for the inspected storefront config projection/cache path and admin search sync repair entrypoint. Focused `TenantLineageAuthorityTests` and `StorefrontConfigProjectionConsumerTests` passed locally; Infrastructure and API builds passed.
- residual operational risk: This phase does not retrofit every tenant-scoped derivative writer. CMS page projection, homepage projection, affiliate projections, CommerceHub read-model repair, notification caches, analytics caches, direct search/Mongo repair tools, support/admin caches, and future cache warmers still require explicit tenant-lineage adoption when they mutate or rebuild tenant-scoped visibility.
- prevention rule: Tenant ownership for replay, rebuild, repair, cache hydration, and derivative mutation must be proven from current SQL. Payload tenant ids, raw headers, Mongo documents, Redis keys, Elasticsearch documents, and stale rebuild inputs are candidate evidence only; new tenant-scoped derivative mutation paths must call `TenantLineageAuthority` or document an equivalent SQL authority boundary with replay/rebuild tests.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/TenantLineageAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/StorefrontConfigProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AdminSearchSyncController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/TenantLineageAuthorityTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Storefront/StorefrontConfigProjectionConsumerTests.cs`

### Archived or detached variant resurrection is blocked on inspected runtime paths
- title: Public variant purchasability and visibility now fail closed unless SQL proves current variant-plus-parent lifecycle
- affected systems: `ProductVariantLifecycleAuthority`, storefront cart availability, storefront/product search recomposition, `ProductVariantProjectionConsumer`, CommerceHub cart and checkout validation, CommerceHub listing/search reconciliation, affiliate product intelligence stock recomposition, Mongo product documents, Elasticsearch product documents, Redis-derived availability, replay/rebuild paths
- root cause: Variant identity, SKU/barcode ownership, media linkage, publication visibility, and storefront pricing had already been hardened in separate sweeps, but several runtime paths could still treat projected stock, stale active variants, stale CommerceHub listing state, or stale Mongo/Search product variants as enough evidence after the SQL parent product was archived/unpublished/deleted or after the variant lost valid parent lineage.
- impact: PRODUCT-CON-11A adds a shared SQL public-lineage predicate and applies it to inspected stock, cart, checkout, projection, CommerceHub, affiliate, and search recomposition paths. Storefront cart stock authority now returns `0` when SQL variant lifecycle is missing, CommerceHub cart/checkout rejects invalid current lineage, CommerceHub variant update replay reconciles listing publication/search from current SQL lifecycle, affiliate intelligence stock recomposition ignores invalid-lineage variants, and product search/storefront recomposition tombstones/deletes variant-product visibility when no active priced valid-lineage variant remains.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-22 for the inspected paths. Focused `ProductVariantLifecycleAuthorityTests` passed locally, and Infrastructure/API builds passed after the hardening changes.
- residual operational risk: This is not a universal feed/export/recommendation/import/clone framework. Future bulk importers, clone/merge/split jobs, feed exporters, personalized recommendation engines, standalone autocomplete indexes, direct repair tools, CDN/cache rehydrators, or external marketplace sync writers can still regress if they create or mutate variant-shaped visibility without SQL lifecycle recomposition.
- prevention rule: PostgreSQL `ProductVariants` plus same-tenant parent `Products` own public variant lifecycle. Stale projections, retry payloads, import/clone payloads, CommerceHub listings, affiliate documents, Redis availability, and Elasticsearch documents are triggers only; archived, detached, orphaned, deleted, inactive, unpublished, offline, or invalid-lineage variants must fail closed unless current SQL lifecycle authority proves visibility.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/ProductVariantLifecycleAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Cart/CartService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubCartService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubCheckoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductVariantProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/CommerceHubProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/Affiliate/AffiliateProductIntelligenceProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/ProductSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/MongoDB/Documents/ProductStorefrontVisibilityAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/ProductVariantLifecycleAuthorityTests.cs`

### Variant clone/import semantic carryover is governed on inspected mutation surfaces
- title: Product and variant create/update paths no longer preserve stale cloned/imported SKU, barcode, media, or initial-stock semantics as authority
- affected systems: `ProductVariantCloneImportAuthority`, `ProductsController`, `ProductVariantsController`, `ProductVariantService`, product create/update, variant create/update, simple-product default variant synchronization, SQL SKU/barcode ownership, product media inheritance, initial variant stock fanout
- root cause: The lifecycle hardening made public visibility fail closed, but clone/import-like payloads reaching the normal create/update APIs could still preserve product-level simple SKU drift, attempt barcode carryover, inherit stale media IDs, or submit duplicate initial stock rows unless each controller remembered the same semantic ownership rules.
- impact: PRODUCT-CON-11B adds `ProductVariantCloneImportAuthority` as the shared runtime helper. Product create/update and variant create/update now check current SQL product/variant semantic ownership, deterministically regenerate SKU on create when a cloned/imported SKU is already owned, reject barcode carryover when another live product or variant owns it, require same-product image media before variant media inheritance, reject duplicate initial stock warehouses, and mirror simple-product product identity back from the default variant.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-22 for the inspected product/variant mutation surfaces. Focused `ProductVariantCloneImportAuthorityTests` and `ProductVariantLifecycleAuthorityTests` passed locally; `PointOfSale.API` build passed.
- residual operational risk: No dedicated CSV/Excel/ERP/supplier import framework or named clone endpoint was present in the inspected backend. Future explicit import jobs, clone endpoints, template duplication, external marketplace sync writers, feed importers, or bulk catalog ingestion code can still regress if they bypass the helper, skip request idempotency, write read models directly, or carry external mappings/publication state from source payloads.
- prevention rule: Clone/import payloads are intent only. Current SQL product/variant rows own SKU, barcode, media linkage, stock linkage, lineage, and public lifecycle; external mappings and publication must be recomposed or omitted. New bulk/import/clone writers must use `ProductVariantCloneImportAuthority`, `ProductVariantLifecycleAuthority`, and existing product/variant/publication/stock/media events or document an equivalent authority boundary with runtime tests.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/ProductVariantCloneImportAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/ProductsController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/ProductVariantsController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/ProductVariantService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/ProductVariantCloneImportAuthorityTests.cs`

### Variant merge/split semantic divergence is governed on inspected authority paths
- title: Superseded merge sources and split parents can no longer own public lifecycle, SKU/barcode, stock reservation, search, or CommerceHub visibility on hardened paths
- affected systems: `ProductVariantMergeSplitAuthority`, `ProductVariantLifecycleAuthority`, `ProductVariantCloneImportAuthority`, `ProductVariantsController`, `ProductsController`, `ProductVariantService`, `ProductVariantStockMutationSupport`, `ProductVariantStockRepository`, `InventoryConsistencyService`, `CommerceHubPaymentRecoveryAuthorityService`, `CommerceHubVendorVisibilityService`, product search SQL recomposition, SQL `ProductVariants`
- root cause: The platform had variant lifecycle and clone/import hardening, but no durable semantic lifecycle state for merge/split supersession. If future or ad-hoc merge/split work used ordinary update/delete/stock/projection primitives, stale jobs or rebuilds could treat a merged source or split parent as still owning SKU, barcode, stock, reservation, pricing, media, publication, or channel identity.
- impact: PRODUCT-CON-11C adds SQL semantic lifecycle metadata to `ProductVariants` and `ProductVariantMergeSplitAuthority` for current-owner checks, merge/split operation replay collapse, stale-operation rejection, source identity clearing, split-parent retirement, lineage stamping, and open-reservation rejection. Public lifecycle, SKU/barcode ownership checks, admin variant mutation, barcode lookup, default-variant alignment, stock mutation/reservation creation, CommerceHub reservation/payment recovery, vendor visibility, and product search recomposition now fail closed for superseded variants.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-22 for inspected authority paths. Focused `ProductVariantMergeSplitAuthorityTests`, `ProductVariantLifecycleAuthorityTests`, and `ProductVariantCloneImportAuthorityTests` passed locally; `PointOfSale.API` build passed.
- residual operational risk: No dedicated admin merge/split orchestration endpoint, supplier deduplication worker, or bulk duplicate-cleanup workflow was present in the inspected backend. Future explicit merge/split implementations still need transaction-scoped physical stock transfer or reservation migration semantics, post-commit event fanout, cache/search/listing invalidation, and end-to-end replay/rebuild tests.
- prevention rule: Variant merge/split payloads are operation intent only. Current SQL `ProductVariants.SemanticLifecycleState` and lineage metadata decide the semantic owner; superseded variants are lineage records and must not be made purchasable, reservable, searchable, CommerceHub-visible, feed/export-visible, or SKU/barcode-owning by replay, rebuild, cache, external mapping, or delayed projection fanout.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/ProductVariantMergeSplitAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.Core/Entities/ProductVariant.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/Configurations/ProductVariantConfiguration.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Migrations/20260522143000_VariantMergeSplitSemanticAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/ProductVariantLifecycleAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/ProductVariantCloneImportAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/Repositories/ProductVariantStockRepository.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Inventory/ProductVariantStockMutationSupport.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/InventoryConsistencyService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/ProductVariantMergeSplitAuthorityTests.cs`

### Variant inventory and reservation lineage convergence is governed on inspected runtime paths
- title: Stale reservations, detached warehouse stock, superseded variant stock, and stale stock-sync replay can no longer become inventory authority on hardened paths
- affected systems: `ProductVariantInventoryLineageAuthority`, `ProductVariantStockRepository`, `ProductVariantStockMutationSupport`, `CheckoutInventoryAuthorityGuard`, `InventoryConsistencyService`, `StockSyncOutboxProcessor`, `InventoryReservations`, `ProductVariantStocks`, warehouses, CommerceHub payment/delivery authority, checkout payment guards, stock visibility fanout
- root cause: Variant semantic lifecycle hardening rejected superseded variants for new ownership, but reservation consumption and stock propagation still needed a shared SQL predicate for current variant/product/warehouse/stock/reservation lineage. Without it, delayed payment/fulfillment/recovery work or stock-sync replay could discover active-looking rows after backing stock, warehouse, or variant lineage had changed.
- impact: PRODUCT-CON-11D adds `ProductVariantInventoryLineageAuthority` and routes reservation creation, stock mutation, checkout reservation guards, CommerceHub payment/delivery validation, order-reservation commit, and stock-sync outbox fanout through current SQL inventory lineage. Expired, released, superseded, orphaned, inactive-warehouse, detached-product, or deleted-stock reservations cannot be consumed by replay; stale stock-sync messages are suppressed instead of publishing stale availability.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-22 for inspected reservation, stock mutation, CommerceHub payment/delivery, checkout, and stock fanout paths. Focused `ProductVariantMergeSplitAuthorityTests`, `ProductVariantLifecycleAuthorityTests`, `InventoryConsistencyServiceTests`, and `CheckoutConcurrencyHardeningSourceTests` passed locally.
- residual operational risk: This is not a complete physical stock-transfer, reservation-migration, warehouse-reassignment, external-feed, or bulk-inventory-import framework. Future explicit merge/split stock moves, reservation migration jobs, warehouse reassignment workflows, stock feed/export writers, and specialized reconciliation tools must adopt the helper or an equivalent SQL transaction boundary with deterministic locking and replay/rebuild tests.
- prevention rule: Inventory reservation rows and stock-sync payloads are evidence only. Current SQL variant semantic ownership, active parent product lineage, active warehouse ownership, non-deleted stock rows, and active unexpired reservations decide reservability, consumption, fulfillment linkage, payment recovery, and stock visibility. Stale discovery may repair/release, but it must not consume or publish availability.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/ProductVariantInventoryLineageAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/Repositories/ProductVariantStockRepository.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Inventory/ProductVariantStockMutationSupport.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/CheckoutInventoryAuthorityGuard.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/InventoryConsistencyService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Eventing/StockSyncOutboxProcessor.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/ProductVariantMergeSplitAuthorityTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/InventoryConsistencyServiceTests.cs`

### Storefront product projection dangerous primitives are contained behind a runtime mutation boundary
- title: Tier-1 storefront `ProductDocument` upsert/tombstone writes no longer sit directly inside the SQL projection writer
- affected systems: `ProductSqlAuthorityProjectionWriter`, `StorefrontProductProjectionMutationBoundary`, `ProductReadRepository`, storefront Mongo product documents, admin product rebuild, product create/update/lifecycle consumers, affiliate/public pricing helpers
- root cause: The shared SQL writer already centralized product recomposition, but the low-level dangerous repository primitives (`UpsertAsync`, `TombstoneIfFreshAsync`) still appeared as direct calls inside the writer. That made the approved primitive less explicit and easier for future engineers to copy into nearby rebuild/repair code.
- impact: The 2026-05-21 governance extraction sweep adds `IStorefrontProductProjectionMutationBoundary` / `StorefrontProductProjectionMutationBoundary` as the runtime wrapper for reviewed full-document upsert and visibility tombstone mutation. The writer now recomposes SQL authority and asks the boundary to apply a typed `ReviewedFullDocumentUpsert` or `ReviewedVisibilityTombstone`; the boundary validates tenant/product/event identifiers, requires a review reason, and rejects full-document upsert unless the projection is active and online-visible. Storefront-visible pricing and affiliate/public pricing helpers are also extracted into `ProductStorefrontVisibilityAuthority`.
- mitigation status: Runtime/governance hardened on 2026-05-21. Focused Infrastructure/API builds, product writer tests, pricing snapshot tests, architecture source guards, and rebuild/replay slices passed locally.
- residual operational risk: This is not a repo-wide analyzer and not a universal Mongo mutation framework. `ProductReadRepository` still owns the actual Mongo operations, and other document families still use reviewed local replacements or updates where their authority semantics are different. Future engineers can still introduce bypasses if they add new write surfaces without extending source guards and docs.
- prevention rule: New Tier-1 storefront product write/rebuild/repair code must use `IProductSqlAuthorityProjectionWriter`; direct product repository upsert/tombstone calls must remain inside `StorefrontProductProjectionMutationBoundary`; storefront/affiliate/public pricing code must prefer `ProductStorefrontVisibilityAuthority`-backed helpers over legacy product-level price fields.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/MongoDB/Services/StorefrontProductProjectionMutationBoundary.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/MongoDB/Services/ProductSqlAuthorityProjectionWriter.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/MongoDB/Documents/ProductStorefrontVisibilityAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/MongoDB/Documents/ProductDocument.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/ProductSqlAuthorityProjectionWriterTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/EventOutboxConsistencyArchitectureTests.cs`

### Affiliate/public price visibility is converged onto storefront-authority pricing on inspected paths
- title: Public affiliate cards/search/detail and affiliate intelligence/discovery no longer rely on legacy product-level price shortcuts on the hardened runtime paths
- affected systems: `ProductDocument`, `AffiliateProductPolicyProjectionConsumer`, `AffiliateProductIntelligenceProjectionConsumer`, `AffiliateProductDiscoveryService`, `PublicAffiliateProductsController`, `AffiliatePortalController`, affiliate landing/discovery/detail surfaces, public affiliate search/detail surfaces
- root cause: Storefront runtime had already moved to SQL-authoritative unit-aware pricing, but affiliate/public paths still mixed product-level `EffectivePrice`, event-payload `OnlinePrice`/`SalePrice`, incomplete `DefaultUnit` semantics, and caller-optional stock filtering. That allowed storefront vs affiliate/public split-brain for visible price and purchasability.
- impact: The 2026-05-21 affiliate/public convergence sweep adds shared `ProductDocument` helpers for storefront-authority affiliate/public pricing, forces public affiliate projection recomposition through SQL overlay + shared pricing resolution, moves affiliate intelligence base price recomposition onto current storefront authority, makes product-update replay capable of recreating missing intelligence docs, and fail-closes public/affiliate discovery/category/detail/landing paths on `BasePrice > 0` plus in-stock visibility for the inspected surfaces.
- mitigation status: Runtime and rebuild semantics hardened on 2026-05-21 for the inspected affiliate/public surfaces. Focused deterministic pricing snapshot/source-guard tests plus replay/rebuild convergence slices were re-run locally after the hardening changes and passed in this workspace.
- residual operational risk: This is not a universal pricing-engine rewrite. CommerceHub pricing/search remains on its own SQL-authoritative listing semantics rather than the new storefront helper model, affiliate portal rich-detail still depends on the current intelligence document shape, and future recommendation/feed/export/custom repair paths can still regress if they bypass the shared storefront-authority helpers.
- prevention rule: Affiliate/public price, stock, and purchasability are derivative visibility only. They must converge from current SQL-backed storefront-visible pricing semantics and fail closed when `DefaultUnit`, active priced variants, or in-stock authority no longer proves purchasability. Legacy product-level `EffectivePrice` or event payload price fields must not be reintroduced as public price authority.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/MongoDB/Documents/ProductDocument.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/Affiliate/AffiliateProductPolicyProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/Affiliate/AffiliateProductIntelligenceProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/AffiliateProductDiscoveryService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/PublicAffiliateProductsController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AffiliatePortalController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/AffiliateStorefrontPricingSnapshotTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests.cs`

### CommerceHub suggestion/autocomplete response replay is SQL-revalidated
- title: CommerceHub autocomplete, suggestions, zero-result suggestion products, category suggestions, and popular/product-name completion terms no longer return cached or projected stale product visibility without a current SQL authority check
- affected systems: `CommerceHubQueryService`, CommerceHub autocomplete API, CommerceHub suggestions API, zero-result suggestions, popular search terms, CommerceHub product-name completion text, CommerceHub category suggestions, Elasticsearch/Mongo suggestion fallback reads, in-memory suggestion caches
- root cause: CommerceHub suggestions and autocomplete already queried Elasticsearch or Mongo documents that were themselves produced from SQL-overlaid search visibility, but the final response cache and fallback response shaping only filtered suspended vendors. A cached response or stale ES/Mongo suggestion could replay a deleted, unpublished, invalid-category, stale-title/media, or stale category product suggestion until cache expiry, and popular/search-term suggestions could surface product-name terms after no current SQL-visible listing still matched them.
- impact: PRODUCT-CON-10 runtime hardening revalidated cached and freshly built suggestion/autocomplete responses at the API response boundary, but PASS 5 follow-up review on 2026-05-27 found one narrower residual proof on the same boundary: text-term survival still relied on a raw SQL candidate query that accepted any active variant row and did not enforce the same SQL overlay or vendor-order-suspension suppression as product suggestions. The follow-up hardening now proves text terms only through current SQL listing candidates that survive `CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay.ApplyAsync(...)`, and it suppresses candidate vendors hidden by current governance or suspended from new orders before a term is treated as publicly live. Product suggestions are still recomposed through the SQL overlay from current CommerceHub listing, product publication, variant, stock, price, media, tenant, and category authority before DTO mapping. SQL listing tenant ownership is loaded from `CommerceHubListings` instead of Mongo. Category suggestions must pass live active SQL CommerceHub category lineage, and zero-result suggestion mapping uses the same guards.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-21 for inspected CommerceHub autocomplete/suggestions/zero-result/popular response paths. API build passed. Focused architecture guard passed.
- mitigation status: Extended on 2026-05-27 to close the residual term-level proof gap for superseded-variant replay and vendor-suspension term leakage on the inspected popular/autocomplete/suggestion surfaces.
- residual operational risk: This is response-boundary hardening, not a universal suggestion architecture. Storefront header autocomplete still relies on the hardened product Elasticsearch document path plus short-lived browser memory cache; no server-side storefront suggestion cache was found. There is still no dedicated completion-index/tombstone framework for a future standalone suggestion index. Popular term filtering is conservative and may omit broad historic queries that no longer match a live product/category term. Future dedicated autocomplete indexes, keyword rebuild jobs, cache warmers, external feed writers, or delayed suggestion workers must adopt SQL recomposition and replay collapse explicitly.
- prevention rule: Autocomplete/suggestion caches, Elasticsearch/Mongo search hits, popular query logs, category buckets, query-expansion snapshots, and zero-result suggestion payloads are triggers or hints only. Before public CommerceHub suggestion visibility is returned, the response path must prove current SQL listing/product/variant/category/media/stock/price/publication authority and must not use raw active variant rows, raw product/category LIKE matches, or pre-overlay candidate listings as final term visibility proof. Text/category suggestions that no longer converge to SQL-visible CommerceHub discovery, or that only resolve through vendors hidden by governance or suspended from new orders, must be dropped.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubQueryService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/CommerceHubQueryServiceSuggestionVisibilityTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests.cs`

### Marketplace public reads and curated homepage references now honor current vendor suppression
- title: CommerceHub public product/homepage surfaces can no longer preserve vendors that search already hid through governance or new-order suspension
- affected systems: `MarketplaceReadController`, `HomepageSectionReadService`, `SuperAdminHomepageSectionManagementService`, marketplace Mongo read repository paging/detail reads, CommerceHub public homepage/product caches, marketplace governance freeze tests
- root cause: CommerceHub search and suggestion responses already suppressed governance-hidden vendors and vendors suspended from new orders, but adjacent public marketplace reads still treated listing/category/product SQL visibility as sufficient on their own. Product list/detail reads and curated homepage references could therefore keep a vendor public after search had already failed closed.
- impact: On 2026-05-27 the reviewed public marketplace product list/detail/homepage paths moved under a shared `CommerceHubPublicVendorVisibilityService`. Marketplace paging now excludes currently hidden or order-suspended vendors before public results are cached, public detail reads fail closed when the vendor is no longer visible, homepage product sections scrub suppressed listings, and super-admin curated homepage validation rejects suppressed vendors up front.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardening, focused runtime tests, architecture freeze tests, and certification-documentation updates added on 2026-05-27.
- residual operational risk: This addendum closes the reviewed public product/homepage/curation slice, not every future ranking, review-export, localization, feed/export, analytics, support, cache-warmer, or global-script path that may preserve marketplace visibility. Those remain path-specific certification debt.
- prevention rule: Marketplace public reads, curated references, rebuild verification, and future recommendation/ranking surfaces must treat current vendor suppression as public visibility authority alongside SQL listing/category/product/media/stock/publication authority. Do not preserve public visibility because stale Mongo, Redis, Elasticsearch, or homepage JSON still carries a listing for a hidden or order-suspended vendor.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPublicVendorVisibilityService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/MarketplaceReadController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Homepage/HomepageSectionReadService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/SuperAdmin/Services/SuperAdminHomepageSectionManagementService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/MongoDB/Services/Marketplace/MarketplaceProductReadRepository.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/MarketplaceReadControllerTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/SuperAdmin/SuperAdminHomepageSectionManagementServiceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/MarketplaceGovernanceCertificationTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/marketplace-governance-certification-findings.md`

### CommerceHub search/read counts and suggestion products now suppress both hidden and order-suspended vendors on the inspected query boundary
- title: CommerceHub browse/search totals, facet inputs, and suggestion/autocomplete product payloads no longer drift from the final public vendor-suppression contract on the hardened query surface
- affected systems: `CommerceHubQueryService`, CommerceHub browse/search APIs, autocomplete/suggestions APIs, cached suggestion/autocomplete responses, Elasticsearch/Mongo suggestion fallback reads, search query cache keys, marketplace governance source/runtime freeze tests
- root cause: The final CommerceHub product-card layer already suppressed governance-hidden vendors and vendors suspended from new orders, but the upstream search/suggestion/autocomplete request shaping and cached suggestion-product revalidation still handled those suppression modes inconsistently. That left a narrower integrity gap where public counts, facet inputs, or cached suggestion products could still reflect vendors that the final visible item list would later strip.
- impact: On 2026-05-27 the inspected query surface moved to a combined suppressed-vendor loader before browse/search/suggestion/autocomplete requests and before cached suggestion/autocomplete product revalidation. CommerceHub query cache keys now include the combined suppression state, global browse/search requests exclude currently suspended vendors in addition to governance-hidden vendors, and cached or fallback suggestion/autocomplete product payloads fail closed for governance-hidden vendors instead of only for vendor-financial suspension.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardening, focused runtime tests, architecture freeze coverage, and certification-documentation updates added on 2026-05-27.
- residual operational risk: This closes the reviewed CommerceHub read/query drift, not every search-index maintenance path. Elasticsearch may still contain suppressed-vendor documents until overlay-governed replay/reindex/recovery mutates them, and future standalone suggestion indexes, ranking snapshots, feed/export readers, or cache warmers still need path-specific certification.
- prevention rule: CommerceHub browse/search/suggestion/autocomplete request shaping, cache keys, cached response revalidation, and public count/facet inputs must suppress both governance-hidden vendors and vendors suspended from new orders before the response is returned. Item-only post-filtering is not sufficient because it leaves stale public counts or cached suggestion payloads live.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubQueryService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/CommerceHubQueryServiceSuggestionVisibilityTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/marketplace-governance-certification-findings.md`

### Admin product read-model rebuild is SQL-recomposed and tombstone-aware
- title: AdminSync product-variant repair can no longer resurrect unpublished, deleted, inactive, offline, or invalid-category storefront/homepage visibility by full-replacing Mongo product documents
- affected systems: `AdminSyncController`, storefront Mongo product documents, homepage featured/new-arrival/best-seller and curated product hydration, product search fallback reads, affiliate-mode storefront discovery, recommendation/cache warmup paths that hydrate from storefront product documents
- root cause: The admin product-variant repair path rebuilt Mongo product documents from SQL field copies and previously could preserve `IsOnlineVisible` without requiring current `PublishStatus.Published` or live SQL category lineage. Under delayed repair/replay timing, that could undo a lifecycle tombstone and restore derivative homepage/recommendation visibility metadata.
- impact: PRODUCT-CON-09 runtime hardening routes AdminSync rebuild through `ProductSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay`, includes deleted SQL rows in the repair scan so tombstone convergence can run, upserts only SQL-recomposed public product projections, removes the dormant direct SQL-to-Mongo full-document mapper, and writes `ProductReadRepository.TombstoneIfFreshAsync(...)` when SQL does not prove active, online, published, live-category visibility. The focused source guard now requires this SQL overlay and tombstone behavior.
- mitigation status: Runtime/rebuild hardened on 2026-05-21 for the inspected AdminSync product read-model repair endpoint. Focused architecture guard passed; API build passed after a first parallel build attempt hit a transient Windows `obj` file lock.
- residual operational risk: This is not a universal recommendation rebuild framework. Existing storefront homepage reads, CommerceHub homepage projection, CommerceHub search reindex, and CommerceHub auto-healing were already SQL-overlay protected on inspected paths; future personalized recommendation/ranking engines, external feed exporters, CDN/cache warmers, bulk importers, or new direct repair endpoints must adopt the same SQL recomposition and tombstone/delete convergence explicitly. The single-product AdminSync endpoint still returns 404 for a hard-deleted SQL row because it cannot recover a product timestamp/version from SQL; normal soft-delete tombstones remain covered.
- prevention rule: Admin repair payloads, Mongo product documents, cached homepage cards, Elasticsearch hits, and partial SQL field copies are not rebuild authority. Product read-model repair must recompose current PostgreSQL product/category/media/variant/stock/price/promotion/publication authority before mutation and must prefer freshness-aware tombstones over stale rebuild persistence.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AdminSyncController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/ProductSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/MongoDB/Services/ProductReadRepository.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests.cs`

### Storefront product projection governance is consolidated behind a shared SQL-authority writer
- title: Storefront product create/update/lifecycle/admin rebuild paths no longer each carry their own SQL overlay + tombstone + upsert sequence or expose an optional non-SQL fallback constructor
- affected systems: `ProductCreatedEventConsumer`, `ProductUpdatedEventConsumer`, `ProductVisibilityLifecycleProjectionConsumer`, `AdminSyncController`, storefront Mongo `ProductDocument` writes, future test harnesses or repair tooling that instantiate those paths
- root cause: The hardened storefront product projection paths already recomposed from SQL, but the authority sequencing was duplicated across multiple callers. Two runtime consumers still allowed optional construction without `ApplicationDbContext`, which created a future-governance hole where event-payload-driven Mongo writes could be reintroduced by misregistration or copied code.
- impact: GLOBAL-REL-CON adds `IProductSqlAuthorityProjectionWriter` as the shared storefront product projection writer. The writer centralizes `ProductSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay` recomposition, event-version stamping, freshness-aware tombstone convergence, and Mongo upsert behavior. Product create/update consumers no longer have an `ApplicationDbContext`-less path, and lifecycle/admin rebuild now use the same writer instead of hand-rolled overlay/tombstone logic. On 2026-05-21 the follow-up consolidation sweep also closed a real completeness regression by forcing SQL recomposition of `ProductDocument.DefaultUnit`, `ProductDocument.Units`, simple-product purchasability price semantics, purchase price, and storefront detail metadata on the shared overlay path, and removed the remaining inspected direct full `ProductDocument` replacement from promotion refresh.
- mitigation status: Runtime governance hardened on 2026-05-21 for the inspected storefront product create/update/lifecycle/admin rebuild paths. `PointOfSale.Infrastructure` build passed and focused architecture/source-guard tests passed locally.
- residual operational risk: This is a storefront-product projection governance primitive, not a universal projection framework. CommerceHub listing/search writes, affiliate product docs, promotion repair, order repair, and future recommendation/feed/cache-warmer writers still keep their own guarded paths and may still contain legitimate local duplication. Future tooling can still bypass the rule if it writes Mongo directly outside the shared writer and outside the covered source guards. Promotion/category overlays are still not a universal partial-update framework; only the inspected product-promotion full-replacement bypass is closed.
- prevention rule: Storefront `ProductDocument` mutation is derivative visibility only. Product create/update lifecycle consumers, admin repair endpoints, and future product rebuild tools must use `IProductSqlAuthorityProjectionWriter`; optional non-SQL product projection fallbacks and hand-rolled overlay/tombstone/upsert copies are forbidden.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/MongoDB/Services/ProductSqlAuthorityProjectionWriter.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductCreatedEventConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductUpdatedEventConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductVisibilityLifecycleProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AdminSyncController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/EventOutboxConsistencyArchitectureTests.cs`

### Storefront simple-product unit/default-unit projection drift is closed on the shared SQL overlay path
- title: Storefront simple-product `DefaultUnit` / `Units` and non-purchasable-without-default-unit semantics no longer drift when shared SQL recomposition or promotion fan-out touches `ProductDocument`
- affected systems: `ProductSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay`, `IProductSqlAuthorityProjectionWriter`, storefront Mongo `ProductDocument`, storefront detail/list/category/homepage reads, cart simple-product price validation, product search documents, CommerceHub product detail fallback pricing, promotion overlay refresh
- root cause: The shared storefront SQL overlay had been extracted around category/media/variant/stock/pricing/promotion/tombstone recomposition, but it did not reload current SQL `ProductUnits` or rebuild `DefaultUnit` / `Units`. Storefront runtime still depended on those fields for simple-product price and purchasability, and promotion refresh still had one direct full `ProductDocument` replacement path that could preserve or overwrite stale in-memory unit state.
- impact: The 2026-05-21 consolidation fix sweep now reloads current SQL `ProductUnits` + `Units` on every shared overlay pass, repopulates `DefaultUnit` / `Units`, recomposes purchase price plus structured storefront metadata, makes simple products without a current default unit non-purchasable on storefront-facing price helpers, moves product promotion refresh to field-scoped overlay updates, and adds deterministic writer/source-guard tests for unit add/update/delete convergence plus guarded-writer enforcement.
- mitigation status: Runtime and rebuild semantics hardened on 2026-05-21 for the inspected storefront writer/search-overlay/promotion-refresh/search-mapper/cart/detail/list/homepage/category paths. Focused deterministic xUnit and source-guard slices passed locally. A standalone `dotnet build PointOfSale.Tests\\PointOfSale.Tests.csproj` timed out twice under the current workspace, but the targeted test slice compiled/reran successfully.
- residual operational risk: This closes the inspected shared storefront overlay regression, not every product-shaped projection in the system. Affiliate public/intelligence price display, AI/analytics product-context readers, future feed exporters, custom Mongo repair scripts, and any new direct product-document overlay writers must still be reviewed if they read or mutate simple-product price/purchasability outside the covered storefront helpers. The broader promotion consumer remains only partially migrated even though the inspected product full-replacement bypass is gone.
- prevention rule: `DefaultUnit` and `Units` are storefront-authority-critical. Product events, Mongo snapshots, search documents, promotion overlay state, cache warmers, rebuild inputs, and legacy product-level price fields are never sufficient authority for simple-product purchasability. Every shared storefront writer/rebuild/search-overlay path must recompose current SQL unit state, and missing default unit must fail closed to non-purchasable visibility.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/ProductSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/MongoDB/Documents/ProductDocument.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/PromotionProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Storefront/StorefrontProductService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Storefront/ProductSearchService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Storefront/StorefrontHomeService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Cart/CartService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Documents/ProductSearchDocumentMapper.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/ProductSqlAuthorityProjectionWriterTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests.cs`

### Multi-channel product publication replay is SQL-recomposed on hardened storefront/search/affiliate/CommerceHub paths
- title: Storefront publication lifecycle, product search, affiliate public/intelligence visibility, and CommerceHub listing/search visibility no longer treat stale publication projections as final authority on inspected paths
- affected systems: storefront product Mongo documents, product Elasticsearch documents, affiliate public product policy documents, affiliate product intelligence, CommerceHub listing Mongo documents, CommerceHub search documents, homepage/discovery consumers that use product/search/listing visibility, category/media/stock/promotion publication convergence, delayed publication retry and repair paths
- root cause: Product publication changes were not emitted as a first-class lifecycle event from the main product update path, the storefront publication lifecycle consumer was not registered, search overlays did not consistently require current SQL `PublishStatus.Published`, CommerceHub listing projection did not consume product publication changes, stale listing-unpublish replay could hide a currently SQL-published listing, and affiliate publication/intelligence propagation did not consistently require SQL published state.
- impact: PRODUCT-CON-07 runtime hardening now emits `ProductPublishStatusChangedEvent` for publish-status/online-visibility mutations, registers the storefront lifecycle consumer, fences duplicate storefront lifecycle side effects, recomposes storefront documents from SQL through `ProductSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay`, and tombstones when SQL no longer proves publication. Product and CommerceHub search overlays require SQL `PublishStatus.Published`; CommerceHub search also requires SQL online visibility. CommerceHub listing projection consumes product publication changes, recomposes from current SQL product + listing authority, field-updates listing visibility with freshness metadata, applies the SQL overlay before listing upsert/search sync, and ignores stale unpublish replay when SQL says the listing is currently published. Affiliate public/intelligence consumers now consume publication changes and require SQL published state before retaining visibility.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-21 for inspected storefront lifecycle, search, affiliate public/intelligence, and CommerceHub listing/search publication paths. Infrastructure/API builds passed; focused architecture guard coverage passed.
- residual operational risk: This is not a full publication architecture rewrite. Dedicated external feed/export publication writers were not found as separate runtime writers and remain future review targets if introduced. Future bulk product import, direct admin repair, direct SQL, marketplace sync, or delayed publication retry paths that mutate `PublishStatus`/`IsOnlineVisible` must emit the publication lifecycle trigger or call equivalent SQL recomposition/tombstone behavior. Affiliate intelligence republish still relies on adjacent product update projection for rich-content rebuild. No database constraint ties every derivative search/Mongo/feed document to current publication truth; enforcement is runtime/rebuild on migrated paths.
- prevention rule: Product publication events, stale Mongo/read-model documents, affiliate/CommerceHub/search documents, homepage/discovery state, feed/export snapshots, caches, repair payloads, and delayed retries are triggers or derivative visibility only. Public publication visibility must be restored or removed from current PostgreSQL product publication authority and current SQL listing publication authority where applicable; stale publish replay must not resurrect hidden/deleted products, and stale unpublish replay must not hide currently SQL-published products.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/ProductsController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Program.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductVisibilityLifecycleProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/ProductSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/CommerceHubProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/Affiliate/AffiliateProductPolicyProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/Affiliate/AffiliateProductIntelligenceProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests.cs`

### Product cache invalidation fanout replay is fenced on all product cache event paths
- title: Product create/update/delete/stock/price/publication cache invalidation no longer repeats Redis product/homepage invalidation side effects for duplicate active EventId replay on the hardened consumer path
- affected systems: Redis product list/detail caches, promotion-by-product caches, storefront homepage Redis caches, publication-driven homepage invalidation, product update/delete cache fanout, retry/redelivery paths for `ProductCacheInvalidationConsumer`
- root cause: `ProductCacheInvalidationConsumer` had duplicate side-effect collapse for stock and price events only. Product create, update, delete, and publication events still directly performed product-list, product-detail, promotion, and homepage pattern invalidation, so bulk publication or replayed product update/delete events could amplify Redis invalidation storms. Product detail invalidation also used payload slug when present instead of preferring current SQL product slug.
- impact: PRODUCT-CON-08 runtime hardening now requires `ApplicationDbContext` for `ProductCacheInvalidationConsumer`, acquires `ProjectionConsumerEventFence` before Redis side effects for product create/update/delete/stock/price/publication events, completes the fence only after the best-effort invalidation attempt, and reloads current SQL product slug with query filters ignored before product-detail cache invalidation. Duplicate or active EventId overlap now collapses before Redis product/homepage/promotion cache fanout.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-21 for the inspected product cache invalidation consumer. Focused architecture guard passed; Infrastructure and API builds passed after a first parallel build attempt hit a transient Windows `obj` file lock.
- residual operational risk: Cache invalidation remains best-effort infrastructure and not business authority. Distinct older EventIds can still invalidate caches, lower-risk CMS/navigation/footer cache invalidation remains lighter-weight, and Redis pattern deletion can still be operationally expensive under a legitimate large publication batch. Future cache warmers, feed exporters, CDN purge workers, or bulk import/admin repair paths must add their own replay collapse before costly fanout.
- prevention rule: Product cache events, publication payloads, Redis keys, cached slugs, and homepage cache state are triggers/caches only. High-volume product cache invalidation must require SQL context, collapse duplicate active EventId overlap before Redis side effects, and prefer current SQL product identity/slug over stale payload cache keys.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductCacheInvalidationConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests.cs`

### Homepage and recommendation visibility replay is SQL-recomposed on the hardened storefront and CommerceHub homepage paths
- title: Storefront homepage product cards, curated homepage product hydration, homepage section references, and CommerceHub homepage projections no longer trust cached/replayed discovery state as final visibility authority
- affected systems: storefront homepage featured/new-arrival/best-seller shelves, curated homepage products-by-id hydration, homepage section product/category JSON, CommerceHub homepage read model, Redis homepage caches, affiliate-mode homepage product visibility, stock/price/media/promotion homepage convergence
- root cause: Homepage reads could serve Redis/Mongo-derived product card snapshots until TTL expiry, curated product hydration could return Mongo product documents without SQL visibility recomposition, homepage section authoring validated CommerceHub product references from Mongo, and CommerceHub homepage rebuilds copied section JSON without scrubbing deleted/hidden product or inactive category references. Stock cache invalidation also left homepage caches live on stock-only changes.
- impact: PRODUCT-CON-06 runtime hardening now recomposes storefront homepage product cards from current PostgreSQL product, category lineage, variant stock, media, price, promotion, publication, and affiliate-policy authority before returning cached or projected results. Homepage section authoring and read paths validate curated CommerceHub listing/category references against SQL listing/product/variant/category/publication state. CommerceHub homepage projection scrubs stale curated product/category IDs and drops curated sections that lose all SQL-live references. Stock events now invalidate homepage caches, and homepage publish invalidation removes all localized CommerceHub homepage cache entries for the scope.
- mitigation status: Runtime/rebuild hardened on 2026-05-21 for the inspected storefront homepage product shelves, curated homepage hydration, homepage section reference validation/scrubbing, CommerceHub homepage projection rebuild, and related cache invalidation paths. API and Infrastructure builds passed; focused marketplace homepage projection, super-admin homepage, and source guard tests passed.
- residual operational risk: This is not a recommendation architecture rewrite. Dedicated ML/personalized recommendation engines, ranking snapshot stores, autocomplete-only recommendation indexes, future feed warmers, and external affiliate/CommerceHub bulk feed exporters were not found as separate runtime writers in this sweep and remain future review targets if introduced. Storefront product list/search pages retain their existing projection/cache model outside the homepage-specific SQL recomposition added here.
- prevention rule: Homepage/recommendation events, Redis homepage caches, Mongo product documents, Elasticsearch ranking/search hits, homepage section JSON, CommerceHub homepage documents, affiliate discovery docs, and repair/rebuild payloads are triggers or derivative visibility only. Before public homepage or recommendation visibility is served or rebuilt, the path must recompose current SQL product/listing/category/media/stock/price/promotion/publication authority and prefer deletion/tombstone/section omission when SQL no longer proves visibility.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/Storefront/StorefrontHomeService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Homepage/HomepageSectionReadService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/SuperAdmin/Services/SuperAdminHomepageSectionManagementService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/ReadModels/Marketplace/MarketplaceReadModelProjectionServices.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductCacheInvalidationConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Consumers/RedisInvalidationConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/MarketplaceReadModelProjectionTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests.cs`

### Product search visibility replay can no longer use stale Mongo/search lifecycle state as final authority on hardened paths
- title: Storefront product search create/update/stock/price/delete/archive/publication indexing now recomposes current SQL search authority before Elasticsearch mutation
- affected systems: storefront product Elasticsearch documents, product search autocomplete fields embedded in ES mappings, product stock/price search sync, product create/update search sync, product delete/archive/publication search lifecycle sync, category/search lineage, thumbnail/search convergence, promotion/search overlays, CommerceHub search overlay authority
- root cause: Some product search mutation paths still treated Mongo product visibility or lifecycle event type as enough authority for Elasticsearch side effects. Stock/price search sync checked Mongo hidden visibility before applying the SQL overlay, product delete search sync deleted unconditionally, archive/publication lifecycle events were not handled by the delete-search consumer, and the product search overlay did not explicitly reject invalid SQL category lineage.
- impact: Runtime hardening now forces product search consumers to require SQL context, collapse duplicate EventId side effects through `ProjectionEventFence`, apply `ProductSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay` before indexing/deleting, and reindex rather than delete when a stale delete/archive/publication replay arrives after SQL visibility was restored. The overlay now validates SQL category parent lineage and refuses searchable output when category authority is missing, deleted, self-referential, cyclic, or `Guid.Empty` in a parent chain.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-21 for inspected storefront product search lifecycle, stock/price, category lineage, media/thumbnail, promotion overlay, and CommerceHub overlay paths. Focused search/source guard and data-authority slices passed; API and Infrastructure builds passed.
- residual operational risk: This is not a full search architecture rewrite. Storefront search indexing still starts from Mongo in event consumers and then overlays SQL authority; normal projection lag remains an accepted tradeoff. Autocomplete/suggestion behavior is protected through the product ES document fields, but no separate dedicated suggestion index was found and hardened. Affiliate and CommerceHub search propagation remain partially migrated outside the already-hardened SQL overlay paths, especially promotion/policy refresh branches and any future feed/repair tooling.
- prevention rule: Product search events, Mongo product documents, Elasticsearch documents, affiliate search documents, CommerceHub search documents, stale thumbnails, stale category names, stale publication payloads, and search repair candidates are triggers or derivative visibility only. Before public search mutation, runtime and rebuild paths must recompose current SQL product/variant/stock/category/media/promotion/publication/listing authority, collapse duplicate side effects where migrated, and prefer delete/tombstone convergence when SQL no longer proves visibility.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/ProductSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductSearchIndexConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductUpdatedSearchIndexConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductStockPriceSearchIndexConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductDeletedSearchIndexConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/SqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests.cs`

### Category hierarchy replay can no longer resurrect invalid storefront/search/affiliate/CommerceHub lineage on hardened paths
- title: Category lineage rebuilds and category-dependent product mutations now derive live lineage from SQL instead of stale projections
- affected systems: SQL `Categories`, product create/update default category assignment, admin category sync, category search sync, storefront category tree/read repository, affiliate category product-intelligence projection, CommerceHub listing/search category paths, breadcrumb/search/category visibility, rebuild/reindex/repair paths
- root cause: Category projection and rebuild paths could trust stale parent references, Mongo category documents, event payload category names/parents, or cached/category path strings. Under replay, rebuild, or delete/archive timing, those paths could promote orphans to roots, keep invalid categories searchable, preserve stale CommerceHub category paths, or let product/default-category assignments point at deleted/orphan/cyclic category lineage.
- impact: Runtime/rebuild hardening now centralizes SQL category lineage recomposition through `CategoryLineageAuthority`, rejects deleted/missing/self/cyclic/`Guid.Empty` parent chains, and treats invalid descendants as not live. Product create/update requires a live SQL category lineage before accepting `CategoryId`. Admin category sync writes field-scoped Mongo upserts from the SQL lineage snapshot and marks invalid lineage inactive/offline. Admin category search sync deletes invalid lineage from Elasticsearch. Storefront category reads prune inactive/orphan/cyclic projection lineage and the storefront tree no longer promotes orphans to root. Affiliate category update/deactivate consumers acquire `ProjectionConsumerEventFence`, reload current SQL affiliate-category authority, and nullify product category labels when SQL lineage is no longer valid. CommerceHub listing/search category resolution rejects inactive, missing-ancestor, and cyclic global category paths and unpublishes derivative listing/search visibility when category authority is gone.
- mitigation status: Runtime/rebuild hardened on 2026-05-21 for the inspected category projection, rebuild/reindex, product-category mutation, storefront visibility, affiliate category visibility, and CommerceHub category propagation paths. Focused deterministic xUnit/source guards passed locally.
- residual operational risk: This is not a full category architecture rewrite. Storefront category documents and Redis category caches can still lag until projection/rebuild/cache expiry catches up, category mutation events still do not expose a dedicated monotonic aggregate version on `Categories`, and future raw import/admin repair tools can bypass the helper unless they are explicitly guarded. CommerceHub global category constraints remain partly application-enforced; there is no new database-level active-parent invariant in this sweep.
- prevention rule: Category events, Mongo category documents, breadcrumb/path strings, cached storefront trees, affiliate category payloads, and CommerceHub category paths are triggers or derivative visibility only. Runtime mutation and rebuild/recovery paths must reload current SQL lineage, treat invalid descendants as tombstoned/offline/deleted visibility, and prefer delete convergence over stale visibility persistence.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/Catalog/CategoryLineageAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/ProductsController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AdminDataSyncController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AdminSearchSyncController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Storefront/StorefrontCategoryService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/MongoDB/Services/CategoryReadRepository.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/Affiliate/AffiliateCategoryUpdatedConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/CommerceHubProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CategoryLineageAuthorityTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CategoryTreeBuilderTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests.cs`

### Offer promotion usage confirmation can no longer bypass projection-version authority
- title: Paid-offer usage confirmation now mutates the locked SQL promotion row through EF tracking before staging `PromotionAppliedEvent`
- affected systems: `PaymentLifecycleService`, SQL `Promotions.CurrentUsage`, `Promotions.ProjectionMutationVersion`, `PromotionAppliedEvent`, `PromotionProjectionConsumer`, storefront promotion usage visibility, promotion repair/rebuild freshness
- root cause: The payment success path locked the promotion and wrote a durable `PromotionUsage`, but then recomposed `Promotions.CurrentUsage` with `ExecuteUpdateAsync`. That bulk update bypassed the tracked `IProjectionVersioned` mutation guard, so the following `PromotionAppliedEvent` could be enriched with a non-advancing promotion version. A same-version usage event could then be rejected by promotion projection freshness filters or drift projection watermarks even though SQL usage truth was correct.
- impact: Runtime hardening now updates `promotion.CurrentUsage` on the locked tracked `Promotion` entity and stages `PromotionAppliedEvent` before the final transaction save. `ApplicationDbContext` advances `ProjectionMutationVersion` in the same SQL boundary that persists the usage-count mutation and outbox event, preserving monotonic promotion visibility truth for replay, repair, and rebuild consumers.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-21. Focused promotion visibility guard coverage passed, and Infrastructure/API builds passed locally.
- residual operational risk: Checkout authority already revalidates promotion usage from SQL, so the closed gap primarily affected derivative usage visibility and replay/rebuild freshness. Coupon usage still uses a different, non-`IProjectionVersioned` path and remains governed by coupon-specific SQL usage recomposition.
- prevention rule: Promotion usage-count mutations must not use bulk SQL paths that bypass `ProjectionMutationVersion`. If a promotion event is emitted after a SQL promotion mutation, the mutation must be tracked or explicitly advance the same monotonic version before the event is staged.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Payments/PaymentLifecycleService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/PromotionProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CheckoutPromotionVisibilityTests.cs`

### Promotion and product cache side-effect fences now complete durably after successful fan-out
- title: Promotion projection, affiliate promotion propagation, and product stock/price cache invalidation no longer collapse only active overlap while allowing later same-EventId side-effect replay
- affected systems: `PromotionProjectionConsumer`, `AffiliateProductPolicyProjectionConsumer`, `ProductCacheInvalidationConsumer`, storefront promotion overlays, affiliate public product visibility, Redis product/home/promotions cache invalidation, promotion search/cache fan-out
- root cause: Several hardened consumers acquired the SQL-backed projection fence before side effects, but selected paths did not call `CompleteAsync` after the side effects finished. That meant the advisory lock prevented concurrent duplicate delivery only while the first handler was active; a later redelivery of the same EventId could reacquire the fence and repeat Mongo, search, affiliate, or cache invalidation side effects.
- impact: Runtime hardening now records processed-event completion after promotion projection fan-out succeeds, completes the affiliate promotion fan-out fence instead of leaving it incomplete, and completes product stock/price cache-invalidation fences after best-effort cache deletion. Product stock cache invalidation also now uses the same duplicate-collapse boundary as price invalidation.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-21. Focused source guard coverage was added in `GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests`; build/test validation is recorded in the current sweep output.
- residual operational risk: Product create/update/delete/publish cache invalidations remain best-effort and unfenced because duplicate Redis deletion is intentionally low-risk. `ProductCacheInvalidationConsumer` still supports legacy optional `ApplicationDbContext` construction; production DI should continue supplying the context so high-risk stock/price cache fan-out gets durable duplicate collapse.
- prevention rule: Any consumer that acquires `ProjectionEventFence` or `ProjectionConsumerEventFence` for nontrivial visibility/search/cache side effects must complete it only after required side effects have finished or been durably staged. Acquiring without completion is only active-overlap protection, not durable replay collapse.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/PromotionProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/Affiliate/AffiliateProductPolicyProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductCacheInvalidationConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests.cs`

### Promotion search reindex can no longer replay stale promotion payload authority into Elasticsearch
- title: Product promotion search side effects now recompose current SQL promotion authority before indexing
- affected systems: `ProductPromotionSearchIndexConsumer`, storefront product Elasticsearch documents, promotion activation/update/delete/expiration search propagation, promotion search replay and background-expiration fan-out
- root cause: The main promotion projection had SQL reloads, version validation, and duplicate fences, but the adjacent search-index consumer still selected affected products from promotion event payload scope/target and indexed promotion fields from Mongo product documents. A delayed promotion update/delete/expiration or duplicate background-expiration event could therefore trigger Elasticsearch writes from stale Mongo promotion visibility after SQL promotion truth had moved on.
- impact: Promotion search indexing now treats promotion events as triggers only. The consumer requires `ApplicationDbContext`, acquires `ProjectionConsumerEventFence`, validates the event version against current SQL `Promotion.ProjectionMutationVersion`, reloads current SQL promotion scope/target, recomposes the winning active product promotion from SQL, applies that overlay in memory before indexing, and deletes/no-ops when SQL product authority no longer permits visibility.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-21. Infrastructure and API builds passed; focused reliability, event-outbox, data-authority, and promotion-visibility source guard slices passed locally.
- residual operational risk: Hard-delete promotion events necessarily use the event's last scope/target as deletion evidence when the SQL row is gone; the indexed promotion overlay is still recomposed from remaining SQL promotions before Elasticsearch mutation. Category/banner promotion visibility remains a category/storefront projection concern rather than product-search authority.
- prevention rule: Promotion search consumers must never use event payload or Mongo promotion fields as final search authority. Before Elasticsearch mutation, they must collapse duplicate EventId delivery, validate the event against current SQL promotion version when the row exists, and derive product promotion overlays from current SQL promotion truth.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductPromotionSearchIndexConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests.cs`

### Storefront config, CommerceHub metadata fan-out, and marketplace rebuild paths no longer expose the newest replay/rebuild authority gaps
- title: Duplicate storefront-config projection delivery, stale CommerceHub store metadata payloads, and marketplace rebuild of offline products are now collapsed or recomposed from SQL authority
- affected systems: `StorefrontConfigProjectionConsumer`, `CommerceHubProjectionConsumer`, `MarketplaceReadModelProjectionServices`, storefront config Mongo/Redis visibility, CommerceHub product/listing metadata/search fan-out, marketplace product/order/homepage rebuilds
- root cause: The prior hardening pass closed tenant-currency freshness and SQL currency recomposition, but adjacent derivative paths still had narrower authority gaps. Storefront-config full-document replacement reloaded SQL and preserved currency watermarks, yet duplicate active delivery could still repeat Mongo replacement and Redis invalidation before a durable EventId fence. CommerceHub store-name metadata fan-out used event payload store name while shipping/currency metadata already recomposed SQL, and metadata fan-out lacked duplicate side-effect collapse. Marketplace product rebuild deleted SQL-deleted products, but could preserve or recreate public marketplace read documents for SQL-inactive or not-online-visible products.
- impact: Storefront-config projection now uses `ProjectionEventFence` before Mongo/Redis side effects and completes the fence only after the projection/caches finish. CommerceHub store, shipping, and currency metadata fan-out now uses `ProjectionConsumerEventFence`; store name is recomposed from current SQL `StoreConfigs`/`Tenants`, not event payload authority. Marketplace product rebuild now tombstones/deletes when SQL truth is deleted, inactive, or not online-visible, and order/homepage rebuild entrypoints now explicitly declare SQL authority overlays through `SqlTruthRebuildGuard`.
- mitigation status: Runtime/rebuild hardened on 2026-05-21. API build passed; focused Storefront projection tests and deterministic architecture/reliability guard slices passed locally.
- residual operational risk: Storefront config publish/rollback semantics remain specialized and are not converted to a generic projection base. CommerceHub metadata fan-out is now fenced for duplicate EventId delivery, but broader search/index operations still depend on their existing SQL overlays and local retry semantics. Marketplace global homepage rebuild remains partly tenant-shape constrained; tenant-scoped rebuilds declare SQL homepage/store metadata authority, while truly global composition is still an aggregate visibility rebuild rather than a single-row SQL authority claim.
- prevention rule: Rebuild, replacement, and metadata fan-out paths must not use event payload, Mongo, cache, or stale visibility state as authority.
