Before derivative storefront/CommerceHub/marketplace visibility mutation, runtime code must either acquire duplicate side-effect collapse and reload current SQL authority or tombstone/delete when SQL no longer proves visibility.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/StorefrontConfigProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/CommerceHubProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/ReadModels/Marketplace/MarketplaceReadModelProjectionServices.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/SqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/ProjectionEventFence.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Storefront/StorefrontConfigProjectionConsumerTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CheckoutConcurrencyHardeningSourceTests.cs`

### Coupon and shipping-method projection replay can no longer use stale payloads or amplify cache side effects
- title: Storefront coupon visibility now rebuilds from SQL coupon/usage authority, and shipping-method projection collapses duplicate active delivery before Mongo/cache writes
- affected systems: `CouponProjectionConsumer`, `ShippingMethodProjectionConsumer`, storefront coupon validation visibility, storefront shipping-method visibility, Mongo coupon/shipping documents, Redis shipping-method cache
- root cause: Coupon projection was still a Tier 1 checkout-visibility path that mapped create/update fields and usage counters directly from event payloads. A delayed create/update or usage event could therefore rewrite Mongo coupon visibility with stale discount, scope, activation, or usage values even though checkout mutation paths revalidated SQL. Shipping-method projection already reloaded SQL and used Mongo freshness filters, but duplicate active delivery could still repeat Redis invalidation and Mongo side effects.
- impact: Coupon projection now acquires `ProjectionEventFence`, reloads current SQL `Coupon` authority for create/update/delete visibility, writes inactive tombstones when SQL authority is missing or deleted, parses product/category scope from current SQL, and derives usage projection from current `CouponUsages` rather than event counters. Shipping-method projection now acquires `ProjectionEventFence` for update/delete events and completes the fence only after Mongo/tombstone/cache side effects have safely finished.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-20. API build passed; focused coupon/promotion/checkout/event architecture guard slice passed locally.
- residual operational risk: Coupon projection is now SQL-reloaded and event-fenced, but coupon events still do not carry a true aggregate mutation version; freshness remains timestamp/version-event based with SQL recomposition. Shipping-method projection still uses its existing repository freshness/tombstone implementation rather than a shipping-specific SQL overlay. Checkout money/order authority was already SQL-recomposed and does not rely on these projections as final authority.
- prevention rule: Coupon and shipping-method events are visibility triggers only. Projection consumers must reload current SQL coupon/shipping authority, collapse duplicate EventId delivery before side effects, derive usage from SQL usage rows, and tombstone rather than preserve or resurrect visibility when SQL authority is missing or deleted.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/CouponProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ShippingMethodProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CouponProjectionConsumerConsistencyTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/EventOutboxConsistencyArchitectureTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CheckoutConcurrencyHardeningSourceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CheckoutPromotionVisibilityTests.cs`

### Payment-method and CommerceHub listing projection replay can no longer use stale payloads as visibility authority
- title: Checkout payment-method visibility and CommerceHub listing lifecycle projection now recompose SQL truth and collapse duplicate active replays before Mongo/search writes
- affected systems: `PaymentMethodProjectionConsumer`, `PaymentMethodReadRepository`, CommerceHub listing projection, CommerceHub search indexing, storefront checkout payment-method visibility, CommerceHub discovery visibility
- root cause: Two Tier 1 visibility paths still treated event payload fields as projection mutation input. Payment-method projection used event payload display/config/enabled fields instead of reloading `TenantPaymentMethods`, and CommerceHub listing create/update/unpublish used listing event payload category, price, publish state, and attributes before writing Mongo and triggering search sync. Freshness filters existed, and checkout/payment commit paths revalidated SQL, but stale replay could still resurrect or drift customer-facing visibility and repeat downstream search writes.
- impact: Payment-method projection now acquires `ProjectionEventFence`, reloads current SQL `TenantPaymentMethod` authority, validates exact SQL version, decrypts/evaluates current encrypted provider config, and writes Mongo only from SQL-derived public configuration. CommerceHub listing lifecycle projection now acquires `ProjectionConsumerEventFence`, reloads current SQL `CommerceHubListing` and SQL variant price authority, uses SQL category/publish/attribute state, and unpublishes/deletes derivative visibility when SQL listing authority is missing or deleted. Duplicate active delivery collapses before Mongo/search side effects on both paths.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-20. Infrastructure/API builds passed; focused architecture source guards were updated for the new runtime boundaries.
- residual operational risk: This hardens projection visibility and search fan-out, not customer checkout/payment authority, which was already SQL-recomposed in checkout/payment services. Payment-method events remain legacy non-`IntegrationEvent` contracts, so the shared fence exposes a legacy EventId overload rather than requiring an event-contract migration in this sweep. Other Tier 1 checkout-visibility consumers remain mixed as documented in the reliability adoption strategy.
- prevention rule: Checkout payment-method and CommerceHub listing projection events are triggers only. Runtime projection must reload current SQL payment-method/listing/variant authority and use duplicate EventId fences before Mongo/search/cache side effects; stale payloads must not recreate enabled payment options, marketplace publication, category, price, attributes, or search visibility.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/PaymentMethodProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/CommerceHubProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/ProjectionEventFence.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/EventOutboxConsistencyArchitectureTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CheckoutConcurrencyHardeningSourceTests.cs`

### Reservation cleanup can no longer release active inventory from stale candidate snapshots
- title: Inventory reservation cleanup rechecks locked SQL order/payment/reservation authority before local stock and coupon release
- affected systems: `InventoryReservationCleanupJob`, storefront reservation cleanup, CommerceHub reservation cleanup, stock release outbox propagation, coupon reservation release
- root cause: The cleanup job selected stale reservation candidates and order/payment state before entering the mutation path. The lifecycle payment-expiration branch had stronger downstream guards, but the direct local release branch could still release stock and coupon reservations from an old candidate snapshot if order/payment/provider truth advanced after discovery.
- impact: Cleanup candidate discovery is now evidence only. Before any local `ReleaseOrderReservationsAsync(...)` call, the job clears stale tracking, opens a serializable cleanup transaction, reloads current reservation, storefront order, CommerceHub order, payment-intent, CommerceHub payment, and unresolved-webhook authority, and collapses to skip or lifecycle expiration if current SQL no longer permits direct release. Current remaining reserved quantity is recomputed at mutation time for side-effect metrics.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-20. API build passed; focused `CheckoutConcurrencyHardeningSourceTests` passed locally.
- residual operational risk: This hardens the cleanup release boundary, not every possible future reservation repair utility. Direct admin cancellation and payment lifecycle paths retain their existing lifecycle/repository guards. Future bulk reservation repair tools must reuse the same SQL recomposition pattern before stock or coupon release.
- prevention rule: Reservation cleanup scans are not authority. Before releasing inventory or coupon reservations, cleanup/repair code must re-read current SQL reservation plus order/payment/provider evidence under a transaction and row locks, and skip when active or uncertain authority is present.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Jobs/InventoryReservationCleanupJob.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CheckoutConcurrencyHardeningSourceTests.cs`

### Search maintenance rebuilds can no longer resurrect stale storefront or CommerceHub product visibility from Mongo
- title: Admin search reindex, CommerceHub reindex, and CommerceHub auto-healing cannot re-index stale Mongo product/listing documents after SQL product/listing/variant authority has changed
- affected systems: `SearchReindexController`, `CommerceHubReindexService`, `CommerceHubAutoHealingService`, `CommerceHubProjectionConsumer`, storefront product search index, CommerceHub search index, CommerceHub search-sync status repair
- root cause: Search maintenance paths are intentionally rebuild/repair side-effect tools, but several of them still started from MongoDB read models. Storefront search reindex overlaid only media/variant details and could leave Mongo `IsActive` / `IsOnlineVisible` as indexing authority. CommerceHub reindex and auto-healing could re-index a stale published Mongo listing without first proving the current SQL `CommerceHubListing`, product, variant, tenant, category, stock, price, currency, and tombstone authority still permitted marketplace search visibility.
- impact: Storefront search reindex now applies `ProductSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay` and deletes the Elasticsearch document when SQL product authority is missing or no longer active/online-visible. CommerceHub search maintenance now uses `CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay` before indexing from Mongo in reindex, catch-up reindex, auto-healing, and consumer search-sync retry paths. The overlay reloads current SQL listing/product/variant/tenant/category/shipping/stock/price/currency authority and returns no visibility authority when SQL is deleted, unpublished, inactive, missing, or otherwise not eligible; callers collapse that to Elasticsearch delete instead of re-indexing stale Mongo.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-20. API build passed; focused `GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests` source guard passed locally.
- residual operational risk: This prevents stale Mongo from resurrecting Elasticsearch visibility on the hardened maintenance paths. It does not yet turn CommerceHub reindex into a full SQL-origin rebuild for listings missing entirely from Mongo; those still require projection repair/outbox replay to recreate Mongo before search can index them. CommerceHub query fallback paths still read Mongo by design and remain derivative visibility, not checkout/payment/fulfillment authority.
- prevention rule: Search rebuild, reindex, catch-up, and auto-healing paths must treat Mongo documents as candidates only. Before indexing storefront or CommerceHub product/listing visibility, they must reload SQL authority or delete/no-op when SQL does not prove visibility.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/SearchReindexController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Jobs/CommerceHubReindexService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubAutoHealingService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/CommerceHubProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests.cs`

### Tenant currency visibility replay can no longer resurrect stale storefront, affiliate, or CommerceHub currency
- title: Delayed tenant-currency events and storefront-config rebuilds cannot overwrite current SQL tenant currency or amplify duplicate cache/search side effects from stale payload authority
- affected systems: `TenantCurrencyProjectionConsumer`, `StorefrontConfigProjectionConsumer`, `CommerceHubProjectionConsumer`, affiliate product intelligence projection, affiliate public product policy projection, storefront config Redis cache, CommerceHub search, affiliate search
- root cause: Tenant-currency visibility was field-freshness fenced, but several projection consumers still used the event payload currency as mutation input. `StorefrontConfigProjectionConsumer` also replaced the full `storefront_configs` document from SQL and could clear the independent `LastCurrencyEvent*` watermark, allowing an older delayed `TenantCurrencyChangedEvent` to pass the currency-specific filter later. That path could regress derivative storefront/affiliate/CommerceHub currency visibility and repeat cache/search side effects even though SQL tenant currency remained correct.
- impact: Currency projection consumers now reload current `Tenant.Currency` from PostgreSQL immediately before visibility mutation. `TenantCurrencyProjectionConsumer` also uses `ProjectionEventFence` to collapse duplicate active/completed EventId delivery before Redis cache invalidation. Storefront config replacement preserves existing `LastCurrencyEventId`, `LastCurrencyEventOccurredAtUtc`, and `LastCurrencyEventVersion` so rebuild/replace paths do not open a stale currency replay window. CommerceHub and affiliate currency fan-out now converge from SQL tenant currency instead of provider/event payload currency before search/cache propagation.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-20. API build passed; focused architecture/source guards for tenant currency, CHECKOUT-CON-08 visibility, and affiliate currency projection passed locally.
- residual operational risk: This hardens tenant-currency visibility convergence, not every Tier 1 projection. Payment-method, shipping-method, coupon, promotion, storefront-config, and CommerceHub listing projections still have mixed local/shared guard adoption as documented in the reliability strategy. Checkout/payment/ledger authority already recomposes SQL and does not rely on these projections for money truth.
- prevention rule: Tenant-currency events are triggers/evidence only for visibility consumers. Any storefront, affiliate, CommerceHub, search, cache, rebuild, or repair path that writes currency visibility must reload current SQL `Tenant.Currency` or preserve an existing stronger SQL-derived freshness watermark; full-document rebuilds must not clear field-specific currency fences.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/TenantCurrencyProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/StorefrontConfigProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/CommerceHubProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/Affiliate/AffiliateProductIntelligenceProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/Affiliate/AffiliateProductPolicyProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/EventOutboxConsistencyArchitectureTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CheckoutConcurrencyHardeningSourceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/AffiliateCurrencyProjectionArchitectureTests.cs`

### Recovery/reconciliation workers now recompose locked SQL truth before clearing external-money evidence
- title: Stale payment and payout reconciliation workers cannot clear unresolved money, release marketplace holds, enqueue suspense clearance, or quarantine payouts from stale candidate snapshots
- affected systems: `CommerceHubFinanceReconciliationJob`, `StorefrontUnresolvedPaymentReconciliationJob`, `CommerceHubPayoutReconciliationHostedService`, accounting outbox suspense-clearance enqueue, CommerceHub marketplace hold release, payout reconciliation status mutation
- root cause: Several recovery sweeps selected candidate rows before opening the recovery mutation boundary. The downstream payment lifecycle, webhook, refund, payout, and fulfillment services already carried strong guards, but these worker-local reconciliation updates could still use stale candidate/payment/order/payout snapshots for recovery metadata, suspense clearance, marketplace hold release, or processing-payout quarantine.
- impact: CHECKOUT-CON-14 now makes candidate discovery read-only. Each CommerceHub unresolved captured-payment candidate, storefront suspense-routed payment webhook, and stale processing payout is reloaded inside a serializable recovery transaction and locked with provider-specific row locks (`FOR UPDATE` / `UPDLOCK, ROWLOCK, HOLDLOCK`) before mutation. CommerceHub captured-payment clearance also locks the current paid payment row and current marketplace order row before suspense-clearance outbox enqueue or hold release. Storefront unresolved payment recovery locks the webhook log, payment intent, and order before payment lifecycle replay and resolved-unresolved accounting enqueue. Payout reconciliation locks the current payout row before moving `Processing` to `ReconciliationRequired`.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-20 for CHECKOUT-CON-14. API build, focused `FinancialIntegrityRiskHardeningTests`, and adjacent finance/refund/fulfillment/checkout regression source slices passed locally.
- residual operational risk: This is targeted recovery/reconciliation authority hardening, not a universal recovery framework. Refund recovery, carrier recovery, shipment authority recovery, accounting outbox replay, affiliate commission approval, and provider callbacks remain protected by their existing specialized lifecycle/transaction/readiness/idempotency guards. Some candidate discovery still inspects provider/webhook evidence, but mutation authority now re-enters SQL truth on the hardened paths.
- prevention rule: Recovery candidate scans are not authority. Before resolving unresolved external money, clearing suspense, releasing marketplace holds, marking payout reconciliation state, or enqueueing recovery accounting outbox work, the current SQL evidence row plus the current payment/order/payout authority rows must be reloaded under a transaction and row lock. Terminal or changed SQL truth must collapse the replay or remain in reconciliation/manual review.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubFinanceReconciliationJob.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/StorefrontUnresolvedPaymentReconciliationJob.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPayoutReconciliationHostedService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/FinancialIntegrityRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### Post-commit financial accounting fast paths now recompose SQL truth before ledger mutation
- title: Immediate payment/refund/settlement accounting shortcuts cannot post ledger mutations from stale in-memory lifecycle snapshots after partial capture/refund/settlement authority has advanced
- affected systems: `PaymentLifecycleService`, ecommerce payment collection accounting, ecommerce refund accounting, gateway settlement accounting, COD settlement accounting, accounting outbox completion, CommerceHub payment/refund propagation after accounting confirmation
- root cause: The durable accounting outbox retry path already reloads SQL authority before posting, but the immediate best-effort post-commit shortcuts could dispatch ledger work from the just-mutated in-memory order/refund/settlement values. Under delayed provider callbacks, duplicate settlement replay, partial refund overlap, or stale recovery timing, that shortcut was a narrower stale-authority surface before ledger mutation.
- impact: The immediate collection, refund, gateway settlement, and COD settlement accounting paths now clear tracked state, open a lifecycle SQL transaction, reload locked SQL order/refund or settlement-batch authority (`FOR UPDATE` / `UPDLOCK, ROWLOCK, HOLDLOCK` where supported), reload the staged accounting outbox row, collapse already-processed replay, and only then post accounting and mark the outbox processed. Refund accounting context is rebuilt from the SQL `OrderRefund`; settlement accounting derives amount, fees, provider, and settlement id from the SQL `PaymentSettlementBatch`.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-20 for CHECKOUT-CON-12. API build and focused financial integrity/refund/payout/refund-execution/ledger/checkout-concurrency deterministic slices passed locally.
- residual operational risk: This is targeted partial financial lineage hardening, not a rewrite of accounting, payout, provider settlement, or reconciliation architecture. Some discovery/recovery paths may still inspect provider evidence or stale projections to find work, but mutation paths must re-enter SQL authority before side effects. A broader adjacent integration slice was blocked by existing local fixture/schema drift (`Orders.FinancialPostedAtUtc` missing in the local database and unrelated tenant co-ownership fixture setup gaps).
- prevention rule: Provider settlement payloads, payout payloads, cached settlement state, stale reconciliation payloads, replayed callbacks, and stale accounting rebuild inputs are evidence only. Before capture, refund, settlement, payout, accounting, commission, reconciliation, or fulfillment-financial mutation, current SQL payment/order/refund/fulfillment/accounting truth must be recomposed under the appropriate lock/transaction and stale replay must collapse or fail closed.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Payments/PaymentLifecycleService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/FinancialIntegrityRiskHardeningTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Accounting/AccountingOutboxProcessor.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Payments/CommerceHubPayoutService.cs`

### Delayed provider callbacks are now fenced by terminal SQL payment truth
- title: Delayed or out-of-order provider success/failure callbacks cannot overwrite terminal SQL payment/order truth or recreate CommerceHub payment mutation authority
- affected systems: `PaymentLifecycleService`, Stripe/Paymob/Fawry lifecycle callbacks, CommerceHub provider webhooks, CommerceHub financial workflow, fulfillment release after payment, accounting/payout eligibility derived from payment state
- root cause: Provider callbacks are external derivative signals and can arrive after SQL payment, order, reservation, fulfillment, refund, capture, accounting, or payout truth has already advanced. Without a final locked SQL recomposition immediately before provider-driven mutation, a stale success/failure/capture/settlement replay could attempt to regress terminal payment state, resurrect cancelled authority, or amplify fulfillment/accounting side effects.
- impact: Payment lifecycle success/failure handling now reloads locked SQL payment/order truth before provider callback decisions. Stale provider success against terminal non-success SQL truth is rejected into manual review without payment/order/accounting/fulfillment mutation. Stale provider failure/cancel after terminal SQL success/refund truth collapses as a no-op replay. CommerceHub webhook outcome application now reloads and locks the current SQL `CommerceHubPayment` and marketplace order rows inside a serializable transaction before provider identity, amount, state, financial workflow, fulfillment, or order-state mutation.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-20 for CHECKOUT-CON-11. Focused financial-integrity source tests, checkout concurrency regression slices, CommerceHub payment service slices, and API build passed locally.
- residual operational risk: This is targeted delayed/out-of-order provider callback hardening, not a universal provider framework rewrite. Refund/capture/settlement/payout/rebuild flows still use specialized local guards and some operational/manual review branches rather than uniform `ProjectionEventFence` coverage at every ingress point. Recovery candidate discovery may inspect provider evidence, but mutation paths must re-enter SQL authority before side effects.
- prevention rule: Provider callbacks, redirect payloads, settlement payloads, cached payment state, provider reconciliation payloads, and replayed webhook payloads are evidence only. Before payment, capture, refund, fulfillment, payout, accounting, reconciliation, or order-state mutation, the service must recompose current SQL order/payment/fulfillment truth under the appropriate lock/transaction and either collapse replay, reject stale authority, or route unresolved external money to reconciliation/manual review.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Payments/PaymentLifecycleService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/FinancialIntegrityRiskHardeningTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CheckoutConcurrencyHardeningSourceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentServiceTests.cs`

### Fulfillment replay after inventory consumption is now fenced by SQL fulfillment authority recomposition
- title: Delayed or duplicate fulfillment/shipment replay cannot advance outbound fulfillment from stale callback, session, carrier, warehouse, or recovery payload authority after inventory consumption
- affected systems: `OrderFulfillmentService`, `ShipmentService`, carrier shipment reconciliation, inventory delivery commit, affiliate delivery commission approval, CommerceHub payout eligibility reads, fulfillment recovery/reconciliation
- root cause: Fulfillment lifecycle mutation and shipment mutation are separate replay surfaces from checkout, inventory reservation, payment, and stock-consumption authority. Without a final SQL recomposition immediately before shipment mutation, warehouse allocation/status mutation, fulfillment release, recovery, or fulfillment-derived commission eligibility, stale callback/recovery payloads could attempt to recreate shipment eligibility, regress fulfillment state, or amplify downstream side effects after SQL inventory truth had already moved forward.
- impact: Fulfillment lifecycle transitions now open a serializable SQL authority boundary when they own the transaction, reload and lock the current SQL order row (`FOR UPDATE` / `UPDLOCK, ROWLOCK, HOLDLOCK`), and collapse duplicate or older outbound fulfillment targets against current SQL fulfillment truth before lifecycle events are emitted. Shipment creation, shipment status mutation, and shipment authority repair now use serializable SQL transactions around their existing locked order recomposition and fulfillment synchronization. Affiliate delivery approval now reloads locked SQL order fulfillment/payment/accounting authority inside a serializable transaction before approving delivered commissions. Inventory delivery commit remains protected by existing event fencing, reservation/stock row locks, `alreadyCommitted`/`remainingToCommit` accounting, and stock movement idempotency.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-20 for CHECKOUT-CON-10. Focused fulfillment architecture tests, shipment/inventory replay tests, CHECKOUT-CON-01 through 09 regression slices, and STOCK/PRICE/VAR smoke slices passed locally.
- residual operational risk: This is targeted fulfillment replay hardening, not a universal fulfillment engine rewrite. Carrier webhook and warehouse/provider-specific callback edges still keep specialized local idempotency/status-progression protections rather than uniform `ProjectionEventFence` coverage at every ingress point. Recovery candidate discovery may still inspect provider/read-model evidence, but mutation paths must re-enter SQL fulfillment/shipment authority before side effects.
- prevention rule: Fulfillment payloads, carrier callbacks, warehouse callbacks, shipment sessions, caches, and recovery payloads are evidence only. After inventory consumption, any mutation that can affect shipment lineage, warehouse allocation, carrier release, inventory movement, fulfillment lifecycle, commission eligibility, or payout eligibility must recompose current SQL order/payment/reservation/inventory/fulfillment truth and fail closed or collapse replay before side effects.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Fulfillment/OrderFulfillmentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Shipping/ShipmentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Services/Affiliate/AffiliateService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/InventoryConsistencyService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/OrderFulfillmentArchitectureTests.cs`

### Carrier registration success can no longer persist over newer shipment/order SQL truth
- title: Stale carrier registration completion cannot resurrect cancelled, terminal, or otherwise advanced shipment authority after the external carrier call returns
- affected systems: `CarrierShipmentService`, carrier registration recovery, shipment external registration consumer, shipment authority recovery, shipment/order status propagation, fulfillment synchronization, carrier-side operational release evidence
- root cause: Carrier create calls correctly execute outside the local SQL transaction, but the previous success-persistence helper could apply the carrier result to the shipment/order instances loaded before the external call. If cancellation, terminal shipment state, or payment/accounting authority changed while the carrier call was in flight, the post-call local persistence path was a stale authority surface.
- impact: CHECKOUT-CON-13 hardens carrier success persistence and registration reconciliation. After carrier create or registration lookup succeeds, the service now opens a serializable SQL boundary, clears stale EF tracking, reloads and locks current `Shipments` and `Orders` rows (`FOR UPDATE` / `UPDLOCK, ROWLOCK, HOLDLOCK`), rejects cancelled/terminal/already-advanced/incomplete-authority SQL states into `ReconciliationRequired`, records external evidence without advancing shipment status, and only applies successful carrier registration when current SQL still proves shipment authority.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-20 for CHECKOUT-CON-13. API build, focused `OrderFulfillmentArchitectureTests`, checkout/payment/inventory regression source slices, shipment external registration tests, and STOCK/PRICE/VAR smoke slices passed locally.
- residual operational risk: Carrier webhooks and tracking sync still use domain-specific webhook logs, status progression checks, and shipment authority preparation rather than a universal projection fence. External carrier execution can still succeed after local cancellation because distributed transactions are intentionally not used; the local system now preserves that as reconciliation-required evidence instead of resurrecting shipment authority.
- prevention rule: Carrier responses, carrier registration recovery payloads, delayed carrier confirmations, and carrier lookup/reconciliation data are evidence only. Before local shipment success, status progression, order shipment stamping, or fulfillment synchronization, current SQL shipment/order authority must be recomposed under lock and terminal/cancelled/stale authority must fail closed into reconciliation.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Shipping/CarrierShipmentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ShipmentExternalRegistrationRequestedConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Shipping/ShipmentAuthorityRecoveryHostedService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/OrderFulfillmentArchitectureTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Shipping/ShipmentExternalRegistrationRequestedConsumerTests.cs`

### Paymob legacy callback fallback no longer recreates payment authority after checkout finalization
- title: Replayed Paymob legacy callbacks cannot mint a new pending CommerceHub payment row after SQL order/payment/reservation authority is finalized or lost
- affected systems: `CommerceHubPaymentService`, Paymob webhook fallback handling, CommerceHub payment recovery, marketplace reservations, unresolved payment reconciliation, suspense accounting
- root cause: The legacy Paymob webhook fallback could create a `CommerceHubPayment` row from a provider/order reference when no payment row existed. Without a final SQL authority recomposition at that fallback, a stale callback after order payment collection, non-retryable order state, reservation consumption, or reservation loss could recreate stale payment authority instead of becoming unresolved external-money evidence.
- impact: The fallback now clears stale tracking and reloads current SQL marketplace order truth through `RecomposeLegacyPaymobPaymentCreationAuthorityAsync(...)` before payment row creation. It rejects already-collected and non-retryable order/payment state through `EnsureInitiationAllowedAsync(...)`, then requires active SQL reservation authority through `EnsureMarketplaceOrderReservationsActiveAsync(...)`. Replayed captured-money callbacks that cannot prove SQL authority now continue into unresolved/suspense reconciliation rather than minting a payment lane.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-20 for Paymob legacy fallback payment-row creation. Focused CommerceHub payment service and checkout source guard coverage passed locally.
- residual operational risk: This is targeted CHECKOUT-CON-09 hardening, not a universal rewrite of every provider/refund/payout/accounting recovery path. Existing payment lifecycle, CommerceHub webhooks, refund, payout, accounting, and fulfillment protections remain specialized and partially manual. Fawry webhook regression execution was blocked by local PostgreSQL schema drift (`Orders.FinancialPostedAtUtc` missing) until migrations are applied.
- prevention rule: Payment recovery and legacy callback compatibility paths must never create or resume payment authority from provider payloads alone. They must re-read SQL order/payment/reservation truth and fail closed or persist unresolved evidence when checkout finalization has already consumed authority.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentServiceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CheckoutConcurrencyHardeningSourceTests.cs`

### Coupon checkout visibility delete replay now writes a tombstone even when the prior projection is missing
- title: Out-of-order coupon visibility replay can no longer recreate stale checkout coupon eligibility after a newer delete projection arrived first
- affected systems: `CouponProjectionConsumer`, storefront coupon read models, checkout coupon visibility, stale projection replay, discount read-model repair
- root cause: `CouponProjectionConsumer` previously soft-deleted a coupon document only when the old Mongo projection already existed. If a newer SQL delete/disable event propagated before an older create/update projection ever created the read model, the delete event skipped the tombstone and a later stale V10 create/update replay could upsert active coupon visibility.
- impact: The delete consumer now treats the event as a tombstone write even when the prior projection is missing. It uses `ProjectionTombstoneGuard` to ignore already-fresher projection state, then upserts an inactive tombstone carrying the delete event version/timestamp. Later older create/update/usage replays hit the existing freshness fence and cannot resurrect coupon checkout visibility.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-20 for coupon visibility projection. Focused architecture/source validation added for CHECKOUT-CON-08 visibility derivatives and coupon tombstone-on-missing behavior.
- residual operational risk: Coupon Mongo visibility remains derivative and may lag. Checkout commit paths must continue recomposing SQL coupon/promotion/pricing authority before order, reservation, payment, affiliate, or fulfillment side effects. Broader coupon consumer migration to `ProjectionEventFence` remains incremental debt.
- prevention rule: Coupon visibility delete/expiration events must leave a freshness tombstone even when the read model is missing. Storefront coupon visibility must never become checkout authority; final checkout must revalidate SQL discount authority.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/CouponProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CheckoutPromotionVisibilityTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CheckoutConcurrencyHardeningSourceTests.cs`

### Affiliate checkout final authority now revalidates affiliate identity and link authority inside the final SQL transaction
- title: Stale affiliate account, referral, or promotion-link visibility can no longer be stamped onto a storefront checkout after affiliate authority changes before final order commit
- affected systems: `CheckoutService`, affiliate storefront checkout, affiliate funnel cart metadata, affiliate referral middleware fallback, affiliate promotion links, affiliate attribution creation, affiliate commission snapshots, payment-intent placeholder creation, inventory reservation commit
- root cause: Storefront checkout already recomposed SQL catalog, coupon/promotion, payment method, inventory reservation, and affiliate commission-policy truth inside the final serializable checkout transaction, but the resolved affiliate account/promotion-link identity was selected before that final transaction. A stale affiliate session, cart metadata snapshot, middleware context, or request referral could therefore survive until final order stamping if the affiliate account or promotion link changed between pre-checkout resolution and final commit.
- impact: The active storefront affiliate checkout path now reloads and row-locks affiliate account authority, affiliate promotion-link authority, and affiliate-user bridge authority inside the final serializable order transaction after transaction-scoped cart/pricing recomposition and before commission policy snapshots, payment-intent placeholder creation, inventory reservation commit, order attribution, or order commit. If the affiliate account is inactive/deleted, the promotion link is inactive/deleted, the link no longer belongs to the checkout tenant/product set, or the link no longer resolves to the same affiliate account, checkout fails closed with affiliate authority stale errors instead of committing stale affiliate financial context.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-20 for current storefront affiliate checkout finalization. Focused API build and checkout authority source validation passed locally.
- residual operational risk: Affiliate-facing product/search/category/banner/session/cache visibility remains derivative and can lag by design. Some affiliate visibility consumers and coupon/session test harnesses remain partially migrated/manual rather than uniformly using shared reliability primitives. Commission recording still intentionally uses immutable SQL order-item commission snapshots captured at checkout, not current affiliate projections; future affiliate checkout/recovery entrypoints must preserve that SQL-first boundary.
- prevention rule: Affiliate checkout visibility is never financial authority. Before order creation, payment-intent placeholder creation, inventory reservation commit, attribution creation, or commission snapshot persistence, the checkout transaction must recompose SQL pricing/promotion truth and revalidate current SQL affiliate account/link/policy authority.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/Checkout/CheckoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CheckoutConcurrencyHardeningSourceTests.cs`

### CommerceHub active checkout overlap now collapses before idempotency and cart side effects
- title: Duplicate CommerceHub checkout submissions for the same active customer cart cannot run as independent order/reservation/promo mutation authorities merely by changing idempotency keys
- affected systems: `CommerceHubCheckoutService`, CommerceHub cart checkout, marketplace order creation, CommerceHub checkout idempotency, marketplace promo consumption, inventory reservation, marketplace order outbox staging
- root cause: CommerceHub checkout already used idempotency-key locking and SQL recomposition, but the distributed checkout lock was scoped to `customer + idempotency key`. A mobile/browser/provider retry using a different retry key against the same active customer cart could pass the Redis key lock and rely on the later cart concurrency claim for collapse, leaving less direct active-overlap containment and a stale-idempotency cleanup edge on failed cart claims.
- impact: The active CommerceHub checkout path now acquires a customer-cart checkout lock before the idempotency-key lock and before inserting a checkout idempotency claim. The SQL cart claim also runs inside a serializable transaction and reloads the cart through provider-specific row locking (`FOR UPDATE` / `UPDLOCK, ROWLOCK, HOLDLOCK`) before marking it locked. If cart claiming or cart/request fingerprint validation fails after idempotency insertion, the pending idempotency row and cart lock are cleaned up deterministically.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-20 for current CommerceHub checkout submission. Focused architecture/source and CommerceHub checkout regression slices passed locally.
- residual operational risk: Storefront checkout already had cart-scoped execution locks, durable idempotency binding, active-order uniqueness, and serializable final authority before this sprint. CommerceHub payment initiation, provider callbacks, refunds, payouts, and reconciliation remain on their specialized payment/evidence/readiness protections rather than the checkout customer-cart lock.
- prevention rule: CommerceHub checkout idempotency is not sufficient by itself for active cart overlap. Active marketplace checkout execution must first collapse per customer cart, then bind request idempotency, then claim SQL cart/order/promo/reservation authority inside SQL transaction boundaries.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubCheckoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CheckoutConcurrencyHardeningSourceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/CommerceHubCheckoutFinancialControlTests.cs`

### Storefront and CommerceHub payment initiation now revalidates active SQL reservation authority
- title: Stale checkout replay, payment-intent recovery, or marketplace payment initiation cannot advance provider payment authority after checkout inventory reservation authority was lost
- affected systems: `CheckoutInventoryAuthorityGuard`, `PaymentService`, `StripePaymentService`, `PaymobPaymentService`, `CheckoutService` idempotency replay, `CheckoutPaymentIntentRecoveryService`, `CommerceHubPaymentService`, storefront inventory reservations, CommerceHub inventory reservations, provider payment sessions
- root cause: Storefront and CommerceHub checkout creation already recomposed final SQL catalog/pricing/payment/stock authority and reserved inventory inside checkout authority boundaries, but follow-up payment-initiation and recovery paths are separate retry/replay surfaces. Without an explicit final reservation recheck at those surfaces, a stale checkout replay or payment-intent repair could resume or create provider payment authority after cleanup/release/expiry had removed the active SQL inventory reservation.
- impact: The inspected paths now fail closed before provider payment side effects when active SQL reservations no longer cover the order. Storefront payment creation, Stripe, Paymob, checkout idempotency replay, and checkout payment-intent recovery call a SQL guard that locks the order/reservation/stock authority rows, verifies tenant/product/variant ownership, requires active unexpired reservations for stock-tracked non-backorder lines, and rejects split reservation/stock truth. CommerceHub payment initiation now rechecks marketplace order reservations again after acquiring the payment lock and before selecting/reusing payment session rows.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-20 for storefront payment-intent creation/replay/recovery and CommerceHub payment initiation. Focused source regression coverage passed for the new boundary.
- residual operational risk: Existing provider sessions created before reservation loss still require normal payment/webhook reconciliation, refund, cancellation, or manual review if external money later arrives. The guard is scoped to checkout/cart orders and CommerceHub payment initiation; lower-risk read visibility, analytics, and historical reporting remain derivative.
- prevention rule: Payment intent generation and recovery are not inventory authority. Before creating, resuming, or replaying provider payment authority for checkout orders, the service must reload SQL order item, product/variant ownership, reservation, and stock truth and fail closed if active reservation authority no longer covers the order.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/CheckoutInventoryAuthorityGuard.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/PaymentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/Stripe/StripePaymentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/Paymob/PaymobPaymentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Checkout/CheckoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Checkout/CheckoutPaymentIntentRecoveryService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CheckoutConcurrencyHardeningSourceTests.cs`

### CommerceHub checkout final authority now locks SQL composition rows
- title: Stale CommerceHub checkout replay cannot finalize order authority from marketplace/cart visibility while catalog, store, payment, promo, shipping, or stock authority is mutating
- affected systems: `CommerceHubCheckoutService`, CommerceHub cart checkout, marketplace order creation, marketplace promo usage, shipping selections, payment-method eligibility, variant/product/listing snapshots, inventory reservations, CommerceHub order events
- root cause: CommerceHub checkout already recomputed pricing, promo, shipping, payment, stock, and reservations from SQL/services inside a serializable transaction, but the final composition reads for listings, variants, products, tenants, and payment methods were no-tracking reads without explicit row-level authority locks. That left less precise protection than storefront checkout for concurrent catalog/eligibility/payment-method mutation during final order snapshot composition.
- impact: The active checked path is now runtime-hardened. CommerceHub checkout reloads and locks CommerceHub listings, product variants, variant attributes, products, tenants, and tenant payment methods inside the final serializable checkout transaction before promo validation, cache-bypassed shipping recalculation, marketplace order snapshot creation, stock reservation, outbox staging, and idempotency response persistence. Expired promo replay, stale unit price, failed stock reservation, and duplicate idempotency replay are covered by focused tests; PRICE/STOCK/VAR smoke slices passed after the change.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-20 for the current CommerceHub checkout order-creation path. Storefront checkout already had serializable SQL recomposition and idempotency binding before this sprint.
- residual operational risk: CommerceHub payment initiation, provider callbacks, refunds, payouts, and financial recovery remain on their existing specialized SQL/evidence/readiness protections rather than generic checkout row locks. CommerceHub read models, cart projections, affiliate/marketplace visibility, and customer-facing snapshots remain derivative and may lag. Future checkout rebuild or marketplace import paths must keep using SQL current-state recomposition and must not rebuild financial authority from Mongo, Redis, browser state, or marketplace payloads.
- prevention rule: Checkout sessions, carts, marketplace payloads, affiliate state, and cached totals are visibility or intent only. Final checkout authority must be composed from PostgreSQL inside the order/payment/reservation transaction, with duplicate submit/idempotency collapse before side effects and SQL-truth rebuilds for recovery.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubCheckoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/CommerceHubCheckoutFinancialControlTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CheckoutConcurrencyHardeningSourceTests.cs`

### Checkout promotion usage mutations now reload SQL authority before consumption
- title: Expired, deactivated, or exhausted promotions can no longer be consumed from stale checkout, CommerceHub, or in-memory promo payload authority on the hardened paths
- affected systems: `PromoService`, `PaymentLifecycleService`, CommerceHub promo consumption, storefront offer promotion usage confirmation, marketplace orders, promotion usage counters, payment lifecycle promotion confirmation
- root cause: Checkout finalization already recomposed promotion eligibility from SQL, but two mutation edges still needed tighter authority checks: CommerceHub promo consumption revalidated the caller-supplied promo instance before writing usage, and storefront offer promotion confirmation locked the promotion row but did not explicitly recompute current active/date/usage/discount authority before `PromotionUsage` insertion.
- impact: The active mutation paths are now runtime-hardened. CommerceHub promo consumption detaches stale tracked promo instances, reloads the current PostgreSQL `PromoCodes` row under `FOR UPDATE` for relational providers, validates current active/expiry/usage/customer authority, and only then writes usage. Storefront offer usage confirmation revalidates current SQL promotion activity, date window, minimum order, max usage, and recalculated discount before adding `PromotionUsage`.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-20 for CommerceHub promo code usage and storefront offer promotion usage confirmation. This complements, but does not replace, the existing checkout final SQL recomposition and promotion visibility projection fences.
- residual operational risk: The payment-lifecycle check intentionally fails closed if a promotion expires or no longer matches the order discount before usage confirmation. Provider-side payment reconciliation and operator handling must treat that as an authority conflict, not as permission to resurrect the expired discount. Lower-risk analytics and manual reporting remain derivative.
- prevention rule: Promotion usage writes are authority mutations. They must reload PostgreSQL promotion truth, revalidate active/date/usage/customer/order discount eligibility, and collapse duplicate usage before committing any usage, payment, reservation, inventory, cache, affiliate, or CommerceHub side effect.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/PromoService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Payments/PaymentLifecycleService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/PromoServiceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CheckoutPromotionVisibilityTests.cs`

### Reliability primitives are now partially extracted into shared infrastructure
- title: Projection event fencing, SQL authority overlays, version/timestamp validation, tombstone checks, and SQL-truth rebuild guardrails now have shared platform primitives for future consumers
- affected systems: `ProjectionEventFence`, `ProjectionConsumerEventFence`, `ProjectionAuthorityValidator`, `ProjectionTombstoneGuard`, `SqlAuthorityOverlayResult`, `SqlTruthRebuildGuard`, product search overlay, variant/media/stock/search/CommerceHub projection consumers
- root cause: VAR-CON, PRICE-CON, and STOCK-CON hardening repeatedly implemented the same reliability rules directly inside consumers, making future projection paths easy to harden inconsistently.
- impact: The current worktree now has additive shared infrastructure while preserving existing consumer behavior. `ProjectionConsumerEventFence` remains a compatibility facade, selected high-risk media/stock/search consumers use the extracted fence directly, product search SQL overlay exposes reusable overlay result metadata, variant/media/CommerceHub consumers call shared version/timestamp/tombstone validation helpers, and the adoption strategy now classifies major consumers into Tier 1/Tier 2/Tier 3 migration paths.
- mitigation status: Partial/incremental runtime hardening added on 2026-05-20. The extraction is future-migration-ready rather than a full-system migration; many existing consumers still keep local freshness filters and SQL reload logic.
- residual operational risk: Promotion, affiliate, CommerceHub, cache, rebuild, repair, and reconciliation paths are not universally migrated. Future consumers must use the shared primitives or explicitly document equivalent idempotency, SQL authority, rebuild-from-SQL, freshness, and tombstone behavior. Financial lifecycle, payment/refund/payout recovery, accounting outbox, stock mutation, and fulfillment/shipment recovery intentionally remain on specialized manual protections rather than projection primitives.
- prevention rule: New projection, search, cache, affiliate, CommerceHub, rebuild, or repair paths must treat events/read models as triggers only and should start from `PointOfSale.Infrastructure.Reliability` primitives before adding consumer-local reliability code.
- references: `docs/architecture/reliability-adoption-strategy.md`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/ProjectionEventFence.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/ProjectionAuthorityValidator.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/ProjectionTombstoneGuard.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/SqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/SqlTruthRebuildGuard.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/ReliabilityAdoptionStrategyArchitectureTests.cs`

### Variant projection side effects now collapse duplicate delivery and rebuild search from SQL authority
- title: Variant stock/delete/search/affiliate/CommerceHub projection delivery can no longer amplify duplicate side effects or treat payload/Mongo/search state as authority on the hardened paths
- affected systems: `ProductVariantProjectionConsumer`, `ProductMediaChangedEventConsumer`, stock/price search indexing, product search indexing, affiliate product intelligence/policy projections, CommerceHub variant listing synchronization, PostgreSQL `ProcessedEvents`, Elasticsearch product documents, Mongo product documents, Redis/cache invalidation, replay/rebuild/reconciliation paths
- root cause: Earlier VAR-CON coverage proved deterministic convergence for many variant races, but several runtime consumers still depended on local idempotency slices or projection freshness and could execute duplicate search/cache/affiliate/CommerceHub side effects before a durable projection fence. Several search/index refresh paths also mapped from Mongo product documents without first overlaying PostgreSQL variant/media truth.
- impact: The reviewed gaps are now runtime-mitigated on the current variant paths. Variant stock and delete consumers acquire `ProjectionConsumerEventFence` before side effects; duplicate/active EventId delivery collapses before reindex, cache invalidation, affiliate projection, or CommerceHub synchronization; SQL is re-read before applying variant stock/delete authority; stock side-effect propagation requires the incoming product/variant version and stock timestamp to match current SQL authority; and search hydration overlays product media, variants, stock, SKU/barcode, visibility, and default-variant fields from PostgreSQL before Elasticsearch indexing.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-19 by the VAR-CON hardening review. A new SQL-authority search overlay is shared by product, media, stock/price, promotion, and variant-triggered indexing paths. CommerceHub variant stock sync now uses SQL stock/version/timestamp authority instead of payload totals. Default variant and live variant-attribute-name invariants are promoted to database-enforced migration checks/indexes.
- residual operational risk: The hardening covers the current named variant consumers and rebuild/index paths. Future projection consumers, direct Mongo/search/cache repair tools, CDN-specific workers, bulk importers, or detached synchronization jobs must explicitly reuse the SQL overlay and projection fence before rollout. The normalized live attribute-name uniqueness index is enforced by migration SQL; EF model metadata represents the closest standard unique index shape and must not be treated as a substitute for applying the migration.
- prevention rule: Variant projection events are triggers only. Before side effects, consumers must fence by EventId/consumer and re-read PostgreSQL when the payload could carry variant identity, stock, media, SKU/barcode, attribute, tombstone, or visibility authority. Search, cache, Mongo, affiliate, storefront, CommerceHub, CDN, and replay timing remain derivative visibility only.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProjectionConsumerEventFence.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductVariantProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductMediaChangedEventConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductStockPriceSearchIndexConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductStockChangedConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/Affiliate/AffiliateProductIntelligenceProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/Affiliate/AffiliateProductPolicyProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/CommerceHubProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/ProductSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Migrations/20260519183000_VariantDbInvariantAuthority.cs`

### Pricing visibility consumers now re-read SQL before derivative propagation
- title: Payload, Mongo, search, affiliate, and CommerceHub timing can no longer become pricing authority on the hardened propagation paths
- affected systems: `ProductSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay`, `ProductStockPriceSearchIndexConsumer`, `ProductPromotionSearchIndexConsumer`, `PromotionProjectionConsumer`, `DiscountReadModelRepairService`, `AffiliateProductPolicyProjectionConsumer`, `CommerceHubProjectionConsumer`, Redis product cache invalidation, Elasticsearch product documents, Mongo product documents, public affiliate product documents, CommerceHub product listings
- root cause: The PRICE-CON proof suite already covered deterministic convergence for the current pricing scenarios, but several production derivative consumers still used event payloads or Mongo/search projections as the immediate source for price-visible side effects. Duplicate `ProductPriceChangedEvent` or `VariantPriceUpdatedEvent` deliveries could also re-run cache/search/affiliate/CommerceHub side effects before any active overlap was collapsed.
- impact: The reviewed gaps are now runtime-mitigated on the current propagation paths. Product search indexing and promotion repair apply SQL product/variant price plus current SQL promotion decoration before indexing; affiliate public product publication overlays SQL numeric pricing while preserving projected stock freshness; CommerceHub fallback price updates re-read SQL variant effective price and reject version mismatches instead of trusting `VariantPriceUpdatedEvent.NewPrice`; and price-triggered search, affiliate, cache, and CommerceHub side effects acquire active event fences before executing duplicate-overlap side effects.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened in code on 2026-05-20. Focused validation passed locally against real integration slices: full `ProductVariantPriceDistributedReliabilityIntegrationTests` PRICE-CON 01-10, `SalesInvoiceConcurrencyIntegrationTests.PRICE_CON` 11-13, full `ProductVariantDistributedReliabilityIntegrationTests` VAR-CON slice, and `STOCK_CON` replay/duplicate slice.
- residual operational risk: The derivative active fences intentionally do not all write durable `ProcessedEvents` completions because existing projection freshness contracts already own durable replay completion for those paths. Future pricing consumers, bulk reindexers, affiliate/CommerceHub feed writers, direct Mongo/search/cache repair tools, and marketplace price importers must either reuse SQL overlays plus an explicit idempotency/freshness contract or add their own durable processed-event ownership with regression coverage.
- prevention rule: Pricing events and projection documents are triggers only. Canonical price, variant price, cost basis, current promotion overlay state, search pricing, affiliate numeric pricing, and CommerceHub fallback pricing must be recomputed from PostgreSQL authority before propagation. Duplicate delivery may refresh derivative visibility, but it must not amplify cache/index/feed/sync side effects or make replay timing authoritative.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/ProductSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductStockPriceSearchIndexConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductPromotionSearchIndexConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/PromotionProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/ReadModels/DiscountReadModelRepairService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/Affiliate/AffiliateProductPolicyProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/CommerceHubProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductCacheInvalidationConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Integration/ProductVariantPriceDistributedReliabilityIntegrationTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Integration/SalesInvoiceConcurrencyIntegrationTests.cs`

### Promotion visibility side effects now use SQL authority and durable fences
- title: Promotion replay timing can no longer amplify the hardened storefront, search, affiliate, CommerceHub, cache, or repair side effects
- affected systems: `PromotionProjectionConsumer`, `ProductPromotionSearchIndexConsumer`, `AffiliateProductPolicyProjectionConsumer`, `CommerceHubProjectionConsumer`, `DiscountReadModelRepairService`, Mongo promotion/product/category documents, Redis promotion/product caches, Elasticsearch product documents, public affiliate products, CommerceHub listings
- root cause: Promotion activation, update, expiration, and delete events affected multiple derivative surfaces, but parts of the propagation path still relied on event payload scope/values plus local freshness checks. Duplicate delivery could repeat expensive cache/search/affiliate/CommerceHub side effects before a shared runtime fence, and corrupted same-version payloads could influence promotion read-model fields if they arrived before the correct projection.
- impact: The current high-risk promotion visibility paths now acquire `ProjectionConsumerEventFence` before derived side effects, reload current PostgreSQL promotion rows before promotion-document and overlay writes, reject stale/future promotion versions through `ProjectionAuthorityValidator`, recompute product/category overlays from SQL promotion truth, reapply product search SQL authority overlay before indexing, and mark promotion repair as a `SqlTruthRebuildGuard` SQL-authority rebuild. Affiliate and CommerceHub promotion-triggered refreshes also fence active duplicate deliveries before public product/listing/search side effects.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened in code on 2026-05-20. This is a targeted promotion reliability migration, not a rewrite of the promotion engine.
- residual operational risk: Same-event promotion replay is still allowed to rerun deterministic SQL-backed overlay repair after partial projection loss, so this sprint collapses active duplicate overlap rather than banning every later duplicate recomposition. Deleted promotions with no remaining SQL row still use the tombstone event plus existing projection state only to identify derivative surfaces that need SQL recomputation; the visibility values written remain SQL-derived. Cart-level promotion checkout authority and lower-risk analytics/telemetry remain on existing specialized/manual protections. Future promotion feed writers, bulk repair tools, banner/search rebuilders, or cache warmers must reuse the fence plus SQL-reload pattern.
- prevention rule: Promotion events are triggers only. PostgreSQL `Promotions` rows own promotion truth; Mongo/search/cache/storefront banners, affiliate products, and CommerceHub listings are derivative visibility and must be rebuilt from SQL authority or explicitly fenced tombstone cleanup.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/PromotionProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductPromotionSearchIndexConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/Affiliate/AffiliateProductPolicyProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/CommerceHubProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/ReadModels/DiscountReadModelRepairService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests.cs`

### Affiliate and CommerceHub inventory visibility now reload SQL stock authority
- title: CommerceHub listings and affiliate product intelligence can no longer derive inventory availability from Mongo projection freshness alone
- affected systems: `AffiliateProductIntelligenceProjectionConsumer`, `CommerceHubProjectionConsumer`, Mongo product documents, affiliate product intelligence documents, CommerceHub product listings, stock projection replay, storefront/affiliate/CommerceHub inventory visibility
- root cause: The hardened stock-event paths already used projection fences and SQL stock overlays, but some product/listing refresh paths still copied `AvailableStock`, `InStock`, or variant stock from Mongo product documents after checking projection freshness. That made a visibility read model a practical stock authority during product refresh, listing upsert, or stale replay overlap.
- impact: The active authority leak is now closed on the reviewed paths. Affiliate product intelligence loads SQL variant stock snapshots before computing public variant stock and rejects stock events whose SQL version/timestamp does not match the incoming stock event. CommerceHub product/listing refresh reloads SQL variant stock authority before writing `AvailableStock`, `InStock`, and stock timestamp fields. Duplicate `ProductVariantStockChangedEvent` delivery is collapsed through `ProjectionConsumerEventFence` before affiliate side effects.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened in code on 2026-05-20 during the STOCK-CON hardening review. Focused architecture validation passed for affiliate SQL stock authority, CommerceHub listing SQL stock authority, and variant stock event projection routing. Broad STOCK-CON and VAR/PRICE distributed regression commands were started but timed out, so their prior deterministic coverage remains the regression baseline for those large overlap suites in this pass.
- residual operational risk: Future feed writers, bulk listing rebuilds, direct Mongo repair scripts, search/cache rebuild tools, or optional consumer construction paths must keep the same SQL reload and event-fence rule. Reservation natural uniqueness and some historical movement lineage boundaries still rely on application idempotency plus SQL idempotency keys/check constraints rather than a single universal database uniqueness constraint for every business-natural duplicate shape.
- prevention rule: Mongo product documents, search documents, cache rows, affiliate documents, storefront projections, and CommerceHub listings are visibility only. Inventory availability propagation must fence duplicate stock events before side effects and reload PostgreSQL stock/reservation truth before writing external availability.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/Affiliate/AffiliateProductIntelligenceProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/CommerceHubProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProjectionConsumerEventFence.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Utilities/StockProjectionTimestamp.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### POS sale historical COGS no longer depends on mutable product cost during concurrent cost-price mutation
- title: Concurrent variant cost mutation during active POS sale can no longer retroactively change committed sale COGS
- affected systems: POS sales, product variant cost mutation, accounting outbox, journal posting, stock movements, sales returns, margin/profitability analytics, replay/rebuild paths
- root cause: Historical sale COGS could be derived from mutable `ProductVariant.CostPrice` or `Product.PurchasePrice` if a later cost mutation overlapped sale commit or replay. Stock projection timestamping could also reuse a stale tracked `ProductVariant` row version after a concurrent committed cost mutation.
- impact: The validated corruption path is closed for new committed sales. Sale lines and return lines now persist immutable cost price/amount snapshots, COGS posting prefers those snapshots, stock movements link to the sale-line cost basis, return valuation reuses the original sale cost snapshot, and stock projection reloads the current locked variant row instead of aborting sale commit through a stale tracked row version.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-17. PRICE-CON-11 deterministically pauses an active sale after cost-basis capture, commits a concurrent cost mutation from 50 to 80, proves fresh-reader visibility rules, releases the sale, and verifies historical COGS, journals, stock movement cost, replay/duplicate delivery, and derived analytics convergence stay on the single sale cost basis.
- residual operational risk: Rows created before the snapshot migration can have null snapshots and still rely on fallback cost reads until a controlled backfill/non-null migration is planned. Runtime rollout must apply the snapshot-column migration before deploying code that expects those columns.
- prevention rule: Never recompute committed sale COGS, historical margin, journal valuation, or return cost basis from latest product/variant cost. Cost mutation is future valuation input only; historical sale valuation lives on immutable SQL sale/return cost snapshots plus accounting journals.
- references: `PointOfSale.Core/Entities/SalesInvoiceItem.cs`, `PointOfSale.Core/Entities/ReturnItem.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/SalesController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/SalesReturnsController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Accounting/CommerceAccountingService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Inventory/ProductVariantStockMutationSupport.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Integration/SalesInvoiceConcurrencyIntegrationTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Migrations/20260517120000_PosSaleImmutableCostBasisSnapshots.cs`

### Cost-basis recalculation replay can no longer become historical financial authority
- title: Duplicate or stale cost-basis recalculation replay cannot mutate committed sale/return COGS or accounting journals
- affected systems: inventory valuation recalculation, processed-event replay/retry/reclaim, POS sales, sales returns, accounting outbox, journal posting, stock movements, weighted valuation, margin/profit analytics, rebuild/reconciliation paths
- root cause: Recalculation and replay workers are allowed to rebuild derived valuation projections, but without explicit immutability and idempotency coverage a duplicate or stale worker could be mistaken for financial authority and overwrite historical COGS, weighted valuation state, accounting replay state, or derived analytics during active recalculation overlap.
- impact: The validated corruption path is closed for the covered POS sale/return valuation model. Recalculation overlap is fenced by SQL `ProcessedEvents`, fresh readers see no uncommitted partial valuation or mixed weighted-average truth, duplicate/retry/reclaim delivery converges to one recalculation application, and accounting replay completes from existing SQL journal truth instead of recomputing immutable historical snapshots.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-17. PRICE-CON-12 deterministically seeds sale, return, stock movement, weighted valuation, current-cost future valuation, and accounting state; pauses Worker A inside the recalculation transaction; forces Worker B to overlap the same idempotency claim; verifies fresh-reader visibility during overlap; releases Worker A; then proves historical sale/return snapshots, COGS journals, stock valuation, processed-event count, accounting replay, and derived analytics fingerprints converge unchanged.
- residual operational risk: PRICE-CON-12 is focused on the POS sale/return valuation path and the current recalculation projection model. Any future generalized cost-layer rebuild engine, migration backfill, or bulk valuation repair job must carry the same immutable snapshot guard, SQL idempotency fence, and fresh-reader visibility tests before rollout.
- prevention rule: Recalculation workers are projection rebuilders only. They may rebuild derived valuation/analytics state, but they must never rewrite committed sale/return snapshots, historical COGS, immutable accounting journals, or stock movement financial linkage from mutable current product cost or replay timing.
- references: `PointOfSale.Tests/Integration/SalesInvoiceConcurrencyIntegrationTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/ApplicationDbContext.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Accounting/AccountingOutboxProcessor.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/TestAuthController.cs`, `.ai-testing/TESTSPRITE_MCP_RUNBOOK.md`

### Duplicate financial projection delivery can no longer become accounting or profitability authority
- title: Duplicate, replayed, or stale financial projection delivery cannot double-count profitability, margin, BI/reporting snapshots, or dashboards
- affected systems: financial projection workers, SQL `ProcessedEvents`, profitability analytics, margin analytics, BI/reporting aggregates, valuation dashboards, POS sale/return snapshots, accounting journals, rebuild/reconciliation paths
- root cause: Derived financial visibility can be delivered at least once and rebuilt asynchronously. Without deterministic overlap coverage, duplicate projection workers, duplicate outbox delivery, stale replay, or out-of-order propagation could be mistaken for authority and double-apply profit/margin values or overwrite a newer financial dashboard snapshot.
- impact: The validated corruption path is closed for the covered POS sale/return projection visibility model. PRICE-CON-13 proves Worker A can pause after the SQL projection claim and before derived visibility commit while Worker B overlaps the same projection delivery; fresh SQL readers see no partial projection, duplicate delivery is suppressed by the processed-event fence, stale replay is rejected by projection freshness, rebuild recomputes from immutable SQL authority, and sale/return snapshots plus accounting journals remain unchanged.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-17. The focused test uses real PostgreSQL transactions, fresh DbContexts, immutable POS financial snapshots, accounting journal fingerprints, `CommerceHubDailyFinancialAggregates` as derived BI/reporting visibility, and dedicated TestSprite runner endpoints for TC037.
- residual operational risk: PRICE-CON-13 covers the current SQL aggregate/read-model projection shape used in the test. Future financial projection engines, dashboards, cache/search financial visibility, or bulk analytics rebuild jobs must preserve equivalent processed-event/freshness fencing and must add scenario-specific duplicate-overlap coverage before rollout.
- prevention rule: Financial projections are derived state only. Duplicate or stale projection delivery may refresh visibility from SQL snapshots and accounting journals, but must never create new financial authority, double-count profit/margin, resurrect stale dashboard state, mutate immutable historical valuation, or alter journals.
- references: `PointOfSale.Tests/Integration/SalesInvoiceConcurrencyIntegrationTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Core/Entities/CommerceHubFinance/CommerceHubDailyFinancialAggregate.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/Configurations/ProcessedEventConfiguration.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/TestAuthController.cs`, `testsprite_tests/testsprite_backend_test_plan.json`, `.ai-testing/TESTSPRITE_MCP_RUNBOOK.md`

### POS auth bootstrap no longer lets a stale anonymous session check blank the protected shell or clear a newer login
- title: Explicit POS login and protected-route rendering no longer lose to an older anonymous `/api/auth/session` bootstrap result
- affected systems: `PointOfSale.Web` POS auth bootstrap, `ProtectedRoute`, persisted auth store hydration, login/session continuity
- root cause: The POS web booted an anonymous `/api/auth/session` request on mount and treated its completion as authoritative even if a newer explicit login had already succeeded. During that same window, `ProtectedRoute` returned `null` while `hasHydrated` was still false, so protected navigation after sign-in could show a blank page until the stale bootstrap settled.
- impact: A real runtime desynchronization path existed in the POS SPA. On cold boot, a successful sign-in could still be followed by a blank protected shell or a stale bootstrap `401` clearing the newly authenticated client session.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-12. `authStore` now marks explicit login/logout/session hydration as hydrated immediately, `AuthBootstrap` ignores stale bootstrap completions once session state changed after request start, and `ProtectedRoute` renders authenticated content immediately when a valid client session already exists while showing a visible restore state instead of a blank route for unresolved unauthenticated bootstrap. Focused regression coverage was added in `PointOfSale.Web/src/components/AuthBootstrap.test.tsx`.
- prevention rule: Browser auth bootstrap is continuity-only. A later completion from an older anonymous session probe must not override a newer explicit login/logout transition, and protected-route guards must never render a blank screen simply because bootstrap state is still settling.
- references: `PointOfSale.Web/src/components/AuthBootstrap.jsx`, `PointOfSale.Web/src/components/ProtectedRoute.jsx`, `PointOfSale.Web/src/store/authStore.js`, `PointOfSale.Web/src/components/AuthBootstrap.test.tsx`

### Critical storefront and CommerceHub order reads now prefer the freshest Mongo projection when duplicate documents exist
- title: SQL-backed repair and critical customer/order reads no longer trust an arbitrary duplicate Mongo order document
