- affected systems: `OrderReadRepository`, `CommerceHubOrderReadRepository`, `StorefrontOrderProjectionRepairService`, `StorefrontOrderProjectionReconciliationService`, `CommerceHubOrderProjectionRepairService`, Mongo `orders` / `commercehub_orders`, `OrderProjectionConsumer`, `CommerceHubOrderProjectionConsumer`, `MongoDbIndexInitializer`
- root cause: Order-projection uniqueness in Mongo depended on startup/consumer index creation that intentionally failed open with warning-only logging. If duplicate documents survived a historical race or index-creation failure, critical read and repair paths used `FirstOrDefaultAsync(...)` and could therefore repair one document while serving a different stale duplicate.
- impact: The active stale-authority gap is closed. Critical storefront and CommerceHub order reads now choose the freshest matching projection by authoritative mutation/version markers instead of trusting arbitrary Mongo insertion order, so stale duplicates no longer win customer-visible or operator-visible reads while SQL-backed repair/reconciliation remains the authority anchor.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-12. `OrderReadRepository.GetByIdAsync(...)`, `GetByOrderNumberAsync(...)`, and `GetByIdsAsync(...)` now select the freshest document by `LastEventVersion`, `LastEventOccurredAtUtc`, `UpdatedAt`, and `CreatedAt`; `CommerceHubOrderReadRepository.GetByMarketplaceOrderIdAsync(...)` now prefers `LastSourceMutationTicks` plus the same freshness markers; storefront repair/reconciliation and CommerceHub repair now sort existing projections by freshness before deciding reuse/repair; and architecture guard coverage asserts these protections remain in place while Mongo index creation still fail-opens.
- recovery/reconciliation: Recovery remains possible through the existing SQL-backed projection repair/reconciliation paths. Duplicate stale rows no longer permanently override repaired truth before cleanup, although background cleanup or operator investigation is still appropriate if duplicate rows are discovered.
- prevention rule: Critical read-model paths must not trust `FirstOrDefault` when runtime uniqueness is best-effort or historically violable. If more than one Mongo projection can match the same authoritative order identity, choose the freshest document by authoritative version/freshness markers and repair from SQL rather than serving arbitrary read-model state.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/MongoDB/Services/OrderReadRepository.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/MongoDB/Services/CommerceHubOrderReadRepository.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Storefront/StorefrontOrderProjectionRepairService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Storefront/StorefrontOrderProjectionReconciliationService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubOrderProjectionRepairService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/MongoDB/MongoDbIndexInitializer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/OrderProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/CommerceHubOrderProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### CommerceHub unmatched provider webhooks now persist durable unresolved evidence even when payment-initiation lock contention blocks the serialized authority path
- title: CommerceHub no longer drops the first unmatched captured-money or provider-refund callback when webhook persistence races the initiation lock
- affected systems: `CommerceHubPaymentService`, `CommerceHubPaymentsController`, `CommerceHubUnresolvedPaymentWebhooks`, suspense accounting, reconciliation workers, customer payment-status visibility
- root cause: `InitiateAsync(...)` legitimately holds the payment-initiation lock across provider handoff and provider-session persistence, but unmatched provider callbacks must not treat that lock as permission to discard the only externally confirmed money/refund evidence.
- impact: The active durability gap is closed. When the unresolved-webhook path cannot acquire the payment lock, it now still commits a durable `CommerceHubUnresolvedPaymentWebhook` row with frozen order/scope authority when available and an explicit deferred-reconciliation error, so controller retry semantics no longer depend on provider redelivery to preserve the first callback.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-11. `PersistUnresolvedPaymentWebhookAsync(...)` now degrades from serialized suspense/hold execution to durable unresolved-evidence persistence when the lock is busy or lock infrastructure is unavailable, preserves prior routed-suspense state instead of clearing it on replay, and returns success once local evidence is durably stored. Focused regression coverage now asserts the lock-contention branch persists unresolved evidence and the architecture guard asserts the method no longer returns `false` before durable persistence.
- recovery/reconciliation: Recovery remains possible through the existing unresolved-payment reconciliation path even if the provider never retries, because local SQL now keeps the first externally confirmed callback as the reconciliation anchor.
- prevention rule: Distributed payment locks are serialization aids, not permission to drop externally confirmed money evidence. If exact order/payment linkage is still racing, persist a tenant-scoped or scope-less unresolved webhook row first and defer suspense routing, marketplace hold, or payment linkage to reconciliation.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentServiceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`, `.ai-context/ARCHITECTURE_TRUTH.md`

### Storefront serializable checkout retry now preserves the durable idempotency bind before commit
- title: Serializable checkout retries no longer reuse a detached claim row when binding a committed order to its durable replay record
- affected systems: `CheckoutService`, `CheckoutRequestIdempotencyCoordinator`, `OrderRequestIdempotency`, storefront checkout replay/recovery, guest/customer checkout continuity, inventory reservation and payment-intent authority
- root cause: The original serializable retry hardening added `_dbContext.ChangeTracker.Clear()` after serialization/deadlock failures but still wrote `OrderId` / `CompletedAtUtc` directly to the previously claimed `OrderRequestIdempotency` object. After tracking reset that object was detached, so the order could commit without the durable replay bind.
- impact: The active corruption window is closed. The checkout transaction now routes the in-transaction order bind through `BindOrderCreatedForCommitAsync(...)`, which reloads or reuses the owned claim row under claim-token validation before `SaveChangesAsync()` and `CommitAsync()` run. A successful retry therefore preserves the same durable exactly-once boundary as the first attempt.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-11. `CheckoutService.CreateOrderAsync(...)` now binds the claim through the coordinator inside the final transaction, `CheckoutRequestIdempotencyCoordinator` rehydrates the owned record for mutation when EF tracking was cleared, and focused regression coverage now asserts the tracker-reset path persists the durable bind before commit.
- residual operational risk: `CompleteAsync(...)` still enriches the stored replay payload only after the order transaction commits, so a post-commit crash can still delay payload persistence. That is an accepted continuity degradation, not a duplicate-order or stale-authority corruption path, because the durable `OrderId` bind is already committed and replay can recover through existing-order authority.
- prevention rule: Any checkout retry path that calls `ChangeTracker.Clear()` must route order-binding mutations through coordinator-owned claim rehydration or an equivalent claim-token-validated reattach step inside the same SQL transaction that commits order authority.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/Checkout/CheckoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Checkout/CheckoutRequestIdempotencyCoordinator.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CheckoutRequestIdempotencyCoordinatorTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CheckoutConcurrencyHardeningSourceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/VerifiedSafeFlowsArchitectureTests.cs`

### Storefront paginated order history now uses SQL authority for count, page shape, and exact-page projection repair
- title: Storefront account order history no longer lets Mongo projection gaps silently shrink or reorder customer-visible history pages
- affected systems: `StorefrontAccountController` authenticated order history, `StorefrontOrderProjectionRepairService`, Mongo `orders` read model, `OrderReadRepository`, storefront customer self-service visibility
- root cause: The prior history endpoint always derived `TotalCount` and paginated rows from Mongo, while its repair path only rebuilt a bounded recent window. Older missing projections could therefore hide valid SQL orders from page 2+ and underreport total history.
- impact: The active storefront history authority gap is closed. Customer-visible page count and requested order ids now come from SQL authority, the repair service rebuilds the exact requested history page before Mongo is served, and the endpoint fails closed with `503` instead of returning a partial/incomplete page when Mongo still cannot prove the SQL page after repair.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-11. `StorefrontAccountController.GetOrders(...)` now counts and selects the requested ecommerce order ids from SQL, `EnsureCustomerOrdersProjectedPageAsync(...)` repairs that exact page range, `OrderReadRepository.GetByIdsAsync(...)` returns the Mongo read model in authoritative SQL order, and cross-system architecture coverage was extended to assert the new sequence. Focused validation passed on 2026-05-11 with `dotnet test PointOfSale.Tests\PointOfSale.Tests.csproj --filter FullyQualifiedName~CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests -nologo -v minimal` and `dotnet build PointOfSale.API\PointOfSale.API.csproj -nologo -v minimal`.
- residual operational risk: Mongo remains the served storefront read model, so a requested history page can still fail closed as temporary unavailability if projection repair cannot rebuild every authoritative SQL row in the requested page. That is an accepted integrity tradeoff and no longer silently downgrades into a false partial history response.
- prevention rule: Paginated storefront history must derive page shape and total count from SQL authority, repair the exact requested page before trusting Mongo, and fail closed rather than returning incomplete history when the projection page cannot be proven.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/StorefrontAccountController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Storefront/StorefrontOrderProjectionRepairService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/MongoDB/Services/OrderReadRepository.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Storefront/StorefrontOrderProjectionReconciliationService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### Storefront promotion calculation now fails closed for checkout pricing authority
- title: Storefront checkout no longer creates payment/order/accounting authority after silently dropping promotions on calculation failure
- affected systems: Storefront pricing preview, storefront checkout, POS/Admin promotions, payment capture amount, order totals, accounting, affiliate attribution
- root cause: Checkout promotion calculation had a fail-open branch that could catch promotion-engine exceptions, clear promotion fields, and continue with a no-discount total even though customer-visible pricing authority depended on promotion resolution.
- impact: The active divergence path is closed. Promotion calculation failure now returns `PROMOTION_CALCULATION_FAILED` for checkout/preview instead of creating an order whose charged total differs from customer-visible promotion authority.
- mitigation status: Revalidated in code on 2026-05-11 after a same-day regression was corrected. `ReconcileCheckoutDiscountAuthorityAsync(...)` now routes the real exception path back through fail-closed promotion handling, and architecture guard coverage was strengthened to assert the live catch branch no longer degrades into a warning-only checkout.
- residual operational risk: Checkout availability can drop while promotion calculation is unhealthy. This is intentional integrity preservation; operators must repair promotion calculation rather than bypass pricing authority.
- prevention rule: Customer-visible pricing authority must not fail open by removing discounts after a promotion calculation exception. Retry or repair the promotion calculation before creating order, payment, inventory, affiliate, or accounting authority.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/Checkout/CheckoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Checkout/ICheckoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Storefront/src/store/checkoutStore.ts`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CheckoutPromotionVisibilityTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CheckoutPromotionCorrectnessControllerTests.cs`, `.ai-context/ARCHITECTURE_TRUTH.md`

### Storefront final checkout now commits inside a serializable authority boundary
- title: The checkout commit-window race for newly introduced promotion winners or affiliate policies is closed
- affected systems: `CheckoutService`, `StorefrontPricingService`, `PromotionEngine`, `CouponValidationService`, `AffiliateService`, `Order` / `OrderItem` immutable snapshots, affiliate commission ledgering
- root cause: The prior final checkout transaction refreshed authority and row-locked existing rows, but it still ran below `Serializable`, so promotion-set membership and previously absent affiliate-policy existence could change after the refresh and before commit.
- impact: The active financial snapshot drift path is closed. The final storefront checkout transaction now commits at `IsolationLevel.Serializable`, retries bounded serialization/deadlock races, and fails closed with `CHECKOUT_CONCURRENCY_CONFLICT` if it cannot safely win the final authority boundary.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-11. Checkout still treats the pre-transaction cart snapshot as advisory, re-locks storefront/shipping/payment/catalog authority, reruns coupon/promotion authority, reloads affiliate policy authority, refreshes immutable order and order-item money snapshots, and now serializes the final commit so newly authoritative promotion or affiliate-policy rows cannot slip between refresh and commit.
- residual operational risk: Under heavy concurrent admin/catalog mutation, some checkout attempts may lose the serializable race and return a retryable conflict after bounded retries. That is an accepted availability tradeoff in favor of financial correctness.
- prevention rule: The final storefront order-creation transaction must remain serializable or use an equivalent authority-version fence that covers promotion-set membership and affiliate-policy existence, and any terminal serialization failure must fail closed rather than committing a stale immutable financial snapshot.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/Checkout/CheckoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Checkout/ICheckoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/CheckoutController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CheckoutConcurrencyHardeningSourceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### CommerceHub recovery authority now shares the same provider-session persistence-gap classifier as initiation
- title: Customer-visible CommerceHub retry guidance no longer understates backend provider-session reconciliation authority
- affected systems: `CommerceHubPaymentRecoveryAuthorityService`, `CommerceHubPaymentService`, `CommerceHubPaymentSessionRecoveryAuthority`, `CommerceHubPaymentsController`, `CommerceHubOrdersController`, `CustomerOrderDetailsPage`, `OrderPaymentRecoveryPage`
- root cause: The prior browser recovery contract blocked only unresolved captured-money states and the re-init fence sentinel, while initiation used a broader provider-session persistence-gap classifier.
- impact: The active frontend/backend drift path is closed. Authenticated order details, anonymous recovery, and browser recovery-context persistence now fail closed on the same provider-session ambiguity states that initiation already treats as recovery-only authority, including outcome-unknown and accepted-provider-identity-with-missing-handoff evidence.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-11. Recovery now evaluates provider-scoped payments with `Attempts` and `Logs` loaded and uses the shared `CommerceHubPaymentSessionRecoveryAuthority.HasProviderSessionPersistenceGap(...)` classifier that initiation uses before it blocks or recovers a pending payment.
- residual operational risk: The system still intentionally blocks customer retry while provider-session ambiguity exists. This is operational degradation, not money corruption, and reconciliation/recovery remain available through the existing provider-session recovery path.
- prevention rule: Customer-visible retry/manual-review authority must stay derived from the same provider-session persistence-gap classifier that the initiation boundary enforces. New provider-session ambiguity states must be added once in shared authority code rather than copied into separate UI-facing checks.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentRecoveryAuthorityService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentSessionRecoveryAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.CommerceHub/src/pages/CustomerOrderDetailsPage.tsx`, `PointOfSale.CommerceHub/src/pages/OrderPaymentRecoveryPage.tsx`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentRecoveryAuthorityServiceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### CommerceHub pending-payment recovery now preserves durable browser authority and backend-derived retry guidance
- title: Authenticated CommerceHub checkout can now hand a pending online order into a public self-service recovery flow without leaving the customer dependent on transient in-memory state
- affected systems: `CommerceHubCheckoutController`, `CommerceHubPaymentsController`, `CommerceHubOrdersController`, `CommerceHubPaymentRecoveryAuthorityService`, `CommerceHubOrderDetailsDto`, `CommerceHubOrderAccessCookie`, `PointOfSale.CommerceHub` checkout flow, public recovery page, active payment recovery context
- root cause: The prior gap combined three issues: the signed marketplace-order recovery token existed only in the immediate checkout response path, the anonymous order-detail contract did not expose the backend retry/manual-review authority already used by authenticated customer order detail, and the browser recovery pages could remember an active recovery path from generic `Pending` state even when backend provider-session reconciliation fences would deny another initiation attempt.
- impact: The active frontend/backend continuity gap is closed. Non-COD checkout now stores signed order-access authority in an HttpOnly `/api/commercehub` cookie and strips the raw token from the JSON response; the SPA persists only non-secret active-order context in session storage; the anonymous order-detail API now returns `CanRetryPayment` and `PaymentRecoveryMessage`; CommerceHub exposes a dedicated public payment-recovery page plus checkout empty-state recovery link that continue the same authoritative order instead of inferring from cart state; and backend recovery authority now suppresses retry guidance when unresolved provider-session reconciliation fences still block initiation.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-11. Payment re-initiation now fails closed on unresolved captured-money/manual-review states and on `PROVIDER_SESSION_REINIT_FENCE` / provider-session reconciliation-required states, and the authenticated plus anonymous recovery UI now persists recovery context only when backend-derived retry/manual-review authority exists instead of treating generic `Pending` or `OnHold` as actionable truth.
- residual operational risk: Anonymous browser recovery is intentionally bounded by the configured recovery-cookie TTL and is a continuity bridge, not the primary long-lived authority path. When that short-lived cookie expires, authenticated customer order detail remains the authoritative recovery surface.
- prevention rule: Browser recovery state may remember only non-secret order identity. Signed order-access authority must stay server-issued and HttpOnly, anonymous recovery contracts must expose backend-derived retry/manual-review guidance directly, recovery pages must not persist or re-enable continue-payment from generic `Pending` alone, and clearing local cart state is safe only after the same authoritative order remains recoverable without recreating payment authority from cart contents.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/CommerceHubCheckoutController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/CommerceHubPaymentsController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/CommerceHubOrdersController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentRecoveryAuthorityService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Security/CommerceHubOrderAccessCookie.cs`, `PointOfSale.CommerceHub/src/pages/CheckoutPage.tsx`, `PointOfSale.CommerceHub/src/pages/CustomerOrderDetailsPage.tsx`, `PointOfSale.CommerceHub/src/pages/OrderPaymentRecoveryPage.tsx`, `PointOfSale.CommerceHub/src/features/checkout/activePaymentRecoveryContext.ts`, `PointOfSale.CommerceHub/src/services/order-recovery-service.ts`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### CommerceHub auth transitions now clear anonymous recovery authority
- title: Shared-browser logout or account switch no longer leaves a previous customer's recovery cookie and visible recovery context active
- affected systems: `CustomersAuthController` login/logout/revoke-all-sessions, `CommerceHubOrderAccessCookie`, `PointOfSale.CommerceHub` auth store, header logout flow, checkout empty-state recovery CTA, public recovery page, order-detail/payment-initiation authorization
- root cause: CommerceHub anonymous order recovery intentionally persisted a signed HttpOnly recovery cookie plus non-secret browser recovery context. The hardening model already required auth-boundary scrubbing, but the live logout path still expired `commercehub_order_access` only when the customer access-token cookie was present. If the auth cookie had already expired or been removed, explicit logout returned success while leaving the server-controlled recovery cookie behind.
- impact: The active cross-customer confidentiality gap is closed. Customer login/register/logout/revoke-all-sessions now expire `commercehub_order_access`, the SPA clears visible recovery context on explicit logout and on successful new customer session establishment, and shared-browser reuse no longer carries one customer's anonymous order-recovery authority into another auth session.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-11. `CustomersAuthController` now clears the recovery cookie on login, logout, and revoke-all-sessions without depending on customer-auth cookie presence, `CommerceHubOrderAccessCookie` exposes an explicit clear helper, and the CommerceHub frontend clears `activePaymentRecoveryContext` on header logout plus successful `setSession(...)`. Focused validation passed with `dotnet test PointOfSale.Tests\\PointOfSale.Tests.csproj --filter "FullyQualifiedName~CookieAuthSecurityTests|FullyQualifiedName~CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests" -nologo -v minimal`, including coverage for logout when the access-token cookie is already absent, and `npm.cmd run build` in `PointOfSale.CommerceHub`.
- residual operational risk: Explicit auth transitions now intentionally drop anonymous recovery continuity on that browser. Customers can still recover through authenticated order detail or the original signed recovery link, but logout/login no longer preserves a convenience bridge across user boundaries.
- prevention rule: Anonymous CommerceHub recovery cookies and browser recovery context are continuity-only authority between auth boundaries. Any customer login, register, logout, or revoke-all-sessions path must clear prior browser recovery authority so shared devices cannot carry one customer's order access into another session, and the server-side cookie scrub must not depend on the access-token cookie still being present.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/CustomersAuthController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Security/CommerceHubOrderAccessCookie.cs`, `PointOfSale.CommerceHub/src/store/customer-auth-store.ts`, `PointOfSale.CommerceHub/src/components/layout/Header.tsx`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Security/CookieAuthSecurityTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### Super Admin payout queue totals now fail closed across mixed currencies
- title: Vendor payout queue global totals no longer collapse different currencies into a single fabricated operator-visible money number
- affected systems: `SuperAdminPayoutsService`, `SuperAdminPayoutsController`, Super Admin payout queue API contract, `PointOfSale.Web` payout queue page, payout planning and reconciliation visibility
- root cause: The queue service summed `TotalPayable`, `EligibleAmount`, and `HoldbackAmount` across all vendor queue snapshots without partitioning by currency, and the frontend rendered those mixed totals as `EGP`. That made operator-visible payout authority depend on cross-currency arithmetic that is not financially valid.
- impact: The active operator-authority mismatch is closed. Queue-wide payout summaries are now derived from backend currency-scoped totals, single-number aggregates are returned only when the full queue is homogeneous in one known currency, and mixed/unknown currency queues fail closed into per-currency summaries instead of a fabricated combined total.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-11. The backend now groups payout queue totals by normalized currency, exposes `CurrencySummaries`, `HasCurrencyMix`, and `AggregateCurrency`, and returns zeroed legacy aggregate totals when no safe single-currency aggregate exists. The Super Admin frontend now renders per-currency summary cards and warns operators when cross-currency aggregation is intentionally disabled.
- residual operational risk: Historic screenshots, exports, or operator notes created before this fix may still contain fabricated mixed-currency totals and require manual review. The underlying vendor, payout, and ledger rows remained currency-scoped, so the remaining issue is stale human interpretation rather than durable SQL corruption.
- prevention rule: Operator-visible payout queue totals are financial authority only when they come from backend currency-scoped aggregation. Frontend payout boards must never infer a combined queue total across mixed currencies or hardcode a display currency for backend financial aggregates. This Super Admin protection does not authorize relabeling tenant/POS/storefront money to `EGP`; tenant-facing currency remains tenant-scoped truth unless a separate FX authority model exists.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/SuperAdmin/DTOs/SuperAdminPayoutDtos.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/SuperAdmin/Controllers/SuperAdminPayoutsController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/SuperAdmin/Services/SuperAdminPayoutsService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Web/src/super-admin/payouts/payoutsService.ts`, `PointOfSale.Web/src/super-admin/payouts/PayoutsPage.tsx`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/SuperAdminPayoutsServiceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### CommerceHub customer session revocation now has a durable per-account cutoff
- title: CommerceHub customer tokens can now be invalidated per account even when exact-token blacklist persistence fails
- affected systems: `CustomerJwt` authentication, `CustomersAuthController` logout and revoke-all-sessions, CommerceHub customer order/account/payment-recovery APIs and UI, customer-session incident response
- root cause: CommerceHub customer auth previously lacked a storefront-style durable session cutoff, so targeted compromise response depended on exact-token JTI blacklist persistence or coarse whole-account governance action.
- impact: The active stale-authority gap is closed. `CustomerJwt` validation now enforces per-token JTI revocation, governance status, and a durable `CommerceHubCustomers.TokensValidAfterUtc` cutoff. Logout falls back to advancing that cutoff if exact-token blacklist persistence fails, and authenticated customers can revoke all sessions explicitly.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-11. CommerceHub tokens now carry standard `iat` plus a precise `session_iat_ticks` claim, validation rejects tokens issued at or before the durable cutoff, and focused security plus architecture regression coverage passed.
- residual operational risk: Older already-issued tokens that predate the precise claim format still fall back to standard JWT `iat` second precision during cutoff validation. That fallback now fails closed for the whole cutoff second instead of preserving stale authority, so the remaining tradeoff is coarse revocation precision rather than copied-token survival.
- prevention rule: CommerceHub customer session authority must remain backend-enforced through durable SQL cutoff plus exact-token blacklist checks. Cookie clearing alone is not sufficient compromise response, and any future auth changes must preserve fail-closed validation plus revoke-all-sessions authority.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubCustomerAuthService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CustomerTokenRevocationService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/CustomersAuthController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Program.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Security/CustomerSecurityConstants.cs`, `PointOfSale.Core/Entities/CommerceHubCustomer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Migrations/20260511143000_S79_CommerceHubCustomerSessionCutoff.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Security/CustomerTokenRevocationServiceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Security/CookieAuthSecurityTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### Storefront customer session cutoff now uses precise issued-at authority
- title: Storefront access-token cutoff now fails closed even when a copied token was minted in the same second as the cutoff boundary
- affected systems: `StorefrontJwt` authentication, `StorefrontAuthService`, `StorefrontRefreshTokenService`, `StorefrontSocialLoginService`, password reset, storefront account session revocation, blacklist-fallback incident response
- root cause: Storefront durable session cutoff previously evaluated only second-level JWT `iat` and treated `tokenIssuedAt == TokensValidAfterUtc` as valid, so a copied token minted in the same cutoff second could survive when blacklist persistence failed or when cutoff enforcement was the remaining protection.
- impact: The active stale-authority gap is closed. Storefront-issued access tokens now include precise `session_iat_ticks`, the middleware resolves that claim before falling back to `iat`, and cutoff enforcement rejects tokens issued at or before `TokensValidAfterUtc`.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-11. The storefront auth, refresh-token, and social-login issuers now stamp the precise claim, and the cutoff middleware now enforces fail-closed equality semantics.
- residual operational risk: Legacy storefront tokens minted before the precise claim rollout still fall back to second-level `iat` during cutoff evaluation. That fallback is intentionally fail-closed for the whole cutoff second, so the remaining tradeoff is coarse revocation precision rather than stale-session survival.
- prevention rule: Storefront compromise-response paths that rely on `TokensValidAfterUtc` must preserve precise issued-at claims and reject cutoff-equal tokens. Blacklist persistence remains valuable for immediate revocation, but cutoff fallback must not leave same-second copied tokens active.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/Storefront/StorefrontAuthService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Storefront/StorefrontRefreshTokenService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Storefront/StorefrontSocialLoginService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Middleware/StorefrontJwtBlacklistMiddleware.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/StorefrontAccountController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Storefront/StorefrontPasswordResetService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### Provider webhook ingress now bypasses generic tenant middleware and resolves tenant inside webhook authority
- title: Payment and shipping provider webhooks are no longer rejected before payload/stored-record tenant resolution can run
- affected systems: Stripe, Paymob, Fawry, generic payment webhooks, shipping carrier webhooks, payment lifecycle reconciliation, carrier delivery/COD synchronization, unresolved-webhook recovery
- root cause: Provider webhook controllers were `[AllowAnonymous]` but not `[AllowAnonymousTenant]`. `TenantResolutionMiddleware` strips raw `X-Tenant-Id` and rejects tenantless API-host requests before controller code runs unless the endpoint explicitly allows anonymous tenant resolution, so API-host provider callbacks could never reach the webhook pipelines that resolve tenant from signed payloads and stored payment/shipment authority.
- impact: The active ingress block is closed. Webhook endpoints can now accept tenantless provider callbacks, then derive tenant authority inside `PaymentWebhookRequestPipeline` or shipping webhook processing before lifecycle, idempotency, suspense, reconciliation, or carrier fulfillment logic runs.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-11. Payment and shipping webhook controllers now carry `AllowAnonymousTenantAttribute`, and focused regression coverage passed for the provider webhook controller guard.
- residual operational risk: Webhook processing still depends on valid provider signatures/HMACs and stored payment or shipment references. If provider payloads cannot be resolved, existing fail-closed unresolved/retry behavior still applies.
- prevention rule: Any provider webhook endpoint whose tenant authority is resolved from signed payload plus stored SQL records must be marked `[AllowAnonymousTenant]`; generic tenant middleware must not block it before the provider-specific trust pipeline runs.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/PaymentWebhooksController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/StripeWebhooksController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/PaymobWebhooksController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/FawryWebhookController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/ShippingWebhooksController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Middleware/TenantResolutionMiddleware.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Payments/WebhookSecurityHardeningTests.cs`

### CommerceHub payment-method authority now fails closed per store and drives customer selection from backend pricing authority
- title: CommerceHub checkout no longer treats missing store payment-method authority as provider support, and the checkout selector no longer relies on a hardcoded provider list
- affected systems: `PointOfSale.CommerceHub` checkout UI, `CommerceHubCartService`, `CommerceHubCheckoutService`, marketplace order creation, inventory reservation, provider handoff, tenant payment-method authority
- root cause: CommerceHub checkout previously had two aligned authority gaps: the frontend hardcoded `COD`, `PAYMOB`, and `STRIPE` as selectable methods, and `ValidateRequestedPaymentMethod` treated zero enabled `TenantPaymentMethod` rows as permissive support for both COD and online providers.
- impact: The active cross-system authority violation is closed. CommerceHub cart pricing now returns authoritative payment-method availability for the exact multi-store cart being repriced, the checkout selector binds to that capability surface and disables unavailable providers, and checkout fails closed when any participating store lacks an enabled row for the requested provider.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-10. Focused CommerceHub helper and checkout tests now cover zero-config fail-closed behavior, the architecture regression guard asserts the selector consumes backend capability authority, the frontend production build passed, and the focused cross-system regression slice passed with 173 tests.
- residual operational risk: Inventory can still remain reserved while an otherwise valid online payment is pending or being retried. That is intentional authority containment rather than provider-selection drift.
- prevention rule: Zero enabled `TenantPaymentMethod` rows means unavailable, not compatible. CommerceHub payment selection must be driven by backend capability authority for the exact store set being repriced, and checkout must independently fail closed if the client submits a stale or unsupported provider.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentMethodAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubCartService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubCheckoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/DTOs/CommerceHub/CommerceHubCartDtos.cs`, `PointOfSale.CommerceHub/src/checkout/PaymentMethodSelector.tsx`, `PointOfSale.CommerceHub/src/pages/CheckoutPage.tsx`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentMethodAuthorityTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/CommerceHubCheckoutFinancialControlTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### Tenant-facing tax and settings authority is now tenant-scoped in SQL
- title: Tenant admin settings no longer mutate shared storefront tax, company-profile, or receipt authority across tenants
- affected systems: `SettingsController`, `TaxCalculationService`, checkout totals, receipts, customer-facing company profile data, multi-tenant isolation
- root cause: Tenant-facing `Tax.*`, company, and receipt settings were previously read and written through shared global `SystemSettings` rows with no tenant scope, `Tax.Rate` persistence depended on the process culture while authoritative checkout tax reads parsed with `InvariantCulture`, and the tenant-scoped backfill migration did not originally preflight duplicate live global keys.
- impact: The active cross-tenant leakage and tax-setting drift paths are closed. Tenant-facing settings now persist and resolve by `SystemSettings.TenantId`, `Tax.Rate` is stored in invariant numeric form so runtime locale cannot silently change authoritative checkout math, and the tenant-scoped backfill migration now fails closed with explicit operator action if duplicate global tenant-facing keys exist before per-tenant copy begins.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-10. `SettingsController` now stores tax rate canonically and tolerates legacy decimal formats on read, `TaxCalculationService` continues to parse authoritative tax settings invariantly, and `20260510210000_TenantScopedSystemSettingsAuthority` now preflights duplicate global tenant-facing keys before backfill.
- residual operational risk: Production environments must still apply `20260510210000_TenantScopedSystemSettingsAuthority` before relying on the new isolation guarantee at runtime, and operators must reconcile duplicate legacy global keys if the migration preflight blocks rollout. Historic orders created under leaked global tax authority still need manual audit if operators suspect incorrect tax/settings were charged.
- prevention rule: Tenant-facing settings that influence storefront pricing or customer-visible business identity must be stored and queried through tenant-scoped SQL authority, numeric financial settings must be serialized canonically with culture-invariant formatting, and authority-shifting backfill migrations must preflight duplicate live legacy rows before copying them into tenant-owned scope.
- references: `PointOfSale.Core/Entities/SystemSetting.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/ApplicationDbContext.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/SettingsController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Pricing/TaxCalculationService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Migrations/20260510210000_TenantScopedSystemSettingsAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Controllers/SettingsControllerCurrencyTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/StorefrontPricingAuthorityTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/SettingsAuthorityIsolationArchitectureTests.cs`

### CommerceHub payment retry now fails closed when unresolved captured-money evidence already exists
- title: CommerceHub customer payment recovery no longer reopens online payment initiation while provider-success evidence is still unresolved or suspense-routed for the same order
- affected systems: `CommerceHubPaymentService`, CommerceHub customer order detail API/UI, provider webhook reconciliation, suspense accounting, duplicate payment prevention
- root cause: CommerceHub persisted unresolved provider-success webhook evidence by placing the marketplace order `OnHold` and routing or retaining captured-money ambiguity in SQL, but both the payment-initiation service and the customer order details page still treated `PaymentStatus.Pending` plus `OrderStatus.OnHold` as retryable payment state.
- impact: The active duplicate-money authority gap is closed. Payment initiation now rejects retries with `PAYMENT_RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED` whenever unresolved captured-payment evidence still exists for the order/provider, and customer order details surface the same order as manual-review state instead of rendering a retry action.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-10. Focused CommerceHub payment service tests now cover unresolved-webhook retry blocking, and source regression coverage asserts customer order details consume backend retry authority instead of deriving it from raw `Pending`/`OnHold` state alone.
- residual operational risk: The checkout success page can still hold stale local knowledge until it re-queries backend authority, but any later retry attempt fails closed and tells the customer the payment is under review rather than creating new payment authority.
- prevention rule: A marketplace order that has unresolved provider-success evidence, suspense-routed captured cash, or pending suspense-clearance reconciliation is not a retryable customer payment state. Customer recovery surfaces and payment-initiation services must consume that backend review authority directly instead of inferring retryability from generic order/payment statuses.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubCustomerOrderService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/DTOs/Customers/CustomerOrderDtos.cs`, `PointOfSale.CommerceHub/src/pages/CustomerOrderDetailsPage.tsx`, `PointOfSale.CommerceHub/src/types/customer-orders.ts`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentServiceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### Storefront canonical payment status now preserves quarantined captured-money authority
- title: Storefront payment recovery UI no longer tells customers "payment failed" when provider-confirmed cash is quarantined into suspense/manual-review authority
- affected systems: `CanonicalPaymentStatusResolver`, storefront payment-status API, `PaymentLifecycleService`, storefront pending/confirmation pages, late-success and amount-mismatch quarantine flows
- root cause: `PaymentLifecycleService` intentionally routes late provider success after expiry/cancel and provider amount/currency mismatch into `PaymentStatus.Expired` or `PaymentStatus.AmountMismatch`, `PaymentIntentStatus.Suspicious` or `PaymentIntentStatus.AmountMismatch`, `OrderStatus.OnHold`, and suspense accounting instead of recognizing safe success or failure. The old canonical resolver and storefront recovery UI collapsed those quarantined states into retry-safe `FAILED` guidance.
- impact: The active frontend/backend authority mismatch is closed. Storefront canonical payment status now emits `MANUAL_REVIEW` for quarantined captured-money states, and customer-facing recovery pages keep the order in a manual-review/reconciliation-visible state instead of routing the customer back into checkout retries.
- mitigation status: Re-closed in code on 2026-05-11 after a resolver precedence regression reopened `PaymentStatus.AmountMismatch` as `FAILED`. Focused backend resolver coverage and the storefront manual-review source regression both pass again in the current branch.
- residual operational risk: Financial truth still depends on the existing suspense/manual-review/reconciliation workflows before the order can converge to paid or safe-failed authority. This is accepted unresolved-money containment, not frontend corruption.
- prevention rule: Customer-facing canonical payment status must not collapse externally confirmed but locally quarantined money states into terminal failure. If provider cash may already exist, the storefront contract must remain pending/manual-review/reconciliation-required until explicit refund proof or local reconciliation establishes a true safe-failed outcome.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/CanonicalPaymentStatusResolver.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/DTOs/Payments/PaymentDtos.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Payments/PaymentLifecycleService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/PaymentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Storefront/src/features/checkout/paymentFlow.ts`, `PointOfSale.Storefront/src/pages/CheckoutPendingPage.tsx`, `PointOfSale.Storefront/src/pages/OrderConfirmationPage.tsx`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Payments/CanonicalPaymentStatusResolverTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Security/StorefrontProductionBlockerFixTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Integration/InventoryReservationLifecycleIntegrationTests.cs`


### CommerceHub order detail now validates access from SQL authority before projection-dependent reads
- title: Customer-facing CommerceHub order detail no longer returns a false `404` solely because the Mongo projection is missing before request-time repair runs
- affected systems: CommerceHub order detail read model, MongoDB projection, signed order-access-token recovery, cookie-authenticated customer order recovery
- root cause: The controller previously depended on a best-effort Mongo read to obtain `OrderNumber` and ownership fields before calling the SQL-backed repair path, which reopened the false-`404` branch when the projection was absent.
- impact: The active stale-authority regression is closed. Order access validation now uses SQL authority for `CustomerId` and `OrderNumber`, then invokes projection repair before serving the Mongo-backed response. If SQL proves the order exists but the projection still cannot be served after repair, the endpoint fails closed as temporary unavailability instead of falsely claiming the order does not exist.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-10. Regression coverage now verifies call ordering and the CommerceHub architecture test compile break caused by `RepoRoot` references is removed.
- residual operational risk: Background reconciliation is still required for non-critical read-model freshness, and the endpoint may return `503` during a true projection/Mongo outage after SQL authority has already been proven. This is intentional fail-closed behavior instead of false absence.
- prevention rule: Customer-critical CommerceHub order reads must resolve access metadata from SQL authority or repair the projection before any projection-derived `404` or token/ownership check.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/CommerceHubOrdersController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubOrderProjectionRepairService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubProjectionReconciliationService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### Storefront payment-method eligibility now aligns with backend final-total authority
- title: Storefront checkout no longer gates payment methods on subtotal when backend min/max enforcement uses final total
- affected systems: Storefront checkout payment step, pricing preview state, payment method selector, checkout API validation
- root cause: The payment step previously used subtotal for min/max checks even though backend checkout enforces payment-method limits on the authoritative final total after shipping, tax, discounts, and fees.
- impact: The active frontend/backend contract drift is closed. The Storefront payment step now uses the authoritative preview total when available, so customer-visible method eligibility aligns with backend checkout enforcement and no longer creates avoidable false accepts or false blocks at submit time.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-10. Source-based storefront architecture coverage now asserts `pricing.total` is the client-side gating value.
- residual operational risk: Before pricing preview is available, subtotal remains advisory and backend checkout still revalidates the final total before order authority is created. This preserves fail-closed behavior if the client is stale.
- prevention rule: Customer-visible payment-method min/max gating must follow the same authoritative total the backend uses for enforcement whenever preview pricing is available.
- references: `PointOfSale.Storefront/src/components/checkout/PaymentStep.tsx`, `PointOfSale.Storefront/src/types/checkout.ts`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Checkout/CheckoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/StorefrontReliabilityUxSourceTests.cs`

### CommerceHub cart persistence is now scoped to the active customer authority and waits for auth hydration before bootstrap
- title: CommerceHub persisted cart state no longer crosses authenticated customer boundaries or double-merges authenticated cart snapshots during reload/bootstrap
- affected systems: CommerceHub SPA local cart persistence, guest-to-auth cart adoption, customer login/logout/session hydration, authenticated cart bootstrap, shared-browser cart safety
- root cause: The cart store previously persisted a single browser cart snapshot under `commercehub-cart` and bootstrapped it before customer auth authority was guaranteed. That allowed stale guest or prior-customer local state to race ahead of the authenticated bootstrap path, and earlier merge semantics could also re-add locally persisted authenticated quantities into the already authoritative server cart.
- impact: The active cross-customer cart contamination path is closed. Persisted CommerceHub cart state now carries an auth scope key and a one-time guest-adoption flag, authenticated bootstrap prefers server cart truth unless it is explicitly adopting a guest cart into the newly authenticated customer cart, and the sync orchestrator waits for customer auth hydration plus scope convergence before bootstrapping or syncing.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-10. The fixed browser-cookie auth path and the cart scope synchronization now converge on the same authenticated customer authority before cart bootstrap runs.
- residual operational risk: Guest cart intent remains browser-local by design and can still be lost when the browser storage is cleared or when an authenticated customer switch intentionally rebases local state. This is accepted user-session behavior, not cross-customer authority leakage.
- prevention rule: Browser-persisted CommerceHub cart state is intent only. It must be scoped to `guest` or the exact authenticated CommerceHub customer, and authenticated cart bootstrap must not run until customer auth hydration completes and local cart scope matches current customer authority. Merging local cart state into an authenticated server cart is allowed only for the one-time guest-to-auth adoption path.
- references: `PointOfSale.CommerceHub/src/store/cart-store.ts`, `PointOfSale.CommerceHub/src/cart/useCommerceHubCartSync.tsx`, `PointOfSale.CommerceHub/src/store/customer-auth-store.ts`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### CommerceHub cookie-authenticated cart and order-recovery flows now honor customer authority without requiring a raw Authorization header
- title: CommerceHub browser-authenticated customer cart and public order recovery no longer fall back to guest because cookie-backed JWT auth lacked an `Authorization` header
- affected systems: CommerceHub cart bootstrap/save/clear/pricing APIs, CommerceHub checkout cart-lock boundary, browser-authenticated customer sessions, public order recovery surface
- root cause: `CustomersAuthController` intentionally authenticates CommerceHub customers through an HttpOnly cookie and returns an empty browser `AccessToken`, while `CommerceHubCartController` and `CommerceHubOrdersController` treated raw `Authorization` header presence as the signal that customer auth should be evaluated. The SPA uses `withCredentials` cookie auth and does not inject a bearer header, so authenticated browser requests could be downgraded to guest or anonymous behavior.
- impact: The active authority mismatch is closed. CommerceHub cart APIs now resolve customer identity directly from the customer auth scheme even when the browser authenticates only by cookie, so persisted customer carts, promo/customer pricing scope, and checkout cart locking converge on the same SQL customer authority. The public order-recovery endpoint now accepts signed-in cookie-authenticated customers without forcing an order access token solely because no raw bearer header was present.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-10. Cross-system architecture guards now assert that cookie-backed CommerceHub customer flows do not depend on raw `Authorization` header presence.
- residual operational risk: Anonymous callers still fail closed on protected CommerceHub customer paths, and the public order-recovery endpoint still requires either authenticated customer ownership or a signed order access token. Shared-device local cart persistence remains a separate user-experience concern, but it no longer blocks authenticated cart persistence behind a false guest downgrade.
- prevention rule: Browser-facing CommerceHub customer APIs must authenticate through `CustomerSecurityConstants.AuthenticationScheme` and accept the platform's cookie-backed JWT flow. Do not use raw `Authorization` header presence as a prerequisite for evaluating authenticated customer authority when the supported browser contract is `withCredentials` cookies.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/CustomersAuthController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/CommerceHubCartController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/CommerceHubOrdersController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Program.cs`, `PointOfSale.CommerceHub/src/api/http-client.ts`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### CommerceHub payment initiation now enforces customer/order authority
- title: CommerceHub online payment initiation no longer acts as an anonymous order lookup surface
- affected systems: CommerceHub payment initiation API, marketplace order confidentiality, provider session creation/reuse, customer order authority, payment-provider handoff
- root cause: The endpoint previously accepted anonymous requests and resolved marketplace orders by id, order number, or payment reference before proving customer ownership or signed guest-order access.
- impact: The active authorization gap is closed. `CommerceHubPaymentsController` now resolves authenticated customer identity from the CommerceHub customer auth scheme, normalizes signed order-access authority from either the explicit request token or the server-issued HttpOnly recovery cookie, requires either customer ownership or that signed order authority, and passes the normalized credential into `CommerceHubPaymentService`. The service enforces the same boundary before payment-session reuse, recovery, or provider-session creation can return any order/payment metadata.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-10. Focused CommerceHub payment service tests and cross-system architecture guards cover the authority check.
- residual operational risk: Authenticated customer cookies or signed order-access tokens remain the recovery channel for legitimate customers. Unauthorized callers now fail closed before payment authority or provider-session metadata is returned.
- prevention rule: CommerceHub online payment initiation must require authenticated customer ownership or a signed order-access token bound to the marketplace order before order lookup, payment-session reuse, or provider-session creation returns any order/payment data. Browser recovery may source that signed token from the request payload or from the server-issued HttpOnly recovery cookie, but both paths must converge on the same fail-closed access gate.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/CommerceHubPaymentsController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/CommerceHubOrdersController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentServiceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### CommerceHub online payment handoff now preserves provider-specific continuation authority
- title: CommerceHub customer payment continuation no longer assumes every online provider redirects immediately
- affected systems: CommerceHub checkout frontend, CommerceHub customer order details, CommerceHub checkout API, CommerceHub payment initiation API, Stripe and Paymob marketplace payments, inventory reservations, customer payment recovery UX
- root cause: The earlier frontend hardening correctly left online orders in a non-terminal pending state, but it still treated `paymentUrl` as the only successful handoff shape and did not expose the authoritative payment-method code on order details for later retries.
- impact: The active contract drift is closed. CommerceHub now branches on the backend handoff contract: redirect providers continue through `paymentUrl`, Stripe client-secret sessions render an embedded payment form on both checkout success and customer order details, and the order detail surface remains a durable server-authorized retry entrypoint. Checkout creation also now validates the concrete online provider per store instead of treating any `SupportsOnline` method as interchangeable.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-10. CommerceHub frontend production build passed, focused payment/order architecture guards passed, and the broader cross-system regression slice passed with 213 tests.
- residual operational risk: Inventory can still remain reserved while payment is pending or provider initiation is being retried; this is intentional authority containment rather than false terminal success.
- prevention rule: Customer-facing CommerceHub online checkout must distinguish `order created` from `payment completed`, validate the exact requested provider against each store's enabled method set, and branch continuation UX by authoritative provider handoff type rather than assuming every online provider returns a redirect URL.
- references: `PointOfSale.CommerceHub/src/pages/CheckoutPage.tsx`, `PointOfSale.CommerceHub/src/pages/CustomerOrderDetailsPage.tsx`, `PointOfSale.CommerceHub/src/payments/EmbeddedStripePaymentForm.tsx`, `PointOfSale.CommerceHub/src/checkout/PaymentMethodSelector.tsx`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubCheckoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### Frontend stale-state mutations are backend-contained at checkout and POS authority boundaries
- title: Cached client totals, stock views, payment eligibility, and optimistic payment outcomes do not create order/payment/inventory authority without backend SQL validation
- affected systems: POS/admin sales, payment status UI, inventory reservations, stock mutation, idempotency replay, accounting/projection outboxes, and the non-transactional storefront stale-client checks that still run before final order commit
- root cause: The architectural risk surface is stale or optimistic frontend state. Storefront, admin, or POS clients can hold cached totals, inventory visibility, payment method assumptions, or provider client outcomes that are older than SQL authority. If the backend treated those fields as authority, stale clients could create incorrect orders, duplicate payment authority, oversold stock, or false terminal payment state.
- impact: Most of the audited stale-client corruption path is contained. POS sale creation recomputes authoritative price/tax server-side, validates normalized payment splits, locks stock rows before decrement, and stages stock-sync outbox intent. Storefront checkout rejects stale client totals before order creation, re-checks storefront/customer SQL authority, and now also re-locks shipping/payment/storefront authority plus revalidates pricing/payment-method limits inside the final order transaction before inventory reservation continues. Online payment UI/provider client outcomes remain non-terminal until backend canonical payment status and webhook/reconciliation paths confirm them. Idempotent replay is separated from new authority creation, and cleanup preserves committed order/payment authority.
- mitigation status: Mitigated and revalidated on 2026-05-10. Focused hardening tests passed across checkout concurrency/idempotency, cross-system authority guards, payment authorization/status resolution, refund hardening, and critical risk regression coverage, including explicit source coverage for transaction-scoped storefront pricing/payment-method revalidation after `BeginTransactionAsync`.
- residual operational risk: Frontend and read-model surfaces may still temporarily show stale totals, stock, or pending payment state. Those are accepted eventual-consistency effects so long as backend authority continues to recompute, lock, and fail closed before durable order/payment/inventory mutations.
- prevention rule: Treat frontend/admin/POS request fields as intent or evidence only. Any path that creates money, order, inventory, payout, commission, or accounting authority must recompute authoritative totals/status from SQL, validate stale-client expectations, lock the relevant SQL authority rows where needed, and keep replay/cleanup paths from mutating committed business truth.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/Checkout/CheckoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/SalesController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Inventory/ProductVariantStockMutationSupport.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/PaymentRequestAuthorizationService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/CanonicalPaymentStatusResolver.cs`, `PointOfSale.Storefront/src/services/paymentFlow.ts`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CheckoutConcurrencyHardeningSourceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemAuthorityRegressionGuardTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Payments/PaymentRequestAuthorizationServiceTests.cs`

### Production dependency audit warnings are cleared for API, Infrastructure, and tests
- title: Known vulnerable transitive packages no longer resolve in the production/test dependency graph
- affected systems: API runtime package graph, Infrastructure package graph, MongoDB driver stack, Elasticsearch/NEST JSON dependency, OpenTelemetry export pipeline, Hangfire runtime packages, CI dependency audit
- root cause: Restore/build verification exposed package-level production risks: Hangfire API package skew, vulnerable OpenTelemetry OTLP exporter version, vulnerable MongoDB-driver transitive compression packages, and an old `Newtonsoft.Json` version declared by the legacy NEST/Elasticsearch dependency stack.
- impact: The active package-audit findings are closed for the audited projects. Hangfire package versions are aligned, OpenTelemetry packages resolve to patched versions, MongoDB driver/compression packages resolve to non-vulnerable versions, and Infrastructure pins patched `Newtonsoft.Json` while retaining the legacy NEST 7.x client to avoid a high-blast-radius search-client migration.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated and revalidated on 2026-05-10. `dotnet list ... package --vulnerable --include-transitive` reports no vulnerable packages for `PointOfSale.API`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure`, or `PointOfSale.Tests`.
- residual operational risk: The legacy NEST 7.x client is still a migration risk because there is no newer NEST 7.x package line. Future Elasticsearch client migration should be planned separately with search-index compatibility tests rather than mixed into authority hardening patches.
- prevention rule: Production hardening passes must run dependency vulnerability checks after restore. When a vulnerable package is transitive, prefer upgrading the owning direct package; if the owning package cannot safely move, pin a patched direct dependency and verify the resolved graph, build, and focused production-risk tests.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/PointOfSale.API.csproj`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/PointOfSale.Infrastructure.csproj`

### Vendor payout reconciliation now bypasses post-handoff governance blocks
- title: CommerceHub payout reconciliation no longer leaves bank/provider transfer truth ahead of SQL ledger truth when vendor governance changes after payout handoff
- affected systems: CommerceHub vendor payouts, Super Admin completed vendor payout recognition, external payout rails, vendor earnings, platform cash ledger, financial readiness gates, vendor governance controls
- root cause: `CommerceHubPayoutService.MarkCompletedAsync` previously required `EnsureVendorPayoutAllowedAsync` before loading the payout and before distinguishing a new outward transfer from completion of an already `Processing` or `ReconciliationRequired` payout. `MarkFailedAsync` applied the same governance gate before accepting non-execution proof. `SuperAdminPayoutsService.ExecuteAsync` also treated completed payout evidence recognition as blocked by vendor payout governance and tenant active status.
- impact: The active divergence path is closed. Completion of a previously handed-off payout now reconciles executed external transfer evidence into SQL ledger truth without re-running vendor payout governance. Failure of a previously handed-off payout now accepts durable non-execution proof without governance blocking release. Super Admin completed-payout recognition records externally confirmed vendor money movement even if vendor suspension/governance changed after the bank/provider transfer executed.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-09. Focused CommerceHub finance, Super Admin payout, and architecture regression tests cover governance-blocked reconciliation paths.
- residual operational risk: Governance still authorizes new payout creation and `Pending -> Processing` external handoff. Operators must not use completed-payout recognition as pre-transfer authorization; it is only valid when immutable external evidence proves the transfer already executed.
- prevention rule: Governance may authorize new payout creation or `Pending -> Processing` external handoff, but completion of a previously handed-off payout and failure with durable non-execution proof are reconciliation of external truth. Those paths must load the payout first, branch on status/evidence, and avoid blocking local truth recognition behind vendor governance that changed after the external outcome occurred.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPayoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/SuperAdmin/Services/SuperAdminPayoutsService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/CommerceHubFinanceServiceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/SuperAdminPayoutsServiceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`, `.ai-context/ARCHITECTURE_TRUTH.md`, `.ai-context/CROSS_SYSTEM_AUTHORITY_HARDENING.md`

### Storefront customer deactivation is now enforced at cart and checkout authority boundaries
- title: Already-issued storefront customer JWTs can no longer create cart or checkout authority after SQL `OnlineCustomer.IsActive` is disabled
- affected systems: Storefront identity, storefront cart, checkout, POS orders, inventory reservations, payment intent creation, affiliate attribution, accounting/projections
- root cause: Storefront login blocked inactive customers, but already-issued tokens remained accepted. `CheckoutService.ValidateCheckoutCallerOwnershipAsync` verified customer existence and tenant ownership but did not reject inactive SQL customers; storefront cart validation checked tenant claim shape without re-reading current customer active status.
- impact: The active divergence path is closed. Authenticated storefront cart and checkout paths now re-check PostgreSQL customer authority and reject inactive or tenant-mismatched customers with `CUSTOMER_INACTIVE` before order, reservation, payment, or attribution authority can be created.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-08. Architecture regression coverage asserts SQL active-status checks exist in cart and checkout paths.
- residual operational risk: Existing tokens can still authenticate cryptographically until expiry, but they no longer authorize cart/checkout business mutations when SQL customer authority has been disabled.
- prevention rule: Any authenticated storefront path that creates or mutates customer-owned business authority must re-read SQL customer status or enforce an equivalent token-version/revocation authority boundary before write-side effects.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/Checkout/CheckoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Checkout/ICheckoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/CheckoutController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/StorefrontCartController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### Vendor repayment approval now recognizes inward cash without outward-money readiness gating
- title: CommerceHub vendor repayment proof approval no longer blocks ledger recognition of already-received vendor cash behind the outward-transfer readiness gate
- affected systems: CommerceHub vendor debt, vendor repayment proofs, platform cash ledger, financial reconciliation, vendor suspension/debt controls, payout/readiness gates
- root cause: `CommerceHubVendorPaymentsService.ApprovePaymentRequestAsync` required external proof, then called `EnsureOutwardMoneyMovementAllowedAsync` before recording platform cash inflow and reducing vendor debt. Unrelated unresolved external-money, accounting, refund, payout, or backbone ambiguity could block recognition of cash that had already arrived externally.
- impact: The active divergence path is closed. Vendor repayment approval still requires one-time external evidence, duplicate-proof protection, pending-request ledger checks, serializable locking, and financial safety verification, but it posts the inward platform-cash/vendor-debt ledger effect without using the outward-transfer authorization gate.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-08. Focused CommerceHub vendor payment and architecture tests cover inward cash recognition despite outward readiness blockers.
- residual operational risk: If local ledger persistence or safety verification fails, the repayment remains unapproved and must be retried/reconciled from the durable payment request/proof evidence rather than silently updating vendor debt.
- prevention rule: Do not use outward-money readiness gates to authorize recognition of already-received inward cash. Inward cash evidence must be durably recognized into SQL ledger truth or left in an explicit retry/reconciliation state.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/Finance/CommerceHubVendorPaymentsService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/CommerceHubVendorPaymentsServiceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### Shipment delivery now converges through SQL fulfillment authority
- title: Shipment and carrier delivery paths no longer publish fulfillment truth without first advancing authoritative `Order.FulfillmentStatus`
- affected systems: Admin shipment updates, shipment authority recovery, carrier shipment registration/tracking/webhooks, fulfillment lifecycle, COD payment lifecycle, accounting outbox, affiliate settlement, affiliate projections
- root cause: `AdminShipmentService`, `ShipmentService`, and `CarrierShipmentService` could set shipment delivery/progress state and emit `FulfillmentDeliveredEvent` from mapped shipment status while SQL `Order.FulfillmentStatus` stayed behind. COD and affiliate consumers reload SQL authority, so projected delivery could diverge from money-recognition eligibility.
- impact: The active corruption path is closed. Shipment progress now calls `IOrderFulfillmentService.SynchronizeFromShipmentAsync`, which advances `Order.FulfillmentStatus` through the legal fulfillment state machine and emits fulfillment lifecycle events from the service that owns SQL fulfillment authority. Shipment services no longer construct `FulfillmentDeliveredEvent` directly.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-08. API build and focused architecture/shipping tests passed.
- residual operational risk: Source ownership now fails closed. Manual shipment updates against carrier-managed orders, or carrier webhooks against manual orders, are rejected by fulfillment source validation instead of silently creating cross-system delivery truth.
- prevention rule: Shipment services may emit shipment/order projection events, but fulfillment lifecycle events must originate from `IOrderFulfillmentService` after SQL fulfillment authority is advanced. COD delivered-event handling must throw on failed COD transition so message retry/recovery remains available.
- references: `PointOfSale.Core/Interfaces/Fulfillment/IOrderFulfillmentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Fulfillment/OrderFulfillmentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Admin/AdminShipmentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Shipping/ShipmentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Shipping/CarrierShipmentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/OrderDeliveredEventConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### Affiliate payout readiness now globally scans malformed critical marketplace backlog
- title: Affiliate payouts no longer skip malformed critical marketplace backlog hidden by literal payload prefilters
- affected systems: Affiliate payout execution, marketplace event backbone, MassTransit bus outbox, authoritative integration outbox, CommerceHub financial readiness, vendor/refund/order propagation, affiliate commission settlement
- root cause: `CommerceHubFinancialReadinessGate.EnsureAmbiguousMarketplaceAggregateBackboneHealthyAsync` scanned unhealthy critical outbox rows without a `MarketplaceOrderId` prefilter so malformed payloads reached fail-closed ambiguity handling, but `AffiliateSettlementOrchestrator.EnsureAffiliateAmbiguousMarketplaceAggregateBackboneReadyAsync` still prefiltered authoritative outbox, MassTransit bus outbox, and marketplace outbox rows with `EF.Functions.ILike(..., "%MarketplaceOrderId%")`.
- impact: The asymmetry is closed. Affiliate payout readiness now applies the same non-tenant-prefiltered malformed/ambiguous critical-event scan as CommerceHub readiness across authoritative outbox, MassTransit bus outbox, and marketplace outbox rows. Malformed or wrapper-shaped critical marketplace payloads that cannot prove affected tenant scope fail closed before affiliate money movement is authorized.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-08. Regression tests assert the affiliate ambiguous-backlog scan does not contain the literal `MarketplaceOrderId` prefilters.
- residual operational risk: Availability can be intentionally reduced when a malformed critical backlog row exists. Operators must repair, publish, or reconcile the row rather than authorize affiliate payouts against unknown marketplace/refund/order propagation state.
- prevention rule: Affiliate and CommerceHub outward-money readiness must keep equivalent non-tenant-prefiltered fail-closed scans for malformed or ambiguous critical marketplace aggregate backlog. Literal payload marker prefilters must not be used as proof of absence.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Services/Affiliate/AffiliateSettlementOrchestrator.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/Finance/CommerceHubFinancialReadinessGate.cs`, `PointOfSale.Core/Events/AffectedTenantScopeResolver.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### Affiliate partial-refund payout lifecycle now settles net payable commission
- title: Storefront/POS partial affiliate commission reversals no longer leave remaining approved commission visible in balance but excluded from payout lifecycle selection
- affected systems: Storefront/POS refunds, affiliate commission ledger, affiliate available balance, affiliate payout execution, affiliate payable accounting/projections
- root cause: `PaymentLifecycleService.ReconcileAffiliateCommissionReversalsForRefundAsync` supports proportional refund reversals with custom reversal keys, and `AffiliatePayoutService.GetAvailableBalanceAsync` computes net approved commission as approved minus reversed per order item. The payout orchestrator previously excluded every approved order item that had any reversal row, which treated partial reversals as full settlement blockers.
- impact: The divergence path is closed. `AffiliateSettlementOrchestrator` now groups reversal totals per approved order item, fails closed if reversal totals exceed approved authority, selects exact FIFO payout plans using the unreversed payable commission amount, and writes `CommissionLedger.Paid` rows for that same net amount through `AffiliateCommissionLedger.CreatePaidNetOfReversals`.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-08. Targeted regression tests verify the net-paid ledger factory and the architecture rule that payout planning must net reversals instead of excluding partially reversed approved entries.
- residual operational risk: The exact FIFO payout rule still requires the requested payout to match a set of net-payable commission rows. This is intentional: unmatched amounts fail closed instead of creating partial or synthetic paid lifecycle rows.
- prevention rule: Affiliate payout planning must use the same net-per-order-item authority as balance calculation. Partial reversals reduce payable commission amount; only fully reversed or already paid order items are excluded from settlement eligibility.
