- references: `PointOfSale.Core/Entities/Affiliate/AffiliateCommissionLedger.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Services/Affiliate/AffiliateSettlementOrchestrator.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Services/Affiliate/AffiliatePayoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Payments/PaymentLifecycleService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Affiliate/AffiliateSettlementTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### Storefront provider-confirmed but locally quarantined captures now route to suspense truth
- title: Late success or provider-authority mismatch on storefront payments no longer leaves externally confirmed customer cash without durable accounting recognition
- affected systems: Storefront checkout, payment lifecycle, accounting outbox, unresolved storefront payment recovery, inventory/coupon compensation follow-up
- root cause: The lifecycle already quarantined unsafe late-success and mismatch states, but manual review status alone did not durably represent the externally confirmed money fact across accounting and recovery workflows.
- impact: The cross-system divergence is now contained in code. Quarantined provider-confirmed storefront captures enqueue unresolved-payment suspense accounting and mark the unresolved webhook/review record as routed to suspense so cash authority is durable while local order completion remains blocked.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-08. Regression assertions verify suspense outbox enqueue on late-success and mismatch quarantine paths.
- residual operational risk: Inventory/order/customer state can still remain blocked until reconciliation or compensation finishes; this is intentional integrity preservation, not silent corruption.
- prevention rule: Provider-confirmed customer cash that cannot safely complete the normal lifecycle must create tenant-scoped suspense/recovery truth, not just a manual-review flag.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Payments/PaymentLifecycleService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/StorefrontSuspenseRecoveryPolicy.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Integration/InventoryReservationLifecycleIntegrationTests.cs`

### CommerceHub suspense-routed provider-success webhooks now converge to local payment truth
- title: CommerceHub provider-success suspense rows no longer stay indefinitely detached from the later paid local payment they belong to
- affected systems: CommerceHub payment reconciliation, marketplace order hold state, suspense accounting, vendor payout readiness, unresolved webhook review
- root cause: The previous hardening pass could route unscoped provider cash into suspense and hold the marketplace order, but it did not fully converge the row when the paid local `CommerceHubPayment` appeared later.
- impact: The under-recovery path is closed. Reconciliation now matches suspense-routed webhooks to paid payments, queues tenant-scoped suspense clearance, releases the marketplace hold under the proper lifecycle boundary, publishes status updates, and marks the webhook resolved only after those local intents are staged.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-08. Focused CommerceHub finance tests verify suspense clearance enqueue and order hold release.
- residual operational risk: Rows with irrecoverably ambiguous tenant suspense scope still fail closed in `SuspenseMatchedPaymentPendingClearance` until repaired.
- prevention rule: A suspense-routed provider-success webhook must not be considered resolved until the suspense reversal intent and any blocked order-state recovery are durably staged.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubFinanceReconciliationJob.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/CommerceHubFinanceServiceTests.cs`

### Reconciliation-required manual refunds now recover local truth from persisted evidence
- title: CommerceHub manual refund transfers that already happened externally no longer depend on mutable refund-history repair before local convergence
- affected systems: CommerceHub refunds, refund execution recovery, vendor earnings, financial ledger, payout/readiness blockers, refund/order/payment status events
- root cause: Reconciliation recovery needed to resume from persisted execution evidence, but inconsistent immutable refund reversal fields could still block ledger recognition and keep the refund stuck in `ReconciliationRequired`.
- impact: The recovery path is now resilient. The worker reloads the authority graph, derives an authoritative refund breakdown when persisted immutable values are inconsistent, uses a normalized ledger snapshot instead of mutating immutable history, and completes local refund recognition from the existing external transfer evidence.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-08. Focused refund recovery tests verify the request moves to `Paid`, the refund moves to `Completed`, and execution evidence is marked successful.
- residual operational risk: If persisted evidence is missing or the order graph itself is unrecoverable, the refund remains explicitly blocked for operator reconciliation rather than being silently completed.
- prevention rule: Recovery from externally confirmed refund evidence must reuse immutable historical authority and normalize through derived ledger snapshots, not rewrite immutable refund breakdown fields.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/RefundExecutionService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubRefundRecoveryHostedService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/RefundExecutionServiceTests.cs`

### Authoritative event and direct mutation regression guards now block unreviewed bypasses
- title: Future direct SQL/bulk mutation and direct MassTransit authoritative-event publishes are now CI-blocked unless explicitly reviewed
- affected systems: PostgreSQL authority boundary, MassTransit authoritative event outbox, projection ordering, readiness gates, provider trust projections, future financial/order/inventory/tenant-owned mutation paths
- root cause: The residual cross-system risk was no longer a known active corruption path; it was that future code could bypass EF `SaveChanges` guards through `ExecuteUpdate`/raw SQL or bypass central event-publisher checks through `ConsumeContext.Publish`.
- impact: The practical regression window is narrowed. Authoritative event publishing now validates aggregate-ordering contracts and affected-tenant scope shape at runtime in both MassTransit and in-memory publishers. `ProviderTrustUpdatedEvent` now exposes `ProviderProfileId` as its authoritative aggregate and `ProviderTrustConsumer` stages it through `IEventPublisher` before the SQL commit. Architecture tests fail if new direct SQL/bulk mutation files appear without explicit approval or if authoritative events are published directly from MassTransit consume context.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-08. Regression tests cover central event contract validation, ProviderTrust aggregate ordering, direct consume-context publish prevention, and direct SQL/bulk mutation allowlist review.
- residual operational risk: Existing approved direct SQL/bulk mutation paths still require their local transaction, tenant-scope, lifecycle, outbox, and reconciliation tests. The guard prevents unreviewed expansion; it does not prove each already-approved path is globally correct by itself.
- prevention rule: New direct SQL, `ExecuteUpdate`, or `ExecuteDelete` paths must either move behind an existing authority service or be added to the architecture-test allowlist only after cross-system review. Authoritative integration events must go through `IEventPublisher`; event types that carry affected tenant arrays must implement `IAffectedTenantScopedIntegrationEvent`.
- references: `PointOfSale.Core/Events/IntegrationEventAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/EventBus/MassTransitEventPublisher.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/EventBus/InMemoryEventPublisher.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProviderTrustConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemAuthorityRegressionGuardTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/EventOutboxConsistencyArchitectureTests.cs`

### CommerceHub outward-money readiness now globally blocks ambiguous marketplace aggregate backlog
- title: CommerceHub refunds and payouts can no longer skip unhealthy multi-store marketplace aggregate events whose affected tenant scope is ambiguous
- affected systems: CommerceHub vendor payouts, Super Admin vendor payouts, CommerceHub gateway refunds, marketplace event backbone, MassTransit bus outbox, authoritative integration outbox, vendor balances, refund/debt recognition
- root cause: `CommerceHubFinancialReadinessGate.EnsureCommerceIntakeAllowedAsync` ran `EnsureAmbiguousMarketplaceAggregateBackboneHealthyAsync` before tenant-prefiltered scans, but `EnsureOutwardMoneyMovementAllowedAsync` did not. Its integration check tenant-prefiltered by target tenant id before scope resolution, so an unhealthy multi-store aggregate with base `TenantId` for vendor A and missing explicit affected-store scope could be skipped when authorizing outward money for vendor B.
- impact: The under-blocking path is closed. CommerceHub outward-money readiness now runs the same non-tenant-prefiltered ambiguous marketplace aggregate scan before tenant-prefiltered backlog checks, so ambiguous unhealthy authoritative outbox, MassTransit bus outbox, and marketplace outbox rows fail closed until scope is repaired or the row is processed/reconciled.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-08. Regression tests assert outward-money readiness calls the global ambiguous scan before tenant-specific accounting/backbone checks.
- residual operational risk: Availability is intentionally sacrificed if legacy or malformed critical marketplace events lack explicit affected-store scope. Operators must repair the payload/scope or reconcile the row.
- prevention rule: Every CommerceHub outward-money authorization path must retain the non-tenant-prefiltered ambiguous marketplace aggregate scan, or materialize exact affected-tenant scope at event write time and require it for critical aggregate events.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/Finance/CommerceHubFinancialReadinessGate.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### CommerceHub outward-money readiness now runs inside final serializable authority boundaries
- title: Gateway refund execution, CommerceHub payout creation, and Super Admin completed payout execution can no longer commit from a pre-transaction readiness snapshot
- affected systems: CommerceHub gateway refunds, CommerceHub payout creation, Super Admin vendor payouts, external refund/payout rails, CommerceHub financial ledger, vendor earnings, platform cash, unresolved provider cash/refund recovery, accounting and marketplace backbones
- root cause: Some outward-money paths called `EnsureOutwardMoneyMovementAllowedAsync` before opening the serializable transaction that recorded local refund/payout authorization or completed payout evidence. New unresolved external-money rows or critical backlog rows inserted after the readiness call were outside the transaction's serializable read set.
- impact: The TOCTOU window is closed for the audited paths. CommerceHub payout creation, gateway refund claim/execution, and Super Admin completed payout execution now perform the final readiness read inside the serializable transaction before payout reservation, refund processing claim, payout evidence recognition, earning allocation, or ledger mutation.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-08. Regression tests assert readiness appears after `BeginTransactionAsync(IsolationLevel.Serializable, ...)` in the affected paths.
- residual operational risk: Readiness cannot lock future rows that do not exist yet; if a conflicting ambiguity is inserted only after the authority transaction commits, it becomes the next operation's blocker rather than invalidating the committed operation.
- prevention rule: The final authorization read for outward money movement must execute inside the same serializable transaction or claim boundary that records the local authorization/recognition state, immediately before the state mutation that permits external handoff or completed external evidence recognition.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPayoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubRefundService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/SuperAdmin/Services/SuperAdminPayoutsService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### Storefront checkout writes now enforce SQL storefront availability
- title: Storefront orders can no longer be created after POS Admin marks the storefront disabled or maintenance
- affected systems: POS Admin store configuration, Storefront checkout, order authority, inventory reservations, payment intent creation, accounting/order projection downstreams
- root cause: Public storefront publication guarding only applied to anonymous GET/HEAD read endpoints and derived status from cache/Mongo projection. The checkout POST path did not check the SQL `StoreConfigs.StorefrontStatus` authority before creating order, stock, payment, and outbox authority.
- impact: The confirmed write-path authority gap is closed. New checkout execution now checks SQL `StoreConfigs.StorefrontStatus` after idempotent replay recovery and again inside the order-creation transaction before inventory reservation or order insert. The transaction-scoped check locks the `StoreConfigs` row with `FOR UPDATE` on PostgreSQL or `UPDLOCK, ROWLOCK, HOLDLOCK` on SQL Server, and fails closed unless the status is `live`.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-08. Regression tests assert the SQL availability guard, transaction-scoped row lock, and storefront-unavailable checkout response path.
- residual operational risk: Already committed idempotent checkout responses can still replay after a storefront is disabled; this is intentional because replay does not create new order/payment/stock authority.
- prevention rule: Every storefront write path that creates or advances business authority must synchronously check SQL storefront availability state inside the write boundary and fail closed when not `live`.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/Checkout/CheckoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/CheckoutController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`, `.ai-context/ARCHITECTURE_TRUTH.md`

### Affiliate payout readiness now runs inside the serializable payout transaction
- title: Affiliate payout can no longer commit against a stale cross-system readiness snapshot taken before the payout transaction
- affected systems: Affiliate payout execution, affiliate balance ledger, affiliate commission ledger, POS/accounting outbox, CommerceHub unresolved payment/refund records, marketplace and stock sync backbones
- root cause: `AffiliateSettlementOrchestrator.ExecuteAffiliatePayoutAsync` called `EnsureAffiliateOutwardMoneyMovementReadyAsync` before entering the serializable retry transaction, even though the orchestrator documents readiness/balance validation as part of the protected payout execution pipeline.
- impact: The confirmed TOCTOU window is closed for the payout code path. Affiliate outward-money readiness now executes inside `ExecutePayoutCoreAsync` after the serializable transaction begins and before balance validation, transfer-evidence allocation, withdrawal creation, commission paid rows, accounting, or payout-request reconciliation.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-08. Regression tests assert readiness appears after `BeginTransactionAsync(IsolationLevel.Serializable, ct)` and before the payout authority mutations, and no longer runs in the public pre-transaction method.
- residual operational risk: Readiness cannot lock future rows that do not exist yet; if a conflicting external-money ambiguity is inserted only after the serializable transaction commits, it becomes the next operation's blocker rather than invalidating the already committed payout.
- prevention rule: Affiliate outward-money readiness must remain inside the same serializable transaction and retry scope as the payout balance read, transfer-evidence allocation, withdrawal, commission paid rows, accounting, and payout-request state changes.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Services/Affiliate/AffiliateSettlementOrchestrator.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Services/Affiliate/AffiliatePayoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`, `.ai-context/ARCHITECTURE_TRUTH.md`

### POS financial and inventory FK tenant co-ownership is now enforced at the persistence boundary
- title: POS financial and stock authority can no longer attach current-tenant rows to another tenant's parties, warehouses, products, variants, units, invoices, or accounts through single-column foreign keys
- affected systems: POS sales, POS purchases, sales and purchase invoice items, ProductVariantStocks, WarehouseStocks, StockMovements, account ledgers, customer/supplier transactions, supplier payments, financial transactions, accounting outbox inputs, stock-sync outbox inputs, tenant isolation
- root cause: Several POS authority rows carry their own `TenantId`, but also reference tenant-owned records through single-column IDs. Query filters and controller validation reduced exposure, but did not make co-ownership a persistence invariant for future tracked mutations, worker paths, or query-filter bypasses.
- impact: The audited cross-system corruption candidate is mitigated at the EF commit boundary. `ApplicationDbContext.SaveChanges` now resolves the tenant ownership of referenced customers, suppliers, warehouses, products, product variants, units, purchase invoices, sales representatives, and financial accounts using tracked state first and `IgnoreQueryFilters()` SQL lookups second. If an authority row points across tenants, the commit fails before outbox, ledger, stock, or projection truth can be persisted.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-08. Regression tests cover cross-tenant sale customer, purchase supplier, sale-line product, stock-movement variant, and account-ledger party edges.
- residual operational risk: Database composite tenant foreign keys still remain the stronger final defense for schemas where PostgreSQL can represent the relationship directly. Future raw SQL or `ExecuteUpdate` paths that bypass EF must either use database constraints or reproduce this invariant explicitly.
- prevention rule: Any new POS financial, ledger, stock, or party-balance authority table that stores `TenantId` and references another tenant-owned row must enforce tenant co-ownership before commit and add a regression test for cross-tenant ID injection.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/ApplicationDbContext.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/TenantCoOwnershipGuardTests.cs`, `.ai-context/ARCHITECTURE_TRUTH.md`

### Ambiguous multi-store critical event backlog is globally inspected by readiness gates
- title: CommerceHub and affiliate payout readiness no longer miss failed/stale ambiguous marketplace aggregate events for non-base tenants
- affected systems: CommerceHub checkout intake, refunds, payouts, affiliate payout execution, marketplace event backbone, MassTransit bus outbox, authoritative integration outbox, multi-vendor order/payment/refund propagation
- root cause: `CommerceHubFinancialReadinessGate` and `AffiliateSettlementOrchestrator` previously contained fail-closed logic for ambiguous marketplace aggregate payloads, but their backlog queries first prefiltered rows by `TenantId == targetTenant` or by searching the target tenant id inside the payload/body. A failed multi-store aggregate event whose base `TenantId` was vendor A and whose payload lacked explicit `AffectedStoreIds` could therefore be skipped entirely when checking vendor B.
- impact: The validated under-blocking path is closed. Both CommerceHub and affiliate readiness now run a separate ambiguous marketplace aggregate scan across unhealthy critical authoritative outbox, MassTransit bus outbox, and marketplace outbox rows before tenant-prefiltered scans. Nested MassTransit-style payloads are also detected by `AffectedTenantScopeResolver`.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-08. Ambiguous critical marketplace aggregate rows now fail closed with scope-undetermined readiness errors until affected-tenant scope is repaired or the backlog row is delivered/reconciled.
- residual operational risk: Availability is intentionally sacrificed if legacy or malformed critical marketplace events lack explicit affected-store scope. Operators must repair the payload/scope or reconcile the row.
- prevention rule: Critical readiness queries must keep a non-tenant-prefiltered scan for ambiguous marketplace aggregate rows, or materialize exact affected-tenant scope at write time and require it before the row can enter critical backlog tables.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/Finance/CommerceHubFinancialReadinessGate.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Services/Affiliate/AffiliateSettlementOrchestrator.cs`, `PointOfSale.Core/Events/AffectedTenantScopeResolver.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### POS purchase mutations now use sale-side row serialization and accounting versions
- title: Concurrent purchase invoice updates can no longer replay stock and accounting reversal from the same stale authority snapshot
- affected systems: POS purchase invoices, ProductVariantStocks, StockMovements, supplier transactions and balances, POS purchase accounting outbox, general ledger posting, stock projection outbox
- root cause: `PurchasesController.UpdatePurchase` and `DeletePurchase` previously loaded the purchase invoice with a normal EF query after opening a transaction, but did not lock the invoice row with `FOR UPDATE` / `UPDLOCK`, did not maintain an `AccountingMutationVersion`, and did not carry a mutation version into accounting outbox rows.
- impact: The validated corruption path is closed for newly processed purchase mutations. Purchase update/delete now locks the purchase invoice row before reverting or removing effects, updates carry `ExpectedMutationVersion`, purchase mutations increment `AccountingMutationVersion`, accounting outbox rows persist the aggregate version, synchronous outbox completion verifies the matching aggregate version, and the accounting processor skips stale or legacy-unversioned purchase messages after later mutations.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-08. S78 adds `PurchaseInvoices.AccountingMutationVersion`, and architecture tests assert row locking, mutation-version checks, stale outbox rejection, and versioned synchronous outbox completion.
- residual operational risk: A client that omits `ExpectedMutationVersion` can still intentionally overwrite a later purchase state after the row lock returns the latest invoice; this is consistent with the existing sale behavior and remains financially consistent because the mutation reverts the latest committed purchase state.
- prevention rule: Any POS document mutation that reverts and reapplies stock/accounting effects must serialize on the document authority row and carry a mutation version through accounting/outbox processing.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/PurchasesController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Core/Entities/PurchaseInvoice.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Accounting/AccountingOutboxProcessor.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Migrations/20260508170000_S78_PosPurchaseAccountingMutationVersion.sql`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/SalesFinancialSafetyHardeningTests.cs`

### Provider-confirmed CommerceHub refunds now converge through refund authority and vendor reversal workflows
- title: Provider refund webhooks now create authoritative CommerceHub refund truth instead of a payment-only cash adjustment
- affected systems: CommerceHub payment authority, CommerceHub refunds, vendor earnings and payouts, CommerceHub order/sub-order payment lifecycle, storefront/customer refund visibility, affiliate commission reversal, financial reconciliation
- root cause: The old provider-refund path incremented `ProvisionalRefundedAmount`, set the payment to `RefundRequired`, and queued compensation. The worker then marked the payment `Refunded`, merged `RefundedAmount`, and posted only platform cash accounting without creating `CommerceHubRefund` rows or driving the normal refund lifecycle.
- impact: The validated cross-system corruption path is closed for newly processed provider refunds. Confirmed provider refunds now carry immutable per-sub-order allocation snapshots, reconcile into authoritative `CommerceHubRefund` rows, apply `PartiallyRefunded` vs `Refunded` payment state through `CommerceHubPaymentLifecycleAuthority`, reverse vendor earnings or create post-payout vendor debt adjustments, post through `RecordRefundAsync`, and emit the same refund-driven order state events used by downstream systems. Historical provider-refund compensation rows that lack immutable allocation snapshots are now quarantined into operator reconciliation instead of being processed through the old lossy path.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-08. Provider refund messages now persist `RefundAllocationSnapshotJson`, the worker converges confirmed provider refunds through the authoritative refund lifecycle, and S77 fail-closes old snapshotless provider-refund backlog into reconciliation.
- residual operational risk: Any already-persisted provider-refund compensation row that predates the snapshot field and cannot be deterministically allocated is intentionally blocked until an operator reconciles it.
- prevention rule: Provider-confirmed refunds must never bypass the authoritative refund aggregate. Every externally confirmed refund must carry immutable allocation evidence and converge through the same refund, vendor-reversal, ledger, and event lifecycle used by first-party refund execution.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentCompensationOutboxWorker.cs`, `PointOfSale.Core/Entities/CommerceHubPaymentCompensationOutboxMessage.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/Configurations/CommerceHubPaymentCompensationOutboxMessageConfiguration.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Migrations/20260508143000_S77_CommerceHubProviderRefundAuthorityConvergence.sql`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/DataAuthorityViolationHardeningTests.cs`

### Admin order idempotency cleanup now preserves committed order authority
- title: POS/admin order creation no longer raw-deletes order authority after ambiguous transaction commit outcomes
- affected systems: POS/admin manual order creation, SQL order authority, inventory reservations and stock counters, stock sync outbox, storefront/order projections, marketplace backbone order events, coupon/promotion/affiliate usage records
- root cause: The old `OrdersController.CreateOrder` failure cleanup trusted the in-memory `OrderRequestIdempotency.OrderId` and hard-deleted dependent order rows plus the `Orders` row when cleanup ran before `transactionCommitted` was set. A commit acknowledgement failure could therefore erase committed order authority without releasing inventory reservations or retracting outbox/projection work.
- impact: The validated corruption path is closed. Cleanup now reloads the persisted idempotency row by id, verifies the claim token, and deletes only an unbound/uncompleted claim. If the row has a durable `OrderId`, `ResponseStatusCode`, or `CompletedAtUtc`, cleanup preserves it so retries recover the existing order instead of creating a duplicate or deleting committed truth.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated. The raw `DeleteOrderByIdAsync` cleanup path was removed from `OrdersController`, and `OrdersControllerFinancialIntegrityTests` now asserts that a durable order binding survives cleanup.
- residual operational risk: If a request fails before any durable order binding exists, the unbound claim can still be deleted or expire as a stale claim; that is expected because no SQL order authority has been established.
- prevention rule: Idempotency cleanup may delete only the current caller's unbound claim. It must never hard-delete orders, inventory reservations, coupon/promotion/affiliate usage, outbox rows, or other business authority as a substitute for lifecycle compensation.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/OrdersController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/OrdersControllerFinancialIntegrityTests.cs`

### Storefront promotion expiry and repair now preserve expiration authority against stale replay
- title: Expired storefront promotions no longer resurrect through stale activation replay, delayed worker overlap, or stale rebuild payload trust
- affected systems: `PromotionExpirationService`, `PromotionProjectionConsumer`, `PromotionVisibilityProjectionSupport`, `StorefrontPromotionPriceComposer`, `CartService`, `DiscountReadModelRepairService`, coupon/promotion `sync-to-mongo` admin recovery endpoints, MongoDB coupon/promotion/product/category projections, Elasticsearch promotion/search fields, storefront cart/category/search/banner visibility, affiliate pricing projections, CommerceHub pricing projections
- root cause: The earlier hardening closed invalid clean-scope replay, but expiration authority could still be undermined if downstream consumers or rebuild paths trusted stale activation payload arrival order instead of the newer SQL mutation version. Category banners, promotion documents, cached cart snapshots, and rebuilt storefront read models needed to preserve monotonic replay fencing and recompute from SQL truth instead of replaying payload state.
- impact: The validated stale-resurrection path is closed for the audited storefront pricing surfaces. Promotion expiration now advances durable SQL mutation authority, stale activation replay after expiration fails closed, storefront-visible effective price recomposes from canonical SQL base price plus the current active overlay set, category banners and promotion/search documents converge back to expired state, and promo-adjusted numeric pricing does not leak into affiliate or CommerceHub authoritative pricing.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-17. `PromotionExpirationService` continues to stage SQL deactivation before publish and now runs deterministically under test with `TimeProvider`; `PromotionProjectionConsumer` refreshes category/banner state from authoritative SQL instead of trusting event payloads; `DiscountReadModelRepairService` preserves `Promotion.ProjectionMutationVersion` in `PromotionDocument.LastEventVersion`, uses version-aware freshness fencing during rebuild, and recomputes promotion/category decorations from SQL; `CartService` now refreshes persisted cart promotion snapshots from the current storefront projection so expired overlays are cleared during revalidation; and focused PRICE-CON-08/09 integration coverage proves overlap, replay, rebuild, and downstream convergence behavior against real PostgreSQL, MongoDB, and Redis.
- residual operational risk: Storefront product/category/coupon/promotion reads still do not have the same synchronous request-time SQL repair used by critical order detail paths, so temporary read-model lag remains an accepted CQRS tradeoff until consumers catch up or operators run repair. That lag no longer permits a stale activation replay to overtake a newer expired SQL authority snapshot in the covered projection paths.
- prevention rule: Promotion expiration authority must be fenced by durable SQL mutation version, not message arrival order. Replay handlers, rebuild jobs, banner/product/cart/search projection updates, and cache invalidation paths must treat replay payloads as derivative inputs only and recompute storefront overlay visibility from canonical SQL base pricing plus the current active overlay set.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/Background/PromotionExpirationService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/ReadModels/DiscountReadModelRepairService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Cart/CartService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/PromotionsController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/EventBus/MassTransitEventPublisher.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/PromotionProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Promotions/PromotionVisibilityProjectionSupport.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/MongoDB/Documents/ProductDocument.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Integration/ProductVariantPriceDistributedReliabilityIntegrationTests.cs`

### Duplicate promotion projection delivery now reruns recomposition without creating duplicate storefront authority
- title: Duplicate same-event promotion workers no longer strand stale product/category overlays after partial projection loss
- affected systems: `PromotionProjectionConsumer`, `PromotionVisibilityProjectionSupport`, `StorefrontPromotionPriceComposer`, `DiscountReadModelRepairService`, MongoDB promotion/product/category documents, Elasticsearch product search decoration, storefront cart/category/banner visibility, affiliate pricing projections, CommerceHub pricing projections
- root cause: A duplicate promotion projection with the same `EventId` and `ProjectionMutationVersion` could be classified as already fresh after the `PromotionDocument` write succeeded, causing the consumer to return before rerunning product/category/search recomposition. If a prior worker failed after writing the promotion document but before derivative visibility completed, later duplicate delivery could leave product or category overlays stale until an explicit repair job.
- impact: The partial-projection liveness gap is closed for the audited promotion surfaces. Duplicate same-event delivery now converges by rerunning deterministic SQL-backed recomposition while stale lower-version replay remains fenced. Duplicate workers do not create duplicate overlay truth, duplicate category banner state, duplicate search/cache visibility, or promo-adjusted downstream numeric pricing.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-17. `PromotionProjectionConsumer` now treats already-projected same-event/version documents as eligible for recomposition instead of stale early return, including duplicate-key retry paths. PRICE-CON-10 covers duplicate replay after partial overlay loss, active duplicate worker overlap, expiration, stale activation replay after expiration, and duplicate repair/rebuild execution against real PostgreSQL, MongoDB, Redis-backed cart state, and search-indexing hooks.
- residual operational risk: Storefront promotion read models are still asynchronous derivative state. A true infrastructure outage can delay visibility until consumers or repair jobs run, but duplicate delivery no longer preserves a stale derivative split when SQL authority is available.
- prevention rule: Promotion projection idempotency must distinguish stale authority from same-event duplicate delivery. Same-event duplicates may refresh derivative read models from SQL truth; stale lower-version or older-event replay must still fail closed and must never resurrect expired overlays or create global pricing authority.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/PromotionProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Promotions/PromotionVisibilityProjectionSupport.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/ReadModels/DiscountReadModelRepairService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Core/Pricing/StorefrontPromotionPriceComposer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Integration/ProductVariantPriceDistributedReliabilityIntegrationTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/TestAuthController.cs`, `testsprite_tests/testsprite_backend_test_plan.json`

### Inventory transfer tenant co-ownership is now enforced at application and PostgreSQL boundaries
- title: Stock-transfer and stock-row authority can no longer rely only on query filters or caller discipline for warehouse/product/variant tenant co-ownership
- affected systems: POS/admin stock transfers, ProductVariantStocks, StockMovements, StockTransferItems, warehouses, inventory projections, tenant isolation
- root cause: `ProductVariantStock`, `StockMovement`, and `StockTransfer` rows carry `TenantId`, but their original warehouse/product/variant foreign keys were single-column relationships. A missed tenant predicate, query-filter bypass, raw SQL path, or ID confusion could have attached a current-tenant stock authority row to another tenant's warehouse or product variant.
- impact: The likely corruption path is mitigated for the audited inventory transfer/stock-row boundary. Stock rows, stock movements, and stock transfers now require tenant co-ownership before writes can commit, and transfer read/state-transition paths use explicit tenant predicates in addition to query filters.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated. `StockTransfersController` proves tenant ownership for warehouses, variants, transfer reads, listings, completion, and locked state transitions. S76 adds preflight detection for existing mismatches, composite PostgreSQL foreign keys on `(TenantId, WarehouseId/ProductId/ProductVariantId)` where the schema carries `TenantId`, and constraint triggers for soft-delete, variant-product, stock-transfer, and transfer-item co-ownership checks.
- residual operational risk: The S76 migration intentionally fails if existing corrupt inventory rows are found; operators must repair those rows before enabling the database constraints. Future inventory tables or raw SQL mutation paths still need the same tenant co-ownership discipline.
- prevention rule: Inventory mutation inputs must prove tenant co-ownership of every warehouse, product, variant, stock row, movement, transfer, and transfer item at both service and database boundaries. A valid `TenantId` on the mutated row is not sufficient when referenced rows are tenant-owned.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/StockTransfersController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Migrations/20260508120000_S76_InventoryTenantCoOwnershipHardening.sql`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/StockTransfersConcurrencyHardeningTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Inventory/ProductVariantStockMutationSupport.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/TenantSaveChangesInterceptor.cs`

### Manual refund payout confirmation now preserves external-money ambiguity instead of false failure
- title: A manual CommerceHub refund payout is no longer terminally marked `Failed` after the operator confirms customer money was already sent
- affected systems: CommerceHub manual refund execution, customer refund payouts, CommerceHub refund ledger, vendor earning reversal, platform cash, refund readiness and recovery
- root cause: `RefundExecutionService` required `ConfirmMoneySent` and a transfer reference before executing, but its final-attempt exception path could previously persist `CommerceHubRefundStatus.Failed` and `GatewayStatus = "manual-failed"` for a non-completed tracked refund. Failed refunds are excluded from later refundable-amount calculations, so this created duplicate-refund exposure.
- impact: The validated corruption path is closed. Post-confirmation local persistence, ledger, event, or commit failure now keeps the refund as `CommerceHubRefundStatus.ReconciliationRequired` with `manual_transfer_reconciliation_required` gateway state, preserving the transfer reference and blocking duplicate refund authorization.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated. `TryMarkFailedAsync` now records a reconciliation-required refund state instead of terminal failure after confirmed external transfer evidence; provider accepted-local-completion gaps can also be quarantined as reconciliation-required; readiness gates, health checks, controller responses, and the external-reconciliation index recognize the refund reconciliation state.
- residual operational risk: Availability is intentionally sacrificed while a refund remains reconciliation-required. Operators must confirm the refund into local financial truth or provide explicit external non-execution proof before it can be failed.
- prevention rule: Once customer refund money may have left an external rail, local SQL failure is not proof of external non-execution. The refund must remain blocking and reconciled explicitly before more outward money movement or duplicate refund capacity is allowed.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/RefundExecutionService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubRefundService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Core/Enums/CommerceHubRefundStatus.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Migrations/20260507231500_S75_CommerceHubRefundReconciliationRequired.sql`

### Affiliate payout readiness now honors additive affected-store scope and scope-less external-money blockers
- title: Affiliate payouts can no longer proceed when unresolved CommerceHub external-money authority is scope-less or when the target store appears only in affected-store JSON beside a different base tenant
- affected systems: Affiliate settlement orchestrator, affiliate payout execution, CommerceHub payment compensation outbox, unresolved provider cash/refund records, affiliate commission payable accounting
- root cause: Affiliate payout readiness reused tenant-scope logic that treated non-empty base `TenantId` as exclusive and did not perform the CommerceHub global fail-closed scan for scope-less unresolved external-money rows before authorizing affiliate outward money movement. A later scope-helper regression treated an empty affected-store array as global even when a different base `TenantId` already proved the row belonged elsewhere.
- impact: The validated under-blocking path and the later unrelated-tenant over-blocking path are closed. Unresolved captured-payment compensation, captured provider cash, provider refund authority, or ambiguous critical marketplace aggregate events can block affiliate payouts when scope cannot prove the target store is unrelated, while a different base tenant plus an empty affected-store array no longer freezes unrelated stores.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated. `AffiliateSettlementOrchestrator` now performs a global unscoped external-money readiness pass, checks unresolved provider-refund rows, treats affected-store arrays as additive scope rather than allowing base `TenantId` to mask them, treats empty affected-store arrays as global only when no base tenant scope exists, and applies the same ambiguous marketplace aggregate fail-closed rule used by CommerceHub readiness.
- residual operational risk: Availability is intentionally sacrificed if unresolved external-money rows lack durable store scope; operators must repair scope or reconcile the row before affiliate payouts can proceed safely.
- prevention rule: Affiliate outward money movement must use the same conservative external-money and critical-event scope rules as CommerceHub financial gates. A base tenant may prove a row affects that tenant, but it must not mask explicit affected-store or suspense-routed scope; an empty affected-store array must not broaden a different base tenant into a global blocker, and ambiguous marketplace aggregate events must fail closed until explicit affected-tenant scope is available.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Services/Affiliate/AffiliateSettlementOrchestrator.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Affiliate/AffiliateSettlementTests.cs`

### Critical accounting outbox cleanup now preserves unresolved ledger work
- title: Failed critical accounting outbox rows can no longer age out of the live readiness blocker set before ledger posting is repaired
- affected systems: Accounting outbox, immutable ledger posting, POS sales and purchases, purchase returns, supplier payments, supplier opening balances, party ledger adjustments, POS expenses, CommerceHub readiness gates, payout/refund/checkout intake controls, financial health checks
- root cause: `AccountingOutboxProcessor.CleanupAsync` previously deleted failed accounting outbox rows after `FailedRetentionDays` unless the event type appeared in a partial critical-retention set. That left several dispatched money-affecting event types eligible for hard deletion before journal posting.
- impact: The validated deletion path is closed for dispatched financial-truth event types. Failed rows for sales, purchases, purchase returns, supplier payments/opening balances, party adjustments, expenses, refunds, payments, settlements, unresolved-provider-cash recognition, and CommerceHub ledger postings remain visible to readiness gates and health checks until explicitly retried, repaired, or reconciled.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated. Accounting outbox cleanup excludes `AccountingOutboxProcessor.CriticalFinancialTruthEventTypes` from failed-row retention deletion; the critical set now covers every money-affecting `DispatchMessageAsync` case, and regression tests assert that expired failed financial-truth rows remain while explicitly non-financial failed rows may still be cleaned up.
- residual operational risk: Operators still must repair failed critical accounting rows; the mitigation preserves the evidence and blocker but does not post the ledger automatically.
- prevention rule: Never hard-delete failed accounting outbox rows for event types that can post money, cash, liability, supplier/customer debt, expense, refund, purchase, settlement, payout, or adjustment ledger effects. Keep the critical-retention set in lockstep with the accounting dispatch table.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Accounting/AccountingOutboxProcessor.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Accounting/AccountingOutboxProcessorTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### Purchase mutation hardening no longer removes in-flight accounting outbox intent
- title: POS purchase mutation/reversal can no longer delete claimed, retrying, or previously attempted purchase accounting work
- affected systems: POS purchases, purchase reversal/update/delete flows, accounting outbox, immutable ledger posting, purchase financial mutation safety
- root cause: `PurchaseFinancialIntegrityService` locked pending `PosPurchase` and `PurchaseReversal` outbox rows, but only rejected rows marked `IsFailed`. Unlike the sale hardening path, it could still remove an unprocessed row that had been claimed by the accounting worker, had a retry scheduled, had a nonzero attempt count, or carried a prior error.
- impact: The validated deletion path is closed. Purchase mutation hardening now treats claimed, retrying, attempted, or error-bearing accounting rows as live ledger intent and blocks mutation until posting is processed or explicitly reconciled.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated. `PurchaseFinancialIntegrityService` now mirrors the sale-side guard for `ClaimedUntilUtc`, `AttemptCount`, `NextRetryAtUtc`, `LastError`, and `IsFailed` before removing only unclaimed, unattempted pending purchase outbox rows.
- residual operational risk: Availability is intentionally sacrificed while purchase accounting work is in flight or retrying. Operators must wait for posting, retry repair, or reconcile the outbox row instead of mutating the purchase underneath it.
- prevention rule: POS purchase and sale mutation hardening may delete only unclaimed, unattempted, unfailed pending accounting intent. Once a ledger outbox row has been claimed, attempted, failed, or scheduled for retry, it is authoritative recovery evidence and must remain a blocker.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/PurchaseFinancialIntegrityService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/SaleFinancialIntegrityService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/GlobalFinancialIntegrityHardeningTests.cs`

### Affiliate payout external evidence is now first-class allocation authority
- title: Affiliate payout execution can no longer recognize local withdrawals against reused or under-specified external transfer proof
- affected systems: Affiliate settlement orchestrator, affiliate payout requests, affiliate balance ledger, affiliate commission ledger, tenant cashbox/accounting, affiliate payout reconciliation
- root cause: The prior payout path required a `paymentReference`, but did not model the external transfer as a durable allocation authority with amount, rail namespace, currency, and consumed total.
- impact: The validated evidence-reuse path is closed for new payouts. One affiliate payout transfer reference is now unique platform-wide per rail namespace, can fund only allocations that fit within the recorded transfer amount/currency, and cannot be independently claimed by multiple store tenants.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated and re-hardened on 2026-05-11. `AffiliatePayoutTransferEvidence` remains platform-global per rail/reference in both migration snapshot and EF runtime configuration, `AffiliatePayoutTransferAllocation` idempotency is global per payout reference, `AffiliateSettlementOrchestrator` reuses the global evidence row before creating any withdrawal, and `20260510190000_RestoreGlobalAffiliatePayoutEvidenceAuthority` preflights duplicate evidence/allocation rows before recreating the stronger unique indexes.
- residual operational risk: Pre-S74 legacy withdrawal descriptions remain historical audit inputs until explicitly migrated. If rows were already duplicated during the tenant-scoped drift window, the restore migration now fails closed with explicit reconciliation guidance instead of silently choosing a winner.
- prevention rule: Affiliate outward money movement must allocate first-class transfer evidence before posting local withdrawal truth, and that evidence must stay globally unique per external rail/reference in schema, EF configuration, and orchestrator lookup instead of becoming tenant-scoped duplicate proof.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Services/Affiliate/AffiliateSettlementOrchestrator.cs`, `PointOfSale.Core/Entities/Affiliate/AffiliatePayoutTransferEvidence.cs`, `PointOfSale.Core/Entities/Affiliate/AffiliatePayoutTransferAllocation.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Migrations/20260507224500_S74_AffiliatePayoutEvidenceAndOutboxArchive.sql`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Migrations/20260510190000_RestoreGlobalAffiliatePayoutEvidenceAuthority.cs`

### Failed marketplace outbox cleanup now preserves unresolved recovery state
- title: Failed unpublished marketplace outbox rows are archived for audit and retained as live readiness blockers
- affected systems: CommerceHub marketplace event outbox, marketplace projection/backbone consumers, readiness gates, projection compensation, order/payment/refund/product read-side synchronization
- root cause: Cleanup previously hard-deleted old failed marketplace outbox rows even when publication or compensation remained unresolved.
- impact: The validated unrecoverable-deletion path is closed. Unpublished failed rows are not deleted by retention cleanup, original payloads are copied into a dead-letter archive, and the live outbox row continues to block readiness until repaired, retried, published, or compensated.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated. `MarketplaceEventBackboneCleanupWorker` archives unresolved failed rows into `MarketplaceEventOutboxDeadLetterArchives` and only deletes published/compensated rows through the published retention path. S74 adds the durable archive table and uniqueness by original outbox id.
- residual operational risk: Operators still must repair or compensate failed live rows; the archive preserves evidence but does not by itself resolve the backbone failure.
- prevention rule: Never hard-delete unpublished failed marketplace outbox rows. Retention may delete only rows that have reached a published or compensated terminal state, with payload evidence preserved.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Eventing/MarketplaceEventBackboneCleanupWorker.cs`, `PointOfSale.Core/Entities/MarketplaceEventOutboxDeadLetterArchive.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Migrations/20260507224500_S74_AffiliatePayoutEvidenceAndOutboxArchive.sql`

### Affiliate payout readiness backlog overflow no longer blocks by window size alone
- title: Affiliate payout readiness now streams tenant-prefiltered critical backlogs instead of throwing fixed-window scope-undetermined errors
- affected systems: Affiliate payout execution, affiliate settlement orchestrator, accounting outbox, MassTransit outbox, marketplace outbox, payment compensation, unresolved provider cash checks
- root cause: Affiliate payout readiness used a fixed `ReadinessInspectionLimit` window and could fail closed on backlog size before tenant relevance was proven.
- impact: The availability freeze from unrelated backlog overflow is mitigated while preserving integrity. Payouts still block when a tenant-relevant critical row is failed or stale, but large scans now produce operator warnings rather than scope-undetermined exceptions.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated. `AffiliateSettlementOrchestrator` now prefilters unresolved backlogs by tenant evidence, streams the candidate set, and logs large inspections through `LogLargeAffiliateReadinessInspection`.
- residual operational risk: Very large tenant-relevant candidate sets can be expensive; if this becomes operationally significant, materialize exact affected-tenant scope rather than weakening the readiness gate.
- prevention rule: Do not reintroduce fixed-window overflow as an affiliate payout control-plane blocker. Preserve fail-closed behavior only for tenant-relevant unresolved authority rows.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Services/Affiliate/AffiliateSettlementOrchestrator.cs`, `PointOfSale.Core/Events/AffectedTenantScopeResolver.cs`

### CommerceHub payout evidence is now globally unique across vendors
- title: Completed or live payout transfer evidence can no longer be reused across different vendor tenants
- affected systems: CommerceHub payouts, Super Admin vendor payout execution, vendor settlement controls, CommerceHub financial ledger, platform cash reconciliation
- root cause: External payout evidence uniqueness was tenant-scoped, so the same `ExternalReferenceId` or `BankTransactionId` could be attached to payouts for different vendors even though the platform bank/provider transfer proof represents a platform-wide outward money event.
- impact: The validated cross-vendor evidence-reuse path is closed. A single bank/provider transfer proof can no longer support two local payout completions that debit platform cash and vendor payable twice.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated. Payout services now check evidence reuse globally across non-failed payouts, and the S73 migration replaces tenant-scoped live evidence indexes with global unique filtered indexes after a duplicate preflight.
- residual operational risk: Existing databases with duplicate live payout evidence must reconcile those records before the S73 migration can apply; the migration intentionally fails rather than choosing a local winner.
- prevention rule: Payout transfer evidence belongs to the platform cash rail, not a single vendor tenant. Keep `ExternalReferenceId` and `BankTransactionId` globally unique for all non-failed CommerceHub payouts unless payout rails become provably tenant-owned bank accounts with separate evidence namespaces.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPayoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/SuperAdmin/Services/SuperAdminPayoutsService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/ApplicationDbContext.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Migrations/20260507223000_S73_GlobalPayoutEvidenceUniqueness.sql`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/SuperAdminPayoutsServiceTests.cs`

### Super Admin payout execution surface now matches backend payout authority
- title: Super Admin payout queue and completed-payout recognition now surface the same reconciliation blockers and immutable evidence requirements enforced by backend authority
- affected systems: Super Admin payout queue UI, `CommerceHubVendorPayoutReadService`, `CommerceHubFinancialReadinessGate`, `SuperAdminPayoutsService`, Super Admin payout API contract, vendor payout reconciliation operations
- root cause: The prior queue only reflected payable math and omitted the hard readiness blockers created by `ReconciliationRequired` or stale `Processing` payouts, while the UI modal collected only `transferReference` even though backend completion authority requires immutable external evidence through `ExternalReferenceId` or `BankTransactionId`.
- impact: The confirmed frontend/backend authority mismatch is closed. The queue now carries backend-derived execution authority, disables completed-payout recognition when payout reconciliation is unresolved, and explains the block reason directly in the operator surface. The modal now requires immutable transfer evidence before POS can recognize externally completed payout truth.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-11. `CommerceHubVendorPayoutReadService` now surfaces reconciliation/stale-processing blocker flags, `SuperAdminPayoutsService` derives `CanRecordCompletedPayout` plus explicit block codes and reasons, and the Super Admin frontend consumes that contract while transmitting `ExternalReferenceId` and `BankTransactionId` to the backend authority path.
- residual operational risk: Availability is intentionally sacrificed while a prior payout remains `ReconciliationRequired` or stale `Processing`. Operators will now see an explicit execution block instead of a false-ready action, but reconciliation still must clear or prove non-execution before a new payout can be recognized safely.
- prevention rule: Any read model or UI that advertises payout executability must consume the same reconciliation and stale-processing blockers as the final readiness gate. Completed-payout recognition surfaces must require and transmit immutable execution evidence (`ExternalReferenceId` or `BankTransactionId`) and must describe the action as recording already executed external transfer truth rather than initiating the payout.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/Finance/CommerceHubVendorPayoutReadService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/Finance/CommerceHubFinancialReadinessGate.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/SuperAdmin/DTOs/SuperAdminPayoutDtos.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/SuperAdmin/Services/SuperAdminPayoutsService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Web/src/super-admin/payouts/payoutsService.ts`, `PointOfSale.Web/src/super-admin/payouts/PayoutsPage.tsx`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/CommerceHubVendorPayoutReadServiceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/SuperAdminPayoutsServiceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### Super Admin payout queue no longer hides vendor authority behind first-page truncation
- title: Super Admin payout queue now consumes pagination metadata and backend-computed global totals instead of treating the first page as the full payout authority surface
- affected systems: Super Admin payout queue UI, `SuperAdminPayoutsController`, `SuperAdminPayoutsService`, vendor payout reconciliation visibility, operator payout planning
- root cause: The queue API already paged results (`page=1&pageSize=20` by default), but the frontend requested `/payouts` without pagination state, ignored `meta.totalCount` and `meta.totalPages`, and derived "Eligible Batch" / "Held Back" summary cards from the rendered array. Once the queue exceeded twenty vendors, later vendors and their blockers disappeared from the only payout execution board while the summary cards silently became first-page-only totals.
- impact: The confirmed operator-authority visibility gap is closed. The payout board now consumes pagination metadata, exposes explicit page navigation and loaded-range counts, and renders queue totals from backend-computed full-queue aggregates so the first page cannot masquerade as the entire payout backlog.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated in code on 2026-05-11. `SuperAdminPayoutsService` now returns authoritative full-queue payable/eligible/held totals alongside paged items, `SuperAdminPayoutsController` publishes those totals in the queue contract, and the Super Admin frontend consumes `page` / `pageSize` / `totalCount` / `totalPages` rather than assuming an unpaged queue.
- residual operational risk: The queue remains eventually consistent with the underlying payout read model, so operators may still see a slightly stale snapshot between projection refreshes. That lag is accepted because the execute mutation still rechecks final readiness inside the serializable backend authority boundary.
- prevention rule: Any operator surface that can authorize, prioritize, or defer vendor payouts must either load the full queue intentionally or consume backend pagination metadata plus backend-computed global totals. Never derive full-queue liability summaries from a paged item slice.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/SuperAdmin/DTOs/SuperAdminPayoutDtos.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/SuperAdmin/Services/SuperAdminPayoutsService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/SuperAdmin/Controllers/SuperAdminPayoutsController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Web/src/super-admin/payouts/payoutsService.ts`, `PointOfSale.Web/src/super-admin/payouts/PayoutsPage.tsx`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/SuperAdminPayoutsServiceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### Payment-compensation tenant scope gaps now fail closed
- title: CommerceHub readiness gating now blocks captured-payment compensation when tenant scope is missing or when a non-primary affected store is present only in the affected-store array
- affected systems: CommerceHub payment compensation outbox, refunds/compensation recovery, payout readiness, checkout/payment intake, vendor settlement controls
- root cause: Payment-compensation readiness checks relied on tenant prefiltering and a helper that treated non-empty base `TenantId` as exclusive, so malformed scope-less compensation rows could avoid all tenant gates and base-tenant rows with broader `AffectedStoreIdsJson` could under-block non-primary stores. A later regression also treated an empty affected-store array as global tenant scope even when a different base `TenantId` was present.
- impact: The validated under-scoping path is closed, and the over-blocking regression is closed. Unprocessed captured-payment compensation without durable affected-store scope now fails closed globally, affected-store arrays are evaluated as additional tenant authority rather than being masked by base `TenantId`, and a base-tenant row with an empty affected-store array blocks only the base tenant.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated. `CommerceHubFinancialReadinessGate` now performs a global scan for scope-less payment-compensation rows before tenant-specific checks, prefilters tenant checks by base tenant or explicit affected-store match, and `CompensationMayAffectTenant` treats empty affected-store arrays as global only when the base tenant is also missing.
- residual operational risk: Availability is intentionally sacrificed if a compensation row lacks durable tenant scope; operators must repair scope or reconcile the compensation before CommerceHub money movement resumes.
- prevention rule: Captured-payment compensation rows must always carry complete affected-store scope. Readiness gates must fail closed when compensation scope is missing or malformed, but an empty affected-store array must not convert an otherwise scoped base-tenant row into a platform-wide blocker.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/Finance/CommerceHubFinancialReadinessGate.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/CommerceHubFinanceServiceTests.cs`

### Scope-less unresolved external-money rows now fail closed globally
- title: CommerceHub readiness gating now blocks tenant money movement even when unresolved captured-payment or refund truth exists without durable tenant scope
- affected systems: CommerceHub refunds, payouts, vendor settlement controls, checkout/payment intake, and any operation gated by `CommerceHubFinancialReadinessGate`
- root cause: Unresolved provider-money rows can be durably stored before `TenantId`, `AffectedStoreIdsJson`, or `SuspenseRoutedStoreIdsJson` are known, so relying only on tenant-prefiltered scans was insufficient.
- impact: The previously validated integrity gap is closed. Scope-less unresolved captured-payment or refund rows can no longer bypass financial safety gates simply because ownership has not been resolved yet.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated. `CommerceHubFinancialReadinessGate` now performs a separate global fail-closed scan for unresolved captured-payment and provider-refund rows that still have no durable tenant scope before allowing commerce intake or outward money movement.
- residual operational risk: Availability is intentionally sacrificed for integrity. A single scope-less unresolved external-money row now blocks CommerceHub intake and tenant money movement globally until reconciliation materializes scope or clears the ambiguity.
- prevention rule: Preserve the global scope-less unresolved-money blocker unless unresolved external-money rows become atomically scope-complete before they are eligible to influence readiness decisions.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/Finance/CommerceHubFinancialReadinessGate.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/CommerceHubFinanceServiceTests.cs`

### Durable provider-session re-init fence prevents duplicate external session authority
- title: CommerceHub and Storefront payment initiation persist durable retry blockers before crossing reusable provider boundaries
- affected systems: CommerceHub online payment initiation, Storefront/POS Paymob creation and recovery, Stripe/Paymob provider sessions, provider webhook reconciliation, inventory reservation cleanup
- root cause: Provider acceptance used to rely on a best-effort post-acceptance ambiguity marker if session persistence later failed, leaving a narrow path where retries could create parallel external session authority. Paymob also accepts a reusable provider order before payment-key creation, so a failure after order acceptance but before payment-key materialization must not be treated as an ordinary local initiation failure.
- impact: The validated duplicate-session corruption path is closed on both hardened payment surfaces. Retries encounter persisted reconciliation state before a second external session can be created. Storefront Paymob now checkpoints registration-started, accepted provider-order, and accepted payment-key state on the existing SQL `PaymentIntent`; accepted-order recovery creates the payment key against that same order and accepted-key recovery performs no provider call.
- mitigation status: MITIGATED. CommerceHub `InitiateAsync` saves `PROVIDER_SESSION_REINIT_FENCE` on the payment and active attempt before provider execution. Storefront Paymob uses `PaymobProviderCheckpointStore` before order registration, between provider order acceptance and payment-key creation, and immediately after payment-key acceptance. Incomplete Storefront intents resume the same local/provider authority; the former replacement rehydration path is removed. An indeterminate registration-started state blocks automatic provider-order recreation and remains pending for reconciliation.
- residual operational risk: If provider acceptance occurs and durable provider identity still cannot be recovered, the fence intentionally leaves the payment blocked in reconciliation-required state until webhook resolution, provider-side lookup, or operator intervention resolves the ambiguity.
- prevention rule: Never cross an external provider authority boundary without first persisting a durable re-init blocker that survives later session-persistence failure. After a provider returns any reusable external identity, persist that identity and classify later provider-session materialization failure as unknown/recoverable rather than terminal failed.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/Paymob/PaymobProviderCheckpointStore.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/Paymob/PaymobPaymentAdapter.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/Paymob/PaymobPaymentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Jobs/InventoryReservationCleanupJob.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentServiceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/PaymobCertificationClosure2Tests.cs`

### Paymob tenant configuration and adapter credential authority
- title: Only current validated tenant Paymob configuration can authorize application admission or provider-adapter execution
- affected systems: Storefront/POS Paymob create, retry, recovery, webhook, callback, payment adapter, and dormant advanced-operation adapter
- root cause: Webhook and callback paths had a private unfiltered credential lookup that omitted the soft-delete predicate and bypassed provider, capability, and environment gates; create held a separate copy of similar validation. After admission, payment and advanced-operation adapters still constructed provider requests from injected global `PaymobOptions`, forcing tenant/global equality and leaving global credentials as execution authority.
- impact: PAYMOB-FINAL-003 and PAYMOB-FINAL-004 are closed. A current enabled non-deleted encrypted tenant `PAYMOB` row must pass credential, operation-capability, and Sandbox/Production validation on every admitted operation. The application boundary supplies that exact validated credential set through a scoped one-shot handoff; the adapter checks canonical tenant identity, consumes the handoff before provider I/O, and cannot resolve, fall back to, cache, or reuse credentials.
- mitigation status: MITIGATED. `PaymobTenantConfigurationResolver` is the shared application credential authority and performs a fresh no-tracking SQL read. `PaymobRuntimeApplicationService` begins `PaymobAdapterCredentialHandoff` only after successful resolution. `PaymobPaymentAdapter` and `PaymobAdvancedOperationsAdapter` no longer depend on `PaymobOptions`, `IOptions`, `IConfiguration`, or tenant persistence, and raw global `PaymobOptions` is no longer registered as an adapter dependency.
- residual operational risk: PAYMOB-FINAL-005 direct CommerceHub provider HTTP remains a separate closure. Advanced operations remain dormant without a live application caller, and this closure does not activate them.
- prevention rule: No Paymob application or adapter execution path may decrypt, select, infer, cache, or fall back to credentials independently. Add each new operation to `PaymobOperationalCapability`, require `IPaymobTenantConfigurationResolver`, and use a fresh single-use `IPaymobAdapterCredentialHandoff` before Runtime, HMAC, provider, retry, or recovery work.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/Paymob/PaymobTenantConfigurationResolver.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/Paymob/PaymobAdapterCredentialHandoff.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/Paymob/PaymobRuntimeApplicationService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/Paymob/PaymobPaymentAdapter.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/Paymob/PaymobAdvancedOperationsAdapter.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/PaymobCertificationClosure3Tests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/PaymobCertificationClosure4Tests.cs`, `docs/architecture/PAYMOB_CERTIFICATION_CLOSURE_3.md`, `docs/architecture/PAYMOB_CERTIFICATION_CLOSURE_4.md`

### Paymob durable sensitive artifacts are protected at rest
- title: Paymob reusable payment sessions and webhook evidence no longer persist in plaintext
- affected systems: Storefront/POS Paymob initiation, checkpoint recovery, checkout replay, webhook audit, unresolved webhook reconciliation, and legacy Paymob durable records
- root cause: Paymob payment keys and token-bearing iframe URLs were stored directly in payment routing fields, while raw webhook payloads and HMAC signatures were stored directly in webhook audit rows. Checkout replay and payment-created event snapshots could also retain the iframe URL.
- impact: PAYMOB-FINAL-006 is closed. Reusable payment material is now held in a purpose-isolated Data Protection envelope, webhook bodies are encrypted, raw HMAC signatures are replaced by SHA-256 digests, and durable event/replay projections omit Paymob session material.
- mitigation status: MITIGATED. All new Paymob writes use `PaymobSensitiveArtifactProtector`; checkpoint, checkout, and unresolved-webhook recovery decrypt only in memory; a startup backfill protects legacy rows and fails startup when legacy material cannot be classified without losing recovery evidence.
- residual operational risk: Recovery requires access to the Data Protection key ring used to create the envelopes. Missing, invalid, or corrupt envelopes fail closed and require key restoration or manual reconciliation; the application does not fall back to plaintext or create a second provider session.
- prevention rule: Never persist Paymob payment keys, token-bearing iframe URLs, raw HMACs, provider authorization values, or reusable authentication material. Preserve required evidence only as encrypted envelopes, digests, redacted values, or canonical non-secret projections, and keep all recovery paths protection-aware.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/Paymob/PaymobSensitiveArtifactProtector.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/Paymob/PaymobSensitiveArtifactBackfillHostedService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/Paymob/PaymobProviderCheckpointStore.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/Paymob/PaymobPaymentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/StorefrontUnresolvedPaymentReconciliationJob.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/PaymobCertificationClosure5Tests.cs`, `docs/architecture/PAYMOB_CERTIFICATION_CLOSURE_5.md`

### External outcome ambiguity after money or carrier handoff
- title: External provider truth can temporarily diverge from local SQL truth, but ambiguity is now quarantined, recoverable, and surfaced as readiness state
- affected systems: CommerceHub refunds, CommerceHub payouts, carrier shipment registration and cancellation, unresolved payment and refund webhook recovery
- root cause: Gateway, bank, and carrier side effects still occur outside the PostgreSQL transaction boundary, so external execution certainty cannot be made atomic with the local commit.
- impact: Refunds, payouts, or shipments can remain in `Processing` or `ReconciliationRequired`, but unsafe follow-up actions are blocked and the ambiguity is explicitly observable.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated. Recovery hosted services, unresolved-webhook records, non-execution evidence checks, payout quarantine, and `CommerceHubExternalOutcomeRecoveryHealthCheck` surface and contain the uncertainty instead of allowing silent divergence.
- mitigation details: Storefront unresolved captures now route to suspense accounting, CommerceHub provider-success suspense rows now clear into local payment truth when a match appears, and reconciliation-required manual refunds now recover from persisted execution evidence without rewriting immutable refund history.
- prevention rule: Never promote an uncertain external side effect directly to a terminal local state. Require durable execution evidence, durable non-execution evidence, or an explicit reconciliation path with readiness monitoring.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubRefundService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPayoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPayoutReconciliationHostedService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Shipping/ShipmentAuthorityRecoveryHostedService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/HealthChecks/ProductionHealthChecks.cs`

### Tenant-scoped backlog overflow availability freeze
- title: CommerceHub readiness gating previously blocked tenants when inspection windows overflowed before tenant scope could be proven
- affected systems: CommerceHub checkout intake, refunds, payouts, vendor settlement flows, unresolved compensation and webhook control plane, authoritative and marketplace outbox readiness
- root cause: The old readiness gate stopped after a fixed 500-row window per backlog category and failed closed when tenant scope remained unproven inside that window.
- impact: A tenant could be blocked by backlog size alone even when no inspected row actually affected that tenant.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated. The readiness gate now streams the full tenant-prefiltered SQL candidate set, blocks only on rows that actually affect the tenant, and emits large-scan warnings for operator visibility instead of throwing overflow-specific scope-undetermined errors.
- prevention rule: Do not reintroduce fixed-window overflow as a control-plane blocker for tenant-scoped financial readiness. If scans become operationally expensive, add exact scope materialization without sacrificing deterministic tenant proof.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/Finance/CommerceHubFinancialReadinessGate.cs`, `PointOfSale.Core/Events/AffectedTenantScopeResolver.cs`

### Provider session before durable local payment authority
- title: External payment intent creation could have succeeded before local authority commit
- affected systems: Storefront online payment initiation, Stripe, Paymob, local `PaymentIntent` authority
- root cause: The old initiation order allowed provider-side session creation before the local `PaymentIntent` row and related authority state were durably committed.
- impact: Gateway truth could exist without a matching authoritative POS record, forcing later webhook recovery to reconstruct a missing primary state transition.
- mitigation status: Closed in the current codebase. The local `PaymentIntent` is saved and committed before provider initiation, and incomplete intents resume through a follow-up persistence path.
- prevention rule: External payment-session creation must only occur after the local authority row, idempotency state, and minimum routing metadata are durably committed.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/PaymentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/Stripe/StripePaymentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Payments/Paymob/PaymobPaymentService.cs`, `docs/architecture/cross-system-critical-risk-handoff-2026-05-07.md`

### Critical storefront order read divergence after projection loss
- title: Customers could previously miss valid orders because Mongo projection delivery failed
- affected systems: Storefront account order history, order detail, guest tracking, order-by-number lookups
- root cause: Customer order reads are Mongo-based, so a lost or delayed projection can hide an order even when SQL, payment, and accounting state are already correct. A later controller regression reintroduced projection-first `404` behavior on storefront order detail and tracking by reading Mongo before invoking the SQL-backed repair path.
- impact: Customer-facing "order not found" or stale-order incidents despite correct SQL authority.
