- mitigation status: Re-closed on 2026-05-10 for critical storefront order surfaces. Storefront order detail, tracking, guest tracking, and order-by-number paths now resolve minimal SQL authority first, trigger SQL-backed repair before trusting Mongo, and return `503` instead of a false `404` if SQL proves the order exists but the projection still cannot be served. `StorefrontAccountController` account detail follows the same fail-closed rule, and a background reconciler repairs stale or missing rows.
- prevention rule: Critical customer order reads must prove existence and ownership from SQL authority or execute authoritative repair before returning not-found from a read model. If SQL authority exists but projection repair still cannot serve the document, fail closed as temporary unavailability instead of claiming the order does not exist.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/Storefront/StorefrontOrderProjectionRepairService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/StorefrontAccountController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/StorefrontOrdersReadController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Storefront/StorefrontOrderProjectionReconciliationService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemCriticalRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### Cross-tenant spoofing and mismatched tenant writes
- title: Client-supplied tenant identity and developer write mistakes could corrupt tenant isolation
- affected systems: API tenancy resolution, tenant-owned SQL entities, admin and storefront request pipeline
- root cause: Raw tenant headers and incorrect `TenantId` assignment are high-risk inputs in a multi-tenant system if they are trusted or persisted directly.
- impact: Cross-tenant data access, cross-tenant writes, and permanent data corruption.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated. The middleware strips inbound `X-Tenant-Id`, resolves tenant from trusted sources only, rejects spoofing, and the EF interceptor blocks empty or mismatched `TenantId` writes.
- prevention rule: Resolve tenant identity only from authenticated claims or verified host metadata, and never persist tenant-owned entities when tenant context is missing or mismatched.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Middleware/TenantResolutionMiddleware.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/TenantSaveChangesInterceptor.cs`, `.ai-context/PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`

### Direct lifecycle mutation bypass
- title: Direct status writes could bypass ledger, inventory, and state-machine side effects
- affected systems: Order status, payment status, fulfillment status, admin and background mutation paths
- root cause: Direct entity mutation outside lifecycle services can skip journal creation, reservation release, and transition validation.
- impact: Unjournaled payment recognition, invalid fulfillment transitions, or inconsistent order lifecycle state.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated. Payment and fulfillment interceptors block unauthorized writes, order-status enforcement is enabled at startup, obsolete direct wallet-approval paths are disabled, controllers are steered toward lifecycle services, and CommerceHub refund completion/recovery now keeps the payment lifecycle authority open through the unit-of-work persistence that saves refund-induced payment-status changes.
- prevention rule: All payment, fulfillment, and protected order-status mutations must go through `IPaymentLifecycleService`, `IOrderFulfillmentService`, or an explicitly authorized lifecycle boundary that remains active through `SaveChangesAsync`.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/PaymentStatusSaveChangesInterceptor.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/FulfillmentStatusSaveChangesInterceptor.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/OrderStatusSaveChangesInterceptor.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/CommerceHubBoundaryMutationScope.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubRefundService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/RefundExecutionService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Program.cs`

### Historical marketplace event semantics lost during compensation
- title: Projection compensation could previously replay current SQL state as if it were the original event
- affected systems: CommerceHub marketplace projection recovery, downstream event consumers
- root cause: Terminal compensation could rebuild failed marketplace events from present-day SQL state instead of replaying the original serialized payload.
- impact: Downstream systems could observe a repaired event identity carrying newer semantics than the original transition, blurring historical causality.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated. Compensation now prefers original serialized payload replay and uses current-SQL rebuild only as a fallback when the original payload is unusable.
- prevention rule: Replay original authoritative payload whenever possible; when a fresh SQL snapshot is required, emit it as explicit compensation rather than pretending it is the original historical transition.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Eventing/ProjectionFailureCompensation.cs`, `docs/architecture/cross-system-critical-risk-handoff-2026-05-07.md`

### CommerceHub checkout soft-lease expiry duplicate authority window
- title: Long-running CommerceHub checkout can outlive the soft lock lease without creating duplicate marketplace-order authority
- affected systems: CommerceHub checkout idempotency, cart locking, marketplace order creation, inventory reservation workflow
- root cause: The checkout distributed lock, cart lock, and pending idempotency claim all use fixed TTL-based soft leases, so a later retry can reacquire the cart after lease expiry while an older worker is still running.
- impact: Without a second fence this would permit two workers to try to authoritatively create the same marketplace order from one cart snapshot.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated. The cart row carries an optimistic `Version` concurrency token and the locked cart is deleted inside the same SQL transaction as order creation, so a stale long-running worker should lose commit and roll back its authoritative reservation/order writes instead of persisting a second order.
- prevention rule: Treat Redis/idempotency soft leases as liveness controls only. Keep a SQL-backed ownership fence on the cart or checkout aggregate so lease expiry cannot by itself authorize a second authoritative commit.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubCheckoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/ApplicationDbContext.cs`, `PointOfSale.Core/Entities/CommerceHubCart.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Redis/Services/DistributedLockService.cs`

### Direct vendor financial transactions are now included in reconciliation coverage
- title: CommerceHub readiness no longer blocks vendors whose latest reconciliation report omitted ledger-only vendor activity from checked scope
- affected systems: CommerceHub financial reconciliation, readiness gates, checkout intake, refunds, payouts, vendor settlement controls, vendor adjustment/settlement ledger flows
- root cause: Reconciliation report `checkedVendorIds` was derived from suborders, completed refunds, payments, and payout rows. A vendor with direct immutable financial transactions, such as ledger-only adjustments or settlement/debt activity, could be absent from the latest report scope even though the report had seen financial authority for that vendor.
- impact: The integrity model was already fail-closed, so this did not create silent money corruption. The over-blocking path is now mitigated: readiness gates can accept a fresh healthy reconciliation report when vendor coverage is proven by direct vendor ledger transactions.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated. `FinancialReconciliationService` now materializes direct vendor ids from `CommerceHubFinancialTransactions` at the snapshot boundary and includes them in `checkedVendorIds`; regression coverage asserts ledger-only vendor activity is present in the report scope.
- residual operational risk: Outward money movement still intentionally blocks if no fresh healthy report exists, if the report is stale, or if a critical mismatch affects the vendor.
- prevention rule: Reconciliation coverage must be based on every authoritative financial source that can prove vendor involvement, not only order, refund, payment, or payout workflow rows.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/FinancialReconciliationService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/FinancialReconciliationServiceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/Finance/CommerceHubFinancialReadinessGate.cs`

### Payment refund/settlement replay could leave derivative status stale or replay stale storefront payment visibility
- title: Payment lifecycle fanout and storefront payment facet replay must converge to current SQL refund and settlement truth
- affected systems: `PaymentLifecycleService`, refund and COD/gateway settlement fanout, storefront order payment facet, storefront order repair/rebuild, affiliate payment-status listeners, stale callback/queue/recovery visibility paths
- root cause: Refund and settlement lifecycle paths already guarded SQL status transitions with refund-id and settlement-id replay collapse, but they did not publish canonical `OrderPaymentStatusChangedEvent` when those accepted SQL transitions changed `Order.PaymentStatus`. The storefront payment facet also consumed event `NewStatus` directly instead of reloading current SQL order payment lineage.
- impact: PAYMENT-CON-01 closes the inspected runtime gap. Accepted refunds and settlements now stage canonical payment-status fanout in the lifecycle transaction with explicit internal refund/settlement source lineage, duplicate refund/settlement replay cannot stage a second visibility transition, and storefront payment facet replay reloads current SQL payment status through `OrderPaymentLineageAuthority` before mutation. PASS 4 re-certification on 2026-05-27 closes the residual missing-authority retention case: when no current non-deleted SQL order authority exists, payment replay deletes the tenant-scoped Mongo order projection and invalidates its cache; when replayed status conflicts with SQL truth, it does not append stale payload narrative.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-22 and extended during PASS 4 on 2026-05-27 with fail-closed storefront payment projection deletion and timeline-suppression regression coverage. Earlier focused service/projection build and in-memory payment-lineage tests succeeded; lifecycle integration tests were added but their shared SQLite fixture currently fails at schema `EnsureCreatedAsync` before payment test bodies run with `near "' \t\n\r'": syntax error`.
- residual operational risk: CommerceHub payment documents, affiliate/accounting/reporting exports, support/admin caches, notification retry workers, provider settlement/reconciliation exports, and new callback adapters were not migrated by this focused pass. They must preserve terminal SQL payment truth, refund/settlement idempotency, callback suspense/reconciliation behavior, and SQL-backed visibility recomposition before projecting status from provider or queue payloads.
- prevention rule: Payment callback timing, webhook retries, settlement/reconciliation retries, refund retries, stale queue delivery, cache hydration, Mongo documents, exports, and rebuild payloads are never payment status authority. Mutate through the SQL payment lifecycle owner, stage canonical payment visibility from accepted SQL status transitions, and recompose current SQL order payment lineage before derivative visibility can change.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Payments/PaymentLifecycleService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/OrderPaymentLineageAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/OrderProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Integration/EcommerceRefundLifecycleTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/OrderPaymentLineageAuthorityTests.cs`

## ACCEPTED_TRADEOFF

### Stock projection timestamp precision across SQL and MongoDB
- title: Sub-millisecond SQL stock timestamps can outpace MongoDB precision and stall downstream readiness checks
- affected systems: Stock sync outbox, storefront product stock projection, product search indexing, affiliate public product visibility, CommerceHub listing stock visibility, stock projection compensation
- root cause: PostgreSQL/.NET timestamps can carry sub-millisecond ticks, while MongoDB persists `DateTime` at millisecond precision. Freshness guards that compared the raw event timestamp to Mongo's truncated read-model timestamp could treat an already-applied stock projection as permanently not ready.
- impact: SQL inventory authority stayed correct, but derived stock availability could fail closed and retry indefinitely, leaving storefront/search/affiliate/CommerceHub visibility stale after a valid stock mutation.
- mitigation status: Actively mitigated. Stock projection timestamps are normalized to UTC millisecond precision at stock-event source and compensation paths, monotonic advancement uses millisecond steps, and downstream readiness/staleness comparisons normalize both stored and incoming timestamps.
- prevention rule: Any new stock projection freshness marker must use shared `StockProjectionTimestamp` normalization before persisting or comparing timestamps across SQL and Mongo/read-model boundaries.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Utilities/StockProjectionTimestamp.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Inventory/ProductVariantStockMutationSupport.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/InventoryConsistencyService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/Repositories/ProductVariantStockRepository.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductStockChangedConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductVariantProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/CommerceHubProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/Affiliate/AffiliateProductPolicyProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductStockPriceSearchIndexConsumer.cs`

### Concurrent last-unit stock deduction
- title: Concurrent sales against the same last physical stock unit must fail closed with one authoritative deduction
- affected systems: POS sales, `ProductVariantStocks`, stock movements, accounting-linked COGS snapshots, stock sync outbox, storefront availability, affiliate public products, CommerceHub listings, replay/rebuild paths
- root cause: The critical corruption risk is overlapping stock mutation or same-request replay against one physical unit, where stale reads, retry timing, or non-authoritative projections could otherwise permit double deduction, negative drift, duplicate stock movements, stock resurrection, reservation drift, or stale external availability.
- impact: If violated, the system could oversell, create phantom stock, corrupt fulfillment, diverge accounting valuation from physical inventory, or publish stale marketplace/affiliate availability.
- mitigation status: Actively covered by STOCK-CON-01, STOCK-CON-02, and STOCK-CON-03. STOCK-CON-01 seeds stock quantity `1`, pauses Actor A after authoritative sale mutation before commit, forces Actor B onto the same SQL stock lock, proves fresh readers see no partial SQL/Mongo visibility during overlap, then verifies exactly one sale, one stock movement, stock quantity `0`, no reservation drift, projection convergence, duplicate/stale replay safety, and accounting-linked valuation stability. STOCK-CON-02 uses the same last-unit inventory authority but replays the same mutation identity while Request A is active and uncommitted, proving replay timing cannot amplify deduction, create a second movement, expose partial availability, drift reservations, or resurrect stock through duplicate/stale projection delivery. STOCK-CON-03 runs real duplicate stock-sync worker delivery overlap while Worker A holds the authoritative outbox claim before projection completion and Worker B receives the same event concurrently, proving worker timing cannot create phantom SQL stock, duplicate movements, duplicate processed-event/inventory-inbox completion, partial read-model visibility, stale storefront/affiliate/CommerceHub resurrection, or replay/rebuild/reconciliation residue.
- prevention rule: Inventory deduction must remain SQL-first with pessimistic stock row locking and post-lock availability validation. Storefront, affiliate, CommerceHub, cache, search, and replay projections must never become stock authority.
- references: `PointOfSale.Tests/Integration/SalesInvoiceConcurrencyIntegrationTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/SalesController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Inventory/ProductVariantStockMutationSupport.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/TestAuthController.cs`, `testsprite_tests/TC038_stock_con_01_concurrent_stock_deduction_against_same_variant.py`, `testsprite_tests/TC039_stock_con_02_replay_during_active_stock_mutation.py`, `testsprite_tests/testsprite_backend_test_plan.json`

### Sales-return stock restoration overlap cannot become inventory resurrection authority
- title: Replay or concurrent overlap during sales-return stock restoration cannot restore sold inventory twice or duplicate restoration evidence
- affected systems: POS sales returns, `ProductVariantStocks`, historical returned quantity, sales-return stock movements, accounting refund/COGS reversal linkage, stock sync outbox, storefront availability, affiliate public products, CommerceHub listings, replay/rebuild/reconciliation paths
- root cause: A same-request replay, retry, or different-request concurrent return that overlaps an uncommitted sales-return restoration could otherwise be mistaken for a second authoritative restoration, creating duplicate stock movement evidence, drifting returned quantity beyond the historical sold boundary, duplicating refund/accounting reversal linkage, or publishing stale external availability from replay timing.
- impact: The covered resurrection path is closed. SQL stock quantity remains authoritative, return idempotency and row-locking converge to one restored quantity, fresh readers see no uncommitted restoration or duplicate movement visibility during overlap, historical returned quantity stays within the sold quantity, accounting-linked valuation remains one-to-one with the authoritative return, and storefront/affiliate/CommerceHub availability plus rebuild/reconciliation converge from SQL truth.
- mitigation status: Actively covered by STOCK-CON-04 and STOCK-CON-07 on 2026-05-18. STOCK-CON-04 seeds a completed sale from one physical unit, projects sold-out external visibility, pauses Request A inside the authoritative sales-return stock restoration transaction before commit, forces Request B to replay the same return request identity while A is active, verifies no partial restoration through fresh DbContexts, releases A, then proves exactly one restoration, one return stock movement, one accounting-linked reversal, duplicate delivery safety, stale sale replay rejection, SQL-truth rebuild convergence, and inventory reconciliation convergence. STOCK-CON-07 uses different request identities against the same historical sold quantity, forces Request B to reach the original-invoice `FOR UPDATE` lock while Request A is uncommitted, proves no partial restoration visibility, releases A, then proves the second return fails closed after committed-history revalidation without minting returnable quantity, duplicate stock movements, duplicate refund/reversal linkage, stale availability resurrection, or replay/rebuild/reconciliation drift.
- residual operational risk: STOCK-CON-04 and STOCK-CON-07 cover the current `SalesReturnsController` restoration path, stock sync consumers, and dedicated reconciliation/rebuild flows. Any future bulk return import, admin repair, manual stock adjustment, or alternate return worker that can restore sold inventory must reuse the same SQL authority, idempotency fence, movement uniqueness, returned-quantity boundary, and projection freshness rules before rollout.
- prevention rule: A historical sold quantity may be restored only once per authoritative returned quantity. Replay timing, projection timing, webhook timing, cache state, read models, and rebuild payloads are never restoration authority; PostgreSQL return rows, stock rows, movements, idempotency records, and accounting linkage own the boundary.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/SalesReturnsController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Integration/SalesInvoiceConcurrencyIntegrationTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Integration/SalesReturnDistributedReliabilityIntegrationTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/TestAuthController.cs`, `testsprite_tests/TC044_stock_con_07_concurrent_sales_return_against_same_historical_sale_quantity.py`, `testsprite_tests/testsprite_backend_test_plan.json`, `.ai-testing/TESTSPRITE_MCP_RUNBOOK.md`

### Duplicate CommerceHub return delivery cannot become inventory resurrection authority
- title: Duplicate, delayed, or out-of-order CommerceHub return delivery cannot restore marketplace inventory twice
- affected systems: CommerceHub returns, `ReturnManagementService`, `InventoryConsistencyService`, `InventoryEventInbox`, `ProductVariantStocks`, CommerceHub return stock movements, refund/reversal linkage, stock sync outbox, storefront availability, affiliate public products, CommerceHub listings, replay/rebuild/reconciliation paths
- root cause: Marketplace return delivery is at-least-once and can overlap, retry, or arrive out of order. Without a SQL return row lock, serializable retry handling, and deterministic inventory event fencing, duplicate delivery could be mistaken for a second authoritative return and amplify stock restoration or duplicate movement/refund evidence.
- impact: The covered duplicate-marketplace-return resurrection path is closed. CommerceHub return receipt confirmation locks SQL return/refund authority, retries PostgreSQL serialization conflicts, routes restoration through a deterministic `commercehub-return-confirm` inventory event id, and exits duplicate delivery idempotently after the first commit. Fresh readers see no uncommitted restoration, duplicate movement, phantom availability, or duplicate reversal visibility during overlap.
- mitigation status: Actively covered by STOCK-CON-05 on 2026-05-18. The deterministic test seeds a CommerceHub-linked completed delivery from one physical unit, projects sold-out storefront/affiliate/CommerceHub visibility, pauses Delivery A after authoritative return restoration before commit, overlaps Delivery B on the same CommerceHub return identity, verifies fresh-reader no-partial-visibility assertions, releases A, then proves exactly one SQL restoration, one return movement, one inventory inbox completion, one refund/reversal linkage, duplicate/delayed/out-of-order delivery safety, stale replay rejection, SQL-truth rebuild convergence, and inventory reconciliation convergence.
- residual operational risk: STOCK-CON-05 covers the current CommerceHub return receipt path and stock projection/reconciliation flows. Future marketplace return importers, webhook consumers, bulk return repair jobs, or alternate CommerceHub return ownership paths must reuse the same SQL return authority, serializable retry discipline, deterministic inventory event identity, movement uniqueness, and projection freshness rules before rollout.
- prevention rule: CommerceHub return webhook or delivery timing is never stock authority. A CommerceHub return event may restore inventory only once through PostgreSQL return/refund rows, stock rows, movement rows, and `InventoryEventInbox`; storefront, affiliate, CommerceHub projections, replay timing, and rebuild payloads are derived convergence mechanisms only.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/ReturnManagementService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/InventoryConsistencyService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Integration/SalesInvoiceConcurrencyIntegrationTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/TestDatabaseScope.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/TestAuthController.cs`, `testsprite_tests/testsprite_backend_test_plan.json`, `.ai-testing/TESTSPRITE_MCP_RUNBOOK.md`

### Purchase-return stock deduction replay cannot become duplicate inventory deduction authority
- title: Replay during active purchase-return stock deduction cannot deduct purchased inventory twice or duplicate supplier reversal evidence
- affected systems: POS purchase returns, `PurchaseReturnsController`, `PurchaseReturnRequestIdempotency`, `PurchaseInvoices.AccountingMutationVersion`, `ProductVariantStocks`, purchase-return stock movements, supplier transactions/ledgers, accounting outbox/journals, stock sync outbox, storefront availability, affiliate public products, CommerceHub listings, replay/rebuild/reconciliation paths
- root cause: A same-request replay or retry that overlaps an uncommitted purchase-return stock deduction could otherwise be mistaken for a second authoritative deduction, creating duplicate negative stock movement evidence, drifting returned quantity beyond the historical purchased boundary, duplicating supplier refund/reversal linkage, or propagating stale external availability from replay timing.
- impact: The covered duplicate-deduction path is closed. SQL stock quantity remains authoritative, purchase-return idempotency and purchase-invoice row locking converge to one deducted quantity, fresh readers see no uncommitted deduction or duplicate movement/supplier reversal visibility during overlap, historical purchased/returned quantity stays within the received quantity, accounting-linked valuation remains one-to-one with the authoritative purchase return, and storefront/affiliate/CommerceHub availability plus rebuild/reconciliation converge from SQL truth.
- mitigation status: Actively covered by STOCK-CON-06 on 2026-05-18. The deterministic test seeds an authoritative purchase receipt and visible storefront/affiliate/CommerceHub stock, pauses Request A after staging the purchase-return deduction before commit, overlaps Request B on the same purchase-return request identity, verifies fresh-reader no-partial-deduction visibility, releases Request A, then proves exactly one SQL deduction, one purchase-return movement, one idempotency completion, one supplier/accounting reversal linkage, duplicate/stale stock projection delivery safety, SQL-truth rebuild convergence, and inventory reconciliation convergence.
- residual operational risk: STOCK-CON-06 covers the current `PurchaseReturnsController` deduction path and stock projection/reconciliation flows. Future purchase-return importers, supplier-return repair jobs, bulk return tools, or alternate supplier reversal ownership paths must reuse the same SQL purchase invoice authority, idempotency fence, movement uniqueness, historical returned-quantity boundary, and projection freshness rules before rollout.
- prevention rule: A historical purchased quantity may be deducted only once per authoritative purchase-return quantity. Replay timing, projection timing, webhook timing, cache state, read models, and rebuild payloads are never purchase-return deduction authority; PostgreSQL purchase return rows, purchase invoice mutation version, stock rows, movements, idempotency records, supplier/accounting linkage, and stock-sync outbox own the boundary.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/PurchaseReturnsController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Integration/PurchaseReturnDistributedReliabilityIntegrationTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Integration/PurchaseReturnsConcurrencyIntegrationTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/TestAuthController.cs`, `testsprite_tests/testsprite_backend_test_plan.json`, `.ai-testing/TESTSPRITE_MCP_RUNBOOK.md`

### Concurrent variant attribute-combination creation cannot become split variant identity authority
- title: Concurrent creation of the same variant attribute combination must fail closed with one authoritative variant identity
- affected systems: `ProductVariants`, `ProductVariantAttributes`, SKU ownership, barcode ownership, variant stock linkage, pricing fields, storefront variant visibility, affiliate product visibility, CommerceHub listings, replay/rebuild/reconciliation paths
- root cause: Before VAR-CON-01 hardening, SKU and barcode uniqueness guarded only part of variant identity. Two concurrent requests with the same attribute combination but different proposed SKU/barcode values could attempt to make projection timing, request timing, or replay timing look like variant identity authority unless SQL owned the normalized attribute-combination key.
- impact: The covered split-identity path is closed. PostgreSQL now stores a deterministic `AttributeCombinationKey` on `ProductVariants` and enforces uniqueness per tenant/product for non-deleted variants. The create/update API computes the same key from normalized attribute pairs, rejects duplicate attribute names, catches database uniqueness conflicts as deterministic variant identity conflicts, and the VAR-CON-01 runner proves fresh readers see no uncommitted variant/SKU/barcode/stock/idempotency visibility during the overlap. After commit there is exactly one variant identity, one SKU owner, one barcode owner, one inventory linkage, pricing remains attached to the surviving variant row, replay returns the same authority, and derived visibility cannot mint another variant.
- mitigation status: Actively covered by VAR-CON-01 on 2026-05-18. TestSprite TC045 passed through the dedicated local runner endpoint and verified the deterministic concurrent overlap against the real integration stack.
- residual operational risk: The migration intentionally fails closed if legacy live duplicate attribute combinations already exist, and it requires `pgcrypto` for the backfill hash. Future raw SQL imports, bulk catalog tools, alternate variant creators, or projection rebuilders must compute/populate the same normalized key and rely on the SQL unique index rather than deriving identity from SKU, barcode, Mongo, CommerceHub, affiliate, cache, or sync timing.
- prevention rule: A variant attribute combination may have only one authoritative SQL row. SKU, barcode, inventory ownership, pricing ownership, storefront identity, affiliate identity, and CommerceHub identity must converge from PostgreSQL `ProductVariants.AttributeCombinationKey` plus existing SQL uniqueness boundaries. Projection timing and sync timing are never variant authority.
- references: `PointOfSale.Core/Helpers/ProductVariantAttributeCombinationKey.cs`, `PointOfSale.Core/Entities/ProductVariant.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/Configurations/ProductVariantConfiguration.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Migrations/20260518120000_VariantAttributeCombinationAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/ProductVariantsController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Integration/ProductVariantDistributedReliabilityIntegrationTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/TestAuthController.cs`, `testsprite_tests/VAR_CON_01_TESTSPRITE_REPORT.md`, `testsprite_tests/testsprite_backend_test_plan.json`, `.ai-testing/TESTSPRITE_MCP_RUNBOOK.md`

- prevention rule: Variant deletion timing, inventory mutation timing, replay timing, projection timing, sync timing, cache state, and read-model visibility are never variant existence or ownership authority. PostgreSQL `ProductVariants` owns variant existence and ownership; inventory, pricing, fulfillment, movements, storefront, affiliate, CommerceHub, cache, and search may only converge from SQL truth and must fail closed on stale events.
