Without deterministic corruption-overlap coverage, stale V10 media replay after V11 truth, duplicate V11 delivery, stale delete/replacement replay, CDN/cache/search timing, affiliate/storefront/CommerceHub projection timing, or rebuild timing could be mistaken for media ownership authority and resurrect stale or orphan media.
- impact: The covered media-corruption path is now closed by production enforcement, not only deterministic proof coverage. VAR-CON-09B adds a production SQL event fence before media projection side effects, forces media consumers to re-read authoritative `ProductMedia`/`ProductVariants` state from PostgreSQL, rejects stale or future/corrupted media versions whose event version does not match SQL authority, overwrites same-version corrupted payload media from SQL truth, prevents same-EventId duplicate delivery from reindexing/reinvalidating, constrains variant media linkage to same-tenant/same-product SQL media rows at the database level, and makes media deletion explicitly unlink affected variants before the media row is removed. PRODUCT-CON-04 extends the runtime propagation boundary so `ProductMediaChangedEvent` also refreshes CommerceHub listing images and affiliate public/intelligence media from current SQL media authority instead of leaving stale thumbnails until an unrelated product/policy refresh.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened and covered on 2026-05-19 by VAR-CON-09B, then extended on 2026-05-21 by PRODUCT-CON-04 for CommerceHub and affiliate media propagation. Local validation passed for the focused media/source guard slice and Infrastructure build; the prior deterministic VAR-CON media integration slice remains the deep replay baseline.
- residual operational risk: Runtime media propagation is stronger for storefront product docs, product search, CommerceHub listing/search images, and affiliate public/intelligence images, but this is not a CDN architecture rewrite. No CDN-specific purge/invalidation worker was present to harden; browser/CDN cached URLs may remain visible until cache expiry or external invalidation. Future CDN purge workers, image optimizers, bulk media importers, affiliate feed writers, CommerceHub sync writers, direct Mongo/search/cache repair tools, or non-update variant projection handlers must explicitly reuse the SQL media authority/fence pattern before rollout.
- prevention rule: CDN timing, cache timing, search timing, media projection delivery timing, storefront/affiliate/CommerceHub timing, marketplace payload timing, and replay timing are never media authority. PostgreSQL `ProductVariants.MediaId` plus tenant/product-owned `ProductMedia` rows own variant/media linkage and primary-image ordering; stale media events must fail closed and rebuild/reconciliation must derive visibility from current SQL truth without resurrecting deleted media.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProjectionConsumerEventFence.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductMediaChangedEventConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductVariantProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/CommerceHubProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/Affiliate/AffiliateProductPolicyProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/Affiliate/AffiliateProductIntelligenceProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/ProductVariantsController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/ProductMediaService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/SearchReindexController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/Configurations/ProductVariantConfiguration.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Migrations/20260519153000_VariantMediaSqlOwnershipAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/EventOutboxConsistencyArchitectureTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Integration/ProductVariantDistributedReliabilityIntegrationTests.cs`

### Marketplace reservation replay cannot become reservation ownership authority
- title: Stale or duplicate marketplace reservation replay can no longer pass payment/recovery authority merely because SQL has at least enough active reserved quantity
- affected systems: CommerceHub payment initiation, marketplace payment recovery authority, reusable provider-session replay, provider-session creation, provider-success recognition, CommerceHub fulfillment/delivery commit, marketplace reservations, `InventoryReservations`, `ProductVariantStocks`
- root cause: CHECKOUT-CON-05 already required active unexpired SQL reservations, SQL catalog authority, shipping authority, and reserved-stock backing before payment/fulfillment side effects. The remaining replay-specific weakness was permissive reservation recomposition: duplicate active reservations could over-cover an order item, and extra active reservations for the same marketplace order but a stale/foreign order item could be ignored instead of treated as split ownership.
- impact: CHECKOUT-CON-07 hardens marketplace reservation authority under replay. `EnsureMarketplaceOrderReservationsActiveAsync(...)` now requires active SQL reservation ownership to exactly match current SQL CommerceHub order item authority by tenant/order-item/variant, rejects over-reservation with `MARKETPLACE_ORDER_RESERVATION_OVER_RESERVED`, rejects foreign active reservation ownership with `MARKETPLACE_ORDER_RESERVATION_OWNERSHIP_CONFLICT`, and still verifies backing `ProductVariantStock.ReservedQuantity`. CommerceHub payment initiation recomposes reservations again immediately before provider-session side effects, and payment recovery/reusable-session authority now fails closed when active SQL reservation authority is missing.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-20 for current CommerceHub payment initiation/recovery and inventory/fulfillment guard paths. Focused in-memory xUnit and source regression slices passed locally; API build passed. PostgreSQL-backed STOCK/PRICE/VAR integration smoke slices were attempted but blocked because the configured PostgreSQL endpoint `192.168.1.5:5432` refused connections.
- residual operational risk: Marketplace visibility, cart availability, search/category pages, and customer read models remain derivative and can lag by design. Cleanup/reconciliation jobs can still release or repair stale reservations, but they do not create new reservation authority from marketplace/session/cache truth. Future marketplace importers, provider recovery workers, direct repair endpoints, or bulk reservation tools must preserve exact SQL reservation ownership recomposition before payment, fulfillment, inventory, or rebuild side effects.
- prevention rule: Marketplace reservation replay must prove exact current SQL reservation ownership, not "enough" projected or cached availability. Payment recovery and provider-session replay are subordinate to active SQL reservation authority; stale sessions, callbacks, projections, or rebuild payloads must fail closed rather than resurrecting reservations or availability.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/InventoryConsistencyService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentRecoveryAuthorityService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/InventoryConsistencyServiceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentRecoveryAuthorityServiceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CheckoutConcurrencyHardeningSourceTests.cs`

### Marketplace checkout availability visibility cannot become inventory authority
- title: Stale marketplace checkout, payment, reservation, and fulfillment replay must fail closed against SQL reservation/stock truth
- affected systems: CommerceHub checkout payment initiation/recovery, marketplace reservations, `InventoryReservations`, `ProductVariantStocks`, CommerceHub delivery/fulfillment commit, shipping eligibility, marketplace product availability rebuild, affiliate/search/category/cache visibility that can touch checkout decisions
- root cause: Marketplace and CommerceHub availability are derivative CQRS visibility surfaces. If stale InStock/AvailableQuantity, stale reservation payloads, delayed projection replay, checkout recovery, payment retry, or fulfillment replay were trusted after SQL stock/reservation exhaustion, the marketplace path could oversell or resurrect availability.
- impact: CHECKOUT-CON-05 closes the primary runtime commit gap. Marketplace payment initiation now relies on `EnsureMarketplaceOrderReservationsActiveAsync(...)`, which requires active unexpired SQL reservations, SQL catalog authority, active shipping authority, and backing reserved stock rows. CommerceHub delivery commit now revalidates SQL order/item/payment/catalog/shipping authority and consumes only active unexpired reservations. Marketplace product read-model rebuild explicitly declares SQL authority through `SqlTruthRebuildGuard` for product, variant, stock, pricing, media, and tombstone overlays.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened and covered on 2026-05-20 by focused deterministic xUnit/source-guard validation. Local validation passed for `InventoryConsistencyServiceTests`, `CheckoutConcurrencyHardeningSourceTests`, and narrow STOCK/PRICE/VAR smoke slices (`STOCK_CON_03`, `PRICE_CON_03`, `VAR_CON_07`); broad TestSprite/MCP execution was intentionally not used because deterministic xUnit is the authority for this sprint and the runbook warns against unstable retry loops.
- residual operational risk: Customer-facing marketplace visibility can still lag by design, and some marketplace/order/homepage/search/cache rebuild surfaces remain partial/manual rather than uniformly guarded by `SqlTruthRebuildGuard`. Future marketplace importers, checkout-recovery endpoints, payment-provider recovery workers, shipping/fulfillment retry workers, search/category repair tools, affiliate visibility rehydrators, and direct marketplace read-model repair endpoints must preserve SQL recomposition before side effects.
- prevention rule: CommerceHub payloads, cached availability, marketplace sessions, affiliate visibility, search/category visibility, Mongo read models, delayed projections, and stale rebuild inputs are never inventory authority. Payment, reservation finalization, order finalization, fulfillment commit, and stock deduction must re-read PostgreSQL reservation/stock/order/catalog/shipping truth and fail closed when SQL no longer proves availability.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/InventoryConsistencyService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/ReadModels/Marketplace/MarketplaceReadModelProjectionServices.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/InventoryConsistencyServiceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CheckoutConcurrencyHardeningSourceTests.cs`

### External derivative replay cannot resurrect superseded variant visibility on hardened surfaces
- title: CommerceHub search/reindex and marketplace/homepage rebuild replay must converge to current SQL lifecycle and inventory lineage
- affected systems: CommerceHub listing/search documents, CommerceHub autocomplete/suggestions, CommerceHub reindex/recovery, marketplace product/homepage read models, storefront homepage cached cards, variant availability projection, projection rebuild/replay/reconciliation paths
- root cause: Export-like derivative surfaces can be regenerated from stale Mongo, cache, search, listing, homepage, or queue evidence after a variant is superseded or inventory lineage is detached. If rebuild and reindex paths locally re-evaluate visibility without lifecycle and stock lineage authority, stale public availability or recommendation/search visibility can reappear after SQL truth already removed it.
- impact: PRODUCT-CON-11E closes the inspected runtime gaps by routing CommerceHub search overlay and reindex count authority through public variant lifecycle predicates, filtering CommerceHub and marketplace rebuilt stock through `ProductVariantInventoryLineageAuthority`, deleting marketplace variant-product rebuild visibility when no current public semantic-owner variant remains, scrubbing superseded curated listing ids from marketplace homepage rebuilds, and suppressing storefront homepage cached variant cards when SQL cannot recompose current visibility.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-22 for the concrete derivative surfaces found in this backend. Focused runtime tests cover CommerceHub superseded replay collapse, inactive-warehouse stale stock suppression, marketplace product rebuild tombstoning for superseded semantic owners, and marketplace curated recommendation rebuild scrubbing.
- residual operational risk: No standalone backend product feed/export writer for affiliate/supplier/catalog/inventory/pricing/marketplace payload generation was found in the inspected paths. Future external sync workers, feed exporters, cache warmers that hydrate product visibility, analytics/reporting public projections, recommendation engines, and direct search/read-model repair tools can reintroduce derivative resurrection unless they adopt the same SQL lifecycle, semantic ownership, inventory lineage, tombstone/delete, and replay-collapse rules with runtime tests.
- prevention rule: Feed/export payload timing, delayed outbox delivery, dead-letter replay, cache warming, snapshot rebuild, recommendation regeneration, autocomplete cache state, and search/index recovery are never product/variant visibility authority. Recompose current SQL authority before public derivative emission and fail closed to delete, scrub, tombstone, no-op, or zero availability when current authority rejects visibility.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/ProductVariantLifecycleAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/CommerceHubSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Jobs/CommerceHubReindexService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/ReadModels/Marketplace/MarketplaceReadModelProjectionServices.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Storefront/StorefrontHomeService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/ProductVariantLifecycleAuthorityTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/MarketplaceReadModelProjectionTests.cs`

### Stale shipment replay cannot own order status or storefront shipment visibility
- title: Delayed shipment evidence must collapse against current SQL order/shipment lineage
- affected systems: shipment-triggered order status synchronization, storefront order shipment facet, storefront order projection repair/rebuild, carrier callback/outbox replay fanout, shipment tracking visibility
- root cause: Shipment services already persisted SQL shipment and order snapshots before fanout, but the order status sync consumer and storefront shipment-facet consumer could still map event payload status/tracking after queue replay, detached order linkage, soft deletion, or a later SQL shipment transition.
- impact: ORDER-CON-01 closes the concrete handoff gap. `OrderFulfillmentLineageAuthority` requires a current non-deleted same-tenant SQL shipment joined to its owning SQL order before shipment evidence can affect order status or storefront shipment visibility. Status sync maps current SQL shipment status under the order lock, stale event status collapses to current SQL truth, detached/deleted/wrong-order shipment evidence no-ops, and storefront rebuild snapshots overlay live SQL shipment visibility.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-22 with focused SQL-lineage runtime tests and architecture guard coverage for status sync, shipment facet projection, and storefront rebuild loader integration.
- residual operational risk: This sweep governs shipment-to-order status handoff and storefront shipment visibility. Future warehouse transfer/reassignment, affiliate fulfillment export, shipment analytics/reporting, support/admin shipment cache, external shipment feed, and new provider callback handlers can reintroduce shipment resurrection unless they use the same SQL lineage recomposition rule. Existing `OrderShippedEvent`/`OrderDeliveredEvent` projection paths retain event freshness plus SQL repair semantics and should be moved toward SQL recomposition when their visibility surface is expanded.
- prevention rule: Shipment callback timing, tracking payloads, warehouse/export retries, stale outbox delivery, Mongo documents, Redis cache state, and rebuild seed payloads are never order or fulfillment semantic authority. Current SQL order/shipment lineage must decide status and visible shipment state before derivative fanout.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/OrderFulfillmentLineageAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/OrderStatusSyncService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/OrderProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/MongoDB/Projection/StorefrontOrderProjectionAuthoritySnapshotLoader.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/OrderFulfillmentLineageAuthorityTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Services/OrderStatusSyncShipmentLineageTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/OrderFulfillmentArchitectureTests.cs`

### Accounting outbox replay cannot resurrect stale ledger truth on hardened paths
- title: Order collection, refund, and settlement journal replay now recomposes from current SQL accounting authority
- affected systems: `AccountingOutboxProcessor`, `AccountingLedgerAuthority`, `FinancialPostingService`, `JournalEntries`, `LedgerEntries`, `Orders`, `OrderRefunds`, `PaymentSettlementBatches`, COD/gateway settlement accounting, e-commerce refund accounting, order collection accounting
- root cause: The accounting outbox already had idempotency, aggregate ordering, leases, and non-terminal retry handling, but order collection, refund, and settlement dispatch still used replay payload fields as part of final posting semantics after loading the source aggregate. Stale payload amounts, stale terminal payment state, stale COD delivery evidence, or stale settlement amounts could therefore reach journal generation unless each local path remembered the same authority rules.
- impact: ACCOUNTING-CON-01 adds `AccountingLedgerAuthority` and routes inspected outbox dispatch through current SQL order payment/fulfillment truth, current `OrderRefund` rows, and current `PaymentSettlementBatch` rows before posting. Stale online/wallet/COD collection replay collapses when current SQL no longer recognizes collectible money, refund accounting uses current refund amount/context instead of payload values, and gateway/COD settlement posting uses current settlement batch gross/fees/provider instead of replay payload values.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardened on 2026-05-22 for inspected accounting outbox collection/refund/settlement paths. Focused `AccountingOutboxProcessorTests` passed locally after adding tests for stale terminal payment replay collapse, refund amount SQL recomposition, and settlement amount SQL recomposition.
- residual operational risk: CommerceHub vendor finance dashboards, affiliate accounting exports, BI/reporting snapshots, tax/export feeds, payout export files, support/admin caches, and future reconciliation snapshot rebuilders remain specialized derivative paths. They are not implicitly safe if they generate financial visibility from stale payloads or snapshots rather than SQL ledger/accounting truth.
- prevention rule: Ledger posting payloads are triggers only. Current SQL ledger idempotency, order payment/fulfillment truth, refund lineage, settlement batch lineage, and reconciliation state must decide posting, no-op, or retry. Financial projections, dashboards, exports, caches, and analytics must converge from SQL ledger/reconciliation state and fail closed when current accounting authority cannot prove visibility.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/AccountingLedgerAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Accounting/AccountingOutboxProcessor.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Accounting/FinancialPostingService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Accounting/AccountingOutboxProcessorTests.cs`

### Settlement accounting replay and fast-path posting now reject stale settlement-order snapshots
- title: Settlement accounting must recompose current settlement-order lineage instead of trusting `PaymentSettlementBatch.OrderCount`
- affected systems: `AccountingLedgerAuthority`, `AccountingOutboxProcessor`, `PaymentLifecycleService`, `PaymentSettlementBatches`, `PaymentSettlementOrders`, gateway settlement accounting, COD settlement accounting, settlement replay telemetry
- root cause: ACCOUNTING-CON-01 already recomposed settlement amount/provider metadata from current `PaymentSettlementBatch`, but settlement membership proof still relied on the denormalized batch header `OrderCount`. Accounting replay and immediate settlement-accounting posting could therefore accept a stale batch snapshot even when current settlement-order mappings had drifted or gone missing.
- impact: POS-ADMIN-CERTIFICATION-01 PASS 4 closes that gap. `AccountingLedgerAuthority` now loads both recorded batch count and current live `PaymentSettlementOrders` count, treats soft-deleted settlement batches as missing current authority, retries on empty/mismatched current lineage, and emits `SettlementOrderLineageMismatch` telemetry. `PaymentLifecycleService` immediate gateway/COD settlement posting now enforces the same settlement gross/fees/lineage authority rule before ledger mutation.
- mitigation status: Runtime, regression tests, and documentation hardened on 2026-05-25. Focused coverage was added for settlement-lineage recomposition, deleted settlement-batch rejection, mismatch retry, and fast-path source invariants.
- residual operational risk: `PaymentSettlementBatch` still carries denormalized metadata for convenience, and future settlement repair/import/export/reporting tooling can regress if it treats that header snapshot as posting authority. External bank/provider reconciliation remains a separate explicit reconciliation concern.
- prevention rule: Settlement replay, fast-path accounting, recovery tooling, and financial exports must treat `PaymentSettlementBatch.OrderCount` as recorded metadata only. Current non-deleted SQL `PaymentSettlementBatch` authority plus current `PaymentSettlementOrders` lineage must decide settlement posting, retry, or reconciliation-required behavior.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/AccountingLedgerAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Payments/PaymentLifecycleService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Instrumentation/OperationalConvergenceTelemetry.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/AccountingLedgerAuthorityTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Accounting/AccountingOutboxProcessorTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/FinancialIntegrityRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### Settlement recording and immediate posting now reject invalid gross/fee authority before mutation
- title: Gateway/COD settlement creation can no longer persist invalid gross/fee bounds or rely on a weaker fast-path-only settlement accounting check
- affected systems: `PaymentLifecycleService`, `AccountingLedgerAuthority`, `PaymentSettlementBatches`, gateway settlement recording, COD settlement recording, immediate settlement accounting
- root cause: The reviewed settlement write path validated tenant/order/currency/order-total lineage, but it still allowed non-positive COD gross amounts and settlement fees outside `[0, gross]` to reach settlement persistence. The immediate post-commit settlement accounting fast path also enforced settlement-order lineage locally without reusing the full `AccountingLedgerAuthority` settlement evaluation that replay used.
- impact: PASS 4 addendum closes that asymmetry. Reviewed gateway/COD settlement recording now rejects non-positive gross amounts and invalid fees before settlement persistence, accounting enqueue, or `Order.PaymentStatus` mutation. Immediate gateway/COD settlement posting now reloads `AccountingLedgerAuthority.LoadCurrentSettlementAsync(...)` and requires `EvaluateSettlementPosting(...)` before ledger mutation, so fast-path and replayed settlement accounting share the same gross/fees/lineage authority gate.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardening, targeted runtime tests, source-freeze assertions, and certification/runbook documentation updated on 2026-05-27.
- residual operational risk: This narrows the reviewed settlement creation and immediate-posting boundary only. Future settlement import, repair, export, dashboard, or reconciliation utilities can still regress if they accept provider-export math or batch-header snapshots without current SQL settlement authority.
- prevention rule: Settlement creation must reject non-positive gross amounts and fees outside `[0, gross]` before persistence. Immediate settlement posting must use the same `AccountingLedgerAuthority` settlement evaluation as replay rather than a weaker local fast-path check.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Payments/PaymentLifecycleService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Payments/PaymentLifecycleSettlementAuthorityTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/FinancialIntegrityRiskHardeningTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/orders-payments-refunds-accounting-certification-findings.md`

### Post-settlement refund accounting can no longer reuse stale settlement membership snapshots
- title: Bank-account refund posting now revalidates current settlement batch membership for the refunding order
- affected systems: `PaymentLifecycleService`, `AccountingLedgerAuthority`, `AccountingOutboxProcessor`, `OrderRefunds`, `PaymentSettlementBatches`, `PaymentSettlementOrders`, post-settlement refund accounting
- root cause: Settled-order refund creation already stamped settlement snapshot data onto `OrderRefund`, but refund accounting still treated that stored snapshot as sufficient bank-account refund context. If the current settlement batch was deleted, drifted, or no longer contained the order, immediate or replayed refund accounting could still post as a post-settlement refund from stale membership evidence.
- impact: PASS 4 now closes that adjacent gap. Refund creation and immediate/replayed refund accounting must recompose current non-deleted settlement batch authority, current live settlement-order count, and current refund-order membership before bank-account post-settlement refund posting is allowed. Missing or drifted lineage is now retry/reconciliation territory with explicit refund-settlement convergence reasons.
- mitigation status: Runtime, regression tests, and documentation hardened on 2026-05-25. Focused coverage was added for drift rejection during refund creation, replay retry when the order falls out of the current settlement batch, and source-freeze assertions that the fast path uses the same refund authority helper as replay.
- residual operational risk: Historical settlement snapshot fields remain useful audit evidence on `OrderRefund`, but they are not independent posting authority. Future settlement repair/import/export/reporting or manual refund tooling can regress if it trusts those snapshots without current settlement lineage.
- prevention rule: Post-settlement refunds are not allowed to post from stored refund snapshot fields alone. Current non-deleted SQL `PaymentSettlementBatch` authority plus current `PaymentSettlementOrders` lineage, including the refunding order's live membership, must decide bank-account refund posting, retry, or reconciliation-required behavior.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Payments/PaymentLifecycleService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/AccountingLedgerAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Integration/EcommerceRefundLifecycleTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Accounting/AccountingOutboxProcessorTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/FinancialIntegrityRiskHardeningTests.cs`

### Dangerous derivative primitive governance is strengthened but intentionally incremental
- title: Reviewed derivative mutation primitives now have an explicit runtime precondition path for new narrow chokepoints
- affected systems: `DerivativeProjectionMutationGuard`, `StorefrontFooterProjectionConsumer`, `StorefrontConfigProjectionConsumer`, `ProjectionEventFence`, `CrossSystemAuthorityRegressionGuardTests`, Mongo projection replacement paths, Redis cache mutation paths, future Elasticsearch/cache mutation chokepoints
- root cause: The existing source guard prevented many new unreviewed dangerous primitives, but an approved direct projection replacement could still rely on local convention for runtime intent: SQL authority, freshness filtering, fail-closed behavior, and reviewed reason were not expressed as one reusable precondition.
- impact: GOVERNANCE-CON-01 adds `DerivativeProjectionMutationGuard` and applies it to reviewed storefront footer/config full-document Mongo replacement. Storefront config projection also guards Redis store-config cache invalidation after SQL recomposition succeeds. Storefront footer and config projection use `ProjectionEventFence`, so duplicate or active replay collapses before side effects, and guarded mutations must declare SQL recomposition, freshness protection, fail-closed behavior, event identity, authority kind, and review reason before executing.
- mitigation status: Runtime/governance hardened on 2026-05-22 for the concrete storefront footer replacement path, storefront config replacement/cache path, and shared guard. Focused tests `DerivativeProjectionMutationGuardTests`, `StorefrontConfigProjectionConsumerTests`, and `CrossSystemAuthorityRegressionGuardTests` passed locally.
- residual operational risk: This is not a universal primitive enforcement framework. Existing approved specialized Mongo, Elasticsearch, Redis, direct-SQL, outbox, lifecycle, and repair paths remain domain-specific and must keep their existing SQL recomposition, freshness, tombstone, event-fence, or lifecycle protections. Future allowlist additions can still regress if they add only a static exception without a runtime boundary and documented replay semantics.
- prevention rule: New dangerous primitive exceptions must name their authority boundary, use an existing domain writer/authority where available, or add an equivalent runtime guard with SQL/freshness/fail-closed semantics, focused tests, source guard assertions, and documentation in the same change.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/DerivativeProjectionMutationGuard.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/StorefrontFooterProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/StorefrontConfigProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/DerivativeProjectionMutationGuardTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Storefront/StorefrontConfigProjectionConsumerTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemAuthorityRegressionGuardTests.cs`

### Replay, rebuild, fail-closed, and drift convergence decisions are now centrally observable
- title: Semantic convergence outcomes no longer rely only on scattered logs or implicit no-op behavior on the hardened shared boundaries
- affected systems: `OperationalConvergenceTelemetry`, `ProjectionEventFence`, `ProjectionAuthorityValidator`, `SqlTruthRebuildGuard`, `DerivativeProjectionMutationGuard`, `TenantLineageAuthority`, `AdminTenantAuthority`, `ProductVariantLifecycleAuthority`, `ProductVariantInventoryLineageAuthority`, `OrderFulfillmentLineageAuthority`, `OrderPaymentLineageAuthority`, `AccountingLedgerAuthority`, stock-sync stale suppression, Mongo projection drift health checks, Elasticsearch drift reconciliation
- root cause: The platform had strong replay-safe SQL authority behavior, but many correct no-op/collapse/fail-closed decisions were operationally hard to distinguish from inactivity unless an operator found a local log statement or inferred behavior from a missing mutation.
- impact: OPERATIONS-CON-01 adds bounded semantic convergence telemetry for replay collapse, active duplicate suppression, stale projection rejection, fail-closed lineage rejection, rebuild authority declarations, dangerous primitive acceptance/rejection, accounting replay collapse/retry, inventory fanout suppression, actor authority rejection, and drift detection. Metrics are exported through the OpenTelemetry meter pipeline and also attach trace events when an activity is active.
- mitigation status: Runtime observability hardened on 2026-05-24 for the shared guards and lineage helpers listed above. Focused `OperationalConvergenceTelemetryTests` validate bounded metric classifications, duplicate replay collapse telemetry, dangerous primitive governance telemetry, rebuild auditability, and accounting replay diagnostics.
- residual operational risk: This does not retrofit every specialized consumer, cache warmer, export/report writer, support cache, notification cache, or global runbook. Existing paths that do not pass through the shared guards may still need local convergence telemetry when their replay/rebuild/drift behavior is expanded.
- prevention rule: New semantic convergence decisions must emit through `OperationalConvergenceTelemetry` or an equivalent reviewed low-cardinality boundary. Do not add free-form replay logs as the only diagnostic surface; do not include raw tenant ids, customer/payment data, document keys, payload fragments, or exception text in metric labels.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Instrumentation/OperationalConvergenceTelemetry.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/ProjectionEventFence.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/SqlTruthRebuildGuard.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/DerivativeProjectionMutationGuard.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/AccountingLedgerAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/HealthChecks/ProductionHealthChecks.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Jobs/ElasticsearchDriftReconciliationJob.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/OperationalConvergenceTelemetryTests.cs`

### Operational telemetry export and cardinality are now governed on reviewed survivability surfaces
- title: Reviewed operational meters no longer stay dark or emit raw tenant identifiers on the hardened export paths
- affected systems: OpenTelemetry meter registration in `Program.cs`, `AccountingMetrics`, `AffiliateTelemetry`, `AbandonedCartRecoveryTelemetry`, `StorefrontPublishTelemetry`, `MarketplaceTelemetry`, `AnalyticsReliabilityTelemetry`, `SearchTelemetry`, `InventoryReservationTelemetry`, `CheckoutLockTelemetry`, `CommerceReliabilityTelemetry`, `RedisPerformanceTelemetry`, `PaymentCancellationOutboxReadinessHealthCheck`, `CommerceHubReindexOptions`
- root cause: Several reviewed runtime meters existed but were not wired into the process OpenTelemetry export pipeline, while several exported reviewed meters still used raw tenant/order identifiers or unnormalized free-form labels. `CommerceHubReindexOptions` was configuration-bound but not startup-validated, the reviewed payment-cancellation worker had no dedicated readiness surface despite being a replay-safe recovery boundary, and legacy `AccountingMetrics` still used raw tenant/provider/error singleton counter keys that could grow without bound over multi-tenant runtime lifetimes.
- impact: PASS 8 wires the reviewed affiliate/search/reservation/checkout-lock meters into the global OpenTelemetry pipeline, hardens reviewed exported tenant dimensions to `tenant_bucket`, replaces reviewed raw order dimensions with bounded presence flags, normalizes reviewed free-form operational labels, startup-validates bounded `CommerceHubReindexOptions` values, and now also normalizes the remaining reviewed exported `CommerceReliabilityTelemetry` provider/surface/endpoint/carrier/projection/storage-provider/stock-sync/isolation-level labels at the telemetry boundary itself. PASS 8 long-term survivability follow-up also exports reviewed Redis cache performance telemetry and replaces raw Redis invalidation pattern labels with bounded cache/pattern-family labels, exports reviewed accounting telemetry through `PointOfSale.Accounting` with bounded tenant/gateway/error dimensions, bounds the singleton accounting fallback counters to the same reviewed dimension contract, and adds `payment_cancellation_outbox_readiness` so backlog, repeated failure, terminal failure, active claim, and lease diagnostics for payment-cancellation recovery are operator-visible on the readiness surface.
- mitigation status: Runtime, startup-validation, and regression-freeze hardening added on 2026-05-26. Follow-up hardening removed raw tenant/order labels from exported `CommerceReliabilityTelemetry` currency and recovery-cart failure diagnostics, then normalized the remaining reviewed exported string tags and froze deployment/restore assumptions in the operational survivability runbooks and tests on 2026-05-29. PASS 8 long-term survivability follow-up exported `PointOfSale.Redis.Performance`, bounded its reviewed cache/pattern labels, exported bounded `PointOfSale.Accounting` telemetry, bounded the legacy accounting fallback counters, and added payment-cancellation readiness diagnostics. Focused `OperationalTelemetryCardinalityTests`, `PaymentCancellationOutboxReadinessHealthCheckTests`, `AccountingHardeningTests`, `OperationalRecoveryRunbookArchitectureTests`, and `OperationalSurvivabilityCertificationTests` guard reviewed cardinality, export registration, readiness coverage, and documented deployment/DR boundaries.
- residual operational risk: This pass freezes the reviewed operational surfaces inspected here, not every historical metric in the repo and not a full dashboard product or SIEM rollout. Older specialized metrics still require path-specific review before they are promoted to production operational dashboards.
- prevention rule: Reviewed operational meters must be exported through the process OpenTelemetry pipeline, must not emit raw tenant ids, raw order ids, raw Redis keys, or raw invalidation patterns as labels, and must normalize or bucket free-form label values before emission. Singleton fallback telemetry state on reviewed surfaces must also stay bounded rather than accumulating raw tenant/provider/error keys. Recovery pacing options on reviewed rebuild/recovery paths must be startup-validated and bounded, and replay-safe payment-cancellation recovery must remain visible on the readiness surface before operators widen throughput or declare deployment recovery healthy.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Program.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Instrumentation/OperationalTelemetryDimensions.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Accounting/AccountingMetrics.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Instrumentation/AffiliateTelemetry.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Instrumentation/AbandonedCartRecoveryTelemetry.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Instrumentation/StorefrontPublishTelemetry.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Instrumentation/MarketplaceTelemetry.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Instrumentation/AnalyticsReliabilityTelemetry.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/SearchTelemetry.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Instrumentation/InventoryReservationTelemetry.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/HealthChecks/ProductionHealthChecks.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Checkout/CheckoutLockTelemetry.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/OperationalTelemetryCardinalityTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/PaymentCancellationOutboxReadinessHealthCheckTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/operational-survivability-certification-findings.md`

### Storefront navigation duplicate replay and tenant-lineage drift are governed
- title: Navigation projection replay can no longer repeat full-document Mongo replacement or Redis invalidation from the same event, and stale tenant/store-config lineage now fails closed
- affected systems: `NavigationProjectionConsumer`, storefront navigation Mongo read model, Redis navigation/menu/header/footer cache keys, storefront config publication fanout, derivative mutation governance tests
- root cause: Strategic review found that navigation projection reloaded SQL but still used a freshness filter that admitted the same `EventId`; the path also lacked `ProjectionEventFence`, `TenantLineageAuthority`, and `DerivativeProjectionMutationGuard` around its full `StorefrontNavigationDocument` replacement and Redis invalidation side effects.
- impact: STRATEGIC-REVIEW-01 routes navigation projection through current SQL store-config plus active tenant lineage, collapses duplicate/active replay before MongoDB or Redis side effects, fixes the freshness filter to reject the same `EventId`, deletes tenant-scoped navigation projection when SQL lineage is missing or inactive, and runtime-governs the reviewed Mongo replacement and Redis invalidation primitives.
- mitigation status: Runtime and governance hardened on 2026-05-24 for the inspected navigation projection path. `CrossSystemAuthorityRegressionGuardTests` now treats `StorefrontNavigationDocument` full replacement as a high-risk primitive and asserts fence, tenant-lineage, fail-closed, runtime-guard, authority-kind, and duplicate-EventId freshness markers.
- residual operational risk: This does not retrofit every remaining CMS, SEO, smart-offer, customer-session, analytics, notification, support-cache, or marketplace localization projection. Those paths remain specialized and must adopt tenant lineage, replay collapse, dangerous-primitive governance, and convergence telemetry when their public visibility or repair behavior is expanded.
- prevention rule: Tenant-scoped navigation, CMS, SEO, support, or storefront operational cache projection must treat events and Redis/Mongo state as triggers only. Current SQL tenant/domain lineage must decide upsert/delete/no-op, duplicate replay must collapse before side effects, and reviewed full-document replacements or cache invalidations must carry runtime guard assertions and focused tests.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/NavigationProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/ProjectionEventFence.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/TenantLineageAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/DerivativeProjectionMutationGuard.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/SqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemAuthorityRegressionGuardTests.cs`

### High-volume outbox worker throughput controls are configuration-driven
- title: Marketplace event/projection and stock sync outbox workers can now be tuned for backlog recovery without changing replay semantics
- affected systems: `MarketplaceEventOutboxWorker`, `MarketplaceProjectionOutboxWorker`, `MarketplaceEventOutboxProcessor`, `MarketplaceProjectionOutboxProcessor`, `StockSyncOutboxWorker`, `StockSyncOutboxProcessor`, `PaymentCancellationOutboxWorker`, `PaymentCancellationOutboxProcessor`, `MarketplaceEventBackboneOptions`, `StockSyncOutboxOptions`, `PaymentCancellationOutboxOptions`
- root cause: Operational review found that worker cadence, batch draining, and claim lease behavior were partially hard-coded on high-volume outbox paths despite existing option validation. This was an operability/scalability risk rather than a data-corruption risk.
- impact: STABILIZATION-CON-01 makes batch size, max batches per poll, inter-batch delay, poll interval, and claim lease bounded configuration for marketplace and stock outbox workers. PERFORMANCE-CON-01 adds bounded payment-cancellation recovery controls for batch size, max batches per poll, worker cadence, retry threshold, and retry backoff cap, and binds stock-sync/payment-cancellation options from API startup configuration. Operators can tune backlog recovery and large-tenant replay pressure while preserving SQL ordering, retry delay, lifecycle authority, idempotency, and compensation behavior.
- mitigation status: Runtime/governance hardened on 2026-05-24 with focused runtime tests for marketplace projection batch bounding, duplicate-completion cleanup, payment-cancellation batch bounding, configured retry/backoff behavior, plus source governance checks for throughput-option usage.
- residual operational risk: This is not adaptive throttling and does not prove fixed throughput SLOs. Payment-cancellation outbox still has no processing lease columns, so correctness relies on lifecycle idempotency if duplicate active workers overlap. Production tuning still depends on observed dependency latency, backlog age, dead-letter/repeated failure rates, and tenant hotspot behavior. CMS, SEO, notification, analytics, support-cache, feed/export, and report rebuild workers still require path-specific scalability validation when expanded.
- prevention rule: New high-volume outbox, replay, rebuild, cache warmer, or reindex workers must expose bounded operational controls and tests for batch size, lease/cancellation behavior, retry convergence, duplicate collapse, and tenant-safe scope. Do not hard-code recovery cadence on Tier 1 operational paths.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Eventing/MarketplaceEventOutboxWorker.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Eventing/MarketplaceProjectionOutboxProcessor.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Eventing/StockSyncOutboxWorker.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Payment/PaymentCancellationOutboxWorker.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Payment/PaymentCancellationOutboxProcessor.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/MarketplaceProjectionOutboxOperationalScalabilityTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/PaymentCancellationOutboxOperationalScalabilityTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/operational-maturity-scalability-findings.md`

### Operational recovery playbooks now govern high-pressure replay/rebuild behavior
- title: Operator misuse risk is reduced by production recovery runbooks and dangerous primitive governance, but human-run recovery remains a controlled operational risk
- affected systems: replay/dead-letter recovery, rebuild/reindex tooling, queue backlog recovery, Redis/Mongo/Elasticsearch repair, tenant-scoped repair, payment/accounting/fulfillment reconciliation, global/all-tenant platform repair
- root cause: The platform already has strong SQL-first replay/rebuild convergence, but under incident pressure operators could still misread no-op replay, panic over fail-closed deletion, widen tenant scope, mutate derivative stores manually, or use low-level primitives outside the authority model.
- impact: OPERATIONS-RUNBOOKS-01 documents concrete replay, rebuild, backlog, cache/projection, tenant isolation, payment/accounting/fulfillment, drift, telemetry, escalation, anti-pattern, and primitive-governance procedures. `OperationalRecoveryRunbookArchitectureTests` now guard that these procedures continue to cover required recovery areas and shortcut prohibitions.
- mitigation status: Governance/documentation hardened on 2026-05-24. This is an operability improvement, not a runtime authority rewrite.
- residual operational risk: Runbooks reduce unsafe behavior but cannot guarantee every future support tool, global script, cache warmer, feed/export repair, analytics rebuild, or emergency procedure will be safe unless that path updates docs, tests, actor/tenant scope, telemetry, and dangerous primitive approvals in the same change.
- prevention rule: Future operational tooling must state SQL authority owner, tenant scope, actor/platform authority, replay/rebuild convergence behavior, dangerous primitive use, telemetry diagnostics, escalation boundaries, and remaining debt. Do not rely on tribal knowledge or chat-only instructions for incident recovery.
- references: `docs/operations/operational-recovery-runbooks.md`, `docs/architecture/dangerous-operational-primitives.md`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/OperationalRecoveryRunbookArchitectureTests.cs`

### Tenant isolation certification hardened storefront CMS derivative replay and froze regression boundaries
- title: Storefront CMS page projection now has the same replay and dangerous-primitive guardrails as adjacent tenant-scoped public derivative projections
- affected systems: `CmsPageProjectionConsumer`, storefront CMS page Mongo read model, tenant resolution middleware, admin repair/reindex controllers, affiliate public projection, CommerceHub marketplace rebuild, dangerous primitive governance tests, tenant certification docs
- root cause: Tenant isolation hardening already covered trusted tenant resolution, storefront config, navigation, affiliate public projection, CommerceHub rebuild, and admin actor authority. Certification found that storefront CMS page projection still relied on SQL reload, tenant filters, and freshness checks without a durable replay fence or runtime dangerous-primitive guard around full `StorefrontPageDocument` replacement.
- impact: TENANT-CERTIFICATION-01 adds publish/delete `ProjectionEventFence` use before CMS Mongo side effects, active SQL tenant-lineage validation, fail-closed tombstoning when SQL page/tenant authority is missing, and `DerivativeProjectionMutationGuard` for full CMS page replacement under `SqlAuthorityOverlayKind.StorefrontCms`. New certification freeze tests guard trusted tenant resolution, tenant-admin trusted tenant context, global search-control rejection, tenant-scoped derivative identity, CMS replay/guard markers, and certification documentation.
- mitigation status: Runtime, source-governance, and documentation hardening added on 2026-05-24 for the inspected CMS public projection gap and tenant certification boundary.
- residual operational risk: This does not certify every CMS-adjacent, SEO, smart-offer, online-customer, notification, analytics, support-cache, feed/export, report rebuild, cache warmer, or global runbook path. Some inspected paths still use equivalent local SQL checks rather than shared helpers; future docs must state that precisely.
- prevention rule: Tenant-scoped public derivative full replacements must use a domain writer or `DerivativeProjectionMutationGuard` with current SQL authority, freshness, fail-closed behavior, and a named authority kind. Tenant-admin repair must consume trusted tenant context and stay tenant-scoped. Certification findings must remain documented in `docs/architecture/tenant-isolation-certification-findings.md`.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/CmsPageProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/SqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemAuthorityRegressionGuardTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/TenantIsolationCertificationFreezeTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/tenant-isolation-certification-findings.md`

### Tenant-admin compatibility-header scope and authenticated recovery callback crossing are hardened
- title: Tenant-admin operational scope no longer depends on locally parsed compatibility headers, and authenticated recovery-result logging cannot choose another tenant from its body
- affected systems: admin Commerce AI and AI assistance endpoints, abandoned-checkout dashboard, storefront footer administration, abandoned-cart recovery settings/statistics/callback handling, tenant-isolation freeze tests
- root cause: `TenantResolutionMiddleware` correctly strips raw inbound `X-Tenant-Id` and may re-add a verified compatibility output, but several tenant-admin controllers still parsed that header locally. More critically, the mixed anonymous/admin recovery-log endpoint accepted `request.TenantId` after bypassing callback API-key validation for any authenticated admin principal.
- impact: POS-ADMIN-CERTIFICATION-01 PASS 2 migrates those tenant-admin surfaces to `ITenantContext.RequiredTenantId` and binds authenticated recovery-log calls to current trusted tenant context. A privileged cross-tenant body selection now fails closed with `403` and an `admin_tenant_scope_mismatch` diagnostic before tenant settings or persistence are used.
- mitigation status: Runtime and regression-freeze hardening added on 2026-05-25 for the confirmed tenant-admin scope gap.
- residual operational risk: Anonymous automation callback operation intentionally still selects a tenant from its payload because it authenticates through that tenant's configured callback API key and idempotency contract. Storefront/checkout/webhook and other unreviewed analytics/support/cache-warmer/report/export operational surfaces are not universally certified by this change.
- prevention rule: Tenant-admin controllers must consume `ITenantContext.RequiredTenantId` and must not parse `X-Tenant-Id` as operation scope. Mixed external-callback/admin endpoints must bind any authenticated tenant actor's supplied tenant identity to trusted context before skipping external credential checks.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/Admin/AdminAbandonedCartRecoveryController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AdminCommerceAiController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AdminStorefrontFooterController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Admin/AdminAbandonedCartRecoveryScopeTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/TenantIsolationCertificationFreezeTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/tenant-isolation-certification-findings.md`

### Tenant-admin CommerceHub alias finalization and sensitive admin SQL scope are now fail-closed
- title: Tenant-admin search tooling can no longer trigger global CommerceHub alias promotion or catch-up, and inspected customer/payment admin paths no longer rely on ambient scoping for certification-critical SQL scope
- affected systems: `AdminCommerceHubController`, `OnlineCustomersAdminController`, `PaymentMethodsAdminController`, related tenant-admin scope freeze tests, CommerceHub recovery runbooks
- root cause: PASS 2 review found two adjacent governance drifts: tenant-admin CommerceHub finalization still sat beside tenant-scoped search tooling even though alias promotion/catch-up are global recovery operations, and sensitive online-customer/payment-method admin paths still used optional tenant context or relied on EF query filters instead of explicit tenant predicates.
- impact: Tenant-admin CommerceHub finalization now returns `GLOBAL_SEARCH_CONTROL_FORBIDDEN` and does not call global alias-finalization or catch-up services. The inspected online-customer/payment-method admin reads and mutations now use `ITenantContext.RequiredTenantId` plus explicit SQL tenant predicates for customer lookup, activation/deactivation, wishlist checks, refresh-token/session counts, and payment-method sync reads.
- mitigation status: Runtime, source-governance, regression-test, and documentation hardening added on 2026-05-25.
- residual operational risk: This certification hardens the inspected admin customer/payment/search-recovery/WhatsApp surfaces, not every future support/analytics/report/export or mixed operational endpoint. Global search recovery primitives remain intentionally available under platform authority and runbook scope.
- prevention rule: Tenant-admin controllers must not co-host or invoke global alias promotion, migration catch-up, all-tenant rebuild, or equivalent platform recovery primitives. Sensitive admin SQL paths must bind trusted tenant scope explicitly in query predicates rather than relying on compatibility headers or undocumented ambient filters.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AdminCommerceHubController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/OnlineCustomersAdminController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/PaymentMethodsAdminController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Admin/AdminCommerceHubScopeTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/SuperAdminRecoverySafetyCertificationTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/TenantIsolationCertificationFreezeTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/tenant-isolation-certification-findings.md`, `docs/operations/operational-recovery-runbooks.md`

### Additional PASS 2 tenant-admin scope drift on affiliate, coupon, audit, and vendor-finance admin paths is now hardened
- title: Reviewed affiliate and coupon admin scope no longer depends on JWT tenant claims, ambient EF filters, or primary-key lookup behavior, and adjacent audit/vendor-finance paths now use required trusted tenant context
- affected systems: `AdminAffiliateController`, `CouponsController`, `AuditLogsController`, `VendorPayoutMethodsController`, tenant-isolation freeze tests, affiliate/coupon runtime scope tests
- root cause: PASS 2 follow-up review found one remaining controller-local tenant selector and one remaining certification-critical admin SQL drift cluster: affiliate admin still parsed the JWT `tenant_id` claim directly, coupon admin key lookups still used `FindAsync(...)` without explicit tenant binding, and adjacent audit/vendor-finance admin reads still relied on optional tenant accessors.
- impact: On 2026-05-26 the reviewed affiliate admin surface moved to `ITenantContext.RequiredTenantId`, coupon admin list/detail/update/delete/toggle paths now bind `TenantId` explicitly in SQL and fail closed for foreign-tenant ids, and audit/vendor payout admin controllers now consume required trusted tenant context on the inspected paths. New runtime tests prove that a mismatched affiliate tenant claim cannot redirect scope and that foreign-tenant coupon ids do not mutate.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardening, runtime regression tests, source freeze tests, and certification-documentation updates added on 2026-05-26.
- residual operational risk: Reporting/support/cache-warmer/export paths such as `ReportsController` and broader legacy analytics/reporting read surfaces still require path-specific PASS 2 review before broader repo-wide trusted-context claims. The addendum hardens only the inspected affiliate/coupon/audit/vendor-finance admin slice.
- prevention rule: Tenant-admin controllers must not parse tenant scope from JWT claims or compatibility headers, and certification-critical admin SQL row selection must bind trusted tenant context explicitly instead of relying on ambient query filters or `FindAsync(...)` primary-key behavior.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AdminAffiliateController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/CouponsController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AuditLogsController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/VendorPayoutMethodsController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Affiliate/AdminAffiliateTenantScopeTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CouponsControllerCqrsConsistencyTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/TenantIsolationCertificationFreezeTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/tenant-isolation-certification-findings.md`

### Additional PASS 2 category, transport-admin, and platform-taxonomy scope drift is now hardened
- title: Tenant-owned category administration and WhatsApp transport-admin updates no longer rely on ambient filters or primary-key-only update lookup, and global affiliate taxonomy mutation no longer sits behind tenant-owner scope
- affected systems: `CategoriesController`, `WhatsAppAccountService`, `AdminAffiliateCategoryController`, tenant-isolation freeze tests, super-admin platform-authority freeze tests
- root cause: PASS 2 follow-up review found one remaining tenant-owned admin controller using ambient EF filters plus `FindAsync(...)` for category scope, one shared transport-admin service updating tenant-owned rows by primary key only, and one global shared-taxonomy mutation surface still authorized by tenant-owner role instead of explicit platform authority.
- impact: On 2026-05-26 `CategoriesController` moved list/detail/create/update/delete and default-category seeding to explicit tenant predicates, `WhatsAppAccountService.UpdateAccountAsync(...)` and `UpdateTemplateAsync(...)` now require both `Id` and `TenantId`, and `AdminAffiliateCategoryController` create/update/activate/deactivate now require `RequireSuperAdmin` policy. New runtime/source tests prove foreign-tenant category ids fail closed, tenant-local category seeding does not leak across tenants, mismatched WhatsApp tenant/id updates reject, and global affiliate taxonomy mutation remains platform authority.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardening, runtime regression tests, source freeze tests, and certification-documentation updates added on 2026-05-26.
- residual operational risk: Read-heavy reporting/admin-analytics surfaces such as `ReportsController` and older analytics/reporting/export paths still require path-specific PASS 2 review before repo-wide claims about explicit SQL tenant predicates across every admin read boundary.
- prevention rule: Tenant-owned admin SQL selection and seed behavior must bind trusted tenant scope explicitly; reviewed service-layer tenant updates must keep `Id` plus `TenantId` together; and shared global governance rows must remain behind current SQL platform authority instead of tenant-admin roles.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/CategoriesController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/WhatsApp/Services/WhatsAppAccountService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AdminAffiliateCategoryController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CategoriesControllerTenantScopeTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/WhatsAppAccountServiceTenantScopeTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/SuperAdmin/AdminAffiliateCategoryGovernanceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/TenantIsolationCertificationFreezeTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/SuperAdminPlatformAuthorityCertificationTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/tenant-isolation-certification-findings.md`, `docs/architecture/super-admin-platform-authority-certification-findings.md`

### Additional PASS 2 reporting/admin read scope drift is now hardened
- title: Reviewed dashboard and ledger-report admin reads no longer rely on optional tenant accessors or ambient query filters as the certification contract
- affected systems: `DashboardController`, `FinancialReportsController`, tenant-isolation freeze tests, dashboard/reporting tenant-scope runtime tests
- root cause: The existing PASS 2 documentation left read-heavy reporting/dashboard surfaces as explicit debt. `DashboardController` used tenant-keyed caches but still expressed tenant scope only through ambient `ApplicationDbContext` query filters, and `FinancialReportsController` still used optional tenant accessors instead of the trusted required tenant context contract adopted elsewhere.
- impact: On 2026-05-26 `DashboardController` moved to `ITenantContext.RequiredTenantId` and now binds tenant scope explicitly in SQL for sales, customer, supplier, product, stock, item, and payment aggregates. `FinancialReportsController` now consumes `ITenantContext.RequiredTenantId` consistently before invoking the ledger-backed `IFinancialReportService` tenant boundary. New runtime tests prove dashboard reads stay tenant-scoped even when the `DbContext` lacks an ambient tenant filter.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardening, runtime regression tests, source freeze tests, and certification-documentation updates added on 2026-05-26.
- residual operational risk: `ReportsController` and broader legacy analytics/reporting/export/support read surfaces still require path-specific PASS 2 review before broader claims about explicit SQL tenant predicates across every admin read boundary.
- prevention rule: Tenant-admin reporting/dashboard reads must consume trusted tenant context explicitly. Tenant-keyed cache entries and ambient query filters may assist isolation, but they are not the reviewed authority contract for certification-critical admin read scope.
