### Additional PASS 2 finance and shipping admin scope drift is now hardened
- title: Reviewed bank, expense-category, financial-account, and shipping-method admin paths no longer rely on primary-key lookup or ambient filters as the tenant-scope contract
- affected systems: `BanksController`, `ExpenseCategoriesController`, `FinancialAccountsController`, `ShippingMethodsController`, tenant-isolation freeze tests, operational-scope runtime tests
- root cause: PASS 2 follow-up review found another tenant-owned admin SQL drift cluster. Bank, expense-category, financial-account, and shipping-method controllers consumed tenant context during creation or selected paths, but several list/detail/update/delete/toggle/statement/opening-balance/parent-account/shipping-calculation paths still relied on ambient EF query filters, primary-key `FindAsync(...)`, or `BankId`-only transaction selection instead of explicit trusted tenant predicates.
- impact: On 2026-05-26 the reviewed controllers moved to `ITenantContext.RequiredTenantId` and explicit `(TenantId, Id)` row selection on the certified paths. Bank statements now require tenant-owned bank and transaction rows, bank opening-balance writes only create tenant-owned transaction evidence for tenant-owned banks, financial-account parent validation is tenant-local, and shipping update/delete/calculation cannot publish events or compute prices for another tenant's shipping method.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardening, runtime regression tests, source freeze tests, and certification-documentation updates added on 2026-05-26.
- residual operational risk: `SettingsController`, `ReportsController`, and broader legacy settings/reporting/analytics/export/support/cache-warmer surfaces still require path-specific PASS 2 review before broader repo-wide explicit-tenant-predicate claims. This addendum hardens only the inspected finance/shipping configuration slice.
- prevention rule: Tenant-owned admin SQL row selection must bind trusted tenant context explicitly instead of relying on ambient query filters or primary-key lookup behavior. Bank statement/opening-balance operations must bind both bank ownership and transaction tenant scope; financial-account parent references and shipping-method calculations must remain tenant-local.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/BanksController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/ExpenseCategoriesController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/FinancialAccountsController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/ShippingMethodsController.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Admin/AdminTenantOperationalScopeTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/TenantIsolationCertificationFreezeTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/tenant-isolation-certification-findings.md`

### Marketplace payment recovery and reservation validation no longer trust raw reservation or stock evidence
- title: Marketplace online payment retry and reservation reuse now fail closed when current SQL inventory lineage no longer proves authority, including multi-tenant marketplace order composition
- affected systems: `InventoryConsistencyService`, `CommerceHubPaymentRecoveryAuthorityService`, `ProductVariantInventoryLineageAuthority`, CommerceHub payment recovery, marketplace reservation validation, inventory certification freeze tests
- root cause: Prior PRODUCT-CON/CHECKOUT hardening covered current inventory lineage on checkout and fulfillment boundaries, but the marketplace payment recovery path still duplicated narrower checks and the marketplace reservation validation path assumed a single tenant derived from the first order item. Two adjacent residual gaps remained on the same boundary: CommerceHub reservation creation still accepted raw stock rows before filtering current stock-owner lineage, and CommerceHub delivery commit reused the reservation-creation publication guard even though fulfillment should consume already-authorized stock from current inventory lineage rather than from current public publication visibility.
- impact: PASS 3 adds cross-tenant current reservation filtering, exact reservation-ownership validation for marketplace order items, current stock-owner filtering, and current product publication checks on payment recovery. Reservation creation now fails closed when only stale warehouse/stock-owner rows still show raw availability. CommerceHub delivery commit now stays inventory-lineage-authoritative instead of publication-authoritative, while return receipt still revalidates current stock ownership before restoring resellable or damaged quantities.
- mitigation status: Runtime, regression-freeze tests, and certification documentation hardened on 2026-05-25.
- residual operational risk: This closes the reviewed marketplace payment/recovery slice, not every future stock transfer, reservation migration, warehouse reassignment, feed/export, or bulk inventory import/reconciliation worker. Those remain path-specific certification debt.
- prevention rule: Reservation-backed payment, checkout, fulfillment, rebuild, and return-receipt paths must use current inventory lineage helpers and exact current SQL order-item ownership; do not treat raw active reservations, historical committed reservations, raw stock rows, or public stock visibility as sufficient authority. Reservation creation must filter candidate stock rows through current stock-owner lineage before availability is accepted, and delivery commit for an existing order item must not depend on current public publication visibility once current SQL inventory lineage still proves the reserved stock owner.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/InventoryConsistencyService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentRecoveryAuthorityService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Reliability/ProductVariantInventoryLineageAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPaymentRecoveryAuthorityServiceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/CommerceHub/InventoryConsistencyServiceTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CheckoutConcurrencyHardeningSourceTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/product-inventory-reservation-certification-findings.md`

### Legacy availability reads and derivative stock totals no longer trust raw undeleted stock rows
- title: Reviewed availability and stock-total recomposition paths now fail closed when only stale stock-owner lineage still inflates raw SQL stock totals
- affected systems: `ProductVariantStockRepository`, `ProductVariantStockMutationSupport`, `InventoryConsistencyService`, `ProductVariantProjectionConsumer`, `CommerceHubProjectionConsumer`, CommerceHub cart/checkout availability, stock fanout, storefront variant stock projections, CommerceHub variant stock projections
- root cause: Prior PRODUCT-CON/PASS 3 hardening focused on reservation creation, consumption, payment recovery, and fulfillment lineage, but several reviewed read/fanout/projection paths still recomputed available or total quantity by summing undeleted `ProductVariantStocks` rows directly. Inactive warehouses or stale stock-owner lineage could therefore overstate derivative totals even after mutation paths were hardened.
- impact: On 2026-05-27 the reviewed repository availability reads and reviewed derivative stock-total recomposition paths moved under current stock-owner lineage. Cart/checkout availability, stock-sync/event total fanout, storefront variant projection totals, and CommerceHub variant projection totals now ignore inactive-warehouse and stale stock-owner rows instead of publishing inflated totals.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardening, focused runtime tests, source freeze tests, and certification-documentation updates added on 2026-05-27.
- residual operational risk: This addendum closes the reviewed availability/fanout/projection slice, not every future feed/export, warehouse reassignment, bulk import, inventory repair, or bespoke reporting path that may aggregate stock. Those remain path-specific certification debt.
- prevention rule: Reviewed availability reads and reviewed derivative stock-total recomposition must not sum undeleted `ProductVariantStocks` rows directly. Use current stock-owner lineage (`WhereCurrentInventoryStockOwner(...)`, `WhereInventoryReservableLineage(...)`, and equivalent helper checks) before accepting quantity as cart, checkout, fanout, storefront, or CommerceHub authority.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/Repositories/ProductVariantStockRepository.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Inventory/ProductVariantStockMutationSupport.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/InventoryConsistencyService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/ProductVariantProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/CommerceHubProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/ProductVariantMergeSplitAuthorityTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/product-inventory-reservation-certification-findings.md`

### SQL fallback checkout enrichment and storefront search overlay no longer trust stale variant or warehouse lineage
- title: Reviewed SQL fallback and storefront/search overlay paths now fail closed when only stale variant lifecycle or stale stock-owner lineage survives in raw SQL rows
- affected systems: `ProductVariantReadRepository`, `ProductSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay`, storefront checkout cart enrichment fallback, storefront/search visibility repair, affiliate/search/storefront product overlay reuse
- root cause: Prior PASS 3 hardening fixed reviewed reservation, stock fanout, and projection totals, but two adjacent SQL-backed read paths still bypassed the reviewed lineage filters. `ProductVariantReadRepository` SQL fallback selected variants by raw tenant/id plus undeleted product rows and summed raw stock rows, while `ProductSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay` still summed `variant.Stocks` directly without revalidating active warehouse lineage.
- impact: On 2026-05-27 the reviewed SQL fallback checkout path moved under `WherePubliclyVisibleLineage(...)` plus `WhereCurrentInventoryStockOwner(...)`, and `ProductSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay` now recomposes variant totals only from active current stock-owner lineage. Superseded default variants, unpublished/offline parent products, inactive warehouses, and stale stock-owner rows can no longer survive those reviewed fallback/overlay boundaries merely because raw SQL rows still exist.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardening, focused runtime tests, source freeze tests, and certification-documentation updates added on 2026-05-27.
- residual operational risk: This closes the reviewed SQL fallback/storefront-search slice, not every future bespoke SQL fallback, cache warmer, feed/export reader, recommendation overlay, or reporting path that may compose variant stock or default-variant lineage. Those remain path-specific certification debt.
- prevention rule: Reviewed SQL fallback read paths and reviewed storefront/search SQL overlays must not select variants by raw undeleted rows alone or sum raw `variant.Stocks` directly. Recompose through current public variant lineage plus current stock-owner lineage before exposing checkout/storefront/search availability.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/MongoDB/Services/ProductVariantReadRepository.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Elasticsearch/Services/ProductSearchSqlAuthorityOverlay.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/ProductInventorySqlAuthorityFallbackTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/ReadModelStockConsistencyArchitectureTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/product-inventory-reservation-certification-findings.md`

### Storefront cart fallback, affiliate intelligence rebuild, and stock compensation replay no longer trust raw stock rows
- title: Reviewed derivative SQL snapshot and compensation-replay paths now fail closed when only stale warehouse or stock-owner lineage survives in raw SQL stock rows
- affected systems: `CartService`, `AffiliateProductIntelligenceProjectionConsumer`, `ProjectionFailureCompensationService`, storefront cart fallback, affiliate intelligence stock rebuild, stock-sync terminal compensation replay
- root cause: Prior PASS 3 addenda hardened reviewed mutation, fanout, checkout fallback, and storefront/search overlay paths, but three adjacent derivative SQL snapshot paths still summed undeleted `ProductVariantStocks` rows directly. Storefront cart SQL fallback could preserve quantity from inactive warehouses, affiliate intelligence stock rebuild could keep stale stock visibility, and stock-sync terminal compensation could replay inflated totals back into derivative consumers.
- impact: On 2026-05-27 these reviewed cart/affiliate/recovery paths moved under `WhereCurrentInventoryStockOwner(...)`. Storefront cart fallback, affiliate intelligence SQL stock snapshots, and replacement stock-sync replay totals now ignore inactive-warehouse and stale stock-owner rows instead of preserving derivative stock visibility from raw evidence.
- mitigation status: Runtime hardening, focused runtime tests, source freeze tests, and certification-documentation updates added on 2026-05-27.
- residual operational risk: This addendum closes the reviewed cart/affiliate/compensation slice, not every admin/POS/reporting/read-only stock summary, feed/export writer, repair script, or bespoke derivative stock snapshot that may still aggregate raw stock rows outside public or reservation authority. Those remain path-specific certification debt.
- prevention rule: Reviewed storefront cart fallback, affiliate intelligence stock rebuild, and compensation replay paths must not sum raw undeleted `ProductVariantStocks` rows directly. Recompose through current stock-owner lineage before preserving cart quantity, affiliate visibility, or a "fresh" replay snapshot.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/Cart/CartService.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Consumers/Affiliate/AffiliateProductIntelligenceProjectionConsumer.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Eventing/ProjectionFailureCompensation.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/ProductInventorySqlAuthorityFallbackTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/StockSyncOutboxProcessorTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/StorefrontStockAuthorityArchitectureTests.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/GlobalReliabilityInfrastructureExtractionTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/product-inventory-reservation-certification-findings.md`

### Normal read-model lag after SQL commit
- title: SQL authority can be correct before MongoDB, Redis, Elasticsearch, or downstream consumers catch up
- affected systems: Storefront, CommerceHub, affiliate read models, search, caches, analytics projections
- root cause: The system is intentionally CQRS and outbox-driven, with PostgreSQL as the only authoritative write model.
- impact: Short-lived stale reads, cache lag, and delayed search visibility are expected even when the system is healthy.
- mitigation status: Intentional. This is the designed consistency model, not a defect by itself.
- prevention rule: Do not treat read-model freshness as business authority. Escalate only when lag exceeds recovery expectations or breaks a path designated as critical.
- references: `.ai-context/ARCHITECTURE_TRUTH.md`, `.ai-context/PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`

### Fail-closed availability under financial uncertainty
- title: Outward money movement is intentionally blocked when authoritative backlog or external money truth is uncertain
- affected systems: CommerceHub checkout intake, refunds, payouts, vendor settlement controls, affiliate payout execution
- root cause: The platform chooses integrity over availability whenever unresolved accounting, compensation, webhook, payout, or backbone state makes money movement unsafe.
- impact: Operators can see blocked actions even when the tenant appears healthy from the UI.
- mitigation status: Intentional. The gate is designed to stop unsafe movement rather than maximize throughput.
- prevention rule: Never bypass readiness gates to restore availability. Remove the ambiguity or add stronger scope evidence first.
- references: `.ai-context/ARCHITECTURE_TRUTH.md`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/Finance/CommerceHubFinancialReadinessGate.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Services/Affiliate/AffiliateSettlementOrchestrator.cs`

### Reconciliation states are first-class truth states
- title: `Processing` and `ReconciliationRequired` are intentionally durable states, not accidental limbo
- affected systems: CommerceHub refunds, CommerceHub payouts, carrier shipment registration and cancellation
- root cause: External execution certainty is outside the local SQL transaction boundary, so the system must model "authorized but not yet proven" explicitly.
- impact: Some records remain actionable rather than terminal until evidence arrives or operators reconcile them.
- mitigation status: Intentional, with hosted recovery workers and operator-facing audit trails.
- prevention rule: Treat reconciliation states as pending authority work, not as success or failure. Require explicit evidence before leaving them.
- references: `.ai-context/ARCHITECTURE_TRUTH.md`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubRefundRecoveryHostedService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPayoutReconciliationHostedService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/Shipping/ShipmentAuthorityRecoveryHostedService.cs`

## THEORETICAL_RISK

### Operational helper adoption drift can weaken future replay/rebuild assurance
- title: Some operational repair scripts may enforce tenant lineage through local SQL filters instead of the shared lineage helper, making future reviews easier to overstate
- affected systems: AF16E affiliate repair/reindex script, tenant-scoped repair/rebuild runbooks, future cache warmers, future feed/export writers, support/admin repair tools
- root cause: The platform has incremental helper adoption. CHAOS-CON-01 found that AF16E repair/reindex is tenant-scoped and uses active SQL tenant filtering plus `TenantScopedDerivativeKey`, but does not directly call `TenantLineageAuthority` even though some governance wording implied shared-helper usage.
- impact: No cross-tenant leakage or stale tenant visibility resurrection was reproduced on the inspected path. The risk is governance drift: future maintainers may assume all tenant-scoped operational repair paths use the same runtime helper and telemetry chokepoint when a path actually carries an equivalent local check.
- mitigation status: Classified as theoretical after CHAOS-CON-01. Focused chaos validation now asserts the actual controls: tenant-scoped admin entrypoint, active SQL tenant filtering, tenant-scoped derivative keys, SQL-first rebuild declarations, replay fencing, and dangerous primitive guards on inspected operational surfaces.
- residual operational risk: Local equivalent controls are harder to audit and easier to regress than shared helper calls. Future operational scripts and runbooks can still drift if they add all-tenant fanout, product-id-only derivative keys, or stale repair inputs without SQL lineage proof and convergence telemetry.
- prevention rule: Documentation and tests must state whether a path uses `TenantLineageAuthority` or an equivalent local SQL-lineage boundary. New tenant-scoped repair/rebuild/cache/export paths should prefer shared helpers, tenant-scoped derivative keys, trusted tenant context, actor authority where request-driven, and focused chaos/replay tests in the same change.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Scripts/AF16E_ReindexAffiliateProductsWithStorefrontUrl.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Infrastructure/SemanticConvergenceChaosValidationTests.cs`, `.ai-context/PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`, `.ai-context/ARCHITECTURE_TRUTH.md`

### Future bulk-write, raw-SQL, or raw-bus paths could bypass application guards
- title: New mutation or publish paths could reintroduce tenant, lifecycle, ordering, or event-scope violations if they bypass middleware, interceptors, service boundaries, or the central event publisher
- affected systems: Any future financial, order, fulfillment, inventory, tenant-owned write path, or authoritative integration-event path
- root cause: Several critical invariants are enforced in application code and architecture tests rather than being impossible to violate at the database level in every case.
- impact: A future `ExecuteUpdate`, raw SQL patch, direct provider callback path, raw `IPublishEndpoint`/`IBus`/`ISendEndpointProvider` dependency, or direct MassTransit `Publish` call could mutate or distribute protected state without ledger, tenant, lifecycle, aggregate-ordering, or affected-tenant safeguards.
- mitigation status: Guarded by CI after the 2026-05-08 regression pass. The risk remains theoretical for future changes, but new direct SQL/bulk mutation files and new raw MassTransit publisher usage in API/infrastructure application code now fail architecture tests unless explicitly reviewed and allowlisted.
- prevention rule: Any new bulk update, raw SQL mutation, provider callback, or authoritative event publish path that changes or distributes business state must either use an existing authority service / `IEventPublisher` boundary or be added to the explicit architecture-test allowlist with documented invariant enforcement.
- references: `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/TenantSaveChangesInterceptor.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/PaymentStatusSaveChangesInterceptor.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/FulfillmentStatusSaveChangesInterceptor.cs`, `PointOfSale.Infrastructure/Data/OrderStatusSaveChangesInterceptor.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/CrossSystemAuthorityRegressionGuardTests.cs`

### Future multi-tenant events can become ambiguous if schema discipline slips
- title: Multi-tenant backlog gating and event ordering depend on explicit affected-tenant payload contracts
- affected systems: CommerceHub multi-store events, affiliate cross-store events, readiness gates, critical outbox ordering
- root cause: Scope resolution for distributed events depends on payloads carrying explicit affected-tenant arrays or unambiguous tenant identifiers.
- impact: A new ambiguous event contract could cause over-blocking, under-scoping, or global fail-closed behavior until the payload schema is corrected.
- mitigation status: Guarded by runtime publisher validation and CI after the 2026-05-08 regression pass. `AffectedTenantScopeResolver` still fails closed for malformed persisted payloads, new in-process authoritative event types with affected-tenant arrays must implement `IAffectedTenantScopedIntegrationEvent`, and application/business code is blocked from bypassing `IEventPublisher` with raw MassTransit publishers.
- prevention rule: Every event that can affect more than one tenant must carry explicit `AffectedTenantIds` or `AffectedStoreIds`, implement `IAffectedTenantScopedIntegrationEvent`, and be published through `IEventPublisher` so tenant scope and aggregate ordering are validated. Raw MassTransit publishing is reserved for reviewed event-bus/outbox infrastructure only.
- references: `PointOfSale.Core/Events/AffectedTenantScopeResolver.cs`, `PointOfSale.Core/Events/IntegrationEventAuthority.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/Finance/CommerceHubFinancialReadinessGate.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/EventOutboxConsistencyArchitectureTests.cs`
## PASS 13.7 CommerceHub Price Override Runtime Risks

- Enabling a tenant before applying `20260531170000_ActivateCommerceHubPriceOverrides` will fail persistence.
- Deployment dashboards and alert rules must scrape `PointOfSale.CommerceHub.PriceOverride` resolver override/fallback counters and compare SQL, Mongo, and Elasticsearch `PriceSource` / `OverridePresent` parity.
- New CommerceHub pricing paths must not read `CommerceHubPrice` or `EffectiveOnlinePrice` directly for customer-visible marketplace pricing; they must use `ICommerceHubPriceAuthority`.
## PASS 14.1 CommerceHub Publication Lifecycle Withdrawal Risk

- root cause: the tenant-owned unpublish handler required an active, undeleted variant before clearing SQL listing intent. Public overlays failed closed, but variant deletion or replacement could strand stale listing intent and governance restore evidence.
- impact: `UnpublishFromCommerceHubHandler` now resolves the tenant-owned listing first, explicitly scopes `IgnoreQueryFilters()` reads by tenant, allows withdrawal after variant removal, clears stale restore evidence through `listing.Unpublish()`, and emits convergence with a fail-closed zero price when variant authority is absent.
- residual operational risk: listings remain boolean-state administrative rows, derivative stores may lag, and no universal parity scanner or full chaos harness exists.
- prevention rule: visibility-reducing withdrawal, cleanup, and suppression paths must not require still-live product, variant, category, pricing, or inventory authority. They must preserve tenant ownership checks, clear stale restore evidence, trigger derivative convergence, and update PASS 14.1 tests/docs in the same change.

## PASS 14.2 Merchant CommerceHub Operations Production-Certification Gap

- impact: The merchant can operate small-catalog single-listing and finance workflows, but cannot efficiently manage CommerceHub at scale or observe all listing states from POS Admin.
- current containment: Public visibility remains SQL-overlay hardened by PASS 14.1 and earlier passes. PASS 14.2 preserves actionable listing validation failures in the UI and records the incomplete operational scope explicitly.
- mitigation status: Mitigated for core listing operations by PASS 14.3. SQL-authoritative listing search, filters, suppression visibility, orphan visibility, KPIs, analytics, notifications, and bulk operations with per-row outcomes are now implemented.
- residual operational risk: Parity/drift scanner, alert dashboards, bulk attribute editing, immutable notification inbox, and deeper analytics segmentation remain operational maturity debt.
- prevention rule: Future merchant listing search, filters, suppression visibility, orphan cleanup, bulk operations, KPI, notification, analytics, or parity-monitoring changes must preserve tenant scope, current SQL authority, marketplace governance precedence, per-row bulk outcomes, and regression coverage.
- references: `PointOfSale.Web/src/pages/CommerceHubSettings.jsx`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/MerchantCommerceHubOperationsCertificationTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/merchant-commercehub-operations-certification-findings.md`

## PASS 14.3 Merchant Listing Operations Console Runtime Risks

- impact: Core merchant listing operations are production-capable, but operators still need broader parity and alerting to compare SQL authority against MongoDB and Elasticsearch projections during incidents.
- current containment: Merchant-facing rows, KPIs, analytics, notifications, search, filters, and bulk operations derive from SQL authority and fail closed for governance-hidden or orphaned visibility-increasing actions.
- residual operational risk: No universal SQL-vs-derivative drift scanner, dedicated alert pack, bulk attribute editor, immutable notification inbox, or segmented listing analytics workspace was introduced.
- prevention rule: Do not move merchant listing KPIs, filters, suppression/orphan status, or bulk authority to frontend state, MongoDB, Elasticsearch, Redis, or event payloads. Visibility-increasing bulk operations must keep current SQL product/variant/category/governance checks.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Controllers/AdminCommerceHubController.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/DTOs/CommerceHub/CommerceHubAdminDtos.cs`, `PointOfSale.Web/src/pages/CommerceHubSettings.jsx`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/MerchantCommerceHubOperationsCertificationTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/merchant-commercehub-operations-certification-findings.md`

- impact: Core Super Admin marketplace governance is production-capable, but operators still need stronger parity and alerting to compare SQL governance truth against MongoDB and Elasticsearch visibility during incidents.
- current containment: Super Admin governance dashboard, listing search, filters, suppression/restore actions, tenant suspension/restore actions, and frontend controls derive from SQL authority and audit actor/reason. Listing restore fails closed unless current SQL tenant, vendor, product, variant, and category authority accept visibility.
- residual operational risk: No dedicated SQL-vs-derivative governance parity dashboard, governance telemetry alert pack, immutable operator notification inbox, vendor-governance shortcuts from listing detail, or production query-plan/index review was introduced.
- prevention rule: Do not move governance dashboard counts, listing search, suppression state, vendor governance state, tenant governance state, or restore authority to frontend state, MongoDB, Elasticsearch, Redis, or event payloads. Super Admin governance must remain visibility-only and must not mutate merchant-owned CommerceHub pricing.

They must recompose current SQL authority and update `MarketplaceOperationsConsoleCertificationTests`, `docs/architecture/marketplace-operations-console-certification-findings.md`, and `.ai-context` PASS 15.2 entries in the same change.

## PASS 15.3 Marketplace Finance & Risk Runtime Risks

- impact: Core CommerceHub finance is production-capable under SQL/ledger authority, but operators still need stronger parity drills, alert packs, immutable notifications, production query-plan review, and a PostgreSQL-backed chaos harness before high-scale outward money automation.
- current containment: Order/sub-order snapshots, commission snapshots, vendor earnings, refunds, payouts, financial transactions, accounting outbox rows, and reconciliation states are SQL-authoritative. Existing controls include delivered financial workflow payment lifecycle scoping, snapshot arithmetic validation, earning repricing locks, ledger-confirmed payout eligibility, serializable payout/refund/ledger transactions, external payout handoff/evidence fences, refund overrun validation, refund/payout overlap blocking, readiness gates, and ledger fingerprint idempotency.
- residual operational risk: Source-level and in-memory coverage freeze the architecture, but there is not yet a full concurrent PostgreSQL chaos suite for payout/refund/replay/recovery race execution or dedicated production finance alert pack.
- prevention rule: Future CommerceHub order, payment, commission, refund, payout, balance, settlement, accounting, recovery, reconciliation, dashboard, or operator-control changes must preserve SQL/ledger authority, PASS 13.7 merchant price ownership, immutable ledger fingerprinting, readiness-gated outward money movement, and update `MarketplaceFinanceRiskCertificationTests`, `docs/architecture/commercehub-finance-risk-certification-findings.md`, and `.ai-context` PASS 15.3 entries in the same change.
- references: `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubFinancialWorkflowService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/FinancialLedger/CommerceHubFinancialLedgerService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubPayoutService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/CommerceHubRefundService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/RefundExecutionService.cs`, `PointOfSale.API/Services/CommerceHub/Finance/CommerceHubFinancialReadinessGate.cs`, `PointOfSale.Tests/Architecture/MarketplaceFinanceRiskCertificationTests.cs`, `docs/architecture/commercehub-finance-risk-certification-findings.md`

## PASS 15.4 CommerceHub Emergency Operations Runtime Risks

- impact: CommerceHub emergency operations are now certified for core incident response, including explicit payment-intake and outward-money emergency stops. Operators still need stronger operational UX for universal recovery-worker pause/resume, central emergency dashboards, immutable notifications, alert packs, and full chaos drills.
- current containment: Marketplace visibility can be stopped by listing suppression, tenant suspension, vendor governance, SQL-derived reindex, and verification. `CommerceHub:EmergencyStops` blocks payment intake and outward money movement at `CommerceHubFinancialReadinessGate` before checkout/payment, payout, or refund execution.
- residual operational risk: Recovery/replay stop is only partially certified. Operators can cancel, avoid starting broad work, pace reindex/recovery, and restart SQL-first, but there is no universal durable pause/resume ledger for every hosted worker, outbox processor, and replay consumer.
- prevention rule: Future CommerceHub emergency, break-glass, incident-response, visibility-stop, payment-stop, payout-stop, refund-stop, recovery-stop, rollback, diagnostic, monitoring, or operator-control changes must preserve SQL/ledger authority, explicit auditability, fail-closed emergency stops, and update `MarketplaceEmergencyOperationsCertificationTests`, `docs/architecture/commercehub-emergency-operations-certification-findings.md`, runbooks, and `.ai-context` PASS 15.4 entries in the same change.

## PASS 16.5E CommerceHub refund and financial reversal runtime certifiable; refund monitoring and chaos proof remain incomplete

- impact: CommerceHub can reverse inspected customer refunds, COD commission debt, online-payment payables, post-payout refund impact, return receipt, payout interaction, settlement interaction, ledgers, and reconciliation from SQL/ledger authority without treating frontend state or derivative stores as money truth.
- current containment: `CustomerRefundService`, `SuperAdminRefundsService`, `ReturnManagementService`, `CommerceHubRefundService`, `RefundExecutionService`, `CommerceHubRefundRecoveryHostedService`, `FinancialReconciliationService`, and the financial ledger service enforce customer/order ownership, Super Admin actor authority, POS tenant return authority, serializable locks, transition policies, idempotency, outward-money readiness gates, payout-overlap blocking, financial state validation, cumulative refund overrun checks, ledger fingerprinting, audit evidence, and reconciliation-required states.
- residual risk: Public launch still lacks deployment-verified refund alert packs, refund external-truth dashboards, external WORM/SIEM retention, production query-plan review, and a PostgreSQL-backed chaos harness for concurrent refund, return, payout, settlement, replay, recovery, stale processing, and evidence races.
- prevention rule: Do not introduce CommerceHub refund, return, reversal, dashboard, replay, recovery, payout, settlement, COD debt, online-payment payable, or reconciliation paths that derive authority from frontend state, MongoDB, Redis, Elasticsearch, provider callbacks alone, queue payloads, dashboard counters, or reports. Preserve PASS 16.5E regression freezes and update `docs/architecture/commercehub-refund-financial-reversal-certification-findings.md` with future changes.
